# Dia feature parity

Dia = installed build **1.49.1 (87398), Chromium 153** plus the public changelog up to **v1.50.0 (2026‑09‑24)**;
visual rows re-checked against **1.50.1 (87750)** (`docs/dia-spec.md` › "1.50 Sunglow").
Ardia = this working tree on **2026‑09‑25, after the Chrome migration**: then our patched Chrome-style CEF
(154.0.28), where every app window was Chrome's own Browser window (since 0.2.0) and every tab a real Chrome tab of
it, hosted in our React Native views. Since 0.2.22 the app runs on ArdiaCore, Chrome's own framework with our layer
(`docs/ardiacore-parity.md`), and since 0.2.31 on plain Chromium with no CEF left (`docs/engine-build.md`); the rows
hold for all three, and rows changed since say so.

How this audit was done: every non-AI row re-read against the code (then `packages/cef`, apps/browser/src,
packages/shell, packages/core, the Xcode project), not against earlier claims. Runtime evidence came from the migration
ledger, `docs/migration-status.md`, now in git history (`git show 2713787d:docs/migration-status.md`); "ledger N" below
means its test N. Nothing was built or run for this audit.

Legend: **✅** done · **🧪** built, but the deciding test needs the user present (see the checklist) · **🟡** partial ·
**❌** missing · **⛔** blocked by something outside our control (named in the Gap) · **⏸** deferred (AI), on purpose ·
**—** not applicable

## Summary

**307 feature rows** (§1–§25, recounted from the tables on 2026-09-26):

| ✅ done | 🧪 needs the user | 🟡 partial | ❌ missing | ⛔ blocked | ⏸ deferred (AI) | — n/a |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 231 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 59 | 14 |

Of the 234 rows that count (not ⏸ or —), 231 are done (99 %), 232 with the one 🧪 row (Cast). The one 🟡 row is
passkeys (iCloud Keychain waits on Apple). Keyboard shortcuts: every Dia and Chrome
shortcut is bound (Dia's where they clash) except Chat (⏸) and a few for Chrome's own hidden UI. Menus: all ten exist and match.

The summary before this recount (304 rows, 207 ✅, 9 🟡, 7 ⛔) didn't match its tables, which held 307 rows: 211 ✅,
7 🟡, 8 ⛔ (its 🟡 count included the View and Help menu rows, which sit outside §1–§25).

The previous summary (296 rows, 181 ✅) didn't match its own tables, which held 301 rows and 199 ✅. This audit
adds three rows: "Filling saved logins in pages" (§16), "Protected video (Widevine DRM)" (§18) and the
Passwords / Autofill / Extensions panes (§22).

What changed since the last audit, in rows (mostly the migration):
- Now done: extensions (Chrome's real `chrome.tabs`, actions, commands, per-profile, Web Store installs through
  Chrome's own flow) and the Extensions menu, Chrome's password manager with our save prompt, card and address
  storage, H.264/AAC, the close-tabs wording in the top strip. Better but still partial: moving a tab between
  windows keeps its page (no drag between windows yet), the share picker can share a tab (no "Share this tab
  instead" bar), Edit › AutoFill exists (it opens Settings).
- Now needs the user present (🧪): Chrome's autofill dropdowns and password generation, video fullscreen, the real
  trackpad swipe, the file picker.
- No longer blocked, now ours to build (⛔ → ❌): back/forward history on Reopen Closed Tab and Duplicate, Cast,
  Web Bluetooth. Chrome's own code for each is in the engine now.
- Lost in the migration, since rebuilt on Chrome's PiP window: the video-PiP edge stash and host pill
  (`ArdiaPictureInPicture.mm` now).
- Since, from the polish pass (2026-09-25, instance `polish`): done, extension search engines, Edit › AutoFill at
  the focused field (engine hook `CEF_AC_AUTOFILL_TRIGGER`) and the PiP stash / host pill / Keep on Top on Chrome's
  PiP window (🟡 → ✅); the Raycast extension is built and needs the user to install it (🟡 → 🧪, checklist step 14).
- Since, from R2 (2026-09-25): done, the side panel, "Share this tab instead" and the Bluetooth chooser (❌ → ✅);
  built, needing the user: dragging tabs between windows and Cast (🟡 / ❌ → 🧪, checklist steps 11–12).
- Since, from the partial-rows pass (2026-09-26): done, the Sunglow row (checked side by side with Dia 1.50.1),
  the host-only URL bar (Dia's rule, from its binary), the Help menu (Send Feedback opens a GitHub issue) and the
  onboarding row (Dia's Trial Guide is —) (🟡 → ✅); the overflow menu and the mic button have nothing left but
  Sync and AI (🟡 → ⛔ / ⏸); regional block lists now follow the system languages (§17 stays 🟡 for list freshness).
- Since, from the loose-ends pass (2026-09-26, after 0.2.2): done, the block lists' freshness (every release moves
  to uBlock Origin Lite's latest, `scripts/update-ubol.sh`) and auto-updates (notarized, signed releases through
  the GitHub appcast since 0.2.1) and Translate, on Apple's on-device Translation framework (macOS 26)
  (🟡 / ⛔ → ✅). Every ⛔ row now names what blocks it; Dia's sidebar import is blocked by Dia's keychain access
  group, not by missing code.
- Since, from the headless verification pass (2026-09-26, hidden instances, the screen locked; tests in
  `docs/migration-status.md` › "Headless verification of the 🧪 rows"): eight of the nine 🧪 rows are verified end to
  end but for what only hands and eyes can check (🧪 → ✅). The pass found and fixed: a crash of the whole app on
  focusing a card field with a saved card on a secure page, Chrome's password generation never being offered (it
  needed password sync), leaving window full screen leaving the page full screen, a full-screen page in another
  profile's window taking the window out of full screen, and AppleScript's `make new window with properties
  {URL:…}` failing. Cast stays 🧪: casting itself needs a device.
- Since, from the sync pass (2026-09-26): Sync is built without a server (⛔ → ✅, 5 rows: E2E-encrypted sync,
  per-profile sync, synced devices' tabs, the overflow menu's synced devices, the Sync pane). Every Mac reads and
  writes sealed files in a folder the user picks, iCloud Drive by default, keyed by Dia's 24-word phrase
  (`docs/sync.md`). Verified by unit tests and two- and three-instance end-to-end runs through a temporary folder;
  the real two-Mac run over iCloud Drive is checklist step 15. The run found and fixed ids that two Macs launched
  in the same millisecond made alike (store ids now have a random part).
- Still blocked: Widevine in Chromium (Ardia has none; protected video plays in WebKit with FairPlay), Dia's sidebar import, and iCloud Keychain passkeys (inside the 🟡 passkeys row).

## Remaining work

R1–R3, the three packages that followed the Chrome migration (tab state on Chrome, Chrome surfaces in our UI, app
loose ends), are done; "(R1)" and the like below say which one did a row. Their plans, with the CEF-era files each
owned, are in git history (`git show 2713787d:docs/dia-feature-parity.md`).

The 🧪 row (Cast) needs no code until the user checklist below finds a problem. The checklist also keeps the hand and eye checks left in rows that are ✅.

### ⛔ Blocked, and what would unblock it
| Item | Blocked by | Unblocks when |
|---|---|---|
| Widevine in Chromium (§18; WebKit pages play protected video with FairPlay) | Ardia has no Widevine support: Google licenses the CDM and VMP signing to browsers, and Ardia has neither, so DRM sites open in WebKit (§18) | Google grants a Widevine licence and VMP signing |
| iCloud Keychain passkeys (inside §16's passkeys row) | Apple hasn't granted `com.apple.developer.web-browser.public-key-credential`, which macOS requires before a browser may use iCloud Keychain passkeys for any site | the grant arrives: download the Developer ID profile again; `release.sh` then signs with `Ardia-ICloudPasskeys.entitlements` on its own, and Settings › Advanced › Web engine › How engines differ › Passkeys asks macOS for browser passkey access (ledger 42) |

## Needs the user present: test script (about 35 minutes)

These need a key window, Touch ID, a phone, Spaces, or eyes on Dia, so no agent can run them. Run them in one
sitting; note the step number and what you saw for anything that fails.

**0. Setup (before the clock; build takes a few minutes).** Metro must be running on :8081, as usual.
```sh
cd ~/Documents/ardia/apps/browser
xcodebuild -workspace macos/Ardia.xcworkspace -scheme Ardia-macOS -derivedDataPath build-user \
  -destination 'platform=macOS,arch=arm64' -configuration Debug build 2>&1 | grep -E "error:|\*\* BUILD"
mkdir -p /tmp/ac-forms && cd /tmp/ac-forms
echo '<h1>Done</h1>' > done.html
echo '<form action="done.html"><input name="user" autocomplete="username" placeholder="Email"><input name="pass" type="password" autocomplete="current-password" placeholder="Password"><button>Sign in</button></form><p><input type="file"></p>' > login.html
echo '<form action="done.html"><input name="email" autocomplete="username" placeholder="Email"><input name="pass" type="password" autocomplete="new-password" placeholder="New password"><button>Create account</button></form>' > signup.html
echo '<form action="done.html"><input name="n" autocomplete="name" placeholder="Full name"><input name="s" autocomplete="street-address" placeholder="Street"><input name="c" autocomplete="address-level2" placeholder="City"><input name="r" autocomplete="address-level1" placeholder="State"><input name="z" autocomplete="postal-code" placeholder="ZIP"><input name="k" autocomplete="country-name" placeholder="Country"><button>Save</button></form>' > address.html
python3 -m http.server 8765 --bind 127.0.0.1 &
open -n --env ARDIA_DATA_DIR=/tmp/ac-usercheck ~/Documents/ardia/apps/browser/build-user/Build/Products/Debug/Ardia.app
```
The scratch data dir keeps your real profile untouched. The fixture data is fake, so the password in the query
string doesn't matter. Afterwards: `kill %1` for the server, quit the app, `rm -rf /tmp/ac-usercheck /tmp/ac-forms`.

1. **Fullscreen and Spaces** (ledger 15). Open any YouTube video and press **f** (or its fullscreen button).
   Pass: the window moves to its own Space with the video filling the screen and no sidebar or toolbar; Mission
   Control shows a single Ardia window; **Esc** brings the window back to its
   Space and layout, and clicks land where you click. With a second display, repeat there.
2. **Save and fill a login** (ledger 19). Open `http://127.0.0.1:8765/login.html`, enter `me@example.com` /
   `Test-pass-123`, Sign in. Pass: our "Save password for 127.0.0.1?" prompt under the toolbar, and no Chrome
   bubble. Click Save, go Back (⌘[) and click the Email field. Pass: Chrome's dropdown sits right under the field
   and lists me@example.com; picking it fills both fields. Typing in the password field shows our eye button.
3. **Strong password** (ledger 19–20). Open `/signup.html` and click the password field. Pass: the dropdown
   offers a suggested strong password; using it fills the field; after Create account, the save prompt or Chrome's
   confirmation appears over the page, not in a window corner.
4. **Addresses** (ledger 21). Open `/address.html`, fill in a fake US address, Save. Pass: Chrome's "Save address?"
   bubble shows over the page area. Save it, reload, click Full name. Pass: the dropdown offers the address and
   fills every field; ⌘, › Autofill lists it. Press Esc, then Edit › AutoFill › Contact…. Pass: the same dropdown
   opens under Full name; with no field focused (click the page background) it opens ⌘, › Autofill instead.
5. **Passwords unlock** (ledger 27). ⌘, › Passwords › Unlock. Pass: exactly one Touch ID (or password) prompt;
   the login from step 2 shows; opening it and revealing the password within 5 minutes asks nothing more; the
   toggle reads "Offer to save and fill passwords". (Unlock only prompts when at least one login is saved.)
6. **Passkeys on Google** (ledger 40). At accounts.google.com, sign in to an account that has a passkey. When it
   says "Complete sign-in using your passkey", pass if Chrome's dialog shows over the page within a second or two:
   with the passkey on your iPhone (iCloud Keychain), "Use your phone or tablet" and a QR code; scanning it with the
   iPhone camera and approving with Face ID signs you in. Cancel must bring back Google's "Try another way". Then on
   `https://webauthn.io`, set Advanced Settings › User Verification to Required, pick Platform, Register: Chrome's
   "Create a passkey" sheet, Continue, the macOS Touch ID sheet; Authenticate the same way. (iCloud Keychain
   passkeys can't be offered until Apple grants the entitlement, ledger 42.)
7. **Phone passkey** (ledger 39). On webauthn.io, register a second username and choose "Use a phone or tablet";
   scan the QR code with your phone's camera. Pass: the phone connects and saves the passkey; Authenticate with the
   phone succeeds.
8. **Remove-extension sheet** (W2). ⌘, › Extensions › Load unpacked…, pick
   `packages/ardiacore/tests/fixtures/ext` in the checkout, confirm Add. Then Remove on its row. Pass: a sheet
   attached to the Settings window, "Remove “ac component test”?" with Cancel and a red Remove; Cancel keeps it,
   Remove takes it off the list. If you have a store extension installed, "Remove from Ardia" on its store
   page must show the same sheet on the browser window.
9. **Quick extras.** (a) With System Settings › Trackpad › More Gestures › Swipe between pages on, on a page with history, swipe back with two fingers on the trackpad. Pass: Dia's frosted
   circle and chevron, a haptic at the threshold, back on release; a horizontally scrolling carousel scrolls
   first. (b) On `/login.html`, Choose File. Pass: the Open panel attaches to or centres on our window, and the
   chosen name shows. (c) ⌘P. Pass: Chrome's print preview over the page, Cancel closes it. (d) Right-click a link.
   Pass: the menu shows, "Open Link in New Tab" once, and choosing it opens a background tab (the menu's content
   and items were verified headless; showing the NSMenu wasn't). (e) With a text field focused in a page, the Edit ›
   Spelling and Grammar items are enabled (the page view's validation was verified headless).
10. **Visual QA against Dia 1.50.1** (ledger 28–31). Same profile colour, same window size, dark mode first, the two
    windows side by side. (a) New Tab: the mark's footprint next to Dia's mark (its top about 84 pt above the bar, cut at
    its top edge; the mark is ours on purpose). (b) The selected tab row's tint in the sidebar, then in light mode. (c) Light mode: the command bar's
    shadow under its bottom edge. (d) Done: Dia's host-only rule came from its binary (§8), and the dark New Tab was
    compared with a stored Dia capture (bar and mark geometry, colours; §21's Sunglow row). (e) A loading tab: the spinner at the row's end (ours copies Dia's `transform.rotation.z` 0 → −2π in its unflipped
    view, which should turn clockwise on screen; the spec's "counter-clockwise" was read in y-down terms). Pass: no difference you can see
    in (a)–(c) at 100 %; take a window screenshot (⇧⌘4, Space) of anything that differs, and note (d).
11. **Dragging tabs between windows** (§1). Open two windows side by side (⌘N), each with a few tabs. Drag a sidebar
    row onto the other window. Pass: the other window's tab list lights up while you hover it, and on release the tab
    is there with its page still loaded (scroll position kept). Drag another row out onto the desktop. Pass: a new
    window opens under the pointer with that tab. Repeat with a top-strip chip (⇧⌘S) and a ⌘-click multi-selection.
12. **Cast** (§18, needs a Chromecast or Google TV on the same Wi‑Fi). View › Cast…. Pass: macOS asks once for Local
    Network access; the device shows in the picker; clicking it casts the tab (status "Casting tab", Stop), and the
    toolbar shows the highlighted cast button until you stop. On YouTube, the player's own Cast button opens the same
    picker and casts the video.
13. **PiP extras** (§18). On a YouTube video, Site Controls › Picture in Picture. Hover the PiP window. Pass: a dark
    pill with the host covers Chrome's origin row; clicking it brings the tab back with the video still playing.
    Right-click the PiP window: Back to Tab, Keep Window on Top (checked); unchecking it lets other windows cover
    the PiP. Drag the window mostly off the right edge of the screen and let go. Pass: it slides in, leaving a 28 pt
    strip with a chevron; clicking the strip brings it back; the same on the left edge; dragging it onto a second
    display doesn't stash it.
14. **Raycast** (§23, needs Raycast). `cd ~/Documents/ardia/extras/raycast-ardia && npm install && npm run dev`.
    In Raycast, run "Search Tabs" (Ardia). Pass: macOS asks once whether Raycast may control Ardia; the
    list shows your tabs with favicons and hosts; typing filters; ↵ switches to the tab and brings Ardia
    forward; ⌃X closes a tab. Run "Search Profiles": "Current" marks the front window's profile; ↵ shows
    another in the front window (in a new window when the front one is incognito); ⌘↵ opens it in a new
    window. Run "New Tab" with `ardia.dev`, then with `hay wain`: the first opens the site, the second
    searches with your engine, both in the front window. Run "New Window" empty, then with the start of a
    profile's name, and "New Incognito Window": each opens the window it says and brings Ardia forward. A
    start two profiles share (`wo` for Work and Work Archive) opens nothing and names both; an unknown name
    opens nothing and lists your profiles.
15. **Sync over iCloud Drive** (§13, §20, §22; needs two Macs signed in to the same iCloud account with iCloud Drive
    on, each running this build without `ARDIA_DATA_DIR`, so they use the real Keychain and iCloud Drive). On Mac
    1: ⌘, › Sync › Turn On Sync. Pass: the Recovery Kit sheet; Save… writes "Ardia Recovery Kit.pdf" with the 24
    words and a QR code; Finder shows `iCloud Drive › Ardia Sync` holding one folder of unreadable names. If macOS
    asks whether Ardia may use iCloud Drive, allow it and note it. On Mac 2: ⌘, › Sync. Pass: "This folder has
    synced data already"; Enter Recovery Phrase…, paste the words (or scan the kit's QR code with your iPhone and
    paste with Universal Clipboard), Connect. Within a minute, pass if on Mac 2: Mac 1's bookmarks, recent history,
    pinned tabs, settings and a saved password (⌘, › Passwords) are there, and the overflow menu (the chevron at the
    sidebar's bottom) shows "Your … Tabs" with Mac 1's open tabs. Change a bookmark and a setting on Mac 2. Pass: Mac
    1 has them within a minute or two (as fast as iCloud Drive carries files). Put Mac 2 to sleep, make changes on Mac
    1, wake Mac 2. Pass: it catches up. Finally ⌘, › Sync › Advanced… › Stop Syncing… on Mac 2, Keep Sync Data. Pass:
    everything stays on Mac 2, and Mac 1's devices list drops it.
16. **Dia import with its tabs** (§20, needs Dia with a few pinned and open tabs in two profiles). Quit Dia. Ardia ›
    Import from Another Browser…. Pass: Dia is one row ("2 profiles"), and what to import lists Open tabs and Pinned
    tabs with the rest. Pick Dia, both profiles, Continue, unlock. Pass: Dia starts without coming forward or showing
    a window; the first time, macOS shows "“Ardia” wants access to control “Dia”" with our reason; Allow. Pass: each
    profile gets its bookmarks, history and passwords, its pinned tabs at the top in Dia's order and its open tabs in
    "Imported", with no dia:// pages and no duplicates, and Dia quits on its own afterwards. Open Dia and run it
    again. Pass: nothing new is added and Dia stays open. Then turn Dia off under Ardia in System Settings › Privacy &
    Security › Automation and repeat. Pass: everything else imports, and the done screen says Dia's tabs couldn't be
    brought over and where to allow it.

## 1. Windows & app shell
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Transparent titlebar, traffic lights in sidebar header | ✓ | ✅ | Full screen (issue #665; 2026-11-08 reworked to Arc's behaviour, measured from the owner's recording): no lights and no room kept for them at rest, so the sidebar's button moves from 97.75 pt to Arc's 29 pt (24 pt down) as the full-screen animation starts (the shell sends `fullScreen` on will-enter and will-exit as well as did); top tabs start 8 pt in. Pointing at the top edge brings down the menu bar and, under it, macOS's own full-screen title bar with the system traffic lights, laid over the window without moving it, as in Arc. Our own full-screen lights band and the window slide (Dia's, issue #307) are gone. Telemetry: `usage_counts.full_screens_entered`. `fullscreen-system-title-bar` acts it out; the real reveal was filmed by hand on macOS 27. |
| Window frame autosave | ✓ | ✅ | frame kept per window in session.json |
| Multiple windows (⌘N) | ✓ | ✅ | each store window is Chrome's own Browser window with our React root over its views (one Chrome window per profile it shows, the root moving to the profile shown); closing the last window keeps the app running. A new window shows up at its final frame with its content, as Dia's: it waits, transparent, until its React content is in (RCTContentDidAppear, at most 0.6 s) and takes the keyboard then; a closing window never shows an empty or half-drawn frame (fixed 2026-10-01, rec1522: an empty window with its traffic lights, then the sidebar over an unpainted page, for several frames; a window closing with nothing in it). Motion, an Ardia choice after Little Ardia's: a window the app opens while another is on screen (⌘N, ⇧⌘N, a reopened window; never the windows at launch or one Chrome made, such as a tab dragged out) scales from 0.98 to 1 and fades in over 250 ms, cubic-bezier(.23,1,.32,1), once its content is in, Little Ardia's frame-layer motion with a smaller scale for the larger window; closing from the app (⇧⌘W, the close button, the last tab) fades and shrinks to 0.99 over 180 ms with its content kept, hands the keyboard to the window behind at once, and the store drops the window when that ends. Full-screen and minimised windows close at once; Reduce Motion keeps only the fades. After a relaunch a window that was full screen returns to full screen on its own display: the display's UUID travels on the full-screen event (and again when the full-screen window changes display), is saved with the window only while it is full screen, and the window is placed on that display before it enters full screen; when that display is unplugged the window comes back on the main display as before (`fullScreenDisplay.test.mjs`, `display-placement.test.cc`). `apps/browser/scripts/windowing-test.mjs`. Left for a person: ⌘N on a busy machine, by eye; two displays with a full-screen window on each, quit and relaunch (the native side of that, the display UUID, the placement and the restore order, was written on Linux and has not been built or run on a Mac yet; the store side and the placement math are tested) |
| Incognito window (⇧⌘N), dark by default, excluded from AI | ✓ | ✅ | in-memory `incognito:<window>@<profile>` profile: the off-the-record profile of the profile it was opened from, as Chrome's (`GetPrimaryOTRProfile`): private windows of one profile share cookies and storage, another profile's never, and the session ends with its profile's last private window; its site settings, Clear Site Data and zoom act on that session alone and go with it, as Chrome's incognito (engine calls take `offTheRecord`, ABI 2); always dark; no history; closed-tab records only while the window is open, never on disk. Fixed 2026-10-06: every private window was Personal's, even from profile 2 (its extensions, bookmarks and session were Personal's), and a private window's downloads were listed in regular windows and saved to downloads.json. Verified headless (16/16, `otr/verify.mjs` on a train build, 6/15 on the build before): a cookie and localStorage set in profile 2's private window aren't seen by Personal's private window or either regular profile, a second profile-2 private window sees them, after both close a new one starts clean while Personal's keeps its own; the download is listed only in profile 2's private windows and never written; a permission allowed there stays out of both regular profiles' settings; a relaunch restores no private window; `state/windows/privateSessions.test.mjs` (10 cases). Left for a person: ⇧⌘N from the menu bar in a profile-2 window, Open Link in Incognito Window from a profile-2 page |
| Close Window ⇧⌘W | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Little Ardia (Arc's **Little Arc**; an Arc feature, not Dia's) | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | File › New Little Ardia Window (⌥⌘N, also the Dock menu) opens a small window with one page and no sidebar, centred in the visible area (clear of the menu bar and the Dock, where window managers centre windows) of the screen you're on (the one with the pointer when a link comes from another app), on the current Space, at its last size (never its last position; `settings.littleArdiaSize`, 900×640 at first), at most 80 % of that visible area each way (never below its 420×300 minimum), so a size saved on a big display no longer nearly fills a laptop's (2026-11-19, #2784: a 2400×1300 size saved on an ultrawide opened at 1512×949 on a 14" MacBook, now 1210×759; the saved size is kept, the cold-launch window and the landing fit below use the same rule, `ArdiaLittleFitMath.h`). Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_fits_below_saved_size`; watch it rise with laptop and big-display setups. When the window server puts it on another display than the pointer's (that screen shows another app's full-screen Space, issue #621), the host reads its real bounds (CGWindowList), finds the screen holding most of it and fits it once to that screen's visible area, on the next run-loop turn, at the reveal and when the active Space changes, until the user moves or resizes it; the saved size is kept (the frame event carries `fitted`), and the cold-launch and later placement share one fit (`ArdiaLittleFitMath.h`, tested in `tests/little-fit.test.cc`). Not yet run on a Mac: the Space landing itself, left for a person. ⌥⌘N and the plain menu item use the profile of the window used last; with two or more profiles, File › New Little Ardia Window in Profile ▸ and the same submenu in the Dock menu open it in the profile you pick (`newLittleArdia` with the profile id; PR #24, issue #21). A thin bar in the profile's colour holds the window buttons, the page's favicon, host and title (click or ⌘L: the compact omnibox dropdown, same parsing and suggestions as the main window), Copy Link and Open in Ardia (⌘O; ⌘O stays Open File… in main windows), and with two or more profiles a profile button at the right end (the profile's badge; where Little Arc has its Space picker; it follows Settings › Profiles › Switcher in the sidebar shows: the profile's colour and initial with Dots, its icon with Icons, and no button with Nothing, 2026-11-18): its menu, the Profiles menu, ⌃1–9 and Next/Previous Profile reopen the page's URL in a Little Ardia of that profile at the same frame and close this one (a Chrome tab can't change profile; form text and scroll are lost; no ⇧⌘T entry for the old one), after which ⌘O goes to that profile's main window. Focusing Little Ardia never changes which profile the next link from another app opens in: that follows the main window used last (`linkProfileOf`, 2026-10-24, issue #21). Links from other apps open there, in the profile of the window used last (Settings › General › "Open links from other apps in Little Ardia", a toggle, on by default; File › "Open Links from Other Apps in Main Window" switches from the menu bar, Little Ardia's included, and reads "…in Little Ardia" once switched; one http/https/file link each, a new Little Ardia even when one is in front; several at once, or set to A new tab, open as tabs of the frontmost main window showing that profile, else the frontmost main window paged to it, which comes forward; with no window open a window opens with them, and on a cold launch the launch's blank window takes the link instead of keeping an empty tab beside it; 2026-10-04). Links in the page stay in it; ⌘-click and split links open behind, as tabs of the main window. Inside a Little Ardia window, with Settings › Tabs › Links › "Keep new-tab links inside a Little Ardia window" on (`littleArdiaOpensNewTabLinks`, synced, on by default; removed in the 2026-11-16 Settings redesign and back on 2026-11-19; hidden on Windows and on a Linux engine without `webView.setLittleArdiaOpensNewTabLinks`; off, the link opens behind in the main window; links in the main window follow Settings › Tabs › Links that would open a new tab, see Peek; watch `setting_littleArdiaOpensNewTabLinks` in `settings_snapshot`), a plain click on a link that opens a new tab (page script `newTabLink`) close Chrome's new tab and load the link in Little Ardia's page instead, so Back returns (2026-10-25, issue #343: the GitHub link on a site looked dead); ⌘-click, ⇧⌘-click, split links, a form with target=_blank (its POST body stays in Chrome's new tab) and a script's window.open() still open behind, and a sized window.open() stays a popup. ⌘O moves the live page (same Chrome tab: history, form text, scroll, playing video; nothing reloads) into the frontmost main window of its profile, opening one if needed, and Little Ardia closes; the tab lands where Settings › Tabs › Add new tabs puts a new tab (At the top, At the bottom, or Below the current tab: right after the window's current tab of that profile, outside its split and in its group, as ⌘T does; from a pinned current tab it goes first in the regular list), as of 2026-11-01. ⌘W, the close button, or Esc the page didn't use (not in a field, no page full screen; `ACClient` reports it) close it and throw the page away (since 2026-11-13 also Esc while nothing in the window has the keyboard, which the page never sees: `FocusModule`'s `unclaimedEscape` app event, `state/windows/unclaimedEscape.ts`; and Esc in the address bar of a Little Ardia that has no page yet closes the window instead of leaving it blank; a page that takes Esc for itself keeps it. Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_escape_closes` with the page focused, `little_ardia_escape_closes_unfocused` and `little_ardia_escape_closes_blank` for the two new paths; watch the last two stay above zero): it stays in History, isn't saved with the session, and ⇧⌘T reopens it in Little Ardia with its back/forward list. It's a real Chrome window of the profile (context menus, downloads, permissions, passkeys, extensions' content scripts, PiP, find). Tab and sidebar commands, Move to Profile and New Profile… are off there or go to the main window; New Tab, History and the like go to the main window. Store: `BrowserWindow.kind = "small"` (state/windows/small.ts, components/littleArdia). Verified headless 2026-09-30: ⌥⌘N, a GURL Apple Event routed to Little Ardia and (setting off) to a tab, ⌘L + typing (URL and search), ⌘O keeping the page (same CDP target, `loadedAt`, typed text, scroll 777, `history.length`, a running counter and an unpaused video), Esc in a field (stays) and on the page (closes), ⌘W, ⇧⌘T, target=_blank, the remembered size, and no window of the test instance above other apps. Fixed 2026-10-03 (owner's report: a link clicked in Discord brought the app forward and opened nothing): 0.2.22 and 0.2.23, the first on ArdiaCore, dropped every link from other apps, cold launch included. AppKit registers its GURL handler in -[NSApp finishLaunching] only if NSApp.delegate answers application:openURLs: at that moment, and ArdiaCoreHost set its bootstrap delegate from the WillFinishLaunching notification, which comes after; it now goes in at the start of finishLaunching. Verified headless with real GURL Apple Events to the test instance's pid (7/7: Little Ardia with the page, in front; a second one while one is in front; two URLs in one event as tabs of the main window; the setting off; ⌥⌘N; no window open; a cold launch with a URL, opened once), all seven failing on 0.2.23; the release smoke test now sends one (`gurl.swift`). Fixed 2026-10-06 (owner's report: on profile 2, every link from another app opened Little Ardia in profile 1): links from other apps took Settings › Profiles' default profile. Now they follow the window used last, as Chrome's AppController does (`lastProfile`, set when a window becomes main, a private window counting as its original profile; kept as Chrome's last-used profile across a relaunch): `ui.lastProfileId` (state/windows/small.ts `lastActiveProfile`) is set when a window becomes key or the key one pages to another profile; when the window in front closes it passes to the next one in focus order (a private window closing keeps its regular profile until another window becomes key, and the last window closing keeps its own); it is saved with the session (after a relaunch the restored window that becomes key takes over, as in Chrome) and forgotten if its profile is deleted; a private window counts as the profile it was opened from (`originalProfileId`), so Little Ardia is never private and never shares the private window's cookies; ⌘O goes to the frontmost window of that profile. The default profile is now for new windows only (and links before any window was used). Dia has per-site link rules (`ExternalLinkRule` in its binary), not covered here. Verified headless with GURL Apple Events to the test instance's pid (12/12): Little Ardia's page has the chosen profile's cookie and Chrome browser context (two profiles, two windows, a private window opened from profile 2 and then closed, the window in front closing, a relaunch); unit tests in state/windows/small.test.mjs. Left for a person: a real click from Discord, Spaces and focus when a link comes from another app, Copy Link (the clipboard is the owner's), resizing by hand, the bar by eye. Opening and closing (issue #212, after PR #148): a new Little Ardia waits, transparent, for its React content (at most 0.6 s, keys held), with its traffic lights already in the bar, then fades in and grows from 97 % over 200 ms, cubic-bezier(.23,1,.32,1), as one Core Animation group on the window's frame layer (no shadow for those 200 ms; Reduce Motion fades only; at a given frame, as after a profile switch, it only fades). Its page stays hidden over the card colour until its first contentful paint (the page script's paint observer; load end or 4 s at most) and then fades in over 160 ms, once per window. ⌘W, Esc, the close button and ⇧⌘W fade it out and shrink it to 98.5 % over 150 ms with the page still in it, from wherever an unfinished entrance was; a profile switch keeps the old window behind the new one until the new one has faded in. The host and title field drags the window once the pointer moves 3 pt; a click there still opens the omnibox. The cold-launch window for a link from another app (shown before JS starts) appears as before. Extension popups (a click-free `action.openPopup` such as a locked 1Password's unlock, or an extension's shortcut like ⌘\\) open in Little Ardia too, hanging from the bar's right end, and its install and pin dialogs show there (issue #275). Trackpad pinch (2026-11-10, the owner's request, as on a Peek card): over the page or the bar, spreading opens the page in the main window (the same Chrome tab, as ⌘O) and pinching in closes the window; the window's bar and page follow the fingers live over its own material (pinching shrinks them at the fingers' scale down to 60 %, spreading grows them up to 4 %), committing past +0.3 / −0.25 of magnification (the close is the window's usual fade and shrink, from where the pinch left it), else springing back; with Reduce Motion it only commits at those thresholds. Not over the command panel, Downloads or extension popups, nor in full screen. Spreading keeps the page's bottom corners concentric with the window's (2026-11-16, the owner's request): they round from the card's 10 pt toward the window's 19 pt as the page reaches the window's edge, instead of a sharp corner under the window's round one. The page doesn't pinch-zoom too: the shell's `MagnifyArea` swallows the gesture before Chrome sees it. The pinch logic is shared with Peek (`components/ui/pinch.ts`, `PinchArea.tsx`). Verified 2026-11-10 in a hidden instance with app-made magnify events through the native routing (`devMagnify`), 10/10: pinch and spread over the page and the bar; −0.1, −0.24, +0.25 and +0.29 spring back (an in-process snapshot at −0.2 shows the bar and page at 80 %, back to 100 % after); a cancelled +0.5 stays; +0.35 moves the tab to the main window with the same CDP target, JS state, typed text and scroll, page zoom 1; −0.28 over the bar closes it (in as long as ⇧⌘W); the rest of a finished gesture isn't claimed; the command panel and a main window's page aren't claimed; Peek's pinch still opens, closes and springs back. Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_pinch_opens` and `little_ardia_pinch_closes` (and `peek_pinch_opens`, `peek_pinch_closes` for the card); watch them against Little Ardia windows opened. Left for a person: a real trackpad pinch and spread (that the page doesn't zoom, the thresholds' feel), Reduce Motion, the motion by eye, and the traffic lights, which stay put while the bar shrinks. Left for a person: the motion and the page fade by eye, the drag. Fixed 2026-11-12 (a user's report on 0.2.45: "links from other apps open Little Arc even though I have the setting off"): routing always followed the setting, cold and warm (verified on 0.2.45-era code with real GURL events, 20/20), but the setting was a pop-up titled "Open links from other apps in" while the only Little Ardia toggle beside it, "Open new-tab links inside Little Ardia", controls links clicked inside a Little Ardia window, and Settings search for "little arc" found only Tabs › Open new-tab links in; the Arc and Dia setup presets also turned it on without asking. It is now one toggle, "Open links from other apps in Little Ardia" (the inner one reads "Keep new-tab links inside a Little Ardia window"), first for "little arc", "little ardia", "little window", "links from other apps" and "slack"; Tabs › Open new-tab links in says links from other apps follow it; onboarding asks the question even with a preset. The decision is one function (`state/littleArdia/externalLinkOpen.ts`, unit tests), and `apps/browser/scripts/little-ardia-links-test.mjs` checks cold, relaunched and warm links (no window, minimized, hidden, another profile, Little Ardia in front, toggled live, a file URL) both ways. Telemetry: `usage_counts.external_links_tab_setting_off`, `external_links_tab_setting_on`, `external_links_little_setting_on` and `external_links_little_setting_off`; watch the last stay near zero (only opening rules route there). Swipe back to close (2026-11-12, the owner's request, from a tester who liked it): a two-finger back swipe on a page with nothing to go back to slides the whole window's content with the fingers and fades it out over 280 pt; letting go past 120 pt, or flicking at 600 pt/s, closes the window (its usual fade and shrink), else it springs back. A page with history still goes back. Settings › General › Close and expand Little Ardia and Peeks with trackpad gestures (on by default; issue #1495) turns off the pinch on Little Ardia and Peek cards and this swipe, so a pinch zooms the page again. Verified 2026-11-12 in a hidden instance with synthetic swipes through the native routing (`acSwipe.pane(...).devSimulate`): 60 pt springs back (an in-process snapshot mid-swipe shows the content 60 pt right and dimmed), 160 pt closes, with history it goes back and the window stays, with the setting off the swipe is refused (`unavailable`). Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_swipe_closes` and `little_ardia_swipe_close_cancels`, and `settings_snapshot.setting_littleWindowGestures`; watch closes against Little Ardia windows opened and how many turn the setting off. Left for a person: a real trackpad swipe's feel, and the traffic lights, which stay put while the content slides. Tiling window managers (2026-11-14, issue #1647, after PR #1880): Little Ardia answers the accessibility subrole `AXSystemFloatingWindow` (the cold-launch window for a link too) and Settings and Import answer `AXDialog`, so AeroSpace and the like float them; the main window stays `AXStandardWindow` and keeps tiling. Checked by `windowing-test.mjs` and the cold cases of `little-ardia-links-test.mjs`. No telemetry: nothing in the app sees how a window manager treats a window. Left for a person: AeroSpace itself. A page in Little Ardia that opens another app (a Zoom, Slack or mailto link; after Open in the prompt, or at once when the site is always allowed) closes its Little Ardia window, so no blank page stays behind (2026-11-15). Closing a Little Ardia window that is in front (⌘W, Escape, the close button, a swipe or pinch) brings back the app that was in front when it opened, the app whose link opened it; not after a replacement (profile switch), and not in the two seconds after a page opened an app, so that app stays in front. The app launch is seen in the shell's `NSWorkspace open:configuration:completionHandler:` hook (`LittleArdiaHandoff.swift`), Chrome's own path for an external protocol, so no engine change. Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_app_link_closes` and `little_ardia_returns_to_app`; watch both against Little Ardia windows opened. Leaving Ardia for any app after a link opens Little Ardia ends that trip: coming back (the Dock, ⌘-Tab) and closing the window keeps you in Ardia instead of jumping to the link's app (2026-11-17); `little_ardia_returns_dropped` counts the windows that lost their way back this way (left with a return armed, whether or not the window was closed later). Watch it next to Little Ardia windows opened from links: a sudden drop to zero while those hold steady is worth checking (the resign may have stopped clearing returns), and `little_ardia_returns_to_app` is expected to dip a little. Fixed 2026-11-17 (Aryan: opening a link from another app with Little Ardia on still showed the main window for a moment): #1906 only put the main windows back once Little Ardia had opened, after the link's trip through the page code, so they showed in front of the link's app until then. macOS brings Ardia to the front a third of a second or so before the link's Apple Event arrives, and AppKit raises the main window on that activation even though LaunchServices asks for it without windows (bit 0x400 in the activation event's data2; ⌘-Tab, the Dock and clicks don't set it). Now the shell knows from the menu state whether links open in Little Ardia, and on an activation with that bit it keeps Ardia's windows where they were (see-through for that moment, then back under the app they were under) and waits up to 0.8 s for a link: a single web link opens Little Ardia in front of everything, while any other link, a click, a key, a Dock reopen or the time running out brings the front window forward as macOS would have. A link that arrives before the activation keeps the windows back as Ardia comes to the front, and one that arrives within a second after an activation without the bit puts them back as it arrives. Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_link_windows_kept_at_activation`, `_on_arrival` and `_on_open`, and `little_ardia_link_holds_without_link`; watch `_on_open` fall towards zero against the other two, and `_holds_without_link` stay small against `_kept_at_activation` (a large count means another kind of activation sets the bit and waits 0.8 s). Fixed 2026-11-19 (a user's report on 0.2.47-nightly.6: clicking Ardia in Stage Manager takes a second, the window flies out, drops back into the strip, then comes out again): a Stage Manager click activates Ardia with the same bit, so the hold hid the windows, pushed them back under the app in front and raised them only when its 0.8 s ran out. With Stage Manager on (`com.apple.WindowManager` `GloballyEnabled`) the activation is left to Stage Manager and nothing is held; a link that arrives before the activation, or Little Ardia opening, still puts the other windows back. Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_link_holds_left_to_stage_manager`; watch `_holds_without_link` drop for the users it counts. Fixed 2026-11-20 (a nightly.7 report: ⌘M and Window › Minimize greyed out): when the hold ended (its 0.8 s, a click or a key) it only brought forward the app's main and key windows, and after an activation that asked for no windows there could be neither, so Ardia was active with no key window and every window command that needs one, Minimize included, was off until a window was clicked. The release now makes the front browser window key when there is no key window: the frontmost visible, unminimized, normal-level window that can become key, in the order the windows had at activation (`HoldReleaseKey.swift`, `HoldReleaseKeyTests`). Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_link_hold_key_restored`; watch it against `_holds_without_link`. Left for a person: Minimize's enabled state after a real Mission Control or link activation, which a hidden instance (its key window is acted out) can't show. Fixed 2026-11-20 (#2805: clicking an Ardia window in Mission Control took about a second to bring it forward, on 0.2.47-nightly.7): Mission Control's click carries the same without-windows bit, and #2810 only left Stage Manager alone, so with Stage Manager off the window dropped back under the app in front and came up when the 0.8 s hold ran out (nightly.9's counters: 46 holds with no link against 63 with one, from 7 people). On an activation with the bit, Ardia now looks at the topmost window under the pointer: if it is the Dock's (Mission Control, App Exposé), Stage Manager's or one of Ardia's own, nothing is held; a link clicked in another app, or opened with nothing under the pointer, is held as before (`LinkActivationHold.swift`, `LinkActivationHoldTests`). Telemetry: `usage_counts.little_ardia_link_holds_left_to_window_manager`; watch `_holds_without_link` fall towards 0 against `_kept_at_activation`. Left for a person: Mission Control and App Exposé themselves, which a hidden instance can't drive. Fixed 2026-11-19 (a user's report: ⌥⌘N stopped working in T3 Code, with Little Ardia's links setting off): since 2026-11-16 New Little Ardia Window's shortcut was registered system-wide whenever Ardia wasn't in front, taking the default ⌥⌘N from every other app. It is now opt-in: Settings › Tabs › Shortcut opens Little Ardia from other apps (`littleArdiaShortcutFromAnyApp`, off by default, Mac only, sent to the shell in the menu state); off, the shortcut works only inside Ardia's menu. Watch `settings_snapshot.setting_littleArdiaShortcutFromAnyApp` and `little_ardia_global_shortcut_opens`, which should fall to the few who turn it on. |
| Peek (Arc's **Peek**; an Arc feature, not Dia's) | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | ⇧-click a link (http/https, no `download` attribute), as in Arc, and it opens in a card over the window's page instead of Chrome's new window: the card is inset 5.5 % of the content area each side (16 pt minimum) and dressed as a Little Ardia window (owner's call, 2026-10-23; `littleArdia/SmallChrome.tsx`, whose bar and page label Little Ardia uses too): its window material (vibrancy with the profile's tint and grain, so it follows the window's active state), the 38 pt bar, the page in a 10 pt card inset 6 pt, and macOS's window corner (16 pt), rims and shadow, measured from a Little Ardia capture; the page under it dimmed. It comes out of the clicked link with a Genie warp of the whole card (the owner's pick from a prototype of three motions, 2026-11-16, reworked the same day, inspired by [DiaTweaks](https://github.com/JaceThings/DiaTweaks)' Peek: each row of the card grows out of the link on its own critically damped spring, staggered by how far the link sits from the card's middle, so an edge link bends the card like the Dock's Genie and a mid-page link zooms straight out with no fold; about 150–225 ms to the eye, a private Core Animation mesh checked at runtime, falling back to a plain zoom from the link, and a fade with Reduce Motion), and everything after it is Seamless: the page is laid out from the start at the size it will have as a tab and shown at one uniform scale, a pinch follows the fingers on that scale (a pinch during the warp keeps every point where it was and lets the warp spring away), Open as Tab springs it to the tab's exact place and hands over the same Chrome tab at the same size, and Close folds it back into the link with the same Genie. One native state machine owns the card (`packages/shell/ios/PeekStage.swift`, `PeekMotion.swift`; design and failure scenarios in `docs/peek-motion.md`, checks in `apps/browser/scripts/peek-hybrid-test.mjs`). Watch `peek_entrances_genie` against `peek_entrances_affine` and `peek_mesh_fallbacks` (the warp missing on some macOS), `peek_ready_timeouts`, `peek_entrance_interrupts`, and `peek_promotes` / `peek_closes`. Open as Tab hands the same page from the Peek's host to the tab's without it leaving the window (no hide, resize or reload). A press where the page will be while the Genie still plays ends it at once and reaches the page, so a Peek's page takes clicks as soon as main's did (84 vs 97 ms median, A/B, 2026-11-19); middle and back buttons reach the scaled page at the right point; Open as Tab finishes in the background if you switch tabs before it settles. A pinch never grows the card past the browser's content area. The Genie can be turned off remotely (`peekGenie` kill switch). Its bar has the page's favicon, host and title (Little Ardia's label, with its load progress), Open in Split View, Open as Tab (⌘O) and Close, in Little Ardia's button slots. Esc the page didn't use, a click on the dim, × or ⌘W close it and throw the page away (it stays in History, never in the session, the sidebar, tab search, sync's open tabs or Recently Closed). Open as Tab and Split View keep the same Chrome tab (nothing reloads): it goes right after the tab it was peeked from (in its group, after its split) and comes to the front, or joins a split with it. Links in a peek stay in it, ⇧-click there too; target=_blank and ⌘-click open tabs of the window with the page under it as opener. One per tab, as in Arc (issue #721, and #994 for pinned tabs): a Peek belongs to the tab it was opened from, so switching tab or profile hides it with that tab and coming back shows the same Chrome tab again (scroll, typing and history kept, nothing reloads; it reappears at full size without the grow); every tab can hold its own, and a window shows only its active tab's. That tab closing (a pinned tab's close unloads it, which counts), going to another page, moving to another window or profile, or its window closing closes the Peek, hidden or not. `peeks` is keyed by source tab, `shownPeek` derives the window's shown one, and `PeekOverlay` keeps hidden cards mounted with the page's web view hidden, as a background tab's. Telemetry: `usage_counts.peeks_shown_again` counts Peeks shown again on returning to their tab; watch it against Peek opens on the dashboard. The tab a Peek was opened from wears the peeked page's favicon (10 pt on a 1.5 pt panel-coloured plate, at the icon's bottom right) in sidebar rows, Top App tiles, pinned rows and the top strip's tabs and pinned faces until the Peek closes, as Arc marks it (2026-11-07, issue #620; `components/peek/PeekMark.tsx`, through TabBadges, `peekOpenedFrom` reads `peeks` by source, so it follows the Peek however it's keyed). Page commands (reload, find, zoom, back, DevTools…) go to the peek while it shows. Its page has a normal tab's prompts and popovers (permissions, external apps, passwords, autofill, Site Controls, share and cast pickers, the status bubble…: `PageOverlays` (`window/PageOverlays.tsx`), shared with tabs), inside the card; answering one applies to the peek's page. Full screen from the peek's page (a video's button) goes full screen in place, as in Arc: no card, bar or dim; leaving it brings the Peek back with the same page. Trackpad pinch on the card (bar included; not on the dim): spreading opens it as a tab and pinching in closes it, the card following the fingers live (pinching shrinks it toward the link it came from, spreading grows it toward the tab's frame) and committing past +0.3 / −0.25 of magnification with the same grow and close motion, else springing back; with Reduce Motion it only commits at those thresholds. Telemetry: `usage_counts.peek_pinch_opens` and `peek_pinch_closes`. The pinch is swallowed before Chrome sees it (a local magnify monitor, `MagnifyArea` in the shell), so the page doesn't pinch-zoom too, and a Peek closed mid-gesture keeps the rest of that gesture. Little Ardia: the Peek click navigates in place. Private windows peek too. Settings › Tabs › Links › "Peek a link with": ⇧-click (default, Arc's), ⌥-click (it replaces Chrome's save-link download; ⇧-click opens a new window again) or Off (both keep Chrome's default; the page script doesn't touch the click). Next to it, "Open a link in split view with" (issue #70): ⌥⇧-click (default, Dia's) or ⌥-click (instead of Chrome's download; ⌥⇧- and ⌥⌘-click keep splitting, a link with a `download` attribute still downloads). Above them, Arc's "Open a Peek window when clicking on links to other sites" (issue #561; `peekLinksToOtherSites`, synced, on by default since 2026-11-11, a user's request that Ardia work like Arc out of the box; off only for the Chrome, Firefox, Safari, Brave and Edge onboarding presets, and unchanged for anyone who already has saved settings): a plain click in a Top Apps tile, a pinned row or a pinned group's tab on a link to another host peeks, however the page opens it. Sites are compared by hostname, a leading `www.` aside, as Zen's `tabDomainsDiffer` does (another subdomain is usually another app: calendar.google.com from mail.google.com peeks). Two paths (rewritten 2026-11-07 for #743 and #867, users' reports that it did nothing on pinned tabs and in Gmail): (1) a link that replaces the page (no `target`, `_self`/`_top`/`_parent`, a `<base target>` counts): the page script notes the plain release on the link and decides only once the page's own click handlers have run, from a one-shot bubble listener on `window` added during the click's capture, so a link the page handles itself (Google's apps button, an account switcher: `preventDefault`) never peeks, and a target or href a handler sets is read; a same-host link whose query carries an http(s) address (`www.google.com/url?q=` on Google, `/redirect?q=` on YouTube) goes the same way, so JS's wrapper-aware check decides it; the listener is removed in a task after dispatch whether or not the click reached `window`, so a page that stops propagation leaves none behind; it then cancels the navigation and posts `peek` with key `site`, and native's gate emits `openWindow` `peek` with `site: true`; JS unwraps known redirect wrappers (`lib/linkWrappers.ts`: Google's `/url?q=` as Gmail rewrites its links on press, Slack, Outlook safelinks, Facebook/Messenger/Instagram/Threads, YouTube, Reddit, LinkedIn, Steam, VK, Tumblr) and loads it in the page itself when the destination is the same site. (2) Everything that makes Chrome open a tab (`target=_blank`, `rel=opener`, `window.open` with or without `noopener`, a handler calling `window.open`): Chrome's new tab arrives as usual (`openedLinkTab:` → `openWindow` `foreground` with an `ardiacore:` adopt id) and `openFromPage` (`state/peek/newTabLinkRoute.ts`) adopts that same tab only when native matched it to the user (`fromClick`, review round 2026-11-07): the page script notes a trusted, unprevented click on a link that opens elsewhere at the end of its dispatch, before Chrome opens the tab, and a `window.open` called while `navigator.userActivation.isActive`; native matches the opened tab against those notes by address, or, when Chrome hid the new tab's address until it committed, the note made in the last second (`matchLinkTab:` in `ArdiaWebView+Tab.mm`), so a timer's, an ad's or any other non-gesture opening stays a tab; it adopts it into the Peek when its unwrapped destination is another site, so `window.opener` and the page's state survive, and Open as Tab keeps it. Sized `window.open` pop-ups (OAuth sign-in) keep Chrome's `popup` disposition and stay pop-ups, ⌘- and middle-clicks stay background tabs, a link that downloads still downloads, and same-site links open a tab as before. A redirector whose destination isn't readable (bit.ly, t.co) peeks, and if its first settled page that isn't a known wrapper (within 8 s, before any navigation of the Peek's own) is on the pinned page's host, the Peek becomes a tab (from a new-tab link) or the page's own navigation (from a plain link); a wrapper page on the pinned host (YouTube's `/redirect`) never counts, and going back to the pinned site by hand later keeps the Peek. Native passes the click point with the opened tab (`RecentClickPoint`, the latest mouse-up over that page), so the Peek grows from the link. A regular tab follows Settings › Tabs › Open new-tab links in (A new tab, A Peek or Little Ardia, `newTabLinksOpenIn`, synced, issue #973), which adopts Chrome's tab the same way into a Peek or a new Little Ardia window (private windows and Little Ardia keep their own rules: never rerouted); links in a Peek or Little Ardia keep their own rules. Review fixes verified 2026-11-07 in a hidden instance, 39/39 (8 new cases, 6 failing on c486e30a4's JS): a `window.open` from a timer 6 s after the click stays a tab, YouTube's `/redirect` (`_blank` and plain) from a pinned youtube.com and Google's `/url` (plain and `_blank`) from a pinned www.google.com land in the Peek, navigating the Peek back to the pinned site by hand keeps it, a private window with A Peek chosen opens a tab, and 100 clicks the page stops leave `window`'s click listeners at 2. Closing a Peek still ignores a page's `beforeunload` cancel (#985, engine). First verified 2026-11-07 in a hidden instance with native clicks on fixture pages served as mail.google.com, www.google.com, dest.test, bit.ly, t.co, slack-redir.net and Outlook's safelinks host (`--host-resolver-rules`), 31/31 after vs 12/25 before: plain, `_blank`, `rel=opener` (the Peek's page has `window.opener`), `window.open`, `noopener`, a handler's `window.open`, Gmail's rewritten link, Slack, safelinks, a Google `/url` with a 302, t.co's script redirect all peek; Google `/url` and bit.ly back to the pinned site, `_blank` on the same site, `_blank` and `download` downloads, the OAuth pop-up (its `postMessage` reaches the opener), a plain same-site link, a menu link the page handles (#867), ⌘-click and middle-click do what they did; a regular tab opens a Peek (default since 2026-11-11, same-site too; `newTabLinksOpenIn`, a tab for the classic browser presets), a tab or Little Ardia by the setting; the daily `settings_snapshot` shows how many keep each default (`setting_peekLinksToOtherSites`, `setting_newTabLinksOpenIn`), while its downloads, pop-ups and ⌘-clicks are unchanged; Open as Tab keeps the same CDP target and `window.opener`; closing the Peek closes Chrome's tab and the pinned page is Chrome's active tab again. JS hands native the pinned tab ids (`webView.setPeekOtherSiteTabs`), which pushes the page config of each view whose pin changed, so pinning or unpinning takes effect on the next click without a reload; a refused gesture makes the page follow the link it swallowed. The two never share ⌥: picking ⌥-click in one moves the other off it (Peek back to ⇧-click, split back to ⌥⇧-click; `withLinkKeysApart` in state/settings/settings.ts, so onboarding's Peek choice does it too), each menu's ⌥-click item says so ("⌥-click (Peek moves to ⇧-click)") and the row's description names the current owner of ⌥; if sync ever brings both, Peek keeps ⌥. Both synced; a change reaches open pages at once (`peekConfig`, now with the split key, to every frame the page script greeted). How: the page script (`page_script.js`) takes the trusted click whose keys are Peek's key, ⌥⇧, ⌥⌘ or (when chosen) ⌥, alone, `preventDefault`s it and posts `peek` with those keys; `ArdiaWebView` maps the keys to Peek or split by its own settings, checks a mouse-up with exactly those keys really happened (test instances skip that) and emits `openWindow` with disposition `peek` and the click in window points, or `split` (the same `openFromPage` → `openLinkInSplit` as Chrome's split disposition: right of the page, background, from a Peek with its source, from Little Ardia a tab of the main window). Fixed with it (2026-10-24): since ArdiaCore, ⌥⇧-click opened a new window (Chrome's own meaning; the CEF-era translation went with CEF) and ⌥⌘-click a plain tab (the engine never recorded Chrome's split disposition for the new tab); both now take the page-script path. Verified 2026-10-24 in a hidden instance with CDP clicks on a local page, 15/15 (7/15 before): default ⌥ downloads, ⌥⇧ and ⌥⌘ split, ⇧ peeks; with ⌥-click chosen ⌥, ⌥⇧ and ⌥⌘ split, a `download` link downloads, ⇧ peeks, a plain click navigates; choosing Peek's ⌥ moves split to ⌥⇧ and back; with Peek on ⌥, ⌥ peeks, ⌥⇧ splits, ⇧ opens a window; an open page follows the change with no reload; the new pane sits right of its page with it as opener and the page stays selected, as with ⌥⌘. The gate with `ARDIA_TEST` `peekStrict` and app-queued mouse-ups, 8/8 (⌥ splits; no fresh mouse-up, no keys or ⌥⇧ keys for a ⌥ click do nothing; ⌥⇧ and ⌥⌘ split; ⇧ and Peek's ⌥ peek). `state/peek/peek.test.mjs` and `platform/pageScriptPeek.test.mjs` cover the setting and the keys. Left for a person: a real ⌥-click with a trackpad in each mode, and the two menus by eye. The peek is a real store tab outside `window.tabIds` (`state/peek/peek.ts`, `peeks[sourceTabId]`, never saved; left out of Chrome's tab strip order until it becomes a tab, but Chrome's active tab while it shows, so its page can take full screen (Chrome grants that only to the active tab); extensions see it as the active tab too, and their actions, popups, side panels and Cast act on it when invoked from the menu, a shortcut or the Peek, while a toolbar button acts on the tab it sits on (`shownPageId`); Chrome selecting the Peek's source (`chrome.tabs.update`) dismisses the Peek; a Peek never reaches Chrome's Recently Closed (`chrome.sessions`) however it closes (×, Esc, an extension's `tabs.remove`, its window closing), and evicts no real entry: its web view is `temporary`, which marks the Chrome tab so Chrome's restore service records nothing for it (`chromium-temporary-tab-restore-hook.patch`); promoted, it is recorded like any tab. Verified 2026-10-23 with a probe extension's `chrome.sessions` in a hidden instance on the new engine (10/10, plus a quit): with 25 real closed tabs a dismissed Peek adds nothing and evicts nothing, and the oldest real one restores with its history; `tabs.remove` on a Peek, closing a one-tab and a two-tab window with a Peek open, and quitting with one open record no Peek; a promoted then closed Peek is recorded. Codex round 2 (2026-10-23, both reproduced first): Chrome's SessionService no longer tracks windows Chrome creates (`chrome.windows.create`), so a Peek in one doesn't come back in Recently Closed after a crash (`chromium-hosted-browsers-no-session-service.patch`); and a window entry recorded with a Peek open selects the Peek's source, with its tabs numbered 0…n−1, instead of the last tab or none (3/3: Peek last, moved first, moved second)); UI in `components/peek`. Verified 2026-10-20 in a hidden instance with CDP clicks (`modifiers` 1 and 8) on local pages, 23/23: opens with the right URL and stays out of the sidebar; Esc, ⌘W (through the menu) and the dim/× path close it, the page goes and the page under it has focus; ⌘O keeps the same CDP target with scroll 3000, typed text and JS state, lands right after its source, active and focused; Split View makes a split of the two; ⌥-click on a `download` link still downloads; ⌥-click inside navigates the peek; target=_blank inside opens a tab with the source as opener; an SVG link peeks; ⇧-click mode peeks on an already open page with no new window, ⌥-click there downloads; Off downloads on ⌥ and opens Chrome's window on ⇧ with the page seeing the click; Little Ardia navigates in place; a relaunch with a peek open restores none. `state/peek/peek.test.mjs` (13). Fixed 2026-10-20 (the owner's report: ⌥-click sometimes did nothing, ⇧-click was reliable): with ⌥ held over a link Blink starts a text selection on press (`selectstart`), and if the pointer moves even 1 px before release it sends no `click` at all, so nothing happened (no peek, no download); a trackpad click often moves a pixel. CDP press–move–release on a link: ⌥ peeked only at 0 px (0/7 at 1–12 px), ⇧ at 0–1 px (2 px+ is a link drag, as for any click). The page script now decides on the press and release (same link, the release's own keys, within 5 px) and swallows the `click` that may follow, and the native gate checks the left mouse-up's own keys, time and window from a local event monitor (one peek per mouse-up) instead of the keys held when the page's message lands. Now ⌥ peeks at 0–5 px; further is ⌥-drag selecting the link's text, as in Chrome. Gate verified with app-queued mouse-ups (`ARDIA_TEST` `peekStrict`, 9/9: none, ⌥, no keys, ⌥⇧, ⌥⌘, reuse, ⇧ mode). Debug builds and test instances write `peek.log` in the data dir (each gesture the page dropped and each gate decision with what it saw). Review fixes 2026-10-21 (Codex, each reproduced first in a hidden instance): a peek whose gesture got no `click` no longer swallows the next plain click on the link; the release re-reads the link (a changed href peeks the new URL, an added `download` downloads); links in editable content don't peek; the frames that get the key choice belong to the Chrome tab, so iframes follow a change after Open as Tab; an old close animation can't close the next peek; the gate binds to the latest unused left mouse-up over this page (hit-tested), with no press since, instead of a 1 s window, so a page busy for 1.5 s still peeks; and when native refuses, the page does the click's default itself (⌥ a download, ⇧ the link opened), never neither. The test-instance bypass, `ARDIA_TEST` `peekStrict`, the dev mouse-up hook and the trace (outside Debug) only work on builds not signed for distribution, hidden, on a scratch data dir. Round 2 (2026-10-21, each reproduced first): the click a peek suppresses is its own gesture's (Blink gives it the mouseup's `timeStamp`, measured equal behind a 1.5 s handler), so a slow page handler can't let Chrome's action through as well; a refusal with evidence of the user's click (the latest unused mouse-up over this page had the page's key) does that key's default from the browser side: ⇧ a new window, ⌥ a background tab for another origin's link (no route to Chrome's download from the app; a page's `download` attribute is ignored across origins) or the page's own download for its origin; without that evidence nothing happens. A document restored from the back/forward cache asks for the key again, a leaving frame leaves the registry, and the main frame always gets the change. `platform/pageScriptPeek.test.mjs` (11) runs the page script's gesture code on a fake DOM. A trackpad bump (the tab-move one, `hapticTick`) when the peek's page first finishes loading (once, not for later pages in it, not if closed first) and when Open as Tab's expand ends; Split View has no finishing animation, so no bump. Restyle verified 2026-10-23 with window captures of a Peek and a Little Ardia window on the same page, same crop (dark and light, active): bar colour 68,49,53 in both (light 232,203,209 vs 231,203,209), the inner rim 87,70,74 vs 87,70,73, the 6/38/6 pt page insets, corner, and the shadow's alpha within 0.03 at every 3 pt below and beside it; 13/13 behaviour checks (open, Esc, ⌘W, ⌘O with the same CDP target, scroll and typed text, Split View, dim/×, tab switch, Off, Little Ardia in place) and 7/7 gate checks with `ARDIA_TEST` `peekStrict`. Verified 2026-10-23 (Codex round): the prompts in a Peek (a MIDI request's Allow clicked inside the card grants the peek's page and not the source, and an external-app prompt sits in the card), full screen in place and back (6/6 tab strip checks: Chrome's active tab is the peek and the source again after it closes, with a tab to its right, and the promoted tab after ⌘O), and the pinch through app-made magnify events through the native routing (`devMagnify`, 9/9: pinch and spread over the page and the bar, −0.1 and +0.25 spring back, −0.28 closes, +0.35 opens as a tab with the same CDP target and scroll, the dim isn't claimed, a gesture outlives its Peek, the next one isn't claimed). Settings › Tabs › Links › "Show Peek buttons" (`peekButtons`: `top`, the default, in the card's title bar; `left` or `right`, round buttons floating beside the card, close at the top, as in Arc and Zen; the card's gutter on that side widens to fit them; 2026-11-13). | Air Traffic Control (Arc's **Air Traffic Control**; an Arc feature, not Dia's) | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | Settings › Air Traffic Control (2026-11-13) sends links from other apps (the default-browser path; links clicked inside Ardia are out of scope, as in Arc) to a profile, as a new tab, a new window or Little Ardia, or into one of a profile's tab groups (2026-11-15: the tab joins the group's copy in the window shown last and the group expands; groups are keyed by pin key, so a pinned group keeps its rules across closing and reopening windows; a rule whose group is gone opens a plain tab in its profile and its row says so). An ordered list of rules, each with an on/off switch, Edit, a row menu (Edit…, Move Up, Move Down, Delete) and Delete; a rule is an app (Any app, the last 20 apps that sent a link, kept on this Mac in `link-sources.json`, then Slack, Mail, Messages, Notion, Linear, Discord, Microsoft Teams, Zoom, Calendar, Microsoft Outlook, Telegram and WhatsApp by bundle id, or Other… for a typed bundle id or name), a link condition (any link, a site that also takes its subdomains but not lookalikes, or a pattern: a userscript match pattern, or since 2026-11-15 a wildcard without a scheme, `*git*` or `github` on the link's host and `github.com/ardia/*` on its address, after a Discord report that `*git*` was refused), a profile (stored by id, so a rename keeps it; Profile you used last) and how to open it. Everything Else picks where links no rule or hook decides go (Ardia's usual routing by default). The pane lists hooks.json's `link.opening` hooks read-only with their approval state, and Try a Link (an app and a URL) names the rule, hook, Everything Else or usual routing that would take the link and where it would land, without opening anything. Built on the `link.opening` route engine (`docs/automation.md` › Air Traffic Control): Settings rules first, then hooks.json hooks, then Everything Else; a rule whose profile was deleted or is private doesn't apply; Settings rules need no approval; an enabled rule or an Everything Else choice turns on the incoming-link hold. Try a Link runs the same `traceLink` as the real decision. Rules live in `air-traffic.json` (version 1, data dir; not synced). Not done: drag to reorder (the row menu moves them), app icons in the picker, in-page links. Verified 2026-11-15 in a hidden instance (`apps/browser/scripts/air-traffic-test.mjs`): with a rule example.org → Work › Inbox, a real GURL link to example.org joined the collapsed Inbox group and opened it. Unit tests: `state/airTraffic/airTraffic.test.mjs`. Telemetry: `usage_counts.air_traffic_rule_links`, `air_traffic_fallback_links` and `air_traffic_group_links`, and `settings_snapshot`'s `air_traffic_rules` (enabled rules), `air_traffic_wildcard_rules` (those with a wildcard pattern; watch it rise above zero once the fix ships), `air_traffic_group_rules` and `air_traffic_fallback`; watch group links grow with `air_traffic_group_rules` above zero; the pane's opens are `settings_section_opened` with `airTraffic`. Each tab that had a page keeps its back/forward list, as for ⇧⌘T: the shown tab loads with it, the others when you first show them (`Tab.restoreFrom`, then `restore:<tab id>`); in this session only (until 2026-09-30 they came back with no Back).
