| Page stays drawn on a window's Space leaving and returning | ✓ | 🧪 | GitHub issue #662 (Ardia 0.2.42): with two windows on their own Spaces (native full screen or maximised), a three-finger swipe showed the page blank for 2–3 s. macOS marks a window on another Space occluded, and Chromium hides its page the way a tab switch does; each page's NSView owns its `BrowserCompositorMac`, so the hide released the compositor with the page's last frame. `chromium-keep-compositor-on-occlusion.patch` keeps that compositor while the view's layer is still visible (an occluded window), so the NSView keeps its last frame and the first frame on return is the page. The renderer still stops (`WasHidden`), a tab switch hides the layer first and still releases after 100 ms, and trace events `BrowserCompositorMac::HoldForOcclusion` and `ShowHeld` mark the hold and the return. Built on Chromium 157.0.8103.50 and checked on a Mac in a hidden instance with `allowOcclusion` (2026-11-16): `fakeOcclusion` occluded for 3 s then visible traces one `BrowserCompositorMac::HoldForOcclusion` and one `ShowHeld` (the engine before it: neither), and a tab switch away and back traces no hold, so it still releases. Still to check by eye on a real Mac: a two-Space swipe in both directions (the page on the first frame), and a window left on another Space for minutes (GPU memory flat: the compositor is held until the window returns, the view hides, 10 minutes pass or the system reports memory pressure (#2515: measured on the 2ebf9722fefc engine, the kept frame is one page-sized surface, 15 MB at 2322×1602, released 600 s after the hold and within 3 s of a moderate pressure signal)). Telemetry: none; it is a frame kept on screen, and the trace events above are the signal |
| Drag window from chrome, double‑click to zoom | ✓ | ✅ | Fixed 2026-10-05 (the owner's 0.2.25 report, tabs on top: "can't re-position … or double click to fill"): the tabs' scroll view covers the whole strip, so a press on its empty part never reached the drag region; AppKit moved the window only in its 32 pt title-bar band (not the strip's bottom 10 pt) and a double-click did nothing anywhere. The sidebar header's row covered its drag region the same way. Now each drag region (`WindowDragRegion`, packages/shell) takes the window's presses inside it that land on itself or on a scroll view's empty background: a press moves the window (a real window-server drag, so screen-edge tiling works), a double-click does the title bar's own action on release (System Settings › Desktop & Dock: Fill, Zoom, Minimize or nothing; ⌥ as AppKit), ⌘-drag leaves a background window behind. Tabs, buttons, the profile chip and the address field keep their presses. `tab-drag-test.mjs` (the whole strip height drags, a double-click zooms and back; 19 cases). The empty sidebar under the rows moves the window too, as in Arc, and a double-click there still opens a tab instead of zooming (PR #9 by louis-co, 2026-10-14; `tab-drag-test.mjs`, 20 cases); a press on any drag region still closes an open command bar (over the strip's and header's regions it used to stay open). The margins around the page card (top, right and bottom, and the left one while the sidebar is hidden or the tabs are on top) move the window too and a double-click there does the title bar's action, as in Dia and Arc (a tester's 0.2.39 report, 2026-10-20: "only the sidebar is draggable"): a drag region fills the space behind the card, so the page, the sidebar's resize handle and the window's own resize edge (the outer ~3 pt, Chrome's frame view) keep their presses (`mouse:` dev events, hidden instance: margins 1 drag each in both layouts, sidebar hidden and tabs on top; page, handle and edge 0; a double-click on the right margin zoomed 1200 × 760 → 1512 × 949 and back; an edge drag still resized). The window's top edge too (the owner's 0.2.40 report, 2026-10-24: "unable to re-position the window"; the recording pressed 2.5 pt below the top, where a double-click filled the screen): the top 3 pt inside the window belong to Chrome's frame view and sit in AppKit's top-edge resize zone, so Chrome skipped its drag there and a sideways drag did nothing while the frame's double-click still zoomed. Now a press on that strip moves the window in every window with a drag region (both layouts, sidebar hidden, tabs on top over tabs too, Little Ardia, Settings, onboarding) and its double-click keeps the title bar's action; the corners and side edges keep resizing, and the top edge still resizes from just outside the window (`mouse:` dev events, hidden instance: top strip 1 drag at every x in each layout, corners 0, a press 1 pt above the window grew it, a double-click on the strip filled the screen and back). Changed 2026-11-17 (#1187: the cursor shows resize arrows on that strip, but a drag moved the window, so resizing from the top looked broken): a single press on the strip goes back to AppKit and resizes the window from its top edge, maximized too, as in every Mac app; only a double-click there is still the title bar's action, and the drag region just below the strip still moves the window (a file trace in a Release build with a real mouse: a maximized window's top came down from 1084 to 926 pt, and a press 5.6 pt below the top moved the window). The page under the band keeps its presses and its double-click, as before. On macOS 27.2 (26B5112a) AppKit's -performWindowDragWithEvent: itself no longer moves a window ("Window move completed without beginning", also in a bare AppKit app), so every window drag in the app (the drag regions, Chrome's frame view, AppKit's title-bar path, PiP) now follows the pointer itself whenever the window server doesn't take the move (real HID pointer events on a visible copy: the sidebar's top, the toolbar, the top strip and its region, Arc's header gap, tabs on top, Settings and Little Ardia each moved exactly with the pointer; a press without a move left the window put; double-clicks zoomed and back; the sidebar button, a tab and the page still clicked). Fixed 2026-11-06 (#852, a window dragged to a second monitor slid down by itself and wouldn't snap): drags from the title-bar band are the window server's own again (the `serverWindowDrag` kill switch, on), so they tile, snap to edges and cross displays as any Mac window does; the pointer-following loop that's left (presses below the band) clamps only the window's top under the menu bar of the screen under the pointer, as Chrome's own move loop does, and holds AppKit's re-constraining and its late after-move adjustment for the frame it placed, which had shrunk a window taller than the new display and pulled its top down after the drop. Written 2026-11-08, not yet built or run on a Mac (#732, the window stuttered at 60 Hz and was smooth with ProMotion): the pointer-following loop placed the window once per drag event as it arrived, at any point in the compositor's frame, so at 60 Hz (events up to 16.7 ms off the frame) a frame repeated a position and the next one jumped two steps. Drag events now only record the newest pointer, and the window is placed once per refresh from the window's own display link (`WindowMovePacer`, in `.common` modes so it fires inside the event-tracking loop, asked for the fastest display's rate as `Pager` does, and following the window to another display); the mouse-up position is placed at once, and a link that goes quiet for 50 ms (`WindowDragPacing.linkStall`) falls back to placing on each event, and a pointer held when the link goes quiet is placed once the 50 ms are up even if no further event comes (the event wait ends at that deadline). `WindowMoveProbe.drag` (`devWindowMoveProbe`) reports `pacing`: placements, frames, placements per frame and the min, max, mean and variance of the gap between placements (`WindowDragPacingTests` runs the pacing decisions as code; `windowDragPacing.test.mjs` only reads the wiring of `WindowMove`, `WindowMovePacer` and the probe from the source, so nothing here has run against a real display link). Left for a person: build it, and the drag from the sidebar's empty area and a page margin on a 60 Hz display (the built-in one set to 60 Hz, or an external one) against the same on ProMotion, measured with the probe's `pacing` (expect about one placement per frame and a gap spread of a frame or less) and by eye. Left for a person: tiling and edge snapping with a real mouse on two real displays. Left for a person: the drag and double-click with a real mouse, in both layouts, the top edge's drag and the resize from just above it, and the sidebar's resize handle Idle (#1682, 2026-11-14): the window server's drag region used to be rebuilt 0.1 s after every React Native mount, for every window, so a background tab retitling itself rebuilt it about 6 times a second. A mount now rebuilds only the window whose title bar the pointer is in; the others are rebuilt when the pointer reaches their title bar, when they move, resize, change screen, leave full screen or become key under a still pointer, on a press that finds them stale, or 2 s later (`drag-region-test.mjs`, 10 checks: 39 rebuilds → 2 over 40 title changes). `ARDIA_SWITCHES=dragRegionEveryMount=on` is the old behaviour. Watch `perf_memory.drag_region_stale_presses` (should stay 0), `drag_region_rebuilds` and `browser_cpu_pct` on the dashboard's Regressions › Energy rows. |
| The pointer over Ardia's own views on top of a page | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-16, issue #2319 ("swallowed my cursor until I quit"): a page that hides the pointer (`cursor: none`: video players after a few idle seconds, games) or shows its own (an I-beam) no longer leaves it that way over Ardia's own views drawn over the page (the command bar, the auto-hidden sidebar sliding out, popovers). Chrome resets the arrow only when the pointer leaves the page view's rectangle, and these views sit inside it. Two parts, both in `ArdiaPageCursor.mm`: a page covered at the pointer by one of Ardia's views no longer sets its cursor (`shouldChangeCurrentCursor` on Chrome's page view), and a pointer move over Ardia's views that finds a page's hidden or leftover cursor puts back what that view wants (a React Native `cursor` style, the I-beam over text fields, otherwise the arrow). Over the page itself the page's own cursors are untouched. Telemetry: `usage_counts.page_pointer_restores` and `page_pointer_hidden_restores` (how many of those had hidden the pointer). Watch `page_pointer_hidden_restores` for people who see it often; a count near zero across the fleet with new #2319-style reports means another path still leaves it hidden. Dev: `windows.dev` `pageCursor` (state and counts), `pageCursor:hide` (an invisible cursor, as a page sets). |
| Window backdrop tint + translucency | ✓ | ✅ | Dia 1.50's WindowTreatment stack (dia-spec › Window translucency): behind-window blur (material 29 dark / `.hudWindow` light, emphasized, follows the window's active state, so it goes opaque when inactive or with Reduce Transparency), BaseTint black 0.4 / white 0.8, and the profile colour's HSL gradient (ΔL 0.25) at 0.36 (neutral 0.12) in a view at 0.5 / 0.75. Plain AppKit layers, no per-frame work. Pink/plum tint fitted from Dia (#BB374B); the other colours use their swatch hue at the same saturation/lightness (unmeasured), light mode unmeasured |
| Warn before quitting / closing window with many tabs | ✓ | ✅ | ⌘Q warning; ⇧⌘W / close button on a window with 2+ tabs, and ⌘W on a window's last tab, use Dia's close-window confirmation ("Close 3 tabs?", per-profile breakdown, "Don’t ask me again"); setting in General. The last regular window closes with no confirmation, since nothing is lost: the Dock brings it back (issue #1000, row "Reopen closed window"). With Share tabs across windows on, ⇧⌘W counts only the tabs that close with the window, so a window whose tabs all stay open in another window closes with no confirmation, and a window already closing doesn't count as keeping them, so the second of two windows closed in a row is the last window and closes with no confirmation (issue #720; state/windows/windowClose.test.mjs). A window that still has pinned tiles, pinned rows or a pinned group in the profile it shows stays open when its last tab closes, with a New Tab and no confirmation, as Arc does (2026-10-29, issue #633) |
| Quit guard with active downloads | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Battery Saver / freeze CPU‑heavy background tabs | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's own battery saver mode, as Dia's (ArcCore `SetBatterySaverMode`): with Advanced › Battery Saver at When battery is low (the default, as Chrome's: on battery at 20 % or less) or When unplugged (on battery at all), and in Low Power Mode with either, state/tabs/tabLifecycle sets Chrome's `performance_tuning.battery_saver_mode.state` to enabled (`ac_battery_saver`), else disabled. Chrome's `FreezingPolicy` then freezes hidden tabs whose browsing instance used ≥ 5 % CPU over a 1‑minute measurement (not while audible, capturing, on WebRTC, holding a web lock…) and thaws them when shown; battery saver also throttles frame rate and aligns timers. Our launch flags drop Chrome's 5‑minute just-hidden protection to 0 (`freezing_visible_protection_time`): a tab spinning a core freezes about 3¼ min after it's hidden (7¼ min with Chrome's default; our old sampler took 1¾ min), then uses 0 % (2026-10-15, hidden instance on battery). Dia's Activated/Deactivated toasts. Our CDP sampler and its kill switch are gone (2026-10-28): Battery Saver is always Chrome's |
| Tab discarding (sleep idle tabs, keep last 10 alive) | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's own Memory Saver, as Helium ships it (2026-10-28; our timed and memory-pressure sleep sweep is gone): Settings › Advanced › Performance › Memory Saver (on by default, Helium's `memory-saving-by-default`) with Chrome's three modes, Moderate / Balanced (recommended) / Maximum (a hidden tab is discarded after 6 h / 4 h / 2 h, unless revisited more than 5 / 15 / 15 times), and "Always keep these sites active" (Chrome's rule format, checked as its settings page does; synced like muted sites). The app's settings are the source of truth, pushed at launch and on change (state/tabs/memorySaver.ts, `ac_memory_saver`): the state and mode to local state, the sites to every engine profile. Chrome's `DiscardEligibilityPolicy` keeps visible tabs (a split's panes, Peek), each window's active tab, audible or recently audible ones, PiP, capture, form input, notifications, PDFs, open DevTools and excepted sites; Chrome also discards under critical memory pressure whatever the setting. A loaded tab budget on top since 2026-11-14 (#1680, after #1684 by @Bil0000; state/tabs/loadedTabBudget.ts): Chrome's timer alone left every tab loaded for hours, so memory grew with each open tab. With Memory Saver on, once a minute the app counts the loaded tabs, and past the budget asks Chrome to sleep the ones used longest ago: Balanced keeps one loaded tab per GB of the Mac's memory and never fewer than Dia's 10 (16 on a 16 GB Mac), Moderate two per GB (at least 20), Maximum one per 2 GB (at least 5), shown tabs included. A tab over the budget still stays until it has been hidden for 1 h (Moderate 2 h, Maximum 30 min), so tabs you cycle through never reload; while macOS reports memory pressure the budget is the mode's minimum and the wait 10 min, Chrome's own floor for an urgent discard. Chrome decides each one (`-[ArdiaCoreTab sleepIfHiddenFor:]`: `DiscardEligibilityPolicy` with the proactive reason and that hidden time, then the timer's own discard path), so every protection in this row holds, a kept tab isn't asked again for 10 min, nobody sleeps in its place, and at most 4 tabs sleep per sweep. No page is scripted. Measured in a hidden instance (24 GB Mac, 40 loaded local pages, each its own site): with the real budget (24 tabs, 1 h) nothing slept; with a 2 min wait for the test, 15 tabs slept in 4 sweeps and all processes went from 2931 to 1847 MB (renderers 2561 → 1462 MB), a tab with typed input and a kept-active site stayed (Chrome's reasons 26/27 and 12), a switch to a loaded tab painted in 16–108 ms and to a slept one in 166–360 ms with its scroll position back. One pass over 40 tabs costs 0.2 ms of JS on the minute tick the app already had. Dashboard: `usage_counts.tabs_slept_over_budget` (a count) against `tabs_slept_over_budget_reopened` (those opened again within 30 min): a high share reopened means the budget is too tight; zero slept across heavy-tab users means the sweep stopped. Pinned tabs and Top Apps (Chrome's pinned tabs) sleep on the timer like any tab since 2026-11-12 (issue #1069: at Maximum, five tiles stayed loaded for hours at 200–450 MB each, because Chrome protects a pinned tab from every automatic discard and never times a tab revisited more often than the cap): `chromium-memory-saver-pinned-tabs.patch` keeps a pinned tab only from an urgent discard (memory pressure) and starts its timer however often it was revisited; every other protection in the list still holds for it. The page's `discarded` event carries Chrome's reason (`discardReason`). Acceptance: `memory-saver-pinned` (chrome://discards' Proactive Discard, the timer's own path, on a plain tab and a tile; before the patch the tile stays loaded with "Tab was pinned"). Dashboard: `usage_counts.pinned_tabs_slept_by_memory_saver` (pinned tabs the timer put to sleep, a count); zero across users who keep pinned tabs means the exemption is back. A discarded tab is Chrome's in-place discard (`WebContentsDiscard`): it keeps its back/forward list, `chrome.tabs` lists it `discarded: true`, it shows faded with "This tab needs to reload" and reloads when selected; Play on its sidebar player reloads it and resumes. Settings › Tabs › Fade icons of unloaded tabs (ours, off by default, synced) gives the same faded icon to a tab with no page at all, such as a closed pinned tab or one restored after a relaunch (`isPageUnloaded` in state/pins/pinShare); recent tabs reload on launch. Settings › Tabs › Strike through sleeping tabs (ours, issue #1625, 2026-11-15; `strikeSleepingTabs`, off by default, synced) also draws a sleeping tab's title struck through at 60 % opacity, in sidebar rows, pinned rows, folder rows, split panes, a Top Apps folder's list and the top strip's tabs, and drops it the moment the page loads again; a tab whose icon is faded as unloaded (Fade icons of unloaded tabs on, or a closed Top App with Draw tiles for closed Top Apps off, #1855) gets it too, so the title always matches the faded icon (`useStruckTitle` in state/sidebar/rowState.ts; the native `FadeLabel` gained a `strikethrough` prop, native API 68). Dashboard: `settings_snapshot`'s `setting_strikeSleepingTabs` = true (how many turn it on). Unused engine profiles are released on their own 1‑minute timer. Sleep by hand (issues #500, #1231, 2026-11-16): a tab's menu offers Sleep Tab, for pinned and unpinned tabs, while its page is loaded in Chromium, not asleep and not shown in any window (as the front tab, a split pane, a Peek or a pinned tab's shared page), and a group's menu offers Sleep Tabs for the same tabs in it and its nested groups (greyed out when there are none). Both ask Chrome with `sleepIfHidden(0)` (`state/tabs/manualSleep.ts`). A tab that is in use or would lose work stays awake and is skipped, not interrupted (playing sound, PiP, capture, Bluetooth or USB, open DevTools, form input or edits). A tab Chrome keeps only from automatic sleep is discarded anyway with Chrome's external reason (`state/tabs/sleepRefusals.ts`: background title or icon updates, notifications, recently visible or audible, pinned, extension-protected; kept-active sites, Chrome's active tab and web apps still keep it). Since 0.2.47-nightly.9 (Discord report, 2026-11-20) the menu had silently done nothing on mail and chat pins and Top Apps, because they update their title or icon in the background (Chrome's reason 25); a slept tab keeps its place, shows as sleeping and reloads when selected. The agent's `tab.sleep` op shares the same helper and still discards with Chrome's external reason. Dashboard: `usage_counts.tabs_slept_from_menu` and `groups_slept_from_menu` (uses of each item) and `menu_sleeps_kept_awake` (tabs kept awake), with `menu_sleep_kept_reason` (the kind of reason that kept the last one: playingOrSharing, audio, pictureInPicture, capturing, device, devTools, typedInput, noPage, other) and `menu_sleeps_past_automatic` (tabs slept past an automatic-only protection). Kept close to every ask means a manual sleep is blocked again; `menu_sleeps_past_automatic` at zero across pinned-tab users means the override stopped working |
| Profiles load when opened (#801; an Ardia extra) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-06. A launch loads only the profiles its windows show (Personal always, as Chrome's startup profile and the source of the blocker's settings); the others, with their extensions and uBlock Origin, load the first time a window shows one, or when anything needs their engine data (sync of passwords or extensions waits for it, import and Settings calls load it). Their sidebar, Top Apps, pinned tabs and cached bookmarks and favicons show from launch; bookmarks, history and favicons are read from Chrome within ~70 ms of opening, and the first page waits for that profile's uBlock Origin (~2.1 s, the hold any profile loaded after launch gets), its row keeping its saved title meanwhile. Release, idle 60 s, physical footprint: 2 profiles 499 → 363 MiB, 4: 822 → 351, 8: 1492 → 393. Settings › Advanced › Performance › Load profiles when opened (on; off loads all at the next launch); kill switch `lazyProfiles` (`state/profiles/profileLoading.ts`) |
| Unload inactive profiles (#1367; an Ardia extra) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-14. Settings › Advanced › Performance › Unload inactive profiles: Never (the default), or after 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 1 hour or 2 hours; it needs Memory Saver on. A profile no window has shown for that long (a Little Ardia or private window counts as showing it) is unloaded with its extensions and its uBlock Origin once every one of its tabs can sleep by Memory Saver's own rules (not playing sound, capturing, in Picture in Picture, holding form input, open in DevTools or a kept-active site), none of them asks before it closes (a `beforeunload` handler), each tab's back/forward list is saved and read back from disk, and nothing is downloading in it; otherwise it stays and is asked again a minute later. Personal never unloads. Its tabs stay in the sidebar and load again, at their URL, scroll position and back/forward list, when the profile is opened; bookmarks and history show from the cache and are read again then. Off by default because going back is a load, not a switch: 4 profiles, Release, 450 s idle: 653 → 311 MB and 13 → 6 processes, and the first page back took 0.46 to 0.81 s against 76 ms kept (`docs/perf/README.md` › Profiles no window shows). While unloaded, that profile's extensions, notifications and uBlock Origin list updates don't run. Watch `usage_counts` `profiles_unloaded`, `unloaded_profiles_reopened` and `unloaded_profile_reload_ms` (`state/profiles/profileUnloading.ts`, `-[ArdiaCoreEngine unloadIdleProfile:]`) |
| Freeze monitoring (#2479; an Ardia extra) | — | 🧪 | 2026-11-17. Two run-loop observers on the main thread (AppKit, our native code and Chromium's browser UI thread) and two on the Hermes JS thread time each busy stretch; a stretch of 250 ms or more is a stall, tallied by length (250–500 ms up to over 10 s) with the longest and the clicks or keys it held up, and on the JS thread how much of it was garbage collection. Hourly in `perf_memory` (sharing on only, consent epoch per sharing period), with Hermes's collections in the hour, its heap size floor and Chrome's "Page Unresponsive" episodes by length. No timer: nothing runs while the app sleeps (`docs/perf/README.md` › Freeze monitoring for the idle numbers). `freeze-watch-test.mjs` (hidden instance, 10 checks: each thread blocked 300 ms, 1.2 s and 6 s lands in its bucket, a garbage-heavy JS block shows its GC, sharing off counts nothing, a page hung 22 s counts 5–30 s). Off with the `freezeWatchOff` experiment. Watch `perf_memory.main_stall_*` and `js_stall_*` per version (they should fall), `js_stall_gc_bound` against `js_stall_*` (how many freezes are collection), `js_gc_ms` and `js_heap_mb_floor`, `renderer_unresponsive_*`, and `browser_wakeups_per_s` (must not move). Left for a person: the input-wait fields with real input during a real freeze. Part 2: each freeze of 0.4 s or more while the app is active is sampled (3 stacks of 24 frames from the stuck thread, our own images by UUID and offset, everything else "system" or "other"), written to `freezes.txt` two seconds in (so a force quit keeps it), given the JS job it overlapped, and sent as `app_freeze` (sharing on, 5 a session, 20 a day, one per signature a day); Help › Copy Last Freeze, and a line in Copy Diagnostics. Watch the dashboard's Performance › Freezes card (signatures by people and time frozen) and `app_freeze.activity` and `gc_ms`. |
| Sad‑tab / native error page with Reload | ✓ | ✅ | SadTab + Page Unresponsive (Wait / Exit Page) in layout/PaneOverlays |
| Offline page game (Chrome's dino) | ✓ (dino) | ✅ | Our own: "Where's the lamb?" (apps/browser/assets/offline-game) wherever Chrome shows the dino, in tabs and popups, with the error code, host and Retry; `ardia://game` plays it on its own. The painting and the page take the tab's profile colour (the New Tab mark's recolour; incognito is neutral and dark), light or dark, and follow a change while open. Other net errors keep Chrome's page. Engine patch `chromium-neterror-game.patch` (docs/engine-build.md › "The offline page") |
| Windows: reopening right after closing (#2095, #2044; an Ardia platform fix) | — | 🧪 | 2026-11-16. Reopening Ardia while the last copy is still closing no longer starts a second browser or crashes (Chromium's `RunLoop` CHECK on a quit during startup). The engine's launch guard waits for the copy that is closing (one wait per profile) and lets one launch win when several start at once. After 2 starts that never finished, it sets the restore state aside in `Recovery\<time>` and clears the graphics caches, and the app shows a notice on top. Built on engine `windows-engine-20261116-13`; checked on the Windows 11 VM; the tester hasn't confirmed. Dashboard, `app_launched` on Windows: `launch_waited_for_previous_ms` (its p95 should stay under a few seconds; a jump toward the 45 s cap means a closing copy is hanging again, #1706), `launch_unfinished_starts_before` (mostly 0; a rising share of 1 or more means starts are failing again), `launch_restore_state_set_aside` (rare; a rise means profiles are getting stuck), `launch_profile_held_by_another` (rare; a rise means the wait or the handover is being skipped). |

## 2. Tabs
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| New tab ⌘T | ✓ | ✅ | The new row appears selected in its slot and the rows below slide down under it, bezier(0.25, 0.1, 0.25, 1) over 0.14 s, as in Dia, inside an expanded group too (dia-spec › Sidebar › Opening and closing tabs) |
| New tab next to the current tab (ours; opt-in) | — | ✅ | 2026-10-24, GitHub issue #17. Dia only has "Add new tabs to the top" ("to the left" across the top), Arc the same top/bottom choice, so Settings › Tabs › Add new tabs became a pop-up: At the bottom (default, unchanged), At the top, or Below the current tab ("On the right", "On the left", "Next to the current tab" across the top; `newTabPosition: "nextToCurrent"`). With the third, ⌘T, File › New Tab, + New Tab and its menu, the top strip's +, a double-click on the empty sidebar and a pick in the ⌘T command bar put the tab right after the current tab (after its whole split), inside its group, and show it; from a pinned tab it goes first in the tab list, outside any group. Closing it right away goes back to the tab you were on, as any close goes back to the tab used before it (Close tab ⌘W row). Links already opened next to their page; bookmarks, extensions and other tabs not from New Tab still go to the end (`nextToActive` in `state/tabs/openers.ts`, `newTabCommand`) |
| Close tab ⌘W (+ hover ✕) | ✓ | ✅ | The closed row fades in place (ease-out, 0.10 s) while the rows below slide up over it (0.14 s), as in Dia, inside an expanded group too (its box shrinks with them). The row that slides under a still pointer shows its hover and ✕ at once, while it is still sliding (react-native-macos patch: hover is checked again after every layout). A pinned tab, or a tab of a pinned group, keeps its row and unloads its page (Dia 1.50.1: ⌘W closes with `deselectPinnedIfActive`, and a tab whose container is pinned gets the `dismiss` close button). The row's ✕ on a pinned row or a pinned group's tab that is already closed removes it, as a second middle click does: Recently Closed, and a "Removed Pinned Tab" toast with Undo (Arc's ✕ on a closed pin is "Archive this tab"); it did nothing before, so a tab in a group made with New Group, which is pinned, looked impossible to close (2026-10-24, issue #163). Waking a split wakes every pane as a loaded tab, not only the one clicked. As in Dia (screen recording, 2026-09-29): with no regular tab left, ⌘W on a pinned tab shows another pinned tab whose page is still loaded, and holding ⌘W never reloads unloaded pinned tiles (0.2.7 alternated between a New Tab page and a cold load of the pinned page). Closing the last tab of a window that still has pinned tiles, pinned rows or a pinned group in the profile shown keeps the window, as Arc does: no tile is selected or woken, a New Tab takes the content area (the hidden empty card with the command bar when New Tab opens the bar), and ⌘W on that New Tab closes the window as before; Dia closed the window even with unloaded tiles left (2026-10-29, issue #633). With several tabs Shift- or ⌘-selected in the sidebar, ⌘W closes the whole selection (pinned tabs in it unload, as ⌘W on one does) and the menu item reads Close N Tabs; an open command bar or New Tab bar is dismissed first, and ⇧⌘T brings the tabs back one press each (2026-11-11, issue #762). Dashboard: `usage_counts.selection_close_keys`, `selection_close_key_tabs`. Closing the shown tab goes back to the tab you used before it, as in Arc (Dia and Chrome go to the next tab, or to a tab the page opened); a pane of a split still goes to its other pane, and a tab opened behind and not yet shown never wins, as in ⌃Tab (2026-11-13, issue #421; `closeSuccessor` in `state/tabs/openers.ts`). The rows below a closed tab end in their place also when the close keeps the app busy or a row renders just as its slide ends; either left a row one place down with a blank space above it (2026-11-16, issue #2186; `slideSettle.ts`, `useShift`). Dashboard: `usage_counts.sidebar_slide_overruns` counts list renders that met a slide still running past its deadline; above zero means late slides happen and are left to land |
| Keep Top Apps running when closed (setting) | — | ✅ | Settings › Tabs › Keep Top Apps running when closed (`keepTopAppsRunning`, off by default, so ⌘W unloads a tile as in Dia). On, ⌘W on a Top App switches to the last regular tab, or a New Tab, and leaves its page loaded; a tile split with a regular tab leaves the split and the regular pane comes forward, while a split of pinned panes (a tile with a pinned row or another Top App) stays whole and loaded and ⌘W on either pane switches away from all of it, as an unload takes the whole pinned split; the tile's menu gains Unload. Held ⌘W never cycles through running tiles: once only they are left, it opens a New Tab and keeps the window, and ⌘W on that New Tab closes the window as it does with the setting off (2026-11-19, the owner's report: ⌘W on it made another New Tab forever; `closesBesideKeptTopApps` in `state/omnibox/newTabBarPolicy.ts`; watch `top_app_windows_closed_from_new_tab`) (`closingTabs`/`unloadPinnedTabs` in `state/tabs/tabs.ts`, #1215). Watch `top_app_closes_kept_running` (closes of the Top App in front, by ⌘W or a middle click, that switched away and left it loaded) and `top_apps_unloaded_from_menu` in usage_counts and `setting_keepTopAppsRunning` |
| Close other tabs | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Close tabs above/below/left/right | ✓ | ✅ | sidebar: Above / Below; top strip: Close Tabs to the Left / Right (sidebar/menus.ts) |
| Close All Tabs ⇧⌘K | ✓ | ✅ | keeps pinned tabs and pinned groups, the selected tab (with its split) and tabs playing sound, as Dia's Close All keeps the selected tab (issue #149's recording) |
| Unread badges on tabs, pinned tabs and Top Apps (Arc's Top App badge) | ✓ | ✅ | Issue #967 (an Arc user: no badge on the Gmail tab). Every web app, read from what sites already publish, in one place (`lib/unread.ts`, `state/tabs/unread.ts`, `components/unread/UnreadBadge.tsx`): the title's count, a parenthesised count at the start ("(3) Home / X", "(2) WhatsApp", "(4) Discord \| …", "(5) Chat \| Microsoft Teams", "(99+) Facebook", "[3] …"), after a mailbox-like label or the site's own name before the rest ("Inbox (3) - … - Gmail", "Chat (2) - Google Chat", "Messenger (2)" on messenger.com, "Telegram (14)"), or a phrase ("4 unread", "3 new messages"); Discord's leading "•" is a dot, and a leading "*" only on Slack (Slack marks unread channels that way; an editor's "* Untitled" isn't a badge). A 1900–2100 number never counts ("Dune (2021)", "2024 unread messages"), nor a film-like "Apollo (13) - News". The Badging API: the page script wraps `navigator.setAppBadge` / `clearAppBadge` in the top frame of secure pages, converts the value once as WebIDL's `[EnforceRange] unsigned long long` and hands Chrome that number (Chrome only badges installed apps; where it's missing the page gets a working one that refuses another receiver), reports a badge only after Chrome's promise resolves, and sends at most one message per 250 ms and only on a change; the native side takes it only from a main frame with a real (non-opaque) origin, and a page still parked at launch or mid-transfer keeps its latest badge until it's shown (`ArdiaLaunchTabHolder`, the engine's held page messages). Each site in a tab keeps its own badge, so Back to an earlier site shows it again. A favicon swapped within the same origin for a file named like a badge (`favicon-unread.ico`, `…_notification.png`; the file name only, not the host or folders) shows a dot until the site's own icon returns, measured against the first ordinary icon the site showed; drawn (`data:`) and animated icons never count. A count from the title wins over it. Redesigned 2026-11-12 (issue #1300, a user: "Notification bubbles are unreadable… low contrast and awful shape and size"): Dia's red #EB4648 capsule with white 9 pt bold digits measured 3.8:1 (below WCAG's 4.5:1), repeated the title's "(3)", and on tiles covered most of the favicon. Now calm and neutral, no red: in a sidebar row and a top-strip tab the count sits at the row's right end as a 16 pt capsule that grows with its digits, 11 pt semibold tabular digits in the row's ink on a 16 % (dark) / 8 % (light) tint of it, or a 6 pt dot, and steps aside for the close button on hover; (`components/unread/UnreadBadge.tsx`, `tokens.ts`). Top Apps tiles and the strip's pinned tabs show only the icon by default, as Dia's do (2026-11-12, issue #1339): Settings › Tabs › "Show tab status on Top Apps" (`showTopAppBadges`, synced, off by default) turns on a tile's status marks, drawn in its corners and off the favicon in the rows' flat style (`components/sidebar/TileStatus.tsx`): the rows' outline PiP glyph bottom-left and outline speaker (slashed when muted) bottom-right, 8 pt and 9.5 pt so both draw about 8 pt tall on one centre line, 6 pt in from the edges, and the unread count top-right as an 11 pt neutral capsule with 9 pt semibold digits and no ring, or a 6 pt dot, 4 pt in (still needs "Show unread counts on tabs"); the strip's pinned tabs get the capsule. The filled PiP circle and the ringed corner badge over the favicon are gone. Whatever the setting, a tile keeps its recording dot, its peek mark and the next-meeting countdown, and pinned rows and tabs keep their count and sound icon. On the dashboard: `setting_showTopAppBadges` in settings_snapshot shows how many turn it on. 99+ past 99. When the badge shows the same count as a leading "(3) ", "[3] " or "• " in the title, the shown title drops it ("Home / X"), not a custom title (`titleWithoutUnread` in `lib/unread.ts`). Springs in and out. Only a running page shows one: a sleeping tab's saved title, a discarded tab and a crashed page don't, until the page loads again. A pinned copy whose page lives in another window shows that page's. Each badge watches only its own tab (the per-tab watcher and a per-tab signal map), so with 2,000 tabs a page calling `setAppBadge` in a tight loop no longer stalls the app (2026-11-07: the JS thread fell 219 s behind before, see the commit). Dia's own badge views (`TabBadgeView`, `PinnedTabBadgeView`) weren't measured against a capture: Dia wasn't running and nothing in its bundle draws them. Turning them off (issue #1170, 2026-11-11): per site, by host without `www.` as Mute Site keys it, "Hide Unread Count" / "Show Unread Count" in the menu of a tab, pinned tile or Top App (offered on a tab that shows a badge or whose site is hidden; not in a private window, so no private host is saved), and a "Show Unread Count" toggle in Site Controls › More; it covers every tab of that host, keeps the page's own count (showing it again brings the badge straight back) and is the synced `unreadHiddenSites` list beside `mutedSites`. Settings › Tabs › "Show unread counts on tabs" (`showUnreadCounts`, synced) is **off by default since 2026-11-12** (#1300; session migration 10 drops an untouched saved on, a chosen one stays, and sync only ever sends chosen values); it hides every badge and keeps each site's choice. Settings search finds it first for notification, notifications, notis, badge, bubble, unread, count and dot. With it off, a badged tab's menu offers "Show Unread Counts" and Site Controls › More shows a "Show Unread Counts · All sites" toggle, both turning it on (`state/tabs/unreadSites.ts`, tests in `unreadSites.test.mjs`). Verified in a hidden instance: a "(5) Inbox" Top App and a "(3) Chat" row on localhost lost their badges from the tile's own menu while 127.0.0.1's "(3) Chat" kept its, the hide survived a relaunch, and the global switch hid all three. Watch on the dashboard: `settings_snapshot`'s `setting_showUnreadCounts` = true (who turns it on now that it starts off) and `unread_hidden_sites` (how many sites, never which). Not done: badges from a service worker's `setAppBadge`, Slack's mention favicon (a `data:` icon). In sidebar rows the count sits at the row's right end, vertically centered, and steps aside for the close button on hover; pinned tiles keep the corner badge (issue #1107, from a contributor's fork); since #1300 the top strip's tabs show it at their right end too Fixed 2026-11-16 (#2246): a tile's count pill sits 2 pt from the tile's top right corner (was 4), so on a 41 pt tile it ends at 13 pt, clear of the 16 pt favicon that starts at 12.5 pt; the dot keeps its 5.5 pt inset. No telemetry: placement only. |
| Reopen closed tab ⇧⌘T | ✓ (full state) | ✅ | position, pin, group, custom name/icon, mute and the back/forward list (with the current entry) come back: the closing tab's list is kept (this session) and restored through Chrome's own `chrome::AddRestoredTab` (`restore:<tab id>` adoption, CEF_AC_TAB_HISTORY). After a relaunch it reopens with its list too (Chrome's closed-tab file), and so do open tabs (#273): each tab's list, as much as Chrome's session keeps (6 entries each side of the current one), is saved to the profile's `Ardia Tab History` folder 2.5 s after it changes and at quit, and comes back through `AddRestoredTab` when the tab first loads (lazily, as before). A tab closed for good, slept or unloaded drops its file; one never woken keeps it for 90 days |
| Drag reorder | ✓ | ✅ | rows, pinned tiles, groups, splits, across sections, into/out of groups, onto the page to split. Dia's drop gap is back in the sidebar (2026-11-19, the owner's Dia recording `rec/dia-gap.mov`, after a day of Arc's insertion line): the dragged row lifts out and its slot closes, and the rows part to open a gap of its height where it would land, in the list, the pinned rows, a group, an open Top Apps folder panel and for a Top Apps tile brought over the list; there is no line. Measured at the recording's 60 Hz updates: a row the drag passes slides into place 77 % in 17 ms, 95 % in 50 ms and still by ~83 ms, our reorder spring (critically damped, ω 90); let go, Dia's dropped row settles in ~0.12 s (88 % at 90 ms); ours lands on the same reorder spring (ω 90, ~0.08 s), the pre-Arc landing, after the owner found a softer ω 45 landing laggy (2026-11-19: 251–269 ms from release to settled, against 66–154 ms before the Arc work and now), with no overshoot either way. A row's slot closes inside a pinned section or a group too, with no 3 pt jump of the rows below (a folded section's own gap now closes with the row). The ghost is solid (the owner's call, 2026-11-19, after a day of Arc's see-through white at 0.15): the drag silhouette (`dragSilhouette`, #0F0F14 in dark, white in light) at 0.96 with a hairline `dragBorder` (white 0.33 in dark, black 0.28 in light) and its shadow (black 0.45 in dark and 0.18 in light, 10 pt blur, 4 pt down), its text at full strength, as before the Arc work. Since 2026-11-19 (the owner: "match the colour of the profile") the ghost and the split row's panes take the profile's colour (`profileSurfaces.ts`): the ghost is the profile's selected-row fill made solid and stepped 0.07 OKLab lightness away from its text (deeper in dark, a touch lighter in light) in the profile's hue, so the neutral profile keeps the near-black / white solid and Blue or Green get a deep navy or deep green; split panes are tinted steps of the row they sit on (rest, hover, pressed) with the shown pane a stronger step, every title at 4.5:1 or more (a pill steps further when the profile's backdrop needs it), and the split preview draws the same pills and its empty slot in the hover tone. A ghost carried into another profile takes that profile's colours, like the sidebar it is over. A single tab resting over the middle half of another tab row (0.25 s with the pointer within 4 pt; passing through, or a slow drag that keeps moving, only reorders, so rows never flicker into a split as the drag goes by: the owner's recording of 2026-11-19, where every row passed lit and previewed a split for a moment) lights that row's left or right half (each half the row's height and corner radius, 14 pt apart, white at 0.42 in dark as Arc's) and let go makes a split with the dragged tab on that side (`dropTabOnRow`, the anchor's split takes it as a third pane, a full one says so). While the half is lit the row already shows the split it will become (the owner's request, 2026-11-19): its content gives way to the split row's own pane layout (same inset, gap, pill height and corner), the row's tab or panes in the slots they keep and an empty pane in the lit colour on the dropped side, so the drop changes nothing but the dragged tab filling that pane; across a side switch the panes slide to the other slots; opacity and transform only, critically damped in ~0.1 s, nothing else in the list moves; a full split shows the plain lit half. Meanwhile the ghost turns into the pane the dragged tab will be (the owner's request, 2026-11-19): it shrinks to the preview's empty slot (on a 172 pt row, 80 × 26 pt with a 6 pt corner for a two-pane split, half the row inside its 4 pt inset and 4 pt gap, and 52 × 26 pt, a third, when the row already has two), shows the pane's favicon and title, and settles into that slot instead of following the pointer, critically damped in ~0.1 s; across a side switch it slides to the other slot with the preview's panes, off the half it grows back into its row under the pointer, and the drop puts the real pane exactly where it sat. Size, position and the crossfade run on the ghost's existing Animated path, with no React work per frame (`marks.pane` from `previewPaneSlot`, `splitPaneGeometry.ts`). When the split is made from below the row it joins, the new split row starts where the preview showed and slides up with the rows below on the reorder spring instead of jumping the dragged row's height; a list tab dropped on a pinned row's half becomes a pinned row in that split and a pinned row dropped on a list tab's half becomes a list tab in it (the owner's call, 2026-11-19: the dropped tab takes the anchor's section through the same placing as Pin and Unpin, in one store update, so sync sees an ordinary pin or unpin; it lands right below the anchor, where it stays once the split is undone by Remove from Split View, Separate All Tabs or closing a pane; the page's split targets, Open as Split and a third pane do the same; a new tab or a link opened beside a pinned row still stays unpinned), and a selection, a group, a split row, a dropped file, a Top Apps tile (dropped on a row, or a row dropped next to one) or a platform without split view gets a gap instead, and a drop no longer groups (groups come from the menus: New Group with Tab, Move to Group). A drop on a row's middle no longer makes a group of the two (2026-10-12 to 2026-11-19); while a half is lit, the gap stays where it was, so the rows don't move under the pointer. Over the Top Apps a dragged row's ghost turns into a tile of the grid's tile size with its icon centred under the pointer in 0.1 s (critically damped) and back when it leaves; the reverse holds for a Top Apps tile dragged out (the owner's Arc recording, 2026-11-19): once the drop leaves the grid (the same over-tiles test, so the gap opens as it changes) it becomes a row ghost of the list's row width with its icon and title, the pointer at 37 % of its width and its middle as in Arc, in the same 0.1 s, and a tile again back over the grid (Arc took 9–10 frames at 120 fps, ~80 ms); a folder tile keeps its folder look; the ghost is clipped at the sidebar's edge as Arc's is (Arc cuts it where the page begins; ours at the sidebar's bounds, 190 pt at the default width and following a narrower or peeking sidebar), so a wide row or a tile turned row never overhangs the page, and over the page it gives way to the page's own drag picture as before; the tile ghost is as solid as the row. The dragged tab ends up the shown pane every time: before 2026-11-19 a target that loaded as the split appeared (a restored tab shown for the first time, YouTube or Reddit) could take page focus and with it the active pane (2 of 5 drops); a page in a split pane that isn't selected now never takes focus by loading or by Chrome activating it on its own (`ArdiaWebView` honours `selected` as WebKit pages did). Counted in `tab_presses`: `drops_gap` (`drops_line` while the line was shown), `drops_split_left`, `drops_split_right`, `drops_tile`, and `drops_split_pinned` / `drops_split_unpinned` for a split drop that moved the tab into the pinned rows or out to the list (split counts only when the split is made: a full split or a refused drop counts nothing); watch the split counts against `drags_without_drop` for a drop users miss, and `drops_split_pinned` against `drops_split_left` + `drops_split_right` for how often a split crosses sections. in the top strip a group's member dragged past its chip or close button (a collapsed group's shown tab: off its end) leaves the group, and Chrome's mirrored groups follow without the strip looping (a member dragged out of a pinned group used to set Chrome and the store re-arranging forever). Tabs on top (fixed 2026-10-01, the owner's 0.2.18 report: pressing a tab moved the window instead): tabs, pinned tiles, splits (as one), groups (by their chip) and group members trade places along the strip; a tab dragged into the pinned tabs is pinned, a pinned tab dragged out is unpinned; a tab or a split's pane pulled below the strip goes to the page's split targets, let go away from them or outside the window it opens in a window of its own (or joins the window under the pointer), as in Dia; the window never moves, even with one tab. While it's off its list the tab is Dia's picture of the window under the pointer, anywhere on screen (a non-activating panel; the pill first; over another window's tabs a pill, and that strip makes room where it lands; a background tab shows its last picture). Smoothness matched to Dia frame by frame (2026-10-02, the owner's "not as smooth as Dia"; `dia-spec.md` › Reordering, frame by frame): no stall at the press (the window snapshot waits for the tab to leave its list and takes ~17 ms, not 0.13–0.35 s), the dragged tab opaque, neighbours sliding aside as it passes on Dia's ~0.07 s spring, one store update and one Chrome strip command on drop and the tab settling into its place; nothing writes the store while a tab moves along its strip. The dragged tab draws over the tabs it passes, and with nothing pinned the sidebar's pin target takes no room, so no row moves as a drag starts or ends; hovering it opens the room of the tile the drop makes (41 pt at y 54, 21 pt clear of the window buttons, the pin centred, its dashed outline and pin in the sidebar's drop mark colour (`dropMark`, near-white on dark), accent blue until 2026-11-19) on the reorder spring, the rows sliding to where they'll sit once it's pinned (2026-10-15, the owner's report: the dashed box was 17 pt tall under the window buttons with the pin filling it). `apps/browser/scripts/tab-drag-test.mjs` (18 cases through AppKit's event path, sidebar included; checks that a drag changes nothing until the drop, that a dragged tab draws over its right neighbour, that sidebar rows don't move as a drag starts or ends, that the rows open a gap of one row's pitch where the drop lands and that a row's halves split, and that a sidebar row's ghost lands exactly on its row). Fixed 2026-10-01 (rec1522): the pill cross-faded into the card with its title scaled up to fill it ("ease notes -"), and the card grew into a new window as its top crop stretched to the window's shape (a doubled, offset page behind the real one, with the new window already showing empty under it); the picture now changes at once, grows as the whole window lined up with the new one, and fades only once that window shows its content. Left for a person: the drags with a real mouse, the picture by eye on a real screen A press that drifts up to 10 pt and leaves the tab where it was is a click, in the sidebar, the pinned tiles and the top strip (#947, 2026-11-07: a trackpad click that moved 5–9 pt became a drag that settled back, so the tab didn't open and needed more clicks; measured with window-delivered press, drag and release: 5, 6, 7 and 9 pt drifts did nothing before, all open the tab now, a 14 pt press and a 40 pt out-and-back stay drags). On Linux a drag in any space but the one the window opened in snapped back with no drop gap (#2341, 2026-11-17: the drag measured the launch space's scroll view, off-screen after a switch); checked headless with three spaces, every way of switching, slow and quick drags, a scrolled list and tiles dragged to the list (`apps/linux/scripts/acceptance`, F28). Watch `tab_presses.drags_without_drop` against `drags` on Linux. Across profiles, as in Arc (2026-11-19, #2212): a tab row or Top Apps tile (a selection too) dragged in the sidebar carries over to another profile. Holding it 0.5 s over a profile's icon in the switcher lights the icon (the row selection fill behind it) and pages to that profile; the sideways swipe works the same way. Holding it 0.55 s in the 16 pt band just inside the sidebar's left or right edge pages to the previous or next profile in the switcher's order, as in Arc (no wrap: at the first or last profile that side does nothing and shows nothing); while the hold runs a soft glow lights that edge (a fixed gradient, `edgeGlow`: white at 0.105 at the very edge in dark and black at 0.1 in light, easing to nothing over 36 pt; faded in over 0.18 s and out over 0.15 s on the native driver, mounted only while a drag holds there), and after a switch the pointer has to leave the band and come back before it switches again. A row's split halves are not offered in the band, it ends before the area outside the sidebar where a drag counts as away, and it works in the peeking auto-hidden sidebar, which stays out. Measured during a drag held in the band (4 runs each, interleaved): JS frames 8.3 ms p50 and 9.3 ms p95 with and without the glow, none over 20 ms, and the drag's move step about 0.58 ms either way. The drag goes on there: the ghost stays under the pointer, the other profile's rows open the gap, its Top Apps make room and the tile and row ghosts change shape as at home. Let go at a slot and the tab moves there in one store update: the same move as Move Tab to Profile (the page opens again in that profile, the Move Tab to Profile? warning when it's on), placed at the slot and shown. The slot decides the pin (the list unpins, pinned rows pin it as a row, Top Apps make it a tile), and a shared Top App takes its copy there. Let go on a profile's icon and it moves there as the command places it, keeping its pin. A drop with no slot, Escape or a declined warning puts everything back: nothing moves and the window pages back to where the drag began. Dragging back home mid-drag is an ordinary reorder. A group, a split row, a live folder, a folder's tile or a dropped file can't change profile: a switch ends the group's drag where it stands (#2212's stuck ghost) and a dropped file's drag follows the page now shown. A split by drop and a tile folder stay within one profile, and the page's split targets don't offer another profile's tab. A private window has no switcher. A switch-profile shortcut pressed with the button held switches profile but ends the drag where it stands, with nothing moved: macOS's gesture system drops the press at the next mouse move after any key (checked through the window's real key routing, with keys taken before the window and with the touch recognizer ignoring keys; none kept it). Counted in `tab_presses`: `drag_profile_switches` (`drag_profile_holds` by an icon, `drag_profile_edge_holds` at an edge), `drops_other_profile` (at a slot) and `drops_profile_icon`; watch the drops against the switches for drags that change profile and go back. `components/sidebar/dragProfiles.test.mjs`, `state/windows/dropInProfile.test.mjs` |
| Switch tabs on mouse down | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-12, issue #1294 (a user: "Navigate on click down in tab bar, feels way faster"). As in Chrome, Safari and Arc, a plain press on a sidebar row, a pinned tile or row, a split pane or a top-strip chip switches to that tab as the button goes down (`state/sidebar/heldTabPress.ts`, from `Pressable`'s `onPressIn`, which React Native fires at the responder grant for the left button only, with no delay), so a switch no longer waits out the press: over 30 interleaved clicks with a 95 ms hold, mouse-down to the store's switch went from p50 103 ms (p90 114) to p50 6 ms (p90 14). A drag from that press still reorders (threshold unchanged) with the tab already selected; ⌘-click, ⇧-click and ⌥⇧-click still select on release without switching; a press inside a multi-selection keeps the selection for a drag and the release picks the one tab; a press on the shown tab, right-click, middle-click (closes), the close, mute, PiP and Back to Pinned URL buttons, double-click rename, accessibility and keyboard activation are unchanged. Popover lists (a tile folder's panel, which closes when the shown tab changes, a hover card's rows, a group's panel) still open on release, as menus do. Telemetry: `tab_presses` gains `down_switches` and `held_ms_p50`/`_p75`/`held_n` (the hold each switch no longer waits for); watch `down_switches` ≈ `clicks` and `held_ms_p50` for the time saved. Issue #1386 (a tab sometimes needs a second click, on every version) is still open: `tab_presses` also counts where a press is lost, so the cause can be read per version: `drop_no_release`/`drop_not_dragged`/`drop_no_start`/`drop_start_mismatch` (they add up to `drags`), `press_out_no_press` (Pressable ended the press without `onPress`), `unfocused_presses`/`unfocused_no_press` (the window was not key at press time) and the drift buckets `drift_*` and `no_press_drift_*` (under 5 pt, 5-9 pt, 10 pt or more). Watch which of them is the largest share of `press_out_no_press` and `drags` before and after 0.2.46-nightly.6; nothing about clicking changed. Tests: `apps/browser/scripts/sidebar-click-test.mjs`. 2026-11-16 (#1386, "have to double click to open tabs"): a press in the 1–4 pt gap between two sidebar rows (or two split panes) hit the list behind them and did nothing, the largest bucket of `tab_presses.repeat_landed_on` (`RCTView<RCTView<RCTScrollContentView<NSClipView`); each row now reaches half the gap up and down (`hitSlop` on `TabPressArea`), so the gap belongs to the nearer row. Watch `repeat_presses` and that bucket fall. 2026-11-17, Linux #2391: the outgoing New Tab no longer lingers for 400 ms over the destination. Linux cannot sink it behind the native page as the Mac does. Sidebar clicks and Ctrl+Tab are covered by Linux acceptance F29. Watch `usage_counts.new_tab_leaves_without_linger` count the skipped holds; `new_tab_lingers` retains the native-host path. |
| One click after a menu, and New Tab on a New Tab page | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-12, the owner: "on a New Tab page I have to click twice to switch a tab or even create a new tab". Two causes. (1) A press whose mouse up a menu or drag session tracked (a right-click or ⌃-click menu on a tab, the New Tab row or the command bar) stayed recorded in React Native's touch handler with its responder, so the next click anywhere else did nothing and the one after worked. The touch handler now cancels such a press at the next mouse down of the same button (`patches/react-native-macos`; another button's press is never judged from `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons`, which runs ahead of the event queue), and `ContextMenuArea` releases its own press, and only that one, as it opens its menu. Any menu that begins tracking (a page's, a text field's) cancels the recorded presses, a mouse up ends the gesture only when no other button's press is left, and Pressability reads the button of the touch that changed, so a right-click on a page no longer mutes the next click on a tab or the New Tab row. (2) With New tab opens: New Tab Page, ⌘T, the New Tab row, the + button and a double-click under the tabs on an empty New Tab page stacked another identical empty tab, which read as nothing happening; each now opens another tab (the owner, 2026-11-13: "by default i should be able to create as many tabs i want"); the Reuse an empty New Tab page setting (`reuseEmptyNewTab`) that focused the page's field instead was removed on 2026-11-16, with its `new_tab_reuses` count. Telemetry: `tab_presses` gains `lost_releases`, `repeat_presses`, `repeat_on_new_tab`, `repeat_gap_ms_p50` and `repeat_landed_on` (the view classes a do-nothing first click hit). Watch `repeat_presses` fall to about zero and read `repeat_landed_on` if it does not. Checks: `apps/browser/scripts/sidebar-click-test.mjs` (last check), `newTabBar.test.mjs`. Not yet confirmed with a real mouse: hidden instances post events. |
| Haptic tick while reordering | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › Tabs › Tabs and Top Apps › Haptic feedback when reordering tabs (`tabReorderHaptics`, Mac only, on by default): covers sidebar and top-strip reordering and a tab dragged over another window; the 0.2.47 settings cut had dropped it and it came back under the same key, so a saved off applies again (#2788). Watch `setting_tabReorderHaptics` in the settings snapshot |
| Pin / unpin (grid of pinned tiles) | ✓ | ✅ | pins are mirrored into Chrome's tab strip. Pinned tabs and pinned groups belong to the profile, as in Dia (its `pinned_container` nodes are per space, not per window): every regular window showing a profile shows the same tiles in the same order, and a pin, unpin, reorder, rename, icon or pinned URL in one window shows in the others at once (state/pins/pinMirror.ts). Each window has its own copy of each tile, and since 2026-10-21 a pin has one live page per profile, as in Dia (a Discord report: a playing video kept playing in the old window, and opening a pin in a second window reloaded it): selecting it in window B while window A holds its page moves that page into B, the same WebContents with its history, scroll, form text and a playing video, no reload; A keeps the tab selected showing the page's last frame (captured before the move, so no blank frame), greyed with Dia's wash (40% white in light, measured from the owner's Dia screenshot; 25% black in dark), and clicking it or selecting the tab there pulls the page back. B switching away or another window opening leaves the page where it is; closing B (also with Warn before closing off) returns it to A in place; closing the pin in either window unloads it everywhere; the sidebar in every window shows the same title, icon and audio state; the window focused last loads a shown pin at relaunch and the others show it frozen. A pin in a split keeps its own page; private windows and Little Ardia have no pins (state/pins/pinShare.ts, layout/frozenPage.tsx, pinShare.test.mjs 13 cases). Verified in a hidden instance, 2 windows × 3 pins: 4 Chrome pages instead of 7; the video handed over and back keeps its CDP target, timeOrigin and an in-page marker, currentTime advancing; PiP stays open through a handoff. Left for a person: the greyed frame and an open PiP window by eye. Before that, a copy loaded its own page when clicked; moving a tile's page to another window (drag, Move to Window, tear-off, Merge All Windows) leaves an unloaded tile behind and one tile per pin in the target. Unpinning elsewhere removes a copy that never loaded and keeps one with a page as a regular tab. Private windows and Little Ardia show none. Fixed 2026-10-06 (the owner's 0.2.27 report: a new window showed none of the pinned tabs, which were per window and only handed to the next window when their own closed); a relaunch with windows whose pins differ (0.2.27 sessions) gives every window the union. `state/pins/pinMirror.test.mjs` (9 cases, create/update/delete/relaunch staleness). Left for a person: the tiles in two real windows by eye. Fixed 2026-10-19 (the owner's report: sometimes no gap between the tiles until a profile switch): the tiles' 6 pt spacing came from the drag wrapper, which only the current profile's page has, so the profile pages laid out beside it (seen while swiping, and whenever the pager showed one) drew their tiles touching (x 6, 61, 116 instead of 6, 67, 128); and a switch that didn't come from the pager to a profile earlier in the order (the profile menu, a link opened in another profile) left the previous profile's page on screen, because the next render sent the pages' positions from a stale value after the pager had moved them. The spacing is now the tile's own, and such a switch sets the pager's position before the render (`state/windows/profilePager.ts`). Measured in a hidden instance: 6 pt on both pages, before and after paging to Work and back; the round trip shows the right page. Fixed 2026-10-23 (PR #81): after a tile was dragged and dropped or the drag cancelled, the tile after it sat flush against it (x 122 instead of 128: the drag animated the dragged tile's margin to 0 on the native side and React never set it back), and while a tile crossed rows its neighbours wrapped to the next row and back within a frame (eight tiles: the second row's last tile flicked to the third row for ~100 ms while the pointer was still). The tiles now sit in fixed slots of the grid (6 pt apart, a row every 47 pt from y 54, Dia's numbers) and slide between them on the reorder spring only while a drag is on; the dragged tile only hides. Measured in a hidden instance, eight tiles, every 16 ms through a cross-row drag, a drop, a cancel, a pin and an unpin by drag: no tile leaves the grid's rows, a neighbour reaches 90 % of its move in ~60 ms and settles by ~80 ms, nothing moves at the drop or the cancel, and every gap is 6 pt afterwards. Arc's balanced rows are a setting, off by default (issue #114, PR #20; Settings › Tabs › Grow Top Apps as the sidebar widens): at most 4 across, as few rows as that allows and as few columns as those rows need, so 5 go 3 + 2, 9 go 3 × 3 and 4 in a narrow sidebar 2 + 2, and widening the sidebar makes the tiles larger instead of adding columns (measured from Arc screenshots at sidebar widths of 147–451 pt; `pinZone.ts`, `pinZone.test.mjs`). Off, the rows fill with 50 pt tiles, as in Dia |
| Unpinned tabs shared across a profile's windows (Arc) | — | 🧪 | Settings › Tabs › Share tabs across windows, off by default (issue #111, 2026-10-24). Dia keeps unpinned tabs per window; Arc's sidebar shows a space's tabs in every window. On, every regular window showing a profile lists the same tabs and tab groups in the same order (state/tabs/tabShare.ts, the pin mirror's model: each window holds a copy linked by `pinKey`); each window keeps its own selected tab. A tab is live in one window at a time, as a pin is (state/pins/pinShare.ts): selecting it in another window moves the page there and the first window shows it frozen until clicked; rows elsewhere follow its title and address. Opening or closing a tab in one window does it in all; closing a window keeps its tabs in the others (not in Recently Closed) and its close confirmation counts only the tabs that really close; Move to Window brings the page over and leaves the row; clean up counts a tab as used if any window used it; Search Tabs and the command bar list each tab once. Splits, private windows and Little Ardia stay per window. Turning it off keeps each tab once, in the window where it is live (else the window used last). Unit tests: state/tabs/tabShare.test.mjs, state/windows/windowClose.test.mjs; not yet checked in an instance. A window only lists a profile once it has shown it, so closing the only window holding a profile's tabs used to close them while another window was open (a user's report, issue #1152: "Close 56 tabs?" across 12 profiles); since 2026-11-10 they move, with their groups, splits and live pages, into the open window used last instead, and the close warning no longer counts them (`state/tabs/sharedWindowClose.ts`; verified in a hidden instance: the moved page keeps its DevTools target id). Their pinned tabs and pinned folders went missing in that move (issue #2175: 33 pins and 27 folders lost across 6 profiles); since 2026-11-20 parking is decided before the hand-over and the open window takes the pins of each profile it was handed, so they are there when it switches to that profile (`handOverPins`, unit tests in windowClose.test.mjs, checked in a hidden instance and after a relaunch). Any session save that drops pins of a profile that still exists first keeps them in `pins-backup.json` (`session_pin_backups`, `session_pins_backed_up`; `session_pin_backup_failures` counts failed tries, and the session write waits for the backup to land). Dashboard: `usage_counts.window_close_tabs_kept`, `usage_counts.window_close_pins_kept` |
| Folder tiles in Top Apps (several pinned tabs in one tile) | — | ✅ | The owner's request (2026-10-23); Dia and Arc have no tile folders, so it follows iOS and macOS. Settings › Tabs › Make folders in Top Apps (`topAppFolders`, on by default at the owner's request on 2026-11-17, after two days off by default): off, the hold over a tile and New Folder… make no folder, and folders already made keep working. In the sidebar the folder's icons (14 pt, up to four) sit straight on the tile with no inner square, spread evenly: one row for one or two, two rows of two for three or four. Making one: drag a tile and hold it over the middle of another tile for 0.4 s (the target lights with a 2 pt ring on the reorder spring, in the sidebar's drop mark colour (`dropMark`: near-white on dark, near-black on light; accent blue until 2026-11-19) over a fill of the drag ghost's white) and let go, or pick New Folder with Tab in a tile's menu, which asks for a name. A tile or a list tab let go on a folder's middle joins it at once; passing over a tile's left or right side only reorders, and a tile you're reordering past never runs from the pointer before you reach its middle. The tile is a tile of the grid (same size, gaps and glass) showing its first four favicons as described above; while one of its pages is on screen it shows that tab's favicon in the selected style, with a small folder glyph in its corner. Its name shows in its hover card (with its tabs) and in the strip's tooltip, and Rename Folder… sets it. Sidebar: a click opens a panel under the tile, as wide as the grid, listing its tabs as rows (springing in from 0.92); a click selects a tab and closes the panel, as an iOS folder does. Rows drag inside the panel to reorder, or out of it (the panel fades as the pointer leaves) onto the grid, the pinned rows or the list, which takes the tab out of the folder. Top strip: the folder is one cell of the pinned dock with a compact 2×2 grid of its first four favicons (7 pt each, 2 pt apart), also with no inner square; a click opens a menu of its tabs with the one on screen checked, as 26c13e81's nested folder chips do, and the tab on screen sits right after the folder's cell. Right-click (both layouts): Rename Folder…, Remove Folder (its tabs become tiles again), Unpin N Tabs. Members are plain pinned tiles everywhere else: Chrome and extensions see pinned tabs, closing one unloads it and keeps it in its folder, unpinning or moving it to the rows takes it out, and a folder left with one tab is that tile again (an empty one is gone). Folders are the profile's: mirrored to every window, parked with the last window, kept across a relaunch and synced (`tilefold:` records, `docs/sync.md`; an older Mac shows the tabs as tiles and loses none). Checked in hidden instances: tab-drag-test's three folder cases (make by holding, join on drop, empty by dragging members out), 8 tiles reordered across rows with no row flicker and 6 pt gaps, two instances on a scratch sync folder (make, rename, add, unpin to one, relaunch) |

Not confirmed against Dia's code: whether Dia reloads a tile already at its base URL (its `canResetPinnedPage` suggests it doesn't). Tabs in a pinned group have a pinned URL too, as in Arc's pinned folders (issue #295, from Nitish's PR #296): it's the page the tab was on when it joined the group, a tile or pinned row keeps its own when it moves in, and it goes when the tab leaves every pinned place (state/pins/pinHomes.ts). ⌘W and closing the last window unload it at that URL, and it gets Back to Pinned URL (⌘↩, the row's hover button), Replace Pin with Current Page and Edit Pinned Page…; duplicating or reopening the group keeps it, and copies in the profile's other windows carry it. A tab is silent if either is on (state/tabs/mute.ts). | Auto‑clear abandoned New Tab Pages | ✓ | ✅ | on app resign-active / screen lock |
| Links `_blank` / ⌘‑click open new tab | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome makes the tab in the opener's Browser (`window.opener` kept) and the app adopts it; ⌘-click → background tab next to its opener (ledger 7). Chrome's opener rules (state/tabs/openers.ts, from `TabStripModel`): links opened behind (⌘-click, middle-click, Open Link in New Tab, Open Image in New Tab) line up after the opener in the order opened, after the tabs those opened too; a link opened in front (⇧⌘-click, target=_blank) goes right after the opener; closing the page goes back to the tab you used before it, as any close does (Close tab ⌘W row). Switching to a tab another page opened, typing an address or opening a link in front forgets the openers, as in Chrome (moving between an opener and its tabs keeps them). Only the switched tab's profile forgets them (#2862, 2026-11-19): a window that shows several profiles keeps the other profiles' opener chains, since a tab never opens one in another profile; before, a switch in Work sent the next link from a Personal tab above the ones it opened earlier. A link from a grouped tab joins the group; from a split, it goes after the split. Fixed 2026-09-30: a tab opened behind could come to the front (the owner's report: ⌘-click and Open Link in New Tab switched to the new tab). Its first LoadURL asks CEF for focus (FOCUS_SOURCE_NAVIGATION), CEF's ChromeBrowserHostImpl::OnSetFocus then makes it Chrome's active tab, and the app follows Chrome's active tab (onTabStrip); the page also loaded twice. Only the page on screen takes focus now (`Client::OnSetFocus`), and ACBrowserView drops the second load. Extension popups and side panels open links like tabs do (all dispositions, POST and `window.open` + `document.write` kept); their popups no longer leave an empty 750 × 782 window on screen each. Verified headless (CDP clicks, context menu through `ARDIA_TEST` `contextMenu`, 25 checks): the active tab, the opener page's `document.hasFocus()`, the order, one load per page, `window.opener`, private windows, splits, groups, pinned, Little Ardia. Links opened behind (⌘-click, middle-click, Open Link in New Tab, from Little Ardia too) load only when first shown, as in Dia: the row shows the address ("x.com/home") and the site's icon until then. Unlike Chrome, which loads them at once; the engine keeps each one's navigation (`CEF_AC_OPEN_URL_PARAMS`: POST body, referrer, initiator) for when it's shown, and a relaunch reopens it from its URL. Popups a page makes (`window.open`, target=_blank) still load at once, so the page can talk to them. Every link Chrome asks to open in a new tab or window now loads with its full navigation: a POST form ⇧- or ⌘-clicked keeps its body (it used to reload as a GET), Open Link in New Tab keeps the referrer (verified headless against an echo server, 9 checks). Fixed 2026-11-09: a ⌘-click that moved a few pixels started Chromium's page drag, which AppKit ended only after its 0.6 s slide-back, and the clicks made meanwhile reached the page together and opened duplicate tabs (#677). A failed page drag now ends at mouse-up (`EndFailedPageDragsAtMouseUp` in `ArdiaHostEngineDelegate.mm` turns off `animatesToStartingPositionsOnCancelOrFail` on the session `WebContentsViewCocoa` starts). Verified 2026-11-16 by the `page-drag` acceptance check in a hidden instance (a ⌘-click moved 6 px, then five quick ⌘-clicks): the drag ends 23 ms after mouse-up and each click reaches the page within 45 ms; on the build before, the clicks never arrived within 6 s. Left for a person: a real ⌥⇧-click (split; read from the click's own keys) and ⌘-click with a trackpad in the key window. A link opened in the background shows a "New tab opened" pill at the page's top right for 3.5 s (held while hovered); clicking it switches to the tab, and it goes away when that tab is shown or closed (issue #1107). Watch `usage_counts.new_tab_notice_presses` against `new_tab_notices_shown`. The link status bubble moves to the top left while the pointer is within 48 pt of the page's bottom edge, so it no longer covers footer links; watch `usage_counts.status_bubble_top_shows` (zero across many users means the native pointer distance stopped arriving) |
| ⌘‑click link creates a tab group with opener | ✓ | ✅ | setting in Tabs; links opened behind from an ungrouped tab (⌘-click, Open Link in New Tab). A link from a tab already in a group joins it whatever the setting (Chrome); from a split, the whole split joins. From a pinned tab (or a pinned group's tab), as in Dia (owner recording 2026-09-30): the pinned tab stays pinned and its links gather in a group of their own just below the pinned tabs, which its later links join (`TabGroup.pinnedOpenerId`) |
| Sidebar auto‑scrolls to background‑opened tab | ✓ | ✅ | Closing the selected tab (or unloading a pinned one) leaves the list where it is: the tab selected next isn't scrolled to, so closing a tab in a group no longer jumps the sidebar down toward New Tab (2026-10-25, issue #362) |
| Pill pointing to the selected tab when it is scrolled out of view | ✓ | ✅ | As Dia's (the owner's recording, 2026-11-15, 30 fps frames and a 2x crop): while the selected tab's row is wholly hidden below the list, a capsule about 50 × 28 pt with the tab's 16 pt icon and ↓ floats centred 6 pt above the list's bottom edge; with the row hidden above the list, the same pill with ↑ sits 6 pt under its top edge (Dia's binary has `scrollToSelectedRow` and both arrows; the recording shows only ↓). A row only partly cut off shows none. Its fill is solid (`selectedRowPillFill`, near-black as Dia's in dark, white in light). It enters as Dia's does, measured frame by frame: from 0.3 scale and 18 pt toward its edge it grows and rises over 140 ms, easing out (within 0.03 of Dia's scale at every frame), and leaves in 60 ms, shrinking to 0.8 and sinking 4 pt as it fades; Dia also blurs it, which a React Native view can't. Reduce Motion shows and hides it at once. A click scrolls to the selected tab with the same animated reveal as switching tabs (`revealTab`), and the pill goes once the row is in view. It follows scrolling, tabs opened or closed, layout and switching tabs. `SelectedRowPill.tsx`, `selectedRow.ts`. Telemetry: usage_counts' `selected_row_pill_shows` and `selected_row_pill_presses`; watch presses per show. |
| Title fade at trailing edge | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Favicons | ✓ | ✅ | per-profile cache on disk keyed by page URL and host, downloaded by the tab's own browser (no cookies); incognito icons in memory only; no third-party service; Dia's EmptyFavicon fallback (WP5) |
| Tabs keep running in background | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Move tab to profile | ✓ | ✅ | with Dia's one-time data-loss warning; the page reloads in the new profile and moves to its Chrome window (ledger 11). A shared Top App moved to a profile that shares its data doesn't reload: the window pages to that profile and its copy takes the same live page (see Share data between profiles) |
| Workspace / companion tab | ✓ (agent tasks) | ⏸ | an AI surface: the companion panel belongs to a Chat task |
| Copy URLs of selected tabs / all links as Markdown | ✓ | ✅ | |

## 3. Tab groups & live folders
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
