| Tab groups (create, rename, icon, pin, duplicate, ungroup, delete) | ✓ | ✅ | The header, as Dia's (owner recording 2026-09-30): icon, name, then a chevron right after it (∨ open, › closed, turning with the toggle; 8 pt from the name, white at 0.5), no count when closed, ✕ on hover (– on a pinned group with loaded tabs: Unload Tabs); no hover fill on the header, as in Dia (2026-11-10, the owner's capture; a press or a drop into the group still lights it, its corners concentric with the box's); the box itself (`TabGroupNeutralBackground` fill, `TabGroupSidebarStroke` edge, or the group's colour) shows only while the pointer is over the group (the innermost one: a nested group under the pointer lights its own box, never its parents', issue #206) or a drag is about to drop into it, fading in and out over 100 ms; at rest the header and its rows sit on the sidebar like any row, the header's icon at x 15 and its rows' 16 pt further in; the rows end 3 pt (`SIDEBAR_GROUP.inset`) inside the box's right and bottom edges, at rest too, as in Dia (2026-11-10, the owner's report that the selected row ran flush to the box: measured on Dia at 2.7 pt from the box's outer edge and 3 pt on issue #154's capture; a collapsed header with nothing under it keeps no bottom inset; verified in a hidden instance, the selected last row 3 pt from the box's right and bottom edges, before 0) (GitHub issue #154; the fade time is the hover card's, Dia's own wasn't recovered); opening or closing takes 150 ms (Dia's, measured at 60 fps: 140–160 ms, fast at first; was 240). Closing a group that holds the page on screen shows the tab just above the group (Dia), not the page's pinned opener. + collapse, colour, Move to Group, Remove from Group. Dia's two ways to end a group (1.50.1: `unparentGroup` / `softDeleteGroup`): Close Group (Recently Closed Groups, ⇧⌘T) and Delete Group (not in Recently Closed; the overflow menu's Recently Deleted Groups keeps it 7 days, Dia's purge interval). Closing a tab of a pinned group unloads it and keeps its row, as for pinned tabs. A tab that comes up while its group is collapsed (the hover panel, a link, a background open) stays under the header, focused or not, until it is closed, unloaded or hidden (GitHub issue #311, Arc's hide-on-click): clicking a collapsed header that shows more than the focused tab hides the extras and keeps the group collapsed, and the next click expands it; expanding or collapsing starts over with the focused tab only. Collapsing while a member is selected keeps that member listed after switching outside too (#1214, follow-up to #974), including Chrome-side collapse changes and nested groups; watch the existing `usage_counts.group_tabs_kept` counter for tabs retained on collapse. The list is `TabGroup.shownTabIds`, saved with the group and pruned when a tab leaves it or, in a pinned group, falls asleep. Verified 2026-11-10 in a hidden instance with real pointer events (issue #1131's sequence, several tabs from the hover panel, hide-on-click, start over, "–" in a pinned group, a regular group and a collapsed group inside a collapsed one; `foldReveals.test.mjs`). Dashboard: `usage_counts.group_tabs_kept` (tabs newly kept under a collapsed header) and `group_header_hides` (header clicks that hid them); kept tabs with almost no hides means the rule is wanted, hides close to kept means it gets in the way. Rename: double-click the header, Rename… in the group menu, or name the group ⌃⌘N just made, in the header's field (Return or a click away keeps it, Esc cancels). A double-click closes and reopens the group before the field opens, as each click toggles: Dia's header does the same (1.50.1: `TabGroupCellView` and `TabGroupView.HeaderView` act on every mouse-up and never read `clickCount`, which only tab rows, pinned tiles and favicons do), and Dia has no double-click rename for groups, so ours adds the rename and keeps Dia's toggles; with tabs on top or the sidebar hidden, Rename… opens a Rename Group sheet. The name reaches Chrome's group (`chrome.tabGroups` title), survives a relaunch and shows on a pinned group's copy in every window. Fixed 2026-10-19 (the owner's report "renaming tab groups dont work"): the field closed itself as it opened, keeping the old name, because select-all on focus ended the field's edit (our `selectAll:` for text fields called `selectText:`, which ends editing first); tab renames had the same fault. With tabs on top Rename… did nothing. No Icon (2026-10-24, GitHub issue #155) put Dia's neutral group symbol (SF `square.stack.fill`, which Dia's CustomIconUI loads) on a group; the picker dropped it once a group without an icon became the animated folder below (issue #383, PR #376), and a group still holding that symbol (or set to `"none"` by automation) draws the folder too, wherever a group's icon shows: the sidebar header, the drag ghost, the ghost a closing group leaves, the top tab strip's chips and Search Tabs' recently closed groups. Reset Icon goes back to the folder, and the group menu says Add Icon… for it. Dia's groups show that symbol by default; ours and live folders show an animated folder: open while the group is open or a drag is about to drop into it, closed otherwise, turning in 200 ms (smoothstep, hinged at the bottom, drawn ahead at 240 frames a second and played by Core Animation so a busy main thread drops none; Reduce Motion switches at once), in the group's or live folder's colour or #443AFF, with a dark palette in dark mode (the `ArdiaFolderIcon` view in `packages/shell`, native API 70). It used to be the first tab's favicon (a live folder's, its source's site). 2026-11-18, the owner preferred 0.2.47-nightly.5's folders to nightly.6's: #2250's white or grey folder for None and Neutral and its service icon on a live folder are taken back (#2108 is open again), and stay reachable as `folderStyle: "neutral"` and `liveFolderIcon: "service"` in `config.json` (`docs/config.md`, no Settings row); the box that fades in behind a live folder under the pointer and its fading refresh button stay, and nothing bounces. The web demo draws SF `folder` / `folder.fill` instead. With tabs on top symbol icons from the picker's Symbols tab draw as symbols (they showed their raw name). No telemetry: a default look has no use to count |
| New Tab in Group ⌥⌘T / New Group with Tab(s) ⌃⌘N | ✓ | ✅ | ⌃⌘N titles itself "New Group with N Tabs" for a selection |
| Nested groups (Arc's nested folders) | Arc ✓ | ✅ | A group can hold groups, to any depth, in the pinned section and the list. A group's rows, its own tabs and the groups nested in it alike, sit 16 pt in from its header, and each level steps in another 16 pt, as Arc's folders do (issue #214; before, only a nested group's tabs were indented). A nested group that comes first in its parent sits one row gap (3 pt) below the parent's header, so the header's hover fill never touches the nested group's box (issue #298). ⌃⌘N on tabs inside a group nests the new group there; New Nested Group on a group's menu adds one with a new tab, its name field open. Drag a tab into or out of a group at any depth; drag a group's header onto another group's header (its middle two-thirds) to nest it at the end, into its rows to nest it at that spot, or between top-level rows to take it out; a group never goes into its own subtree. Remove from Group takes a tab up one level, Ungroup hands a nested group's tabs to its parent. Close/Delete Group takes the subtree, and Recently Closed brings the tree back (inside its old parent when that's still open); Duplicate copies the tree; Move to Bookmark Bar files nested folders. Move to Group lists nested groups by path (Outer › Inner). Pinned nested groups mirror to every window of the profile and sync with their nesting. Chrome's groups can't nest: a nested group's tabs belong to its root's Chrome group. In the top tab strip a nested group is a folder chip inside its root's container (see Tab groups in the top tab strip) |
| Shared folders (Arc ✓; Ardia extra, Dia has none) | Arc ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-01, the owner asked for shared folders per workspace through iCloud Drive. A pinned folder's Share Folder… writes its links to `iCloud Drive › Ardia Shared › <name>` (one `.webloc` per link, subfolders as directories, the order in a hidden `.ardia-folder.json`) and opens the macOS share picker on that directory, where Collaborate invites any Apple ID; Add Shared Folder… (sidebar menu) binds a folder someone shared with you. Two-way and live: sidebar edits write the directory, a collaborator's edits show up in about half a second, links iCloud hasn't downloaded wait (an iCloud glyph on the header), and opening a link never writes. Per profile; removing the folder only unbinds it. No CloudKit or entitlement (`docs/shared-folders.md`) |
| Live folders: drag to reorder among pinned rows and pinned groups | — | ✅ | The pinned section below Top Apps is one free order of pinned rows, pinned groups and live folders: drag any of them above or below any other (a live folder by its header, with the group's ghost and gap). The order is kept across relaunches and is the same in every window of the profile (a live folder remembers the pinned row or group it follows, by pin key; when that one goes, however it goes (dragged out, unpinned from a menu, closed, or on another Mac), the folder stays where it was, under the item that was above it). Move Up / Move Down in a live folder's menu step through the same order. A group or live folder that was open folds while it's dragged, so the gap it opens is its header, and opens again once it lands; the fold is the drag's own and never reaches the store, Chrome's tab groups or Recently Closed. A live folder can't go into the list or into a group. The top tab strip keeps every pinned tab in its one pinned container whatever their order in the sidebar |
| Live folders: colour | — | ✅ | A live folder's right-click menu has a tab group's Color submenu (None, Neutral and the nine colours; no Match Site Color, a folder has no site) and its box takes the same tint a group of that colour has; Neutral, the default, is the old look (`color` on the folder in live.json, `liveMenus.ts`, `LiveFolderBlock.tsx`, #854). Watch `live_folder_color_picks` in usage_counts. Since 2026-11-16 (#2216) the header hovers as a group's does: no fill on the header, and a neutral folder's box shows only while the pointer is over it, fading over 100 ms (a coloured one keeps its tint at rest); the refresh button fades in and out with the hover, crossfading with the closed folder's count. No telemetry: a hover look has nothing to count. Since 2026-11-20 (#3003, PR #3004 by YishenTu) the header is laid out as a folder's: it spans the box as a group header does (it sat 2.5 pt in) and draws its title with the folder header's own block (`FolderHeaderTitle`), so icon box, title box and font match a folder's at every sidebar text size, light and dark, open and closed; checked by measuring both headers' native frames in a hidden instance. No telemetry: an alignment has nothing to count |
| AI auto‑naming + emoji, shimmer on rename | ✓ | ⏸ | No AI. Unnamed groups are named from their tabs' titles instead (state/groups/groupNames.ts): the site name titles carry at one end (" / X", " - YouTube", "GitHub - …") gives "X", with the page too when every tab is on the same one ("Home / X" ×5 → "X Home", Dia's AI said "X Feed" then "X Home"); a tab not loaded yet takes its site from a loaded one of its host, else from the host (x.com → "X"). It updates as titles arrive, with the label's fade; a name you give always wins. Dia's grey placeholder bar while its AI thinks isn't needed |
| Group colour from favicon/theme | ✓ until 1.28 | ✅ | neutral by default like Dia since 1.28; colour menu with "Match Site Color". A neutral group draws its box only under the pointer, as Dia does; a coloured one keeps its tinted box at rest too, or the colour wouldn't show at all (2026-11-09, the 0.2.45 film: `ardia groups create --color purple` showed no purple; watch `usage_counts.cli_group_edits`). Each colour in it shows a 12 pt swatch of the colour the group takes before its name (Neutral: the neutral chip's grey, its light and dark shades), the current one checked; macOS 27 hides menu item images unless an item asks to keep its own, so they had stopped showing (2026-10-19, the owner's report). Ours: a "None" colour at the top of the Color menu, above Neutral (issue #206, Mohammad Baqer's PR #179), leaves the group with no background and no outline, like Arc's folders, in the sidebar and the top strip (hovering still shows the fill there); its swatch is an empty ring, and Chrome's mirror of the group (for extensions) sees it as grey. Sync sends None as Neutral in the group's `pin:g:` record, which released builds can draw, and the None itself in a `pgc:` record they ignore (docs/sync.md › `pgc:`) |
| Peek group | ✓ | ✅ | Hovering a collapsed group opens Arc's folder panel, styled like Dia's popovers: a search field over every tab in the group, nested groups' included, most recently used first, each with when it was last used. Typing filters by title, site and URL; ↑/↓ move, Return opens, Escape closes. The field takes the keyboard once the pointer is on the panel and hands it back (page, find bar or address bar, caret included) when the panel closes; with a query typed, the panel stays until Escape, a click elsewhere or hovering another row. Its height is set by the group, not the filter, so it never resizes under the pointer. |
| Single‑tab group auto‑ungroups | ✓ | ✅ | for ⌘-click groups (`autoUngroup`), like Dia |
| Close group → Bookmarks Bar / Recently Closed Groups | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Tab groups in the top tab strip | ✓ | ✅ | As Dia's (owner recording 2026-09-30, numbers in `dia-spec.md` › Top tab strip › Groups): a group is one container (the pinned dock's fill and stroke, lighter while the pointer is on the chip) holding the chip (group glyph + name) and its members. Collapsed, only the window's active tab stays out, attached after the chip. Clicking the chip slides the members out of it at full width, with a divider and Close Group (✕) at the end, and back in; the container and the tabs after it move on Dia's spring (response 0.30 s, damping 0.82; in the app 0.31 s / 0.81 expanding, 0.30 s / 0.82 collapsing, 1.1 % overshoot both ways, as Dia's). Hovering the active tab of a collapsed group shows "–" instead of ✕: it hides the tab in the group (it fades out in 0.12 s while the container closes up) and goes back to the most recent tab outside the group. Picking a member leaves the group open: in the recording it closed only when the chip was clicked. Right-click on the chip: the group menu. Pinned groups and nested groups (2026-10-23, an Arc feature; Dia has neither): every pinned tab, tile or row, stays in the one pinned container, and pinned groups follow it as group containers in the sidebar's pinned order, with the sidebar's open/closed state on the chip. Inside a root group's container, each group nested in it is a folder chip (24 pt tall, radius 7, the group fill and stroke, the group glyph or icon, its name and a ▾) in its place among the root's tabs: a click opens a menu of its tabs, the active one checked, with deeper folders as submenus (as a bookmarks bar folder does), picking a tab selects it, and right-click is the sidebar's group menu. When the window's tab is inside a folder, that tab sits right after the folder's chip, attached to the card like any selected tab, whether the root is open or closed (closed: root chip, folder chip, tab), so the strip shows the path to it. Verified in a hidden instance by hit-testing the strip (closed root: chip 164–218, folder 228–304, the tab 314–484; open: a tab, two folder chips, the active tab after its folder, ✕) and with clicks: the folder chip's menu came up with the active tab checked, its right-click menu was the group menu without Pin Group. Making a group by dragging (2026-10-23, the owner's recording: dragging one tab onto another only reordered them): a tab (or a selection) let go over the middle half of an ungrouped tab makes a new group of the two in its place, as in the sidebar, and let go over a group's container (away from its ends) joins that group at its end; while the pointer is there the target lights with an accent ring and nothing slides, and a neighbour moves aside only once the pointer passes its far quarter. Removed on 2026-11-16 along with its setting (Drag a tab onto another to group them, `dragToGroupInTopStrip`, off by default since 2026-11-04, issue #861) and its `tab_strip_held_onto_drops` count: a drag in the strip only reorders, neighbours moving aside at their middle (`tab-drag-test.mjs`). The tab menu's New Group with Tab and Move to Group, ⌃⌘N, the command bar and ⌘/⇧-click selections already worked in the strip. Left for a person: "–" and the hover states by eye, the folder chip's look next to Dia's chips Departures from Dia (2026-11-20): the group name is neutral (the colour stays on its icon); it runs to 120 pt of text and fades at 80 pt only when the strip is short of room, and a group whose tabs moved into the open tabs menu says how many on its chip ("+1"). Fixed 2026-11-20 (#3257, owner's recording of 0.2.47): opening a group of nested folders flickered for about a second (loose tabs lost their titles and one dropped into the open tabs menu and back, folders arrived one at a time clipped and overlapping, the row re-laid out several times) because folder chips were measured through the clip the opening pill animates, so every frame re-ran the strip's fit. Folder chips are now measured once in an invisible unclipped layer, a group chip is its icon plus its measured name at the current cap, members keep their full width and are revealed by the pill, and the pill and the loose tabs glide on one 180 ms ease-out with no overshoot (Reduce Motion jumps). Filmed by window id at 60 fps: open done in ~0.2 s with every member whole from its first frame. `stripToggle.test.mjs`. Telemetry: `usage_counts.tab_strip_toggle_remeasures` (measurements that change while a group opens or closes) should stay at zero; `tab_strip_group_toggles` gives the base. |
| Meeting tab groups (auto for calls, countdown wiggle, "Open All and Join") | ✓ | ✅ | calendar from macOS EventKit |
| GitHub / Bitbucket PR live folder (checks, stacks, review requests, hover preview) | ✓ | ✅ | the user supplies a token or their own OAuth app. Ours adds (Dia has none of these): Hide Until Refresh from the ✕ on hover of a pull request or a stack's header, shown only while none of them has an open tab (a pull request with a tab open shows − to close the tab instead), which keeps it out through automatic refreshes and relaunches until a manual refresh (header button, folder menu, Settings); one that merges or closes while hidden drops out. Per-folder Group Stacked Pull Requests and Show Completed, both on by default (folder menu and Settings › Live Folders). With Show Completed off, a pull request that merges or closes leaves the folder once its merge has played, and its open tab closes (Reopen Closed Tab brings it back); one you're looking at closes when you switch away from it. Bitbucket merges and declines show under Completed, as GitHub's do. The folder menu's Repositories submenu hides or shows each repo with open PRs, per folder (as Arc's), so a work profile's folder can leave out personal repos; it stays open while you check repos, has Show All and Hide All, and with Show New Repositories off a repo the folder hasn't seen starts hidden; a hidden repo stays listed until it's shown again. A tab opened from a live folder keeps the item's page as its home, as tabs in pinned groups do (GitHub issue #411): once it wanders off, the row's icon turns into Back to Pinned URL on hover, and the row's menu and the Back to Pinned URL shortcut take it back; closing and reopening from the row opens the item's page. The tab keeps that home while the item is listed, hidden until refresh or under Completed; it drops it once the item leaves the folder (past the 20 kept under Completed, merged while hidden, or in a repo hidden from the Repositories submenu, until that repo is shown again). Dashboard: `usage_counts.live_completed_tabs_closed`, `live_row_hides`, `live_stack_hides`, `live_repo_menu_picks`, `live_repo_hide_alls`, `live_new_repos_hidden_turned_on`. Refreshes don't repeat work (2026-11-17, #2182): folders with the same source, account and filters share one request in flight, a save that would write the same live.json is skipped, and finding a row's open tab reads an index built once per tab change instead of scanning every tab per row; watch `usage_counts.live_source_fetches_shared` against `live_source_fetches` (shared requests above zero once a profile has two folders on one account) and `live_saves_unchanged` against `live_saves_written` (unchanged macOS saves were writes before); on Linux watch `live_save_intents_queued` and `live_save_intents_unchanged`, which count queued changes rather than confirmed disk writes. GitHub requests follow T3 Code's rate-limit policy (2026-11-17, `githubLimits.ts`, `githubClient.ts`): every answer's `x-ratelimit-*` headers are kept per token, and automatic refreshes stop at the last tenth of the quota (the quota is shared with `gh` and every other app signed in as the same user) while a manual refresh may still spend it; a rate-limit answer (429, a 403 naming a primary or secondary limit, a GraphQL `RATE_LIMITED`) pauses automatic refreshes until `Retry-After` or the quota's reset, else 30 s doubling to 15 min, and the folder says when it will update again; at most 4 GitHub requests run at once. The listing is three searches sent side by side (authored, review requests, direct review requests; GitHub runs the searches of one query one after another), each asking only for a pull request's updated time, head commit, mergeability and check rollup state (1 point each); only pull requests whose answer changed, whose checks are still running and were read over a minute ago, or whose details are 10 min old, are read again (1 point per 10); folders on the same account share one listing for 30 s. Measured on the owner's account with 65 open pull requests (2026-11-18): a refresh with nothing changed takes about 2.0 s and 15 KB where it took 2.7 to 3.8 s and 59 KB, and a first load about 6 s where it took 8 to 10 s; review decision and draft state left the listing because reviews and draft changes move the updated time. Search results an organization withholds (a fine-grained token over its lifetime limit, an unapproved token, SAML) no longer break the folder (2026-11-18): it shows the pull requests the token can read and flags the rest, as Single Sign-On for SAML or as a token policy otherwise, and pressing the flag opens GitHub's own fix page from the answer. Rate-limit answers from the parallel searches count as one backoff step. Linux runs the same client through its host (the quota comes from each answer's `rateLimit`, as the host passes no headers). A refresh where nothing changed hands back the same item objects, so the store keeps its lists and nothing re-renders, rescans tabs or saves `live.json` (2026-11-17); the client keeps one account's listing ids and change keys plus the mapped items the store already holds, about 250 KB for 57 pull requests where it kept 440 KB of raw answers before. Dashboard: `live_github_requests`, `live_github_requests_held`, `live_github_rate_limited` (should stay near zero), `live_github_details_fetched` against `live_github_details_reused` (reused should dominate), `live_github_blocked_listings` (listings an organization's token policy cut short; should fall once people renew their tokens) |
| Documents live folder (Drive, Notion, Confluence) | ✓ | ✅ | same: user-supplied Google OAuth client / Notion / Confluence tokens |
| Unread pip on live folders | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Chat with a live folder | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Shared split view in sidebar | ✓ | ✅ | one row per split, a segment per pane. A split row moves as one, as Dia's split sidebar item does (2026-10-24, issue #68): Pin / Unpin on a pane pins the split as one pinned row (never Top Apps tiles), Move to Group, New Group with Tab and Remove from Group take both panes, and the row drags into groups, pinned groups and the pinned rows. A pinned split shows as a split in the profile's other windows (separating or flipping it in one follows in the others), is parked with the last window and comes back a split, and survives relaunch. Closing any pane of a pinned split (⌘W, Close Pane, the pane's menu), or of a split in a pinned group, closes the split as the one pinned item it is: all its panes unload together, the split stays one row and the window moves off it, as closing a pinned tab does (Dia's split item has one close button, ⌥-click to unsplit). It used to unload only that pane and drop it from the split, leaving separate pinned rows (2026-10-24, issue #162). In the top strip a pinned split is two pinned cells. Each segment shows its own hover ✕ (closes that pane; ⌥-click pulls it out of the split instead), as Dia 1.55 does (issue #726). Closing the focused pane (⌘W, its ✕, Close Pane) leaves the other pane on screen, the split dissolving into a normal tab when one pane is left; it used to jump to the next tab in the list, so both panes seemed to close (issue #700, 2026-11-10). Close Split View in the pane's menu and the sidebar row's menu closes every pane. Dashboard: `usage_counts.split_pane_closes`, `split_view_closes` |

## 4. Sidebar & layout
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vertical sidebar vs top tab strip (⇧⌘S) | ✓ | ✅ | per window; new windows follow Settings › Tabs. Switching (#1380, #1864): View › Show Tabs in Sidebar (checked while the tabs are in the sidebar), ⇧⌘S (Ctrl+Shift+S on Linux and Windows, rebindable in Settings › Keyboard Shortcuts), the tab menu, `ardia`, and since 2026-11-20 the command bar, whose row reads like the View menu item and names the layout it switches to: "Show Tabs Across the Top" with its icon, or "Show Tabs in Sidebar"; "top tabs", "vertical tabs", "tab layout", "horizontal tabs" and "sidebar tabs" find it whichever layout the window has. They all run the same store toggle as Settings › Tabs › Vertical sidebar / Horizontal row (`switchTabLayout`, `state/settings/actions.ts`), so the window changes the same way. Little Ardia's command bar leaves the row out, with every other command its menus disable there (`LITTLE_ARDIA_DISABLED_COMMANDS`), since running one would act on a main window. Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `tab_layout_switches_to_top` and `tab_layout_switches_to_sidebar`, one per switch from the menu, shortcut, command bar, tab menu or `ardia`, none for Settings or onboarding (the daily `settings_snapshot` has `setting_tabLayout`); `barActions.test.mjs` checks each path counts once. Watch how many users switch at all and whether they settle on one layout or keep flipping. The top strip matches a 2× Dia capture (`dia-spec.md` › Top tab strip): pinned tabs share one container, the selected tab is attached to the card (concave flares, the toolbar's website colour), the card starts at y 42 with 6 pt insets, and the window buttons centre on y 20.75. Tab groups: see Tab groups in the top tab strip. Owner report 2026-11-03 (“weird tab scrolling”): Top Apps and the pinned groups scroll with the tabs, as in Dia, and a scrolled strip is clipped at its start, after the window buttons, so nothing slides under them (the first tab’s flare still shows at the start); closing a tab with the pointer in the strip keeps the tab width and the strip’s length until the pointer leaves, so the next tab’s × lands under the pointer (Chrome’s rule). A split tab is clickable over its whole pill, each half focusing its pane; group emoji draw at the sidebar’s 16 pt and a tile folder’s mosaic at 20 pt, unclipped. Hover card (2026-11-07, an Arc user: the popup over a top tab covered the tabs to its right): the native two-line tooltip is gone; a tab or Top Apps tile shows the sidebar's title and URL card under the strip, left-aligned to the tab, after 0.5 s and at once when moving from a tab whose card is up, fading in and out in place on the sidebar card's 0.1 s curve (no scale since 2026-11-19). It takes no presses, so a click under it reaches what is below, and a press or a scroll of the strip hides it. A split pane pulled up to 9 pt vertically still opens its tab (#947). Menus (2026-11-13, #1580): right-clicking the strip's empty part or `+` opens the sidebar's tab menu (New Tab … Show Tabs in Sidebar, Auto-Hide Tabs, Close All Tabs), and a chevron after `+` opens the open and recently closed tabs menu, as on the sidebar's New Tab row: the same 22 pt plate, 4 pt after `+`'s, and it brightens whenever `+` or the chevron is hovered (tabs give the pair 26 pt). Once the tabs overflow at their narrowest, `+` and the chevron stay at the strip's trailing end outside the scrolling tabs, so they never scroll or clip, and the active tab is revealed clear of them; before, `+` was half cut off whenever the last tab was active on a full strip. The auto-hidden strip stays out while a menu opened in its own window is open. Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `tab_strip_menu_opens`, `tab_strip_overflow_opens`. With more than one profile (#1653): every profile's strip page stays mounted beside the current one, moved aside by a transform, and AppKit's hover tracking and tooltips ignore transforms, so the next profile's tabs sat under the current page: `+` showed another profile's tab card and its Close Tab (⌘W) tooltip, and cards for unseen tabs flashed in as the pointer left. Pages other than the current one are offstage now (`Offstage` in `components/ui/useHover.ts`): they take no hover, open no card and show no tooltips, as the sidebar's rows already did. Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `tab_strip_hover_cards` and `tab_strip_hover_cards_other_profile`, which should stay at zero. Groups (2026-11-14, the owner's recording): a collapsing group's hidden tabs are offstage too, so they open no card, and once collapsed they're hidden (`display: none`), so they take no hover or tooltip; a tab still under the pointer when it comes back on stage gets its card again. Collapsing or expanding a group no longer slides its tabs onto each other and over `+`: they stay in place and the pill covers or reveals them as it moves, and only the tab that stays visible slides. A strip that stops overflowing (a group collapsed, tabs closed, a wider window) no longer stays scrolled with its pinned tabs off the left edge. A hover redraws only the hovered chip's highlight and ×, and a group header's hover only its fill (`tabstrip/reachableHover.ts`); `apps/browser/scripts/perf/strip-bench.mjs` counts the strip's renders per hover sweep, collapse and profile switch. Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `tab_strip_group_toggles`. Fixed 2026-11-13 (#1587, spotted on #1583): a faint line ran along the bottom of the selected tab and curved up at its bottom corners, because its 0.5 pt rim was a closed rounded rectangle; it is now drawn twice as tall with the lower half clear, so only the top and the fading sides show, as in Dia, and the fade is unchanged (top outline within 1/255 of before). Telemetry: nothing new measurable (drawing only) Many tabs (2026-11-15, the owner: “the ux on the horizontal tabs layout is very bad atm with a bunch of tabs”; two rounds, scored by `apps/browser/scripts/topstrip-ux.mjs`: 59 scenarios in hidden instances, numbers from pixels, hit tests and the strip's own fade inputs). Tabs used to stop shrinking at 96 pt and scroll, so 30 tabs in a 1280 pt window showed 10, each with about four characters of title, the selected one too, and nothing said 19 more were off-screen. Now each tab gets just enough room to be told apart, as Dia 1.57.1's `TabStripLayout` suggests (per-item `minWidth` and `hasCloseButton`, `primarySelectedTabID`, `lockedMaximumTabWidth`, `fadeWidth`): tabs share the room evenly down to their own minimum, then the strip scrolls; a tab with its own favicon goes down to 32 pt, favicon only (the pinned dock's 36 pt pitch); a tab whose favicon it shares with another tab, or that has none, keeps 64 pt so its title shows; the selected tab keeps 128 pt. Titles show down to 52 pt with a fade a third of their room (8 to 24 pt), so 15 tabs at 1280 pt keep a title each. The × shows on hover and the title has its room at rest, as in Dia (`showsCloseButtonOnHover`) and the sidebar; an inactive tab under 84 pt has none, so a click meant to select it can't close it (⌘W, middle-click or the menu close it). Closing tabs one after another with the pointer still works at any count: the strip holds its layout while the pointer stays in it, the 128 pt slot stays where it was and the tab that slides into it keeps its width and ×, whatever tab the close selects (the shared rule picks the most recently used awake tab, not the neighbour), and closing the last tab slides the rest right so the new last tab's × is under the pointer. A selection change springs the widths (Dia's group spring, 0.30 s, damping 0.82) instead of moving every tab between the two by 96 pt in one frame. The strip fades over 88 pt at an edge that hides tabs, down to a quarter at the edge, as one mask over its pixels (`EdgeFadeMask`, a gradient layer mask that follows the scroll view natively, as Dia's `TabStripClippingView` does), so a tab crossing the edge fades along its width instead of dimming whole; owner's pick on 2026-11-16 over the per-tab fade, which ran one native Animated graph per tab: at 80 tabs a two-finger pan now takes about a quarter less main-thread time (450 → 339 ms forward, 426 → 312 ms back, `strip-scroll.mjs`, production bundles), its longest main-thread run drops from 16–21 ms to 9.5 ms, and the app makes half as many native-to-JS calls (192 → 101). Telemetry: nothing new; watch `tab_strip_scroll_stalls` against `tab_strip_scrolls`. On Windows the strip has no fade yet (React Native Windows has no layer mask); Linux uses a CSS mask. A vertical mouse wheel over the strip scrolls it 40 pt a line and never reaches the page (strip only: `testID` `ardia-top-strip`, `AppKitPatches.swift`); shift-wheel and precise scrolling are unchanged. A tab dragged within 48 pt of an edge scrolls the strip under it, and stops if the profile page changes. A click's scroll-into-view waits for that press to end and runs once. A collapsed group's chip comes into view with its tab; a split's focused pane gets the selected width in a group too. Measured: 30 tabs at 1280 pt, 20 of 27 favicons on screen (was 10) and 85 pt of selected title (was 34); with a third to half of the tabs on one site, tabs told apart without hovering 0.46 (favicon-only compact tabs: 0.25); closing five tabs in place 5 of 5 at 15, 30, 60 and 120 tabs, start, middle and end, and at 15 tabs in a 1728 pt window (was 1 to 3 closed, none in place); at 120 tabs an open and a close take about as long as in the sidebar and a switch about 20 ms longer, the width spring's relayout (no frame over 50 ms). Left for a person: the fade, badges and width spring by eye over a real backdrop (WindowServer captures fail on this machine with the display asleep), a real trackpad over the strip (synthetic phased gestures don't scroll any NSScrollView). Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `tab_strip_compact_layouts`, `tab_strip_most_tabs`, `tab_strip_edge_scroll_drags`, `tab_strip_reveals_after_press`, `tab_strip_held_closes`, `tab_strip_most_titled_tabs`; watch `tab_strip_held_closes` rise with `tab_strip_edge_scroll_drags`, and the `stuck_drags_ended_*` counts flat Departures from Dia for narrow windows (2026-11-20): the selected tab takes its full 173 pt when everything fits. Under pressure it gives back first, to 150 and then 128 pt, before any neighbour is hidden. Other titled tabs never go below a readable 96 pt. A tab that can only show a few letters moves into the strip's open tabs menu (the chevron), farthest from the selected tab first, instead of shrinking. On hover, a tab's close button overlays the end of its title, so the title doesn't shrink, and the group's close or collapse action is a boxed glyph (xmark.square / minus.square) with its own tooltip. Watch `tab_strip_most_tabs_in_overflow` (new, in the tab strip counts) beside `tab_strip_most_titled_tabs`. Fixed 2026-11-20 (#2946, from the 0.2.47-nightly.8 film): before anything goes icon-only, the strip now tries to keep every tab readable. The selected tab gives back 173 → 150 → 128 pt while favicon tabs keep 80 pt and titled tabs 96. If that doesn't fit, every tab gets 80 pt. Only then do favicon tabs go icon-only, and only after that do tabs move into the menu. Main had shrunk favicon tabs to 55–67 pt, showing only their icon, while the selected tab sat at 173 pt; with ten tabs that happened at windows around 1220–1290 pt. A group whose tabs could all move into the menu keeps its last one until every other candidate has gone, so “Trip +5” with the strip's titled tabs at 138 pt is now “Trip +4” with one member showing. Intended and unchanged: favicon tabs go icon-only before a titled tab shrinks below 96 pt or anything moves to the menu (at 1180 pt the ten-tab strip is full, so they stay icons); and the layout follows which favicons have loaded, not how the window got narrow (`stripWidths.test.mjs` checks 1180 and 760 pt opened directly and resized). Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `tab_strip_readable_layouts` counts layouts that kept favicon tabs titled by giving back width; watch it against `tab_strip_compact_layouts`.. Fixed 2026-11-15 (#724): with "Hide toolbar while scrolling" on (the default), the toolbar under the selected tab was a shade off the tab, a visible seam where Dia shows one surface, because the auto-hide bar painted a second translucent card over the pane's own card (window captures: 7 levels darker in dark, 4 lighter in light, Classic and Glass alike; with the setting off, or a site colour, they already matched). The bar now paints only its band, and the card goes over the page only where the bar overlaps it: the whole bar while peeking, the strip's bookmarks bar when collapsed, and, frame by frame while the bar glides between them, the part of the page it still covers (`TabPane`, `useToolbarMotion` › `cover`, `toolbarMotion` › `pageCover`). The cover is a native transform inside the page group, driven by the glide's own page and bar values; a first version sized it per mode, so leaving a peek dropped the card while the controls were still over the page and a contrasting page flashed through. Turning the setting off or on puts the motion at rest at once, and one value stopping (the controls' fade, when they unmount) no longer halts the page and the bar, so no glide is left halfway. Window captures in a hidden instance, Classic and Glass, light and dark, site colour off and on, shown, collapsed and peeking: the toolbar below the tab is within 0.5 level of the tab in every case, and the peeking bar over a page is unchanged. Window frames of peek to shown and peek to collapsed, light and dark, bookmarks bar on and off, and of the setting flipped or the mode reversed mid-glide: the page under the bar keeps the card all the way through, as before the seam fix, and every run settles with the page where its mode puts it. Telemetry: nothing new measurable (drawing only) |
| Toolbar hides while scrolling | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-20: off by default (the owner's call); Settings › Tabs picks it with two picture cards, Keep the toolbar or Shrink while scrolling. Once per install, on the first launch of this version, a saved on that was never chosen turns off; an on chosen by hand (or set by onboarding or a preset) stays, and an on that sync brings later is never turned off. The marker it leaves in settingsMeta is kept by 0.2.47, so a rollback and return doesn’t flip it again; the session version stays 11. Watch `scroll_toolbar_picks_keep` / `scroll_toolbar_picks_hide`. As in the owner's clip (2026-10-02): with tabs across the top, scrolling the page down collapses the toolbar into a 20 pt strip that keeps only the site's name, small and dim, centred; the page gets the other 21 pt back (its viewport moves up, a sticky header goes with it). Hiding is one 150 ms eased motion matched frame by frame to the clip (2026-10-02, 0.2.23): the page glides up with the bar's band while the controls fade and shrink toward the top centre; showing mirrors it, 200 ms eased out (2026-10-03), the page gliding down with the band as the controls fade and grow back in; either reverses mid-motion from where it is. All on the native driver, no blur, a fade only with Reduce Motion. Hiding and showing don't resize the page (2026-10-03): in 0.2.23 every one changed its viewport by 21 pt, so pages sized in vh reflowed and a canvas re-created on resize blanked for a frame (ardia.dev's 3D hero blinked on each hide and show, owner's 120 fps recording). Now the page keeps the strip's size from its first hide until the next document: the shown bar slides it down and its bottom 21 pt goes under the card's edge (a fixed footer included; the link status bubble stays above it); a page that never hides the bar is never resized. The one resize, at the first hide, is held in place by the transform in the same frame. Changed 2026-10-25 (#324, a tester: GitHub's bottom-fixed Collapse sidebar button sat 21 pt lower and half under the card's edge whenever the bar came back): once the show glide ends with the bar still shown, the page is resized to fit under it, so bottom-fixed content is back in place; the next hide grows it again at its start, held in place the same way. Neither resize lands mid-motion, the hover peek resizes nothing, but a page sized in vh or a canvas cleared on resize sees one resize per hide and one per show again. Direction comes from the page's real scroll, the document or an element scroller filling the window (page_script.js › Scroll direction): 24 pt down in one gesture hides, 12 pt back shows, only while the user is scrolling (scroll restoration or a page scrolling itself doesn't hide it), never on a page with under 120 pt to scroll; the scroll move the bar's own resize causes (the clamp at the bottom) is absorbed, so it can't oscillate. Changed 2026-11-13 (#1301, Shorts and Reels: the bar collapsed and came back on every swipe, resizing the web view twice over hardware-decoded video): a scroller whose computed `scroll-snap-type` is `y` or `both` with `mandatory`, or `block` with `mandatory` (`inline` in a vertical writing mode, where the logical axes swap) (a paged feed, one viewport per swipe, far past the 24 pt) never reports down, so the bar stays as it is; x-axis and `proximity` snapping and no snapping hide it as before, the scroller is the element that scrolls (the page's root element for the document), and going back up in a feed still shows the bar. Changed 2026-11-10 (#631, #1138: Discord's chat bounced the bar while scrolling up): while you wheel, a scroll move against the wheel's direction is the page's own (a chat re-pinning to its bottom after the bar's resize, or loading older messages and moving its scroll position down), so it never flips the bar; keys, clicks, a drag with a button held, touch, a pinch and a sideways wheel clear the wheel's direction, and a gesture's zero-delta start and end keep it. Watch usage_counts toolbar_scroll_hide_bounces against toolbar_scroll_hides. Always shown at the top, on a tab switch or navigation, with the command bar (⌘L), find, a site popover or history menu open, and in page full screen it stays out of the way as before. Resting the pointer on the strip for 150 ms peeks the bar over the page without moving it; clicking the strip brings it back. Per pane in split view; private windows too; Little Ardia and the sidebar's own address bar keep theirs. The sidebar layout with the toolbar on the card hides it too (2026-10-08, the owner's report: it only worked with tabs on top). Settings › Tabs › "Hide toolbar while scrolling" (synced). It is its own switch, not Auto-hide tabs (2026-11-10): with it off a page's scrolling is not even recorded, turning it off shows every tucked toolbar at once, and it never moves the tabs (`autoHideTabs.test.mjs`, `toolbarAutoHide.test.mjs`). Verified headless (2026-10-02, CDP wheel input on local pages: long, sticky header, barely scrolling, inner scroller, 100vh hero; 31 checks incl. no height change over 1–1.5 s at the bottom and when nudging there) and `toolbarAutoHide.test.mjs`; 2026-10-03, 60 fps window captures on a vh hero + resize-cleared canvas page and on ardia.dev, windowed and acted full screen: one resize per document, then no content shift, blank canvas or reflow over every hide, show, reversal and tab switch, and the page's top monotonic through each motion (≤ 6 pt a frame). Left for a person: the feel on a real trackpad, hover peek with a real mouse, and the animation by eye (snapshots can't time it). Linux (#2390): the peeking toolbar is a page overlay region, so the page keeps drawing under it; verified 2026-11-17 under headless sway with a Wayland virtual pointer. Watch `usage_counts.toolbar_peek_page_hides` (Linux) against `toolbar_peeks`; it should stay near 0. Fixed 2026-11-21 (owner's recording, #3285): the page no longer flashes for a frame when the toolbar opens or a peek folds away; watch `toolbar_motion_page_hides` (Linux), near 0 |
| Auto‑hide tabs / Focus Mode (⌘S) | ✓ | ✅ | As in the owner's 240 fps recording (2026-10-06, `dia-spec.md` › Hiding and showing the sidebar): the sidebar, its traffic lights and the card's left edge slide together on an ease-in-out, cubic-bezier(0.42, 0, 0.58, 1) over 200 ms, the owner's pick over Dia's rise (2026-11-17: tried on the owner's own build and kept; slower to start and to land than Dia, never past 1; telemetry: `sidebar_slides`, with `sidebar_slides_past_target` still 0). Before that, Dia's rise without its overshoot (changed 2026-11-14, #1630: Dia's spring, response 0.215 s and damping 0.79, passes its target by about 1 %, so every page was laid out about 3 pt past its final width and resized back over 150 ms; now a 160 ms cubic-bezier(0.7, 1, 0.5, 1) that never passes 1, rms 2 % from Dia's frames with their overshoot taken out, 90 % of the travel in 100 ms; measured in a hidden instance, page innerWidth per frame over four slides: 1161 → 1342 and back with 0 px overshoot, at rest in 116–133 ms, where before it went to 1345 / 1158 and settled in about 250 ms; `dockMotion.test.mjs`. Telemetry: `sidebar_slides` and `sidebar_slides_past_target`, which must stay 0), the card's right edge stays and the page is laid out at every step. Hidden, the window has one bar: the sidebar button at the card's edge (x 27), back, forward, reload and the address; the traffic lights go out of the window with the sidebar (Dia hides them too) and come back with it or with the peek. One setting for every window (2026-11-10, a tester's report with tabs across the top: "I've already disabled auto-hide tabs, but it's auto-hiding again after scrolling, switching tabs or removing a tab"): Settings › Tabs › Auto-hide tabs (off by default, synced, both layouts) sets every window, and any change to it (Settings, sync, `ardia config`) puts every open window back in line. Per window, as in Dia (2026-11-13, a second window's ⌘S hid the tabs in both): ⌘S, View › Auto-Hide Tabs, the sidebar's menu and the toolbar button hide or show the tabs in their own window only (`BrowserWindow.autoHideTabs`, saved with the session and read by the launch window), and the menu's checkmark follows the focused window. New windows follow the setting; a reopened window (⇧⌘T, the Dock) comes back as it was closed, and a window made from tabs moved out of another (dragged out onto another screen, Move to New Window, a group moved to a new window) keeps that window's choice (2026-11-14, #780: it came up with its sidebar shown; `usage_counts.torn_off_windows_kept_auto_hide` counts them). Before, each window also kept its own copy (`sidebarOpen`) and only two of the paths that change the setting updated it, so a window could stay hidden while Settings said off (a second window's ⌘S, a synced or `ardia config` change, a reopened window, an older session): the tester's window was in that state, its strip peeking from the top edge and sliding away again on every scroll, tab switch and tab close, with the switch already off. Then (2026-11-10) no window stored it; now a window stores only its own ⌘S choice (`tabsShownIn`, `state/browser/model.ts`), a saved window without one that disagreed with the setting comes back as the setting says, and the session still writes `sidebarOpen`, from the window's choice or the setting, on each window and each closed window for the launch window and older builds (an older build reopening a window closed here used to get no value: its dock read that as shown and its View menu as hidden). The launch window, drawn before the app's code runs, takes its key from the setting too (`ArdiaEarlyWindowLayouts.mm` › `StateOf`), so a hidden layout cached for a window whose saved copy disagrees with the setting is not shown for the first frames after the update (2026-11-10, Codex review). A session from before the setting existed starts it on when every saved window had its tabs hidden (2026-11-08). The toolbar tucking away as you scroll is a different switch, Hide toolbar while scrolling, and each row's description now names the other. Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `auto_hide_tabs_toggles` (this Mac's choices only, not values arriving by sync), `tab_strip_peeks`, `windows_realigned_to_auto_hide_tabs` (windows put back in line at launch: a one-off bump after the update, then zero) and `tab_peeks_with_auto_hide_off` (hidden tabs peeking, from the side or the top, while the setting is off: since tabs hide per window, these are windows hidden with ⌘S); the daily `settings_snapshot`'s `setting_autoHideTabs` by `setting_tabLayout`. `autoHideTabs.test.mjs`; verified in a hidden instance in both layouts (2026-11-10): the tester's saved state before and after, ⌘S and the menu across two windows, the saved session and two relaunches. Showing or hiding keeps the one sidebar mounted (it was unmounted and a second one mounted: 2282 views and a 1.2–2.3 s main-thread stall per toggle on the 200-tab seed; now the worst frame is about 30 ms). Kill switch `sidebarSlide` (off: no slide). Reduce Motion: no slide. `dockMotion.test.mjs`. ⌘S goes to the page first, as in Dia (#306): a page that takes it on keydown (preventDefault) keeps it, and so does a page with a text field or code editor focused (LeetCode doesn't preventDefault); a second ⌘S within the system double-click interval then hides or shows the sidebar without reaching the page. Anywhere else a single ⌘S toggles as before. With the address bar in the sidebar (Arc's layout), hiding is Arc's (owner's Arc recording 2026-10-06, `dia-spec.md` › Arc note): no toolbar row, the page fills the card, the bar stays in the sidebar and shows in the peek panel; the peek slides in on Arc's spring without fading, docking from it is instant, hiding takes 100 ms. With tabs across the top (2026-10-25, #361, a Discord report: it jumped and the margins changed): ⌘S slides the strip up out of the window, its traffic lights with it, while the card's top edge rises from 42 to 6 on the same spring as the sidebar, the page laid out at every step; showing mirrors it. The card keeps 6 pt on the sides and bottom both ways (hidden, it used to take the sidebar layout's 7). The strip stays mounted until it's out of sight and takes no clicks while it moves; the top-edge peek takes over once it's gone. Same kill switch (`sidebarSlide`) and Reduce Motion as the sidebar |
| Peek sidebar on hover at left edge | ✓ | ✅ | top strip peeks too. With tabs auto-hidden, an empty window shows the peek on its own and holds it, as Arc keeps its sidebar out while nothing is open; loading a page lets it slide away again (#1764). Empty means no page is open in it: only New Tabs and sleeping tabs, so a new window, a launch into a New Tab and closing the last open page beside sleeping pinned tabs. A New Tab beside an open page doesn't count (changed 2026-11-20): with "⌘T opens" on the New Tab page, every ⌘T slid the sidebar out about 150 ms after the press and away again about 280 ms after Enter, two moves on the most used key in the browser, against the rule that what's used all day stays still. Hiding the sidebar by hand in an empty window (⌘S, or turning Auto-hide tabs on) leaves it hidden until the window next empties, so it never comes straight back. Counted once per time it actually slides out for that reason, not when a hover already had it out: watch `usage_counts.sidebar_empty_window_peeks` against `sidebar_peeks`. A peek, from a hover or an empty window, moves the New Tab page's corner items clear of itself instead of covering them (2026-11-20): the notice cards in the corner beside it (Help fix Ardia?, the default-browser check-in) slide over by the peek's overlap, measured against the page in window points, and on a right-side sidebar the Personalize button and the release-notes postcard move back left. They move with the peek in the same 150 ms and start back as it leaves, as the docked sidebar keeps them clear by starting the page beside it. Watch `usage_counts.sidebar_peek_card_moves`. The peek is the window's one sidebar in a panel, not a second copy. Fixed 2026-10-07 (owner's recording): the panel never slid away once out. Its hover handlers are set only while it peeks, and react-native-macos built tracking areas only on geometry changes, so the panel had none (patched: hover props refresh them; the top strip's peek had the same bug). The traffic lights ride in the panel at its 6/6 inset (they stayed at the docked spot, 6 pt high and left of its buttons). Verified headless with a probe of the panel's tracking area (none before, present on every peek after) and the lights' layout log. Fixed 2026-10-08 (owner's recording): with the pointer resting where the edge strip and the panel overlap (x 6–8), the panel slid in and out by itself (AppKit sends no enter for a view that appears under the pointer, only a leave, so a 1 pt move left hid it), and leaving the strip up or out of the window never hid it; each leave now checks where the pointer went (the top strip's peek too). Verified with real pointer events (CGEvent) in a visible test copy, Dia's and Arc's layouts: 11 paths each (rest and jitter at the edge, into the panel and back, out right, up, out the left). Fixed 2026-10-15 (owner's recording): the edge strip started 46 pt down the window, so the top-left corner never peeked; it now runs the window's full height. Verified with real pointer events in a visible test copy, Arc's layout: the corner (4, 14), the very top (2, 6), 26 pt down and mid-edge each peek, and each leave (right, up out of the window) hides it. Fixed 2026-10-15 (contributor's Arc comparison): the panel slid away 1 pt past its right edge, where Arc keeps it with the pointer ≈21 pt out; it now stays while the pointer is within 24 pt of its edge, the window's full height (a click-through hold zone, `SidebarDock.tsx`). And slamming the pointer left overshot the window: with tiling margins it sits a few points in from the screen, and a half-screen window on the right has another app's window to its left. While the sidebar is hidden, the 24 pt left of the window (beside it, the window frontmost at its edge, not over another of our windows) peeks it too and holds it open; away from the screen's edge only while Ardia is the active app, so working in the app beside it doesn't pop the sidebar (`packages/shell/ios/SidebarEdgeWatcher.swift`). Its system-wide pointer monitor runs only while it can fire (2026-10-16): Ardia active, the panel out, or the window within 24 pt of its screen's left edge; otherwise, with the sidebar hidden and Ardia in the background, moving the pointer in other apps no longer wakes Ardia. Verified in a hidden instance with the dev pointer, three runs: 22 paths (2, 21 and 23 pt right of the panel stay, 30 pt hides; 10 and 23 pt left of the window peek and hold, 30 pt hides; gap ↔ panel; out of the gap up or to the page hides). Left for a person: the feel with a real mouse. Fixed 2026-10-21 (a Discord report: a sliver of the page showed with the sidebar peeking): the panel sat at the card's own 6/6/7 inset with a rounder corner (12 against 10), so the card's corners poked out round the panel's left corners; it now sits at Arc's measured 5/5/5 and covers them (0 page pixels round the panel in 2x captures, light and dark, against 50–57 before). Fixed 2026-10-22 (a tester's report on Discord: with tabs auto-hidden, dragging a tab always made a split): the page under the peeking panel counted as the page, so its left split target took every drop in the panel and the dragged tab's card covered the pointer, which slid the panel away; over the panel a drag now reorders as in the docked sidebar (rows up and down, into and out of groups), past its edge it offers the split targets, the panel slides away once the pointer leaves it and comes back at the window's left edge mid-drag to take the drop, and Esc cancels a sidebar drag. `tab-drag-test.mjs` (sidebar auto-hidden case). Left for a person: the same drags with a real mouse. Dock (2026-10-23, PR #19 by narasaka, Arc's behaviour): while Ardia is the active app, an open peek stays open with the pointer on the Dock over its column or left of the window, and the left edge still opens it where an auto-hidden Dock covers it, in the window on top there; clicking another app in the Dock or switching apps closes it at once (activation re-checks the pointer). Only the Dock's own windows at the Dock level count (never a Dock-owned wallpaper or backdrop), only on the window's screen, and the Dock lookup runs only with the pointer in that column (about 25 µs a hit test, one window-list query per new window under the pointer). Verified: the dev-pointer paths in a hidden instance (open from 10 and 23 pt left of the window, hold 21 and 23 pt right of the panel, hide at 30 pt either side). Left for a person: the Dock itself (left, bottom, right; auto-hidden or not; a second display), which a hidden instance can't hover. Fixed 2026-10-23 (issue #46: menus and tab options in the floating sidebar were hard or impossible to use): the panel slid away 120 ms after the pointer left its column, so the hover card's actions (Pin, Bookmark, Split, PiP, Reset), which reach well past it, went with it, and so did a rename or the icon picker started from a menu chosen right of the panel. The panel now stays out while a menu is open, while the pointer is on the hover card, while a row is being renamed or the icon picker or Downloads is open, and leaves once that ends with the pointer away (`state/app/transientUi.ts`). Left for a person: the same with a real mouse. Fixed 2026-10-23 (issue #94): with the address bar in the sidebar, Site Controls, Zoom and the blocked pop-ups panel opened at the page's left edge, under the peeking panel, so the click looked like it did nothing; they now open just right of the panel, as beside the docked sidebar, and the panel stays out until they close. Fixed 2026-11-08 (a reviewer's video, "Weird sidebar bug": with the pointer resting on the window's bottom-left corner, the peek closed and reopened several times a second): the peek's hold stopped exactly at the window's bottom and top edges, so a pointer a point or two outside, where macOS shows the resize cursor, closed it, and coming back in while it slid away opened it again. Once out, it now stays out within 24 pt past the bottom or top edge in its column, as it already did past the side edge, and closes further away; an edge crossing never opens it. `apps/browser/scripts/peek-edge-test.mjs` checks it. Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_peeks` and `sidebar_peek_bounces` (opened again while sliding away); watch the bounce share fall. Fixed 2026-11-08 (owner's recording against Arc, Arc's layout with the page frame at 0): the spring's overshoot carried the whole panel 5 pt off the window's left edge, a strip of page between, and the panel's fill and its contents were two layers, so the first peek could draw them a frame apart (the top drawn further out than the bottom). They are now one layer, and with the panel flush the fill runs 16 pt past the window's edge (clipped by it), so the overshoot moves the contents without detaching the panel; the spring is Arc's traced one held to its 1.5 % overshoot (0.18/0.8, was 0.185/0.77 at 2.3 %). Traced at 2x in a hidden instance: the left edge holds at x = 2 px through every open (was 10–12 px), the right edge overshoots 5–6 px (Arc 7, we were 10); in-app the slide peaks at 1.015 at 149 ms and the close runs its full 150 ms to 0 before the peek drops, every time. The owner's close that seemed to vanish at 25 % isn't cut short: every traced close (eight, before and after) goes from 5 % to 96 % out in 75–85 ms, and at the recording's 17–25 ms frame spacing the ease-in-out's fast middle covers that last quarter in one frame (the app's main thread showed no stall over 12 ms in any open or close). Arc's two closes in that recording are too sparse to settle its curve (one fits an ease-out, one linear), so the close keeps the ease-in-out measured before. `dockMotion.test.mjs` checks the peak against Arc's traces and the bleed against the overshoot at the widest sidebar. Telemetry: nothing new measurable (drawing only); `sidebar_peeks` already counts the peeks. Fixed 2026-11-11 (#1192: the auto-hidden sidebar reopened when the pointer crossed the area it had just left): while the panel slid away it still counted as out, so the whole column it held (its width plus 24 pt) reopened it and its rows still took clicks and raised hover cards. Once it starts sliding away it holds nothing: only the window's edge (the 8 pt strip, or past the window's side) brings it back, its rows take no clicks, and no hover card opens while the sidebar slides, docking or hiding with ⌘S included (`dockMotion.ts` › `edgeEventReveals`, `hover.ts`). `peek-edge-test.mjs` (back into the column mid-slide stays shut, the edge mid-slide reopens), `dockMotion.test.mjs`, `hover.test.mjs`. Telemetry: `sidebar_peek_bounces` now counts only edge reopens; watch its share of `sidebar_peeks` fall. Fixed 2026-11-11 (#1226, a Discord report): the panel was filled with the theme's flat first colour, so a gradient theme turned solid in the peek; it now draws the window's own backdrop (material, theme gradient, grain), laid out in window coordinates and clipped to the panel, so it shows the same slice of the gradient the docked sidebar shows. Measured in 2x snapshots against the docked sidebar at the same points: within 1/255 per channel in Dia's layout, 0 with the sidebar on the right, within 2/255 in Arc's layout with the frame at 0 (its 16 pt bleed); before, a flat `#fa598c` against `#d06cad`–`#9176d5`. Telemetry: nothing new measurable (drawing only). Changed 2026-11-14 (#1297, #1393, #1679): an intent model instead of a hover zone. Only the window's edge opens it: the last 3 pt inside the window or past its side (it was 8 pt, so it opened before the pointer reached a screen edge; a dragged tab still opens it from 8 pt). It stays out while the pointer is over it or within 40 pt of its edge (was 24), and closes 200 ms after the pointer leaves that (was 120), so a quick overshoot and return keeps it. Once it closes, it opens again only after the pointer has left the edge and come back (`edgeSeen`/`edgeClosed` in `dockMotion.ts`): closing with the pointer still at the edge, or ⌘S hiding the sidebar with it there, never springs it back. A press that starts on it holds it out until the button comes up (the native edge watcher, `SidebarEdgeWatcher.swift`), and a resize of its edge holds it too (`transientUi.ts` › `resize`), so dragging its edge resizes it instead of closing it as the pointer got ahead of the panel's last laid-out width (#1679). The duplicate React hover zone beside it is gone; the native watcher alone decides. The edge always reveals hidden tabs: the Reveal hidden tabs on hover setting (`revealTabsOnHover`) was removed on 2026-11-16. `peek-edge-test.mjs` (5 pt in stays shut, 2 pt in opens, 30 pt past stays, 45 pt past closes, out and back in 120 ms stays, a drag on the edge resizes and holds), `dockMotion.test.mjs`, `transientUi.test.mjs`. Telemetry (`usage_counts`): `sidebar_peek_reveals_held_back` (arrivals ignored until the pointer left the edge), `sidebar_peek_resizes` and `sidebar_peek_closes_while_resizing` (must stay 0). Fixed 2026-11-14 (owner's recording, two displays): with the window against a screen edge that another display shares, the pointer slid past the edge onto the other display, so the hidden sidebar almost never peeked. While Ardia is the active app, the pointer now stops at that screen edge in the window's rows and the peek opens; once it has been out 0.35 s, a second push goes on through to the other display (`SidebarEdgeWatcher.swift`). `peek-edge-test.mjs` checks it with the dev pointer (stops and peeks, a second push passes, the next crossing stops again, a window away from the edge lets the pointer cross). Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_screen_edge_stops` and `sidebar_screen_edge_pass_throughs`; watch stops lead to peeks and pass-throughs stay a small share. Left for a person: the feel with a real mouse across two displays. Fixed 2026-11-19 (#2219, three displays): every ordinary sweep to the next display was held at that edge for about 0.4 s and pulled back 4 to 7 times (a haptic mouse buzzed on each), because a crossing only went through once the peek had been out 0.35 s. A pointer that reaches the edge faster than 1200 pt/s, averaged over its last 50 ms, now crosses without stopping (a sweep to another display keeps its speed at the edge; a move to reveal the sidebar slows into it), and once stopped, pushing 64 pt past the edge goes through without waiting for the peek (`ScreenEdgeCrossing.swift`, `SidebarEdgeWatcher.swift`). Measured with real pointer events across the MacBook and an LG display, the window against the LG's left edge: 0.2.47-nightly.7 held a 2200 pt/s sweep for up to 443 ms with 4 bounces; the fix crossed 3 of 3 sweeps with no hold, and a 250 pt/s arrival still stopped and peeked. `peek-edge-test.mjs` (a fast sweep is a fling, a 64 pt push passes, a 40 pt push stays stopped) and `ScreenEdgeCrossingTests`. Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_screen_edge_flings`; watch flings take most crossings while stops still lead to peeks. Fixed 2026-11-19 (owner's recording, Ardia on the second display): every move to the other display still caught and dragged the pointer, and the peek flashed open on each one. The pointer is no longer held or warped at all: it crosses to the other display, and a crossing closes an open peek at once (and dismisses its hover card) instead of after the 200 ms grace, so the sidebar can't stay out behind you. To open it at that edge, overshoot a little and come straight back: a return within 0.7 s from no more than 96 pt past the edge, landing within 96 pt of it, peeks (`ScreenEdgeCrossing.swift`, `SidebarEdgeWatcher.swift`). `peek-edge-test.mjs` (a crossing leaves it hidden, an overshoot that comes back peeks, crossing again starts closing it at once, a late or far return doesn't peek, the right side too) and `ScreenEdgeCrossingTests`. Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_screen_edge_crossings` and `sidebar_screen_edge_returns` replace the stop, pass-through and fling counts; watch returns stay a small, steady share of crossings. Left for a person: the feel with a real mouse across two displays. On Linux there is no stop: its edge watch peeks when the pointer leaves the window on the sidebar side, so the crossing still opens the peek, and it never sends `screenEdge` (`docs/linux-port.md`). Fixed 2026-11-15 (owner's screenshot, 0.2.47 Nightly 4): in a full screen window the peek slid out and closed at once. The screen-edge stop above made the native edge watcher skip full screen windows for the hover too, so the page's edge strip opened the peek but the watcher never held it and it closed after 200 ms; only the screen-edge stop skips full screen now (`SidebarEdgeWatcher.swift`). Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_full_screen_peeks` and `sidebar_full_screen_peek_quick_closes` (closed within 0.5 s); watch the quick-close share stay near 0. Left for a person: full screen with a real mouse (a hidden instance can't go full screen without taking over the screen). Fixed 2026-11-16 (#1041): with a page in full screen (a YouTube video), the window's edge did nothing, docked or hidden sidebar alike, because a full-screen page put the dock in a state that watched no edge. The edge now slides the sidebar out over the page, as the hidden sidebar's peek, and it closes the same way; the page stays full screen (`dockMotion.ts` › `dockState`, `SidebarDock.tsx`). `dockMotion.test.mjs`; checked in a hidden instance with `requestFullscreen()` and the dev pointer (no peek before, peek after). Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_page_full_screen_peeks`; watch it rise from 0 after the release. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the owner's recordings: "the traffic lights go out of place and stay behind"): after a hide the lights could come back alone at their resting peek spot and stay there until the next redraw or peek. The hidden centre arrives while the lights still carry the peek's slide transform; `setTrafficLightsCenter` reset that transform in its own Core Animation transaction, which committed at once, and moved the buttons in the run loop's implicit one, so a frame drawn in between showed them at the peek spot. Both now commit in one transaction, as `SettleTrafficLights` already did. Window capture at about 120 Hz with real edge-pointer peeks: sidebar on the left 4 of 24 hides flashed before, 0 of 24 after; on the right 9 of 23 before, 0 of 24 after. Fixed 2026-11-19 (#2736, the owner's recording on Linux): every peek blanked the page white for about 100 ms. On the peek's first frame the dock's outer box already sat over the page's left edge, taking hits, while the panel was still off screen, so the Linux shell's coverage check found shell UI over the page and hid it for a frame; that box now takes no hits itself (`box-none`), only its contents do, and the peek's overlay region covers the resize handle's overhang too. Acceptance F30 (`docs/linux-acceptance.md`, left and right sidebars and the address bar in the sidebar, two peeks each): a hide and show per peek before, none after. Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_peek_page_hides` (Linux); watch it stay near 0 against `sidebar_peeks`. |
| Sidebar toggle button in nav bar | ✓ | ✅ | The toolbar's first button, centre 21 from the card's edge in both states (Dia: x 211 shown, 27 hidden). Glyphs 13 pt (Dia 1.52's ink at 2×: 30 × 23, 12 × 22, 22 × 26 px; ours were 15 / 14 pt, 35 × 27, 13 × 24, 24 × 29; now 30 × 23, 12 × 22, 22 × 27). Back and forward (2026-11-15, issue #1746, a Discord user: "thin and small" next to Zen's arrows): the chevrons had shrunk to 11 × 20 px with a 2.3 px stroke since the history handoff animation put them in a 13 pt frame, which AppKit scales a 13 pt chevron down to fit; they draw in a 16 pt frame now, as reload does, and at medium weight, a deliberate step past Dia's regular: 13 × 22 px, stroke 3.2 px, centres unchanged (the sidebar button and reload stay regular, since the open chevrons carry the least ink in the row). The same in the sidebar header, the card toolbar and Little Ardia. Telemetry: nothing measurable (drawing only). With the address bar in the sidebar (owner's report 2026-10-11, "so cluttered"): the header's button always shows, then back, forward and reload right-aligned on Dia's 35 pt pitch, plates 30 / radius 7 never closer than 2 pt; reload gives way first, then forward, so the back/forward pair stays together as in Arc and Dia (a tester's 0.2.39 report, 2026-10-20: "Arc shows both back and forward"; 190 pt: sidebar 97.75, back 133, forward 168; ≥ 217 pt all three, 35 apart from 225, where they sit within 2 pt of Arc's 227.5 pt sidebar: back 137.75, forward 171.75, reload 205.75). They had closed up to a 23 pt pitch with 30 pt boxes, so neighbouring boxes overlapped by 7 pt and both tracked the pointer: back and forward lit at once. Each header button now takes the press area around its plate, from the header's top to the field and edge to edge along the row past the traffic lights, and no toolbar button's press is ever the title bar's (`mouseDownCanMoveWindow={false}`): in the owner's recording (2026-10-11) clicks 3–6 pt above or left of the sidebar button were double-clicks on the empty header, which zoomed the window. A double-click on any header or toolbar button leaves the window as it is; on the empty header it still zooms (`mouse:` dev events, hidden instance). Settings › Tabs › Show sidebar button (on by default, synced; Helium has the same switch, a tester's request 2026-10-20) hides it in both layouts: the toolbar's buttons and the address field move left one slot (back at 21 from the card's edge), and the header's back, forward and reload take its place (all three fit at 190 pt: 98, 133, 168); ⌘S and View › Auto-Hide Tabs keep working. With tabs across the top (2026-11-08) the toolbar shows the button too, a tab-strip glyph (`rectangle.topthird.inset.filled`), and its tooltip names what it does, Hide Tabs or Show Tabs, in both layouts (it said Auto-Hide Tabs and existed only with the sidebar, so top-tab users found no way to hide the strip except switching layouts). Arc's layout (2026-10-22, the owner's captures of Arc next to Ardia, measured at 2× with the 23 pt light pitch as the ruler): the traffic lights, the sidebar button, back, forward and reload centre 24 pt below the window's top (Arc 23.75; it was 26.75, which Dia's layout keeps) and the URL field starts at 46 (Arc 46, was 48). A new window opens with the lights already there (its layout's centre goes to the native window before it's shown), so they don't jump 3 pt after the first frame. Their hover plates are Arc's: 32 pt rounded squares, radius 6, 8 pt below the window's top (they were Dia's 30 pt at radius 7), and they take clicks only inside the plate. Before, each button took presses from the window's top edge down to the field and edge to edge between neighbours, so a drag that started anywhere right of the lights pressed a button instead of moving the window, and the plate lit with the pointer 1 pt from the edge (owner's recording, 2026-10-21); now the rest of the row moves the window, as in Arc |
| Resize sidebar (rubber‑band at min/max) | ✓ | ✅ | persisted; double-click resets. Fixed 2026-11-14 (issue #783, a Discord report with Dia clips): the 8 pt handle straddles the sidebar's edge (it was 4 pt wholly inside it) and a drag starts from where the pointer grabbed, so the edge stays under the pointer in the docked and the peeking sidebar; the window keeps the resize cursor while the width is dragged. Verified headless with posted mouse events: grabbed 2 pt inside or outside the edge and dragged 20 to 110 pt, the edge lands on the pointer on the left and the right (it sat 2 pt off before). Telemetry: `sidebar_resizes`. Left for a person: the cursor with a real mouse |
| Sidebar on the right (Arc's **Sidebar position**; an Arc feature, not Dia's) | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | 2026-11-02, issue #216. Settings › Tabs › Sidebar position (Left or Right, a segmented control under the layout pictures, whose Sidebar picture shows the side), the New Tab page's Make it yours panel (a Left/Right control under the Sidebar and Classic cards, dimmed for Classic), View › Sidebar on Right and the same checked item in the sidebar's background menu; a synced setting, in config files and setup codes (`d`). Left stays the default. On the right the sidebar docks at the window's right edge and the card moves left; it resizes from its left edge (drag left to widen, double-click resets); hidden, it peeks and slides in from the right edge (the edge watcher works on either side); hover cards (tab, folder, group, live item, mini player), the icon picker, the meeting alert and the command bar from the sidebar's field open leftward (the folder panel lives inside the sidebar, so it moves with it), and dragging a tab or bookmark onto it tests the right edge. The window buttons ride the sidebar (owner's ask, 2026-11-03): they sit 25 pt into it, as on the left, so its header is the same on either side, and they slide out, peek and come back with it. Native anchors them to the window's right edge (`trailing`), so a live resize moves them with no JS round trip, and the launch picture saves the anchor. The card keeps 9 pt on every side and the toolbar starts at its edge. Tabs along the top and Little Ardia are unchanged. With the address bar in the sidebar, the command bar opens over the field on the sidebar's current side (fixed 2026-11-16, issues #1239 and #2021: after the sidebar changed side, or the window changed width beside a right sidebar, ⌘L and a click on the field opened it where the field had been until a restart, because the field's place was measured only when its own layout changed, and the dock is pinned to the window's edge). Telemetry: `usage_counts.sidebar_field_remeasures`, the times the field was measured again for a side change, a right sidebar's window width or the window buttons' row; watch it stay above zero among users who switch the sidebar's side or resize the window with it on the right |
| New tabs at top (setting) | ✓ | ✅ | links from a pinned tab, a Little Ardia page or a bookmark folder keep their order at the top (they came out reversed). Ours: Settings › Tabs › Put links from other apps first (off by default) puts links from Slack, Mail or any other app that open as tabs (Little Ardia off, or several links at once; a single link otherwise opens in Little Ardia) just below the pinned tabs, in order, whatever Add new tabs says; with Below the current tab they went to the bottom, since an app link has no page to open next to |
| Sticky ➕ New Tab at bottom when overflowing | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › Tabs › New Tab button (At the bottom, the default, or At the top) places the button on its own, whatever Add new tabs says (2026-10-25, issue #320: since the third Add new tabs choice the button had followed that setting, so it couldn't sit at the top while tabs opened below the current one). At the top, New Tab stays above the list however long it gets and scrolls with it, as in Arc; at the bottom it docks to the sidebar's bottom edge once the list fills it (2026-10-24, issue #170, PR #183). A saved Add new tabs: At the top carries over as New Tab button: At the top; onboarding's new-tab question and its Arc and Dia presets set both. Each profile page docks its own New Tab from its own list, and the media player belongs to its page, so a profile swipe slides in the page already laid out as it will rest: the docked row and the player move with it, and nothing below the ↓ Clear divider shifts when the swipe ends (a list end off the current page had 3 pt more per section; 2026-10-24, issue #222, PR #110). Fixed 2026-11-12 (#1280, a user's report: New Tab stayed highlighted after opening a new tab): the row lets go of its hover once the pointer leaves it. Its size changes under the pointer as the new tab slides in, and React Native's view remade its tracking area and forgot it was hovered, so no leave ever came (patches/react-native-macos: a resized area keeps its hover and the hover check sends the leave); `newtab-row-hover-test.mjs`.
