No telemetry: nothing in the app can see a hover that never ended Fixed 2026-11-19 (#2894, the owner: after a display change New Tab sat docked two rows below the last tab, hovered): the dock is decided from one height Yoga lays out, the empty space under the rows in the scroll view, read by a view remade for each dock state, so it can't mix a layout with another render's state (it used to add the tiles region and the list from separate onLayouts and subtract the inline row by whichever render handled the event, and a tile move could leave it a row off with no event to correct it). It docks below 12 pt of room and comes back above 33 pt; an expanded Playing now card leaves it as it was. Window moves, screen changes and backing scale changes recheck hover, so a row a still pointer was on lets go. Telemetry: `sidebar_new_tab_docks`, `sidebar_new_tab_undocks` (watch for a ratio far above the tab open/close rate, which would mean flapping) |
| Double‑click empty sidebar space → new tab | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Top Apps / Favorites dock | ✓ | ✅ | the pinned-tile dock; command bar "Move to Top Apps" / "Unpin from Top Apps" like Dia, and the tab menu, the multi-selection menu ("Move N Tabs to Top Apps") and Tabs in the menu bar say the same (2026-10-24, GitHub issue #100: they said "Pin", which next to "Move to Pinned Tabs" read as the same action). Fixed 2026-10-21 (a Discord report: the sidebar URL field cut the tiles off as soon as the list scrolled): as in Dia's `TabListView`, the dock stays put under the header (and under the URL field in Arc's layout) and only the tabs scroll, below a 1 pt full-width line 4 pt under the tiles (or the URL field) that fades in over 0.1 s once the list is scrolled at all and out at the top (`dia-spec.md` › Sidebar rows › Scroll edge; sidebar/ScrollEdge.tsx). The same line marks the list's bottom edge, above the docked New Tab row, while rows are hidden below, and fades out at the list's end or once the list fits, as in Dia (2026-11-15, the owner's Dia recording; `hiddenBelow`). A dock taller than 40 % of the sidebar (18 tiles in a short window) scrolls with the tabs instead, so every tile stays reachable. At rest every layout is pixel-identical to before; `sidebar-scroll-test.mjs` drags a row onto the tiles after a scroll, selects a tile while scrolled and checks the tall dock. Left for a person: scrolling on a real trackpad (rubber band at the top) Settings › Appearance › Selected Tab › Selected Top App (2026-11-15, #739 and #1557) picks how the selected tile shows: Ring (Dia's, the default), Fill only (no ring) or Outline only (the ring around a clear tile, `outline` on `DockSelection`), in the sidebar and the top strip, Classic and Glass. Telemetry: settings_snapshot's `setting_selectedTopApp`; watch the share not on ring. Settings › Tabs › Draw tiles for closed Top Apps (2026-11-15, #276; on by default, Dia's look): off, a Top App whose page isn't open loses its tile and shows its icon faded, so the open ones stand out; hover, a click, a multi-selection or a drag still draw the tile, and the selected one keeps its Selected Top App look, in Classic and Glass. Telemetry: settings_snapshot's `setting_closedTopAppTiles`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (#1974): in the Glass style a hovered, pressed or multi-selected tile showed almost no change (the glass tint stepped from 1.5 % to 3 % white); it now takes the Classic tile's wash over its glass (`tabHover`, `tabPressed`), measured #332025 → #544448 on a selected tile in a 1x snapshot, as a hovered row lights up. No telemetry: a hover look has nothing to count. |
| Arc-style pinned tabs (rows above the tabs; Dia 1.52) | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-10-19 (the owner's request). Dia 1.52 keeps Favorites (Top Apps, the tiles) and a Pinned section of rows (its `pinned_container`; "Move to Pinned in <space>", "Move to Top Apps"). Ours: a pinned tab is a tile or a row (`tab.pinnedAsRow`); rows sit between the tiles and the divider with "↓ Clear", above pinned groups, 6 pt under the tiles, and look like tab rows. Pin as a row from the tab menu ("Move to Pinned in <profile>", Dia's wording; it said "Move to Pinned Tabs" until 2026-10-24, issue #100; a row's menu has "Move to Top Apps" and "Unpin", and so does Tabs in the menu bar on a row, where Keyboard Shortcuts lists it as "Move Pinned Tab to Top Apps"), the multi-selection menu, the command bar ("Move to Pinned in <profile>" / "Move to Top Apps" / "Unpin Tab"), or by dragging a tab above the divider; drag a row onto the tiles to make it one, a tile below them to make it a row, a row down among the tabs to unpin it. "Move to Top Apps" (was "Pin") still makes a tile. Rows group as list tabs do, into a pinned group where the row was (2026-10-23, the owner's report): New Group with Tab / Move to Group on a row's menu, New Group with N Tabs and ⌃⌘N on a selection of rows, or a tab or row let go on the middle of a row. Rows are pinned tabs in everything else: closing one unloads it and keeps the row (a second middle click on a closed row, or on a closed tab of a pinned group, removes it, as in Arc, where a closed pin's ✕ is "Archive this tab" (issue #164): it goes to Recently Closed and a "Removed Pinned Tab" toast's Undo puts it back closed in place, its earlier close still reopening into it (as with Clear, only while the window is as the removal left it); an open row, a selected one and a tile only close; as in Arc, the hover button says so (issue #231): − (Unload Tab) on an open pinned row or pinned-group tab, ✕ (Remove Pinned Tab) on a closed one, and a pinned group's header has − (Unload Tabs) while any of its tabs is open, which unloads them all and keeps the group, nothing once all are closed; the same in the top strip; closing an unpinned group from its ✕, its menu or ⌃⌘W asks first, "Close “Work”?", with Don’t ask me again, behind Settings › Tabs › Warn before closing a tab group, on by default and synced), Clear keeps them, ⌘↩ and Back to Pinned URL, the profile's windows mirror them, they park with the last window and survive a relaunch, Chrome's strip has them pinned (after the tiles), and the top tab strip shows them in the pinned dock after the tiles. Sync: rows travel as pinned tabs (`pin:t:`) plus a mark per row (`pinrow:`), so Macs on older versions show them as tiles and their edits never turn a row into a tile (`docs/sync.md`). `state/pins/pinnedRows.test.mjs` (7), `state/sync/adapters.test.mjs` (3 new), and a hidden instance: rows at y 101 under tiles ending at 95; drags list → row, row → tile, tile → row, row → list; close keeps an unloaded row. Text-only rows (2026-11-15, issue #370, as in Arc; Dia has none): a row's icon picker has No Icon (Change Icon… on a row), and the row shows its title alone, where the icon would start; Reset Icon brings the favicon back, the drag ghost matches, and a text-only row away from its pinned page shows the icon again for Back to Pinned URL. Rows only: as a tile, in the command bar, the ⌃Tab switcher and the top strip the favicon shows; synced and mirrored as any custom icon; the ardia command's `tab.icon` takes `"none"` for a row. Telemetry: `usage_counts.pinned_rows_made_text_only` |
| Move to Pinned / Move out of Pinned shortcut | — (Arc ⌘D) | ✅ | 2026-10-21, a Discord request. Tabs › Move to Pinned (⌥⇧⌘D; in the menu bar it reads Move to Pinned in <profile>, and Move out of Pinned on a pinned tab), rebindable in Settings › Keyboard Shortcuts. A tab (or the selection) goes to the pinned rows; pressed again on a row it goes back where it was in the list, into its group if that still exists; on a tile it unpins, as Pin does. Arc's ⌘D is Bookmark in Dia and Ardia, ⇧⌘D Bookmark All Tabs, ⌥⌘D the Dock's and ⌃⌘D the Dictionary's, so it keeps D with ⌥⇧. Dia 1.53 has "Move to Pinned in <space>" in its command bar only, no key. For Arc's ⌘D, set Move to Pinned to ⌘D there: both rows show the conflict, and a shortcut you set wins over another action's default on the same keys, so Bookmark loses ⌘D until you give it another (2026-10-24, issue #99) |
| Collapse a profile's pinned section | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | 2026-10-28, issue #521 (three asks: a profile's many pinned groups push its tabs to the bottom). As Arc collapses a space's pinned section: the divider between the pinned section and the tabs (the one with ↓ Clear) has a "⌄ Pinned" toggle at its left end, shown on hover, and "› Pinned" stays shown while collapsed; a click folds the pinned rows, pinned groups and live folders away with the folders' animation (the same `Fold`, chevron turning as a group header's), Top Apps stays. Collapsed, the section takes no room but the divider, and the entry holding the profile's active tab stays shown (a pinned row, or its pinned group, as a collapsed folder keeps its open tab). Also from "Collapse Pinned Tabs" / "Expand Pinned Tabs" in the sidebar menu (right-click the divider, the sidebar background, the New Tab row, header or footer). Pinning a tab as a row, or pinning a group of open tabs, opens the section; a Top Apps tile, a pin from sync or another window's mirror don't; a drag into the collapsed section lands at its end (or by the shown entry) and opens it. Remembered per profile across relaunch (`profile.pinnedCollapsed` in session.json); like a folder's open state it isn't synced. The divider now shows whenever the profile has pinned entries, also with Show Clear button off. `state/sidebar/pinnedFold.test.mjs` (5) and a hidden instance: 3 rows and 5 pinned groups put the tabs at y 593, collapsed at y 148; collapsed with a group's tab or a row active shows just that entry; collapsed after a relaunch; the divider's chevron, its right-click menu and the background menu each toggle it. Left for a person: the fold animation and the hover reveal by eye, a real drag into the collapsed section. Reverted 2026-11-17 at the owner's request: PR #2356 had replaced this toggle with a row named after the profile above the pinned entries, which read as a tab group that did nothing; the divider's toggle is back. |
| Library (chats & files Dia made) | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Downloads button in header | ✓ | ✅ | like Dia 1.50.1 (DownloadsButtonController, from the binary): there only while the window lists downloads, in progress or finished; Clear hides it again. 34 pt box, 7 pt from the edge. With the address bar in the sidebar it sits in the footer, centred on the 28 pt band the profile dots use (22 pt up, so the box is 5 pt off the bottom and 5 pt off the left, concentric with the window's corner); fixed 2026-10-13 (the owner: it was flush on the window's bottom edge, 5 pt below the dots). 2026-11-10 (the owner, on 0.2.46-nightly.2 with page frame thickness 0): its left inset had followed the sidebar's 9 pt inset, so the arrow sat 18.5 pt from the left and 14.5 from the bottom; both are 14.5 now (window capture, 2×: glyph at x 29, 29 px above the bottom). No telemetry: a fixed offset, nothing to count |
| Selected/hover/pressed row styling + selected glow | ✓ | ✅ | Dia 1.50 selected tint (#121212 at 0.5 dark, white 0.7 light). Compared with Dia 1.50.1 captures (1× and 2×, dark, 2026-09-26) and its binary: rows 34 tall on a 37 pitch and pinned tiles 41 tall from y 54, in TabList's symmetric 6 pt inset (x 6 … 184 at 190; were 33, 40 and 7); the selected row's hairline is 0.5 pt, white 0.15 on all sides (was a 1 pt gradient); the resting tile's is white 0.14 (`TabDockItemRestingStroke`, was 0.06); selection no longer shifts the row's content; the New Tab icon is Dia's mark (16 pt, white 0.28). At 2× every row and tile edge, stroke value, favicon, title position, weight and colour now match to the pixel. The capture's rows end at 185 and its card starts at 191 because that Dia window's sidebar is 191 wide (resized; the binary's default is 190, like ours). Light mode not compared (checklist step 10). Loading spinner in rows: Dia's `ActivitySpinnerView` (12 pt, 8 pt from the row's end, track + 72 % arc 1.5 pt wide in secondaryLabelColor, 1.88 s per turn), hidden under the hover close button. The selected glow has 24 pt of room above the list in every layout, also with the address bar in the sidebar and under fixed pinned tiles, so the first row's glow isn't cut off at the list's top; the fixed tiles sit above the list, so their bottoms stay clickable (2026-10-25, issue #153, from PR #314 by Mridul Verma). The bottom gets the same room (2026-11-10, the owner on 0.2.46-nightly.2: a selected tab in a group at the end of a long list had its glow cut flat at the list's bottom edge): switching to a tab scrolls it 24 pt clear of the list's bottom (was 8), and with New Tab docked under the list the list ends in 24 pt of padding so its last row can scroll clear. Window capture, 80 tabs, address bar in the sidebar: the row went from 717–751 against an edge at 750 to 692–726. No telemetry: a scroll offset, nothing to count. Its sides stay clipped at the sidebar's edges: the page card starts there. Settings › Appearance › Selected Tab sets the glow: Full (Dia's, the default), Soft (45 % of its opacity at 60 % of its radius) or Off; synced (2026-10-25, issue #358: people found it too strong). A custom profile colour's selected fill is contrast-aware (2026-11-07, issue #971, an Arc user on a near-black profile; built-in presets, neutral included, keep exactly Dia's fill in both modes, the owner's call for Dia parity): `lib/selectionContrast.ts` composites it on the window's opaque tint (both ends of the gradient) and keeps it if its OKLab lightness differs by at least 0.03 (the least of Dia's colour presets, 0.034–0.055); otherwise it gets more opaque in Dia's direction, and where that can't reach 0.03 (darker than near-black, lighter than near-white) it flips to Arc's look, a white overlay on dark or black on light, at the alpha that gives 0.06. Computed: near-black #131313 ΔL 0.000 → 0.061 (white 6 %); 0.1 grey dark 0.022 → 0.031 (#121212 at 0.6); near-white #FAFAFA 0.012 → 0.060 (black 8 %). The same rule picks a custom colour's pinned-tile selected fill. Measured on screen in a hidden instance: near-black custom dark, sidebar 29,29,29, selected row 23,23,23 → 42,42,42 (ΔL 0.026 → 0.054, ratio 1.063 → 1.174); neutral dark 32 on 48 and neutral light 250 on 238 (Dia's fill, unchanged); plum dark pixel-identical. The top strip's selected tab keeps the card's colour: it is the top of the card, which stays apart from the strip (near-black: card 20,20,20 on 29,29,29, ΔL 0.039), and a lighter tab came loose from it. A custom colour's sidebar ink follows its tint as Arc's does (2026-11-09): every ink derived on the tint keeps the mode's own (white in dark mode, black in light) unless its worst contrast over both tint stops drops below 3:1, then takes the other where that reads better (`inkOn` in `components/ui/theme.ts`). The selected title uses it on the composited row (a dark mid-tone row, 155,131,126, keeps white; it had turned black on raw contrast), and the sidebar (rows, New Tab, folders and groups, header, footer; not the folder panel, drag ghost or toolbar) takes the other mode's palette when its ink flips (`sidebarTheme`, `SidebarInk`): a 0.88 grey dark-mode profile gets black titles, glyphs and hover (white was 2.1:1). Built-in presets and dark custom colours are unchanged, checked pixel-identical in a hidden instance. The top tab strip follows the same tint ink (`tintTheme`, `TintInk` on its chips, groups, New Tab and the profile and downloads buttons); a selected chip, drawn on the card, keeps the window theme, and its ink is `inkOn` over the card on the tint; a group or folder title holding the selected tab uses `textPrimary` on the tint. Popovers and the profile-dot tooltip draw on their own surface and take the window theme back (`surfaceTheme`). The ink always adapts: the Adjust text to the colour setting (`adaptTextToColor`, added 2026-11-09) was removed on 2026-11-16. Telemetry: the daily settings_snapshot carries a `custom_color_profiles` count. Settings › Appearance › Page Frame › Tab corners (2026-11-15, #929) sets `radius.row` (sidebar rows, the New Tab row, the sidebar address field, top-strip tabs) from 0 to 20 pt; unset it follows the theme file (10 pt, Dia's, without one), and once moved it wins over the theme. Telemetry: `setting_tabCornerRadius` in settings_snapshot (`null` while unset). |
| Sidebar text size and contrast (accessibility) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-14, issue #1632 (a low-vision user: tab text smaller than Arc's and light grey; UAAG 2.0 asks for text size and contrast control). Ours, Dia has neither. Settings › Appearance › Sidebar Text: Text size Default (Dia's 13 pt titles, 34 pt rows, 41 pt tiles), Large (15 pt, 39, 47) or Larger (17 pt, 44, 54), with favicons 16 → 18 → 20 and the row glyphs, title frames, address field, split rows, group headers, New Tab, drag ghost and tile-folder panel scaled to match (`sidebarTextSize`, this Mac only, as #1634's interface size would be); Higher contrast text (`sidebarTextContrast`: auto, standard or high, synced) draws tab titles at full ink, secondary labels, the address placeholder and the New Tab label at 82 %, hints and the New Tab glyph at 70 %. Auto turns it on while macOS Increase Contrast is on (`AccessibilityInfo.isHighContrastEnabled` and its change event). Both are config keys. Built as a sidebar scale on the theme look (`withSidebarText` in `lib/themeLook.ts`, `sidebarFont`/`sidebarLineHeight`/`sidebarPoints` in `components/ui/look.ts`, `TextScopeContext` for `Text`, `contrastTheme` in `components/ui/theme.ts`, `SidebarTextScope`), so #1634's app-wide interface size can scale the same look. Default is pixel-identical (`scripts/agent/ui-snapshots`: every sidebar state identical; the only differences were the tinted New Tab mark image, which loads unevenly between runs, and other changes' settings rows). Contrast measured on the snapshots' sidebar backgrounds (plum, dark 62,47,50 and light 231,209,213), ink composited at its real alpha: titles 8.4 → 12.7 dark and 6.7 → 14.5 light; secondary labels 4.4 → 9.1 and 3.6 → 10.3; New Tab label 5.0 → 9.0 and 3.7 → 10.3; hints 3.0 → 7.1 and 2.3 → 7.1; titles in a green group 7.0 → 10.3 and 6.2 → 12.7: every role passes WCAG AA (4.5:1), most AAA. Telemetry: settings_snapshot's `setting_sidebarTextSize`, `setting_sidebarTextContrast` and a new `system_increase_contrast`; watch the share on Large or Larger, and how many with Increase Contrast on switch contrast back to standard. Not done: the command bar field over the sidebar address keeps its size, and the top tab strip is unchanged (#1634). Fixed 2026-11-20 (#2969): from nightly.6 to nightly.9, titles at Large and Larger lost their descenders (“Trio olan”). One shared text field draws every FadeLabel, and its frame stayed at a 13 pt line's 16 pt height, so 15 and 17 pt glyphs were cut below 16 pt. The field now takes each label's ink box before it draws. `SingleLineInkTests` (packages/shell, `swift test`) checks glyph height against the font at 11–17 pt. Telemetry: nothing new measurable (drawing only). |

Every sidebar card opens 8 pt past the sidebar's page-facing edge (the floating panel's while it peeks), never over other tiles, and its icon and title sit on the hovered tab's line with the row's 5 pt gap (2026-11-08: a tester's two rows of Top Apps were covered; the owner asked for space from the page and the title in line) |
| Website colour extended into tab bar / toolbar | ✓ | ✅ | nav bar tint from theme-color / header / background, eased; a clearly light theme-color over a dark page whose top header is dark too (or absent) gives way to the page (dark Gmail's stale #f6f8fc, #544; dark or mid-tone theme-colors and light pages never change; `page_script.js`, `platform/pageScriptTheme.test.mjs`); colours Chromium serializes as `color(srgb …)`, `color(srgb-linear …)`, `color(display-p3 …)`, `lab()`, `lch()`, `oklab()` or `oklch()` are converted to sRGB instead of read as 0–255 numbers, which had turned Perplexity's near-white page `#010101` and Linear's `lch()` grey `#5E0111A` (#884); the bookmarks bar takes the same eased colour and ink as the toolbar (#537); setting in Tabs |

## 5. Profiles & Spaces
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Profiles (separate cookies, logins, passwords, extensions, cards, history, chats) | ✓ | ✅ | a Chrome profile each (cookies, logins, Chrome's password manager, autofill addresses and cards, extensions, site settings), plus our history and bookmarks; chats ⏸ |
| Profile name + theme colour (+ icon) | ✓ | ✅ | name, 9 colours or a custom gradient (§21) (window tint, NTP light, edge light, power-up band); optional initial, emoji or SF Symbol icon on the profile colour, chosen in the icon picker anchored to Settings › Profiles › <profile>'s badge or Change… button (Initial goes back to the letter); the Name field saves on Enter, on a click anywhere else in the window (every settings text field resigns on an outside click) and when the pane closes, and an empty name reverts |
| Profile switcher, ⌃1–⌃9, next/previous, swipe between profiles | ✓ | ✅ | switcher, ⌃1–⌃9, Next/Previous Profile. Paging like Dia's PageSwipeController (layout/profilePager, numbers in dia-spec › Profile paging): two fingers (or a Magic Mouse finger) over the sidebar or the top tab strip page between profiles whatever Swipe between pages is set to; the neighbouring profile's page (its tiles and tabs, kept mounted at stable profile positions) slides in beside the current one at Dia's half-speed tracking, rubber band at the ends, page-detent haptic, flick commit at 5 pt/s, 0.25 s critically damped settle carrying the release speed; the window tint cross-fades between the two profiles' colours; a wheel mouse's horizontal / Shift-scroll pages one profile per burst, the way the scroll goes (left shows the previous profile with Natural scrolling on or off; until 2026-11-13 a wheel with it off paged backwards because the wheel path flipped the sign by `isDirectionInvertedFromDevice` a second time, #325; Dia's flip is deliberately not copied). ⌃1–9, Next/Previous Profile, the profile menu and the page dots slide the same way; the native path switches profile on release while the spring finishes. Page dots (Dia's space switcher: 41 pt footer, 6 pt dots on a 12 pt pitch, labelColor 0.85/0.4/0.2) in the sidebar footer, brightness following the pages. Settings › Profiles › Show the profile switcher in the sidebar (on by default; until #3304 a three-way Dots / Icons / Nothing control) hides them and their 41 pt footer (the footer stays when the address bar is in the sidebar and downloads are listed, for the downloads button); swiping, ⌃1–⌃9 and the profile menu still switch profiles. Watch `setting_showProfileDots` in `settings_snapshot` for how many turn it off. Each profile is a dot unless it has an icon (Arc's space switcher; since #3304 per profile, no global Dots/Icons choice and no onboarding question; an update keeps what each switcher showed: on Dots, a profile that already carried an icon keeps a local `switcherDot` until an icon is chosen for it), which draws a profile's SF Symbol or emoji at 11 pt in a 26 pt cell (1/0.7/0.42; 13 in 22 until 0.2.43-nightly.4, smaller after a tester found them too large in #140; 11 in 18 until #607, where the glyphs sat 3–9 pt apart and were mis-clicked, now 12–17 pt apart, about one glyph width, as the dots sit one dot apart; a 26 × 28 pt click target; 6 icon profiles fit the default 190 pt sidebar, 3 beside the Downloads button; `profile_switched` carries `via_switcher`, `switcher_icons` and `switcher_corrected`, a switcher press within 2 s of one for another profile, so the dashboard shows the mis-click rate with icons against dots), while a profile without an icon keeps its 6 pt dot on the 12 pt pitch; all fall back to dots when the icons don't fit beside the footer's Downloads button. Right-clicking the switcher sets a profile's icon (Add Icon… / Change Icon… opens the icon picker on the dot; a symbol or emoji, or No Icon, which makes it a dot again), toggles the setting or opens the profile's settings; Settings › Profiles rows have the same item, opening the picker on the row's badge; the profile's Icon row has Add… or Change… plus Remove. Icon sizes and brightness not yet measured against Arc. Hovering a dot shows the profile's name in Dia's own tooltip (DiaTooltip, not AppKit's; measured on the owner's 2× Dia capture in #165, 2026-10-24): centred over the dot with its edge 18.25 pt above the dot's centre, 45 × 26 pt for "Work", 4 pt continuous corners, #262626 fill, 1 pt white 10% border, a 0.5 pt black 40% outer ring and a faint shadow, 12 pt medium text at white 85% (6.5 pt sides, 5 pt top and bottom); it shows after 0.5 s and fades in over 0.1 s (Dia's hover-menu timing, the tooltip's own not yet read from the binary) and hides on leave or click. Light mode uses the panel colours and is not yet measured against Dia. Not yet: Dia's strip settle content fade, the switcher's edge scaling past 7 profiles and drag-to-reorder, the footer's Library/overflow buttons. Holding ⌘ in the profile menu (and in the menu bar's Profiles menu) swaps each profile row for an AppKit alternate with a ↗ after the name (the popup rows keep their colour circle) that opens that profile in a new window, even the profile the window already shows; ⌃⌘1–⌃⌘9 do the same from the keyboard, and profiles past the ninth swap with ⌘ alone. The menu bar's ↗ rows follow a remapped profile shortcut (⌘ added to it) and hide when that would collide. Since 0.2.18 AppKit owns recognition, tracking, velocity, interruption and display-refresh settling, and React only receives the selected profile; stable profile ids and sequence acknowledgements keep late state from undoing rapid swipes, dots, shortcuts, reordering or deletion. The window's Chrome window swaps with the commit that lays the profile out, so no empty-window or blank-page frames show. A swipe tolerates 12 pt of initial vertical wobble, then keeps the horizontal axis locked. A quick series of switches costs each the same as the first (2026-10-03): every view leaving the window used to scan a bounds-change observer list that grew with each switch and each tab opened (react-native-macos re-registered a scroll view's rows every time their container changed; patched), and the sidebar page leaving no longer unmounts the rows past its first screen. A swipe's momentum stays with the swipe (2026-10-05): passed on, it started the scroll view's responsive scrolling under the pointer, which ate every later scroll until the pointer moved, so the third swipe at one spot did nothing. `profile_switched` also carries the native swap, timed from `WindowProfileView.apply` (#680: a few seconds of the old profile's page on 0.2.42): `swap_ms` to the new tab's first frame, `swap_active_ms` to the window's active profile being set, `profile_loaded` (the profile was already loaded) and `held_timed_out` (the first-frame hold gave up and showed the page anyway). Each switch carries its own id through the view's `switchId` prop and `onSwapped`, so overlapping switches never share a report; a switch between profiles that share one engine profile (no native swap) goes out at once without these fields, and one whose page has no frame to wait for (a cold profile still restoring its tab) goes out after 12 s without them. Numbers and booleans only. Guarded by `apps/browser/scripts/profile-swipe-test.mjs` (hidden-instance cases: reversals, wheel direction, wobble, drift, momentum, missing ends, overlapping and full-screen windows, renderer hits, blocked JS). Physical trackpad feel needs an installed build. History and measurements: `docs/research/profile-swipe-native-controller.md` (ledger 44). A switch no longer costs React a walk through every profile's rows (2026-11-15): the window's theme context changing reached each row through the pages' nested providers, and the drag and list-motion contexts toggled on two pages, so the card waited for the sidebar. Rows now read a page theme context and a stable per-page drag registry. Debug build, hidden instance, four profiles, JS time of one switch, median: 240 tabs 193 → 47 ms, 1600 tabs 997 → 95 ms. On the dashboard, watch `profile_switched`'s `switch_ms` (store change to the second frame after it): it should fall most for profiles with many tabs. Reorder from the sidebar (an Arc habit Dia lacks; asked for on 2026-11-19): drag a dot or icon in the sidebar's profile switcher sideways and the others slide aside as it passes each one's centre, with a haptic tick at each new place; letting go saves the order (the same `reorderProfiles` as Settings › Profiles, so sync, ⌃1–⌃9 and paging follow it) and the highlighted dot stays on the window's profile. A press that moves 4 pt or less is still a click and switches profile; the tooltip hides while dragging. Geometry in `components/profiles/dotReorder.ts` (tested), checked in a hidden instance with synthetic drags. Telemetry: `profile_dot_reorders` in the hourly extras; watch it for use |
| Create / rename / delete / make default / reorder profiles | ✓ | ✅ | Dia's Create Profile dialog; Settings › Profiles rows drag to reorder |
| Clear browsing data per profile | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Share data between profiles | ✓ | ✅ | profiles sharing one engine profile share cookies, logins, site data, passwords, extensions, zoom, history and bookmarks; tabs stay per profile. Top Apps can be shared too, as Arc's Favorites are across spaces on one profile (#84; chosen per profile, off by default and synced, so a profile keeps its own until it opts in; Top Apps that 0.2.44 merged by default stay where they are, now each profile's own, so removing an extra no longer touches the other profiles; which profile had a tile before the merge can't be told from the saved session (0.2.44 swapped tabs between profiles when a shared tile was opened and handed tabs over when a window closed), so nothing is removed. A Mac still on 0.2.44 keeps sharing on that Mac, and its edits reach the others through pin sync, until it updates (#866). Settings › Profiles › Shared data › Share Top Apps lists every profile that shares data with a toggle each, and the same toggle is in the profile's details and its right-click menu; a profile turned off keeps the tiles it had as its own and its own pages, the others keep sharing): every profile in the group whose toggle is on shows the same tiles (pinning, unpinning, reordering, renaming or foldering one changes it in all of them), and each tile is one live page. Opening it in another profile takes that same Chrome tab along (same WebContents, history and scroll; nothing reloads, one renderer), and the profile it left shows a frozen copy that pulls the page back when you page back to it. Paging between profiles that share data keeps the shared page's pane mounted, so its one page isn't detached and re-attached (#836). Left for a person: that no frame flashes during the switch, by eye. Tiles both profiles already had merge into one by page; pinned rows and groups stay each profile's own; profiles with their own data never share. Paging between two neighbouring profiles that share Top Apps keeps the tiles still, as Arc's Favorites stay put between spaces: only the tab list and the header slide, and the two profiles' grids cross-fade in place (#848, 2026-11-07; click, shortcut and swipe, a cancelled swipe too). Between a profile that shares and one that doesn't, or two sharing different sets, the tiles slide with their page as before. Measured from in-process snapshots: the tile band's mean pixel difference against the resting frame stays 0.07–0.58 across a click switch (9 samples, 56–608 ms) and held swipes at 20–120 pt, against 3.2–6.6 before, while the tab rows move by the swipe's distance. Only tiles docked above the list hold still; tiles that scroll with the list (more than fit the dock) slide. Left for a person: the cross-fade of the glass tiles by eye on a real screen. `state/pins/topApps.test.mjs`, `state/sidebar/sharedTiles.test.mjs` Telemetry (issue #927, sharing on only, counts and flags, never which profiles, tiles or sites): `top_apps_sharing` once per launch (`profile_count`, `sharing_profiles`, `merge_recorded` for the saved session's marker, `merge_recorded_this_launch` when this launch wrote a marker, `merge_marker_cleared` when it cleared a saved one because no profile opted in, `from_version`); `top_apps_mirrored` each time tiles are copied between sharing profiles (`circles`, `joining_circles`, `circles_changed`, `profiles_changed` counted per profile, `tiles` copied in, `tiles_removed`, `from_sync` when sync wrote the tiles or a synced Share Top Apps setting, pairing or added profile started the copy); `top_apps_sharing_synced` when sync turns Share Top Apps on or off (`path`: `synced_record`, `link_profiles` or `linked_profile_created`; `on`); `top_apps_sharing_dropped` when more than 16 came before telemetry started. Watch on the dashboard: launches with `sharing_profiles` ≥ 2 and `merge_recorded` false on a build after 0.2.44 (sharing is on but the merge never ran, the #927 report); `merge_recorded_this_launch` true clustering on one `from_version` (an update that redoes the merge); `merge_marker_cleared` true on launches from 0.2.44 (expected once per such install as 0.2.45+ clears its marker, never twice); `top_apps_mirrored` with `tiles_removed` > 0 and `from_sync` true (sync taking tiles away); `top_apps_sharing_synced` with `on` false right after `link_profiles` (pairing switching sharing off); any `top_apps_sharing_dropped`. |
| Guest profile | ✗ | — | not in Dia |
| Dock menu: New Window per profile | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Unload unused profiles | ✓ | ✅ | a profile no window has shown for 5 min of app-active time: its tabs' browsers close (unlike sleeping, their history is lost), then its engine context is released (checked every 15 s). Whether Chrome then unloads the profile itself (ghost, hidden WebUI pages) hasn't been measured on the new engine |
| Per‑profile extensions | ✓ | ✅ | every extension call takes the profile; Settings › Extensions has a profile picker ("Each profile has its own"); uBOL runs in every profile (ledger 11). 2026-11-11, GitHub issue #1248 (FB-5GA3): a profile whose protected settings were hashed with an earlier Safe Storage key (the oldest one, usually Personal) lost every extension at its next load; an undecryptable hash now falls back to its machine HMAC and is re-hashed with the current key (`chromium-pref-hash-safe-storage-key-change.patch`). Watch `extensions_loaded`: `dropped` should stay 0, and the `disabled_*` counts by reason show what else keeps a profile's extensions off |
| Per‑profile sync | ✓ | ✅ | each profile syncs on its own (its own scope in the sync folder), turned on or off per profile in Settings › Sync. Joining pairs Personal with the synced default and other profiles by name; a synced profile this Mac lacks is offered as "Add to This Mac"; profiles that share data sync their tabs, the data with its owner, and one added from sync shares that owner's data on the new Mac too (`docs/sync.md`) |
| Per‑profile Morning Brief | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Spaces (colour, rename) | flag-gated; changelog says Dia has no Spaces | — | Dia ships without Spaces |
| Warn before closing last tab in a profile | ✓ | ✅ | with "Don't ask again" |

## 6. Split view
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Split view up to 3 panes, horizontal/vertical, add bottom split | ✓ | ✅ | each pane is a Chrome tab at its own size; Chrome's dialogs follow the focused pane (ledger "Split view"). The page menu's Open Link in Split View (issue #338) and the split click put the link in a pane right of its page, which keeps focus; from a page already in a split the link takes the next pane (in a full split, the pane after the page) as a tab of its own, loaded once with the page as referrer, and the tab that was there stays in the list right after the split. Before 2026-10-25 the menu item opened a background tab in a new group with the page (Chrome made the split in its own tab strip, which the app doesn't read). Verified in a hidden instance: acceptance `context-menu-split` (one request each, referrer kept, no group), `state/splits/splitRows.test.mjs`. An empty pane's "Add a tab to this Split View" card takes the arrow keys as the command bar does (2026-11-18, issue #2657): ↓ highlights the first suggested tab, ↑/↓ move through them and back to the field, ↩ puts the highlighted tab in the pane, and ↩ with nothing highlighted still searches what you typed; typing or Esc drops the highlight and hovering a row moves it there. Watch `split_suggestion_key_picks` in the hourly counts |
| Open Split Pane ⌃⇧=, focus next/prev ⌃⇧] / ⌃⇧[ | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Drag tab to edge to split; ⌥‑click ➕ opens split NTP; ⇧⌥‑click link opens right pane | ✓ | ✅ | In the sidebar a tab let go on another tab row's left or right half makes a split on that side, as in Arc (Drag reorder). Dia's split targets from the sidebar and the top strip alike ("Add left split" / "Add right split" at each pane's sides: slide in, grow, the one under the dragged card takes the accent and leans to the pointer; the tab shows as a small window under the pointer; `dia-spec.md` › Dragging tabs and split targets). The page showing the dragged tab alone splits with the tab used before it |
| Per‑pane nav bar / tint / close | ✓ | ✅ | unfocused panes' toolbars dim; the focused pane has Dia's accent ring, a 2 pt stroke in the profile's color 1 pt outside the page, in every address bar layout; with the page frame at 0 it sits just inside the pane, over the page, so no side falls off the window, and takes the window's own corner radius at the corners it shares with the window (`frameCornerRadius`, read live from the window's frame view: 16 pt on macOS 27, where `windowCornerRadius` falls back to 19; square in full screen or where a platform reports none) (`SplitFocusRing`, 2026-11-14) |
| Flip / convert orientation / separate all | ✓ | ✅ | + Move Pane Left/Right, Remove from Split View |
| Double-click a split divider to even the panes | — | ✅ | 2026-11-14, an owner request. Like the sidebar's resize handle: double-click a divider and the two panes it separates go back to even sizes, as in Arc (#2267; 50/50 for two panes, and with three the third keeps its size); the divider inside a stacked pane evens that stack. A press that drags more than 3 pt keeps the dragged size. Watch `split_divider_resets` in the usage event |

## 7. New Tab Page & command bar
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| NTP layout (bar width, position, HUD blur material, fill, border, radius 20) | ✓ | ✅ | 1.50: bar edges fitted to 0.5 pt from the 1.50.1 intro capture: Dia's bar and mark sit 1 pt right of and below NewTabPageViewController's formulas for our card, and the bar is 112 pt tall; ours now match (2x layer snapshot). Inside the bar the input row and the chip row now sit where Dia's do (magnifier, chip borders, mic and send within 0.1 pt of the capture; the chip label 0.5 pt low). Still different: Dia's magnifier is ~3 pt further right and 1 pt smaller, and its placeholder ~5 % narrower |
| Logo: glass orb (1.49) → hand-painted mark per profile colour (1.50) | ✓ | ✅ | Intentionally ours: Ardia's painted hills replace Dia's painted dome, repainted per profile colour as Dia's mark is (`assets/ntp-mark-<colour>-{back,front,sun}*.png`, the brand's green as `ntp-mark-{back,front,sun}*.png`, incognito neutral; `docs/brand/ardia`), one set for both appearances. A 100 pt frame centred on the bar where Dia's mark is, its top 84.1 pt above the bar and clipped at the bar's top edge. The painting is three layers, each rising from just under the bar on its own spring (2026-10-11, the owner's ask): the dark front hill first (80 ms after the page appears, response 0.42 s, damping 0.76, 34 pt), the light hill from behind it (170 ms, 0.46 / 0.84, 40.5 pt; it trails the front hill throughout, so the paint build.py lays under the front hill never shows), then the sun from behind both (250 ms, 0.56 / 0.9, 56 pt, drifting 6 pt left on a critically damped 0.6 s spring); at rest by about 0.8 s after the page appears, native driver, once per tab, none under Reduce Motion. At rest the layers make the old single image within 6 levels (window captures, green, plum and neutral, light and dark). The sidebar's New Tab row shows a one-colour version (`assets/new-tab-mark*.png`) with Dia's tint. Dia's dome measurements stay in `docs/dia-spec.md` |
| Power‑up band intro | ✓ | ✅ | 1.50: one theme colour (grey 0.65 for Neutral), corner radius 20 |
| Area light (intro, breathing, fade) | ✓ | ✅ | palette follows the profile colour; Dia's clock model (skip-ahead, key/occlusion pause, × 0.5 when not key) |
| Edge light around bar | ✓ | ✅ | colour from the profile theme |
| Entrance spring / Reduce Motion | ✓ | ✅ | like Dia under Reduce Motion: no spring, no band, edge light settled, area light still |
| Profile‑colour NTP theme & gradient | ✓ | ✅ | the window colour is the translucent window treatment (row "Window backdrop tint + translucency"): 1.50's "lighter key tint" was the blur's active output, not a different tint, so `activeTint`/`inactiveTint` are gone; the content card is #121212 at 0.5 (white 0.7 light) over it, so the blur shows through the New Tab card at half strength. No grain in 1.50's stack (the Metal backdrop with grain remains only for the New Tab postcards) |
| New tab before its first paint (no white flash) | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-10-22, GitHub issue #23. A new tab showed Chrome's white compositor background until the page's first frame (a flashbang in dark mode): ArdiaCore has no ContentsWebView, so nothing set the web view's background as Chrome does. Each tab now gets the card colour, made opaque over the window tint, as its pre-first-frame background (`contentsBackgroundColor`, `ac_contents_background.mm`), and the prewarmed blank under the New Tab page shows the card instead of Chrome's dark canvas until the next page paints. Websites render as before. Reaches users with the Helium engine; inert on older frameworks. 2026-11-17: switching from the New Tab page to a tab whose page hasn't loaded this session (asleep, restored, a pinned tab) keeps the New Tab page's own background until the page first draws, then fades the page in over 160 ms. A new tab view keeps its page invisible (the same hold Little Ardia uses, `ArdiaWebView+FirstPaint.mm`), so what shows is the tab's empty card over the live window material, the exact New Tab background on any Mac, wallpaper, profile colour, light or dark; no constant colour can match it. The hold starts only for an on-screen view that creates its tab for a real address (a tab warmed in the background isn't held) (not a transferred, offered or prewarmed page, a peek, Little Ardia's own rule, or any later navigation) and ends at the page's first contentful paint or two frames after its load (page script, with a 1 s backstop after load), at the next drawn frame after load for pages without the page script (PDF viewer chrome, error and chrome:// pages), on a press in the page, or after 4 s. The New Tab content no longer lingers 400 ms under a tab that has no page yet (macOS only: `holdsColdPagesUntilPainted`; Linux and Windows keep the linger). The usual pre-paint colour still fills a page that hasn't drawn after 4 s. Measured on 60 fps window recordings in a hidden instance, click to Google's paint: dark neutral before (33,33,33) → (25,23,23) for 0.65 s → page; after, the whole card area stays at the New Tab level (max deviation 0.00 levels) until the page fades in; same in plum dark, light and a page delayed 3 s; an already-loaded switch and Little Ardia are unchanged. Watch `perf_memory.cold_tab_holds` against `cold_tab_holds_timed_out` (should stay a small share) and `cold_tab_hold_ms` / `cold_tab_holds` for the mean wait. Custom themes keep their card over their own tint |
| Same-site link click keeps the old page until the new one paints | ✓ | ✅ | GitHub issue #795. Clicking a same-site link showed the new page's root background (white on most sites) until its first frame: 10 of 30 clicks for up to 58 ms, and 27 of 30 for up to 335 ms when the page has a render-blocking stylesheet. The swap hides the old view before the new view takes its frame, and hiding dropped the old view's compositor and the frame with it. `chromium-keep-old-frame-on-swap.patch` keeps it for the view that takes over until that view has drawn (`docs/engine-build.md`); measured 2026-11-07 in a hidden instance with ScreenCaptureKit at up to 120 fps: 0 of 20 clicks blank in either case (`docs/perf/vs-browsers.md` › Same-site link click). A second click within 100 ms, while the first new page is still blocked, can still flash for a frame or two |
| Drop files, links or text on the New Tab page | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-07, GitHub issue #970. A drop on the New Tab page (or the empty card when New Tab opens the command bar, or an empty split pane) was refused: the page is our React view, the hidden web view behind it takes no drops, and the only drop target there was the window-wide extension-package area, which accepts .crx alone. Now a dropped file (from Finder or the Downloads popover) opens in that tab as its file:// URL, several open the first there and the rest in background tabs, a link navigates and text goes through the address field (a host opens, words search). The search field still takes a text drop itself, and React Native images (the New Tab mark, pinned tile favicons) no longer refuse drops meant for what's under them: every NSImageView registers for drags, so RCTUIImageView now registers none. Web pages keep Chrome's own handling: a page's drop zone gets the files, elsewhere each file opens in a new tab, about:blank included |
| NTP release‑notes postcard, Trial Guide, personalize button | ✓ | ✅ | postcard with Dia's geometry and springs, full-page notes, 1 day, never on fresh installs or incognito; Personalize button. Trial Guide is a plans feature (—). Our own artwork |
| NTP connect‑apps upsell | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| NTP query restore when navigating back | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Command bar panel over toolbar (⌘L / click URL) | ✓ | ✅ | A click on the address always opens the bar at the field, whatever Settings › Appearance › "⌘L opens the command bar" says (2026-11-12, a user's request: "clicking url bar should open inline, only cmd+t should be the centered one"); ⌘L does the same while the address is on screen, and opens in the centre when it isn't (sidebar address with the sidebar hidden, a full-screen page), where it used to drop down at a stale spot. ⌘T and the sidebar's New Tab row keep the centred bar. Telemetry: `command_bar_opened` carries `placement` (`field`, `centred`, `centred_no_field`, `new_tab`); watch `centred_no_field`'s share (a jump means the field check misses a layout) and `field` vs `centred` among users with In the centre. Dia's New Tab page has no address field (its toolbar keeps only the buttons), so with the address bar on the card ⌘L on the NTP focuses and selects its centred box; with the address bar in the sidebar (Arc's layout, ours) a click on the empty field there opens the dropdown at the field as on any page (fixed 2026-10-20, a user's report on 0.2.38: the click only moved the caret to the centred box, which usually had it already, so the field looked dead), and so does ⌘L (fixed 2026-10-20, feedback on 0.2.39-nightly.3: ⌘L still went to the centred box); Esc from it gives the box its focus back; 1.50's two bar shadows (0.08 r2 / 0.04 r1); a click anywhere outside it (the page, the sidebar or its rows, the tab strip, Little Ardia's bar) closes it as Esc does, and the click still does its own thing (fixed 2026-10-06, the owner's 0.2.27 report: a click on the empty sidebar left it open; `OutsidePressArea`); Dia 1.52's geometry and grow (2026-10-09, the owner's recording): the panel's right edge 40pt past the field's end, so a wide field (926pt in a 1358pt window) is covered with its hover icons instead of sticking out past an 888pt cap (the anchor is the field's real width now, without the extensions and split buttons); the text starts where the field's did (0px shift measured, secure and not-secure fields: 48pt in from the panel edge at the field's 13pt, baseline matched; was 10px right and 15pt); it opens at its full size on the first frame after the press, with every row, as do Arc's sidebar dropdown and Little Ardia's, with or without Reduce Motion (2026-11-20, issue #3283: Dia grows it out of the field in 130 ms, but the command bar is used all day, so the owner's motion rule wins; the grow also drew the sidebar dropdown's rows at the field's width for a frame and then hid them for two). Telemetry: `usage_counts.command_bar_open_relayouts`, the opens whose panel changed width in its first 250 ms; it should stay 0. The floating bars (⌘T's command bar and ⌘L in the centre) type at the suggestion rows' 14 pt, so typed text and rows match; the panel over the address keeps the field's 13 pt so the text doesn't move (2026-10-25, issue #359). The page keeps the keyboard around the bar (issue #1893, PR #1894): closing the bar by a click elsewhere gives the page the keyboard when nothing else took it, and a tab you leave with the bar open gets the keyboard back when you return, so Space and the arrows reach a video without a click. Check: `activation-acceptance.mjs keys-after-command-bar`. Telemetry: `usage_counts.page_focus_returns_after_bar`; watch it stay above zero. The sidebar rows lose their hover only while the panel is really drawn over them: with no active tab in the profile the panel draws nothing and covers nothing, and closing or unmounting it hands the rows back (issue #733). Left for a person: the same steps with a real keyboard and mouse |
| URL fixup | ✓ | ✅ | |

The ⌘T command bar (Settings › Tabs › New tab opens: Command Bar) lists the profile's open tabs before anything is typed, most recently used first as in Search Tabs (⇧⌘A), all windows, the current tab left out, up to 8 (2026-10-24, issue #82): the most recent one is selected, as in Arc, so ⌘T then Enter switches to it, and ↓ or ↑ move the selection without writing the tab's address into the field, so you can still type a search right away (2026-11-12; watch `suggestion_chosen` with type `tab`); typing switches to the usual suggestions, where open tabs show as "Switch to Tab" |
| Bookmark suggestions | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Search engine suggestions | ✓ | ✅ | Fetched through the profile's own Chrome network stack (`ac_search_suggest`, as Chrome's omnibox: Chrome's user agent, the profile's cookies sent and never saved, a private window's own session), one request id per bar so windows never cancel each other; Google on Chrome's endpoint (`www.google.com/complete/search?client=chrome`), its navigation and calculator rows left out. None in private windows, as in Chrome. Until 2026-11-12 they went through React Native's fetch (`Ardia/<build> CFNetwork` user agent, no browser cookies, `suggestqueries.google.com?client=firefox`), one per keystroke, a likely cause of Google's "unusual traffic" reCAPTCHA (#1281). Telemetry: `perf_omnibox.search_suggest_ok`, `search_suggest_blocked` (403/429) and `search_suggest_failed`; watch the blocked share |
| Inline autocomplete | ✓ | ✅ | Deleting text in any way (Backspace, Delete, ⌥⌫, ⌘⌫, cut, deleting the selected completion) drops the completion and puts the search for what was typed on top, as in Chrome and Dia (`preventInline` in `packages/core/src/suggest.ts`: a page only goes first then when what was typed is exactly its host or address, and a typed URL still goes first); the next typed character turns completion back on. Unit tests, and the dev driver in a hidden instance ("gi" → page first with "thub.com"; after a delete → search first, no completion; "t" → back). The native selected-completion delete itself isn't reachable from the dev driver (it bypasses the field), so it's covered by `inline.test.mjs` only Prefer Search Engine still completes one word inline, as Chrome does ("you" → youtube.com, 2026-11-16, #2278); a typed or pasted address opens exactly as typed, #fragment and slash included (#851, #870); a renamed tab is found by its name (#606); the search engine's site suggestions are rows (Google Maps for "maps", #2279). Dashboard: `suggestion_chosen.via` (literal, renamed_tab, engine_site, search_first_inline) and `usage_counts.command_bar_site_suggestions`. |
