They're listed under "Extensions" in Settings › Search Engine, can be made the default and have Tab-to-search keywords; like Chrome, an extension that asked to be the default (`is_default`) controls it ("… is controlling this setting", Manage / Disable) until it's disabled or removed, then the user's own choice is back. Verified with two fixture extensions (instance `polish`) |
| Site search (Tab‑to‑search) | ✓ | ✅ | engines, known sites, history scope. Tab enters it whenever the top row offers it ("Search Google ⇥"), also while inline completion is showing ("goo" + "gle.com"); Space after a site does too. Backspace in the empty field leaves it and puts back the text that was there, like Chrome's keyword mode: "x.com " with its space when a space entered it, so another space stays a space (fixed 2026-10-20, feedback on 0.2.39-nightly.3: it gave "x.com"), "x.com" when Tab did, and the bar stays open (fixed 2026-10-20: in the sidebar dropdown and the panel, leaving by Backspace or Esc closed the whole bar, because AppKit ends and restarts the edit of an empty field whose placeholder changes and React Native reported that as a blur; the react-native-macos patch guards it); with text it just deletes. The field gets Tab and Backspace as commands (`insertTab:`/`deleteBackward:`), not key equivalents, so the react-native-macos patch forwards them to JS (`packages/core/src/siteSearch.test.ts`). Entering one pulses the bar in the site's colour, as Arc does (#969): a tint and 1 pt ring rise in 100 ms and settle over 400 ms to a faint tint that stays while the scope does, in the panel, the sidebar dropdown and the New Tab bar. The colour is the site's brand colour for the built-in engines and popular sites (`SITE_BRAND_COLORS`), else its favicon's dominant hue (`IconAccent.swift`, like Arc's `FaviconDominantColorFetcher`), else the profile's colour; it is moved into a luminance band per appearance so it reads on both, and a greyscale brand pulses neutral. Reduce Motion shows the resting tint without the pulse (`components/omnibox/ScopePulse.tsx`, `pulseInk.test.mjs`) |
| Calculator in command bar | ✓ | ✅ | ↩ copies the result |
| !bangs in the command bar (Ardia extra; DuckDuckGo's, t3.chat's unduck) | — | ✅ | 2026-10-22, the owner asked for "t3 bangs". `!gh react`, `react !w` or a bare `!yt` (the site's home) goes straight to the site; the top row reads "Search GitHub for react", then that site's pages from history, and the Go pill names the site. Only a first or last word that is `!` plus a known trigger counts (any case), so `hello!`, `wow ! nice` and unknown `!x` stay normal searches, and `?` still forces one. Terms are escaped as Chrome escapes a search engine's: `+` for spaces after `?`, `/` kept in a path. The list is Helium's (Kagi's bangs plus imput's, MIT, 13,395 triggers): a snapshot ships in the app and loads only when a `!` is typed; that first `!` of a session refreshes it from services.helium.imput.net at most once a day through a native fetch (`fetchBangList`: ETag, no cookies or caches, redirects refused, 5 MB cap, nothing typed), kept in the data dir as one document with its ETag, and a bad response or a corrupt saved list falls back to the last good one. A template with the terms in its host is left out, and a resolved URL must keep the template's scheme, host and port. Enter before the list has loaded waits for it. The web demo uses the snapshot only, as its own chunk (`packages/core/src/bangs.ts`, `components/omnibox/bangs.ts`, `packages/core/scripts/bangs.ts` regenerates it) |
| "new doc / sheet / jira / meeting / figma…" commands | ✓ | ✅ | 17 `*.new` shortcuts + browser actions in the bar, Share and Keyboard Shortcuts included (both now in `runCommand`). Arc's Inspect Elements, JavaScript Console, View Source, Save Page As, Cast, Task Manager, Manage Extensions, Add Extension and New Little Ardia Window too (2026-11-15, issue #451, part of #323): the page ones only with a page open, View Source only on a web page or file; Enter runs one only for its title or a command phrase ("inspect element", "js console", "page source", "save as", "cast tab", "open task manager", "install extension", "little arc", "mini window"), so "html", "memory" or "extensions" still search (`components/omnibox/barCommands.test.mjs`). Watch `suggestion_chosen` with type action, split by its new `command` field, for how often each of the nine runs from the bar. Split view and profile actions (2026-10-24, issue #85), shown only when they apply: Split View Side by Side / Stacked / with Tab… (a menu of the window's tabs), and inside a split Add Tab to Split View…, the split menu's Add / Flip / Convert, Remove from Split View and Exit Split View; Move Tab to Profile… (a menu of profiles, New Profile… included) and one "Move Tab to <profile>" row per profile, Rename Profile… and Edit Profiles…, next to the existing Switch to <profile> and New Profile; "space" finds the profile rows. A query of 4+ characters lists up to 4 matching actions (2 before), so "split" or "move tab" shows the set |
| Extensions in the command bar (Arc's) | — (Arc ✓) | 🧪 | 2026-10-24, GitHub issue #182. Typing an installed, turned-on extension's name (three letters or more, or the whole name) lists it with its icon: "Open Extension" runs its toolbar action as the toolbar button does, so its popup opens under its pinned button or, unpinned, under the Extensions button; the best match also gets "Extension Options" when it has an options page (an extension with no toolbar action offers only that). The whole name goes first unless a visited site completes inline. Private windows list only the extensions they show. Logic and tests in `packages/core/src/extensionRows.ts`. Needs a check in a hidden instance. |
| `@` mentions & `/` skills in bar | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| "+ Add tabs or files" chip | ✓ | ⏸ | attaching needs Chat. Ours says what it does (2026-10-21): "↗ Open a tab or file", a menu of the window's other tabs (switches to one) and Open File… (opens it in this tab) |
| Mic / dictation button | ✓ (streaming, hold‑to‑speak) | ⏸ | the button (tooltip "Dictate") starts macOS dictation in the bar (done); what's left, Dia's streaming hold-to-speak transcription, is its AI voice input (⏸) |
| Paste and Go / Paste and Search | ✓ | ✅ | bar and URL right-click menus; strips trackers |
| ⌘V right after ⌘T / ⌘L / ⌘F | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-10-22, a Discord report ("sometimes I have to hit ⌘V twice"). The first ⌘V after a shortcut that moves focus off a page went to the old page while the new field was still mounting. Now ⌘T, ⌘L and ⌘F take focus off the page at once, and Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All wait for the field the command opens (up to 3 s, renewed by a newer command; it was 400 ms, which a JS thread busy for 450 ms or more outlasted, so letters typed right after ⌘T beeped or went into the old page, #816) and land there, or are dropped with a beep, never into the old page. Escape or ⌘W closing the command bar gives the page its focus back. Not covered (unchanged): ⌘1–9, the tab switcher and profile switch (docs/known-issues/paste-focus-handoff.md). Verified: shortcuts-test (paste/copy/cut/select-all at +0–80 ms, idle and busy JS; abandon and race cases) Dashboard: `usage_counts.focus_handoffs_delivered` with its pickup buckets (`focus_handoff_pickups_under_400ms`, `_400_1000ms`, `_over_1s`; the last two are the handoffs the old 400 ms gate lost), against `focus_handoffs_timed_out` and `focus_handoff_inputs_dropped`, which should stay small. |
| Paste URLs as attachments | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Page takes the keyboard when it loads from a New Tab | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-15, a Discord report: with Vimium or VimiumC, keys did nothing on a page opened from a New Tab until it was clicked (Dia and Arc focus it). ⌘T's New Tab page keeps its tab's page view hidden behind it, so the load you start from its bar asked for page focus while that view was hidden and the request was dropped, leaving the window itself focused. Now a load started in a hidden view focuses the page as it shows, within the usual 1 s and only when nothing else (a field) holds the focus; a tab opened in the background still doesn't take the keys from the page you're in. Checked in a hidden instance: after ⌘T, a URL and ↩, `document.hasFocus()` is true and a typed j reaches the page (was false and lost); film `fixes-page-focus`. Dashboard: `usage_counts.bar_loads` and `bar_loads_page_focused` (addresses opened from a bar, and how many of those pages held the keyboard 2 s later); the ratio should sit near 1. |
| Page takes the keyboard when a link opens it in a new tab | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-07, #630 (h5player's Shift+Enter on a Bilibili video opened in a new tab did nothing until a click) and #649 (monkeytype typing never registered). A tab the engine hands over for a link (`ardiacore:`, `tab:` or `link:` adopt id) is attached and shown with no focus request after, so the window held the keyboard and the page didn't. Now such a tab is marked when it is taken or offered, and the first time it is attached, shown and is the strip's active tab in its window, it asks for page focus once, within the usual 1 s and only when nothing else (a field) holds the focus; a link opened in the background doesn't take the keys. Verified here by the source-wiring test `packages/ardiacore/tests/first-show-focus-wiring.test.mjs` (fails on main, passes) and the store test `linkTabLoadFocus.test.mjs`; not yet run on a Mac: the `link-tab-focus` check in `packages/ardiacore/scripts/acceptance.mjs` (target-blank and window-open tabs must report `document.hasFocus()` with no click) has to pass in a hidden instance before this counts as verified. Dashboard: `usage_counts.link_tab_loads` and `link_tab_loads_page_focused` (link tabs opened in front, and how many of those pages held the keyboard 2 s later); the ratio should sit near 1. |
| New tab opens the command bar (Arc's; opt-in) | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | 2026-10-21, a Discord request. Settings › Tabs › New tab opens: New Tab Page (default, unchanged) or Command Bar. With Command Bar, ⌘T, File › New Tab, the sidebar's + New Tab and its menu, a double-click on the empty sidebar and the top strip's + open the command bar centred over the page (the New Tab box's width, 22% down the card, at that size from its first frame with no entrance, as Dia's New Tab bar); no tab, engine tab or history entry exists until a pick. A URL, search or history pick makes one selected tab where Settings › Tabs › Add new tabs puts new tabs, outside the source group or split unless that's Below the current tab (it went to the top of the list, or the far left across the top, whatever that setting said until 2026-10-25, issue #337); ⌥↩ opens a right split pane instead, or a background tab when the split is already full (issue #1076; from an empty card or the New Tab page it fills that tab as ↩ does; it asks automation's opening rules first; watch `bar_split_opens` and `bar_split_full_tabs` in the hourly counts); an open tab switches; a command just runs; an empty Enter switches to the most recent open tab, or does nothing when there is none. ⌥⌘T in a group sends the pick into the group; ⌥-click + stays Split. Esc gives the page back its field and caret; ⌘T again, a click outside, ⌘W (it only dismisses the bar), another window taking focus and the app going to the background dismiss it and drop the draft. Closing the last tab keeps the window as an empty card; ⌘N and that empty card show the bar once and Esc keeps it down; ⌘T over that bar keeps it up (it used to close it, so ⌘W then ⌘T left an empty page, 2026-11-11), and the next ⌘T closes it; a pick there fills the window. A launch whose window is only an empty card shows the bar over it, as ⌘T does (2026-11-14, issue #1338: the window was an empty page); a restored launch with a page and a link from another app show no bar and add no spare tab. Dashboard: `usage_counts.new_tab_bar_launch_shows`. A tab a page opened (a link with target=_blank, `window.open`) never shows the bar, even in the moment its URL is still empty (issue #761). Little Ardia and private windows' last tab keep today's rules. Policies the owner may flip after checking Arc sit in `NEW_TAB_BAR_POLICY` (`state/omnibox/newTabBarPolicy.ts`); Arc's flow and the evidence: the sprint's `arc-cmdbar-flow.md`. `state/omnibox/newTabBar.test.mjs` and a hidden instance (⌘W on the last tab then ⌘T; engine tabs counted before opening, after cancelling and after a pick, setting on and off). Watch `new_tab_bar_keeps` in the hourly counts: ⌘T presses that kept a bar shown on its own |
| Custom New Tab page address (Chrome's; opt-in) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-15, GitHub issue #567 (continues outside PR #1640 by yagoinved). Settings › Tabs › New tab page address (`newTabUrl`, empty by default, synced, left out of config.json like `customSearchUrl`): ⌘T, + New Tab, the sidebar's New Tab, a double-click under the tabs, a group's New Tab and new windows open that address instead of the New Tab page; links, typed addresses and Little Ardia keep their own. Ignored while New tab opens is Command Bar or Minimalism mode is on. A `tab.opening` hook that answers later still cancels or redirects it. Only http and https addresses, about:blank and Ardia's History, Bookmarks and Downloads pages are taken; anything else (Chrome's debug and internal pages such as chrome://quit, Ardia's New Tab or Settings, mailto: and app links) falls back to the New Tab page. Private windows open the address too. A `tab.opening` rule sees and can cancel or rewrite the address on ⌘T, ⌘N and a group's New Tab alike (`state/tabs/newTabHome.ts`). Watch `settings_snapshot.custom_new_tab_url` |
| ⌘L bar in the centre of the window (Arc's; opt-in) | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | 2026-11-20: picked with two picture cards in Settings › Appearance › Toolbar (At the address, In the center), each drawing the address field where it sits now; "Clicking the address opens it in the center too" is a switch below them. The address bar's place (toolbar or sidebar) is a pair of picture cards above. Watch `command_bar_picks_*` and `address_bar_picks_*`. 2026-10-24, GitHub issue #132. Settings › Appearance, under Address Bar: "⌘L opens the command bar" At the address (default, unchanged) or In the centre. In the centre, ⌘L shows the ⌘L bar where the ⌘T command bar sits (the New Tab box's width, 22% down the card, no grow from the field), pre-filled and selected with the page's URL; Enter navigates the current tab, Esc and a click outside close it as before. Since 2026-11-12 a click on the address opens at the field even with In the centre (only ⌘L follows the setting); a third choice, In the centre, also on click (#1379, 2026-11-15), sends a click on the address, in the toolbar or the sidebar, to the centre too, as before 0.2.46-nightly.6; watch `setting_commandBarPlacement` = `centredOnClick` in `settings_snapshot` and `command_bar_opened` placement `centred`. On the New Tab page ⌘L still focuses its own centred box; Little Ardia keeps its compact dropdown |

## 8. Navigation & page tools
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Back / forward / reload / force reload | ✓ | ✅ | ⌘-click reload/back/forward → background tab |
| Stop (X next to address bar) | ✓ | ✅ | Esc stops a loading page |
| Hold back button for history list; ⌘/middle‑click entries | ✓ | ✅ | our popover (layout/HistoryPopover): up to 15 entries + Show Full History, modifier clicks open tabs |
| Two‑finger swipe navigation (custom UI, works on native pages) | ✓ | ✅ | ArdiaSwipe + Dia's overlay (layout/SwipeOverlay), on web, New Tab and internal pages. ArdiaSwipe sits in front of Chrome's own responder delegate and forwards to it (spelling, speech, dialog focus), except the scroll events Chrome's history swiper would act on. Verified headless (2026-09-26) with phased trackpad events built as CGEvents and sent through the window (the renderer acks them; 15 checks): back and forward go exactly one entry; a 40 pt swipe does nothing; a vertical one scrolls; a carousel that can still scroll keeps the swipe, one at its start navigates; `overscroll-behavior-x: none` opts out; holding past the threshold opens the destination list and moving down picks two back; as in Dia (a clip of 1.55, 2026-11-03), the list opens upward from the capsule's centre line with the nearest entry at the bottom, and each pick slides the whole panel one 38 pt row so the selected row stays on that line (until then the list hung down and a pill moved through it); a three-finger swipe goes back; back to and forward from the New Tab page; back on a tab a link opened, at the start of its history, closes the tab and returns to the tab that opened it, as in Dia (#857), with an ✕ in the swipe capsule instead of the chevron; back on a Peek, at the start of its history, ends the Peek and returns to the tab it was opened from, the same way; the Back button's tooltip then reads "Close Tab and Go Back" (toolbar, sidebar address bar and Little Ardia); watch `usage_counts.back_closes_to_opener`. Like Safari and Chrome it follows System Settings › Trackpad › Swipe between pages (off on this Mac). Left for a person: a real trackpad's feel, the haptic, and the overlay by eye (checklist step 9a) Fixed 2026-11-16 (a tester's report on Discord: with the history list open, pulling the swipe back no longer cancelled it): once the list is open, pulling the swipe back under three quarters of the commit distance (`LIST_FOLD_PROGRESS`) folds the list back into the plain capsule, so letting go navigates nowhere, as reversing before the list opens already did. Moving up and down the list keeps it open. `apps/browser/scripts/swipe-history-list-test.mjs` (quick swipe, pulled back before and after the list opens, hold and pick; the last fails before the fix). Telemetry: `usage_counts.swipe_history_list_opens` and `swipe_history_list_folds`; watch folds stay a small share of opens. |
| Load progress | ✓ | ✅ | A loading pill at the top centre of the page (`components/window/LoadingPill.tsx`): a 140×10 capsule whose fill sweeps in a 1 s loop (0.33 s grow, 0.33 s wipe, 0.33 s rest) while the tab loads, then fills, shrinks and fades in 0.25 s. With the address bar in the toolbar the pill stays away and the address field shows the load, as Dia's does (`components/toolbar/FieldLoadFill.tsx`, measured from the owner's recording of 2026-11-13): an accent glow fills the field from the left to the page's load progress (10% at the start, reached in 0.2 s), 8% strong at the top and 70% above a 1.5 pt accent line along the bottom (Dia's is 5% to 50% over a 1 pt line; the owner asked for a stronger one), and a soft front: the line and the strong glow above it fade out over 72 pt (Dia's take 16 pt; the owner asked for a smoother end), the faint glow over 120 pt; while the page loads the fill stops 120 pt short of the field's right end, so its soft front is never cut off there; when the load ends it sweeps to the right end in 0.3 s while its left fades out, holds 0.2 s and fades in 0.25 s. On a website-coloured toolbar whose colour is close to the accent (RGB distance under 0.25, a blue site), the glow takes the toolbar's ink instead, so it never disappears. Dia's faint 1.5 s shimmer on the line isn't drawn. The glow is `GradientFill`, `Surface`'s new gradient fill (`fillColors`, `fillStops`, `fade`, `fadeStops`; native API 62). The address field in the sidebar shows the same glow; while the sidebar is pinned open it carries the load alone, and while it is hidden (it slides in on hover) the pill shows too, so the load is visible either way. The pill shows wherever no address field is in view: a hidden sidebar, fullscreen, a full-screen Peek, and a toolbar hidden while scrolling; the glow and the pill replace the line under the toolbar, in the sidebar address field and along the collapsed auto-hidden toolbar's strip (#1721's line: with the pill already over the page there, two indicators for one load read as noise) (Little Ardia and a windowed Peek show the same glow in their address field, which spans the bar up to its buttons, and show no pill). It waits 150 ms before showing, so an in-page change (a `#` link, `pushState`/`replaceState` in a single-page app), which Chrome reports as a ~30 ms load, never flashes it. Watch `usage_counts.loading_field_shows` (loads shown in an address field's glow: the toolbar's, the sidebar's, Little Ardia's or a windowed Peek's), `loading_pill_shows`, `loading_pill_long_shows` (shown for over 10 s) and `loading_pill_skips` (loads that ended before the pill showed). |
| URL bar shows host / title; hover reveals full URL; Show Full URL | ✓ | ✅ | Dia 1.50's rule, from its binary: a web page shows its host alone (no `www.`), and View › Show Full URL adds the path, query and fragment without a trailing "/" (`displayPageTitleInURLBarEnabled`, default on, only decides that); only Dia's own pages and chats show `Dia / title`. Ours does the same, and our own pages and local files keep `host / title`. Hover still shows the full URL. Checked in a Release instance (example.com/docs/intro/: `example.com`, then `example.com/docs/intro` with Show Full URL). Settings › Appearance › Address Bar › "Address bar buttons" (#997, 2026-11-15, synced, in config files): Show on hover (default, Dia's) keeps Site Controls, Open Split View and Bookmark or Copy URL hidden until the pointer is over the field; Always show keeps them there. With the address bar in the sidebar (#661, the owner's call: it reverses the always-shown left Site Controls button of c652a3d7c), Show on hover also leaves only the address until the pointer is over the field, as in Arc: Site Controls moves into the hover group next to Bookmark and the pinned extensions show on hover or while one's popup is open; the not-secure lock, the camera and mic dot, blocked pop-ups, zoom and Translate always show. Always show keeps the old sidebar look. Watch `setting_addressBarButtons` in `settings_snapshot` and `site_controls_opens_from_sidebar_field` in `usage_counts` |
| Punycode display | ✓ | ✅ | `core/idn.ts`: Chrome's spoof checks, punycode otherwise |
| Find in page ⌘F / ⌘G / ⇧⌘G | ✓ | ✅ | per-tab state, "n of m", ⇧↩ |
| Use Selection for Find | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Jump to Selection, Find & Replace | ✓ | ✅ | ⌘J, ⌥⌘F with Replace / All for the focused input, textarea or contenteditable. One ⌘Z undoes a Replace All in text fields; in a contenteditable each replacement is its own undo step |
| Zoom ⌘+ / ⌘- / ⌘0 | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's per-host zoom per profile (`HostZoomMap`, Chrome's steps); Settings › Privacy lists and resets levels. Away from 100% the address field (toolbar or sidebar) shows Chrome's magnifier (+ or −), which opens − / + / Reset. Like Chrome's zoom bubble, a zoom change you make (⌘+ / ⌘- / ⌘0, View › Zoom, ⌘-scroll, the popover's own buttons) opens that popover by itself with the new percentage, without taking focus from the page and without a click-catching backdrop; each change restarts its 1.5 s close timer, the pointer over it holds it open, and back at 100% it still shows 100% while the magnifier stays until it closes (`state/site/zoomBubble.ts`, the native `zoom` event's `user` flag from `-[ArdiaWebView zoomStep:]`, set only when the level changed). A remembered level on load, switching to a zoomed tab, or another tab on the same host following the change never opens it. Clicking the magnifier, also while it shows by itself, keeps it until dismissed. ⌘-scroll zooms with a wheel mouse or a Magic Mouse and scrolls with a trackpad (the scroll's HID sender; `apps/browser/scripts/zoom-scroll-test.mjs`). The page gets it first (issues #479, #497): while ⌘ is held the page script listens for wheels, and Ardia zooms only for a ⌘-wheel no page handler cancelled, so maps and Canva zoom their own canvas. Settings › Privacy › Zoom turns ⌘-scroll zoom off (#531); ⌘-scroll then reaches the page as in Chrome, which never zooms on a wheel on macOS (`WebContentsImpl::HandleWheelEvent`). Pinch zoom is reported by the page script but unused |
| Preload pages (preconnect, prefetch, prerender) | — | ✅ | Settings › Profiles › This profile › Preload pages (issue #937, 2026-11-15; in Privacy › Speed until the 2026-11-17 settings redesign), per profile, off by default: it sets Chrome's own preload pages state (`prefetch::SetPreloadPagesState`, standard on, none off), which the ungoogled patches keep off because it fetches pages before you open them. Pages open sooner on a slow connection, and a page a site prerenders through speculation rules is no longer cancelled (`kMojoBinderPolicy`): the page script holds its posts while `document.IsPrerendering()` and sends them in order on activation (at most 64 posts and 2 MiB), and `PageChannel::Post` drops any post from a prerendering frame, so nothing a hidden page says reaches the tab before it's shown. A profile that isn't open yet is loaded before the setting is read or written. Needs a new engine build (`engine/engine-key.sh` changes). Mac only for now: `PLATFORM_FEATURES.preloadPages` is off on Linux and Windows, so their Settings don't show the row (Windows lists `sites.preloadPages` and `sites.setPreloadPages` as missing calls), until each engine answers them. Checked on a Mac in a hidden instance (2026-11-17): with the setting at its default a speculation-rules prerender is refused (`PreloadingDisabled`); with it on, the engine before this change cancelled the prerender with `MojoBinderPolicy` on `ardiacore.mojom.ACPageHost`, and this one keeps it to `Ready` and activates it (`activationStart` 3.5 s, `document.prerendering` true in the page), and the page's title still reaches the tab. Telemetry: `usage_counts` `preload_pages_turned_on` and `preload_pages_turned_off` count the switch being turned on and off; watch both for use, and a rise in turn-offs after turn-ons as the sign something broke |
| Translate page / Never translate this site | ✓ | ✅ | on the Mac's own models (Apple's Translation framework, macOS 26; nothing leaves the Mac), not Chrome's Translate, whose Google servers domain substitution removed. Dia's UX from its 1.50.1 binary: a translate button by the address when the page isn't in one of your languages (click: translate / back to the original); Site Controls' "Translate to <language>", "Choose Another Language…" and "Never Translate This Site" (per profile); toasts "Translated to %@", "Translated back to %@", "Translation error (%@)", with progress while a long first pass runs. A paragraph translates as one text and each text node gets its part back, so links and styles stay (with another word order, each node gets exactly the words the model tagged as its own; an answer missing a node's words goes piece by piece); what's near the viewport first, the rest as you scroll; text the page adds later too; Show Original restores the text exactly. Checked on German and Japanese Wikipedia. Each passage shows as soon as the model answers it (two in flight per tab, on-screen blocks first), so the first text appears in under a second instead of after a batch of eight (#1063); a text node over 800 characters goes in sentence runs, so one long paragraph never holds up other tabs; the low-latency model's unchanged answers go to the default model again only for passages of two or more words. Telemetry: `translate_finished` (first_text_ms, first_pass_ms, passages, outcome) and `page_process_gone`'s translate, translate_stage, translating_tabs and translated_tabs. macOS 14–15: no Translation framework, so no button. The button shows only on pages in another language; the "Show Translate button" setting (#1023, `showTranslateButton`) was removed on 2026-11-16, and Site Controls still translates, picks another language and shows the original |
| Reading mode (an Ardia extra, from Search) | — | ✅ | Not in Dia; borrowed from Search's ⇧⌘R (driceroland/Search, `Reader.swift`). View › Show Reader (⌥⌘R: ⇧⌘R is Dia's Force Refresh, and ⌥⌘R is the key Chrome gave its reading mode), the page menu's Show Reader row and a reader button in the address field turn the current tab into the article: its site, title, byline and reading time, its text and pictures, set at a 65ch measure in the system serif (or sans) at one of five sizes, light or dark with the app; the text size and face are kept (`readerTextSize`, `readerFont`). Chromium 157's own readers are in the framework but don't fit: Read Anything lives in a side panel, which ArdiaCore's Browsers don't have (`engine/ardiacore/apply.sh` only keeps its controller from crashing without one), and DOM Distiller's `chrome-distiller://` viewer needs `--enable-dom-distiller` from the engine and is a navigation of its own, in Chrome's look, so the reader runs in the page: Mozilla Readability 0.6.0 (Apache-2.0, minified into `state/reader/readabilitySource.ts` by `apps/browser/scripts/readability-source.mjs`) reads a copy of the document after the live page has marked what it hides and resolved each picture's `currentSrc`, and the result is drawn in a closed shadow root over the page (top layer, page `inert`, its scroll untouched), so Esc, the reader's ✕, ⌥⌘R again or a new page brings the page back as it was, at the same scroll position, without a reload; reopening keeps the reader's own scroll; Back to the page shows it as it normally looks. The item is greyed and the button hidden on pages Readability's `isProbablyReaderable` turns down; nothing leaves the Mac. Dashboard: `usage_counts.reader_opens`, `reader_no_article` (opened on a page it then couldn't read: a rise means the check offers it where the extractor fails), `reader_pages_checked` and `reader_pages_without_article` |
| Selected‑text popover (Search) | ✓ | ✅ | Search bar over a mouse selection (default engine, new tab next to the page); page menu "Search <engine> for “…”". Settings › Search Engine › Text selection › "Show search button when selecting text" (on by default, synced) turns the bar off: an open one hides, and the page script drops its selection listeners, so nothing runs in the page; the page menu's Search and quote links stay. Both follow the keys held (Chrome's): ⌘ behind, ⇧⌘ in front, ⇧ a new window, ⌥ a split. The menu's keys come from the event that picked the item (CEF gives Chrome's page menu commands no event flags) |
| Selected‑text popover / context menu (Ask) | ✓ | ⏸ | hidden until Chat exists (`kChatEnabled` in ACClient.mm) |
| Clean link copy (trackers stripped), Copy URL as Markdown ⌥⇧⌘C | ✓ | ✅ | every Copy URL / Copy Link (as Markdown) strips trackers. 2026-11-12: a tab whose first page is still loading shows and copies the address it is loading, not about:blank (the app's own tab picture or script no longer counts as another page touching its blank document; a tab a page opened still says about:blank once that page touches it). Watch `usage_counts.url_copies_while_pending` against `url_copies` |
| Quote link / "Super Copy" (text fragment link) | ✓ | ✅ | ⇧⌘C with a selection: Dia's toast with Copy Quote Link; page menu "Copy Link to Highlight" (Chrome's item, run by core/textFragment.ts for Dia's toast and clean link) |
| JS alert / confirm / prompt dialogs | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's tab-modal dialogs, centred over the page area (or the focused split pane); `alert()` and HTTP auth verified (ledger 26); `beforeunload` untested. Chrome's styling, not Dia's |
| `<input type=file>` open panel | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's file picker. Verified headless (2026-09-26; test instances log the panel instead of showing it, ArdiaActivation.mm; 12 checks): a sheet on the app window; one file, several files with the `accept` types in its type menu, or a folder; the chosen files reach the page (`change` with names and sizes), Cancel fires `cancel`; `showOpenFilePicker` and `showSaveFilePicker` (the page wrote the chosen file) go the same way. Left for a person: seeing the sheet (checklist step 9b) |
| Pop‑up blocker (always allow/deny) | ✓ | ✅ | ours (`disable-popup-blocking` turns Chrome's off): toolbar badge + Dia's dialog, Only Once opens a tab with `opener` (ledger 24). Only Once replays the popup as the page asked (2026-09-30): from the frame that opened it (a sandboxed iframe's popup stays sandboxed; a frame that's gone isn't replayed) and with noopener / noreferrer kept (`CEF_AC_POPUP_OPENER_SUPPRESSED`); it used to run `window.open` from the main frame, handing noopener pages an opener. Fixed: a frame without an origin of its own (about:srcdoc, about:blank, data:) counted as allowed, so any page could open popups through one; it now asks as its page's site |
| Open pop-up windows as tabs (ours; opt-in) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-15, GitHub issue #1272. Settings › Tabs › Links › Open pop-up windows as tabs (`popupsOpenAsTabs`, off by default, synced, in config.json): a page's `window.open` with window features (Outlook's Open in New Window, sign-in pop-ups) opens as a foreground tab next to the page instead of a pop-up window, and a pop-up opened from a pop-up goes beside the first page too. It's the same Chrome tab, so `window.opener`, `postMessage` and `window.close()` keep working, and closing it goes back to the page. Extension pop-ups (`chrome.identity`, `windows.create`) and a pop-up with no page to open beside stay windows (`OpenPopupWindow` in `ArdiaPopupWindow.mm`). Watch `usage_counts.popups_opened_as_tabs`, which should be non-zero only where `setting_popupsOpenAsTabs` is true |
| Links that open other apps (`codex:`, `zoommtg:`, `slack:`, `mailto:`, `tel:`…) | ✓ | ✅ | They used to end on "ERR_UNKNOWN_URL_SCHEME": CEF hands them to the OS only if the client allows it in `OnProtocolExecution`, and we never did. Now ArdiaCore's `ac_external_apps.mm` cancels the navigation (the page stays) and asks in our own popover: "Open “ChatGPT”?", the app's icon, "<initiator> wants to open this application.", Cancel / Open and "Always allow <site> to open links of this type in <app>" (kept per profile in Chrome's own `protocol_handler.allowed_origin_protocol_pairs`, never in private windows, only for https/localhost initiators; Settings › Privacy › Apps allowed to open links lists them with Remove). Sized popups (OAuth windows) get the same as a sheet. No app: "No application is set to open this link". Chrome's rules: its denied schemes plus our own URLs are blocked, one request per user input (flood block), the origin shown is the navigation's initiator (cross-origin iframes show their own), a tab opened just for the link closes. Differences: `mailto:` prompts too (Chrome opens it directly); sandboxed/opaque-origin frames need a user gesture (Chrome reads sandbox flags). Verified headless 2026-09-30 (link, 302, iframe, sandbox, spam, target=_blank, popup, private window, blocked schemes). A link an extension opens with `chrome.tabs.update` (Obsidian Web Clipper's `obsidian://new`, #543) has no initiating document, so Open launches it as Chrome does; a page's link still launches only while the page that asked is alive (acceptance `external-app-from-extension`, 2026-10-28). Left for a person: the popover and the popup sheet by eye |
| Site settings menu (security, pop‑ups, cookies, clear cache) | ✓ | ✅ | Site Controls, in Arc's layout with Dia's styling (2026-10-20, a tester's ask): quick-action tiles (Share…, Copy Clean Link, Find on Page, then Picture in Picture when there's video or Cast…); an Extensions grid of every extension the window runs (click: its action or popup, as the toolbar; right-click: Pin to Toolbar / Unpin from Toolbar, Options, Turn Off, Remove, Manage; the same pin state as Settings › Extensions, and the toolbar follows at once) with a + tile (the Web Store; right-click: Add, Pin Extensions…, Manage) and a Manage link on the section header, as Arc's, which closes the panel and opens Settings › Extensions on the window's profile (#891: the only way in was right-clicking the +; watch `usage_counts.extensions_manage_opens_from_site_controls`). The grid shares the action row's columns (2026-10-21, a Discord user's screenshot: six extensions sat three to a row and the + alone below, narrower than the tiles above): rows of 4 equal cells (3 when the action row has 3; 1 or 2 action tiles span whole cells), the + in the next free cell, every row the action row's widths and gaps (58 pt cells, 8 pt gaps, measured at 0, 1, 3, 4, 6, 7, 10 and 12 extensions). The cause was the panel's scroll view reserving a legacy scroller's width when a mouse is connected, which squeezed the content 15 pt and wrapped fixed-width tiles; panels from the address field now hide that scroller, so their right inset matches the left; a Settings list with ad blocking and the permissions set for the site or in use (camera, mic, sound), each a round badge, accent when allowed, and its value; a footer with the connection chip (Secure / Not Secure / Local Page: the site, connection and certificate) and ⋯ (zoom, Cast, translate, the other permissions, clear cookies & site data, full URL). It opens in 150 ms and closes in 100 ms back to its anchor, by Escape, a click outside, the button or an action. Dia's own is a native menu (its 1.50 binary: connection status, content blocking, Copy Link, Share, an Extensions submenu with the pinned ones, Add and Manage Extensions, translate, Site Settings, Clear Cookies, Clear Cache, Advanced settings). Share… opens the system share sheet pointing at its tile, with the page's clean link and title; the popover closes with the sheet. Its content sits on one 16 pt inset on every side (2026-10-11, the owner: the left inset matched the top's), the grid every panel from the address field and toolbar now shares (`PANEL`, docs/dia-spec.md › "Panels from the address field"): the site prompts, zoom, history, Cast, screen sharing, the device chooser and downloads. Closes on a click anywhere outside it (sidebar, toolbar, page) and on Esc, as Zoom and blocked pop-ups do (2026-11-08, a tester's report): every dismissable `Popover` sits in an `OutsidePressArea`, Esc is held while the focused window has one open (`state/tabs/popoverDismiss.ts`), and the click on its own button closes it without reopening; watch `usage_counts.popover_press_closes` and `popover_escape_closes`. Autoplay is in its permissions (2026-11-14, a Discord user had to find it in Settings › Privacy): Allowed or Blocked, set per site, and the next load of the page uses it. Settings › Privacy & Security › Site permissions › Block autoplay on every site (off by default, issue #501, 2026-11-15) blocks it on every site that has no Autoplay setting of its own: a site set to Allow still autoplays, a click still plays, Site Controls shows Blocked for a site that follows the switch and lists Autoplay among its main rows while the switch is on, a site's sheet in Settings reads Default (block), and it applies to pages opened or reloaded after the switch changes; Chromium tabs only, WebKit tabs still autoplay (not built yet); watch `setting_blockAutoplayEverywhere` in `settings_snapshot`. Blocked means media plays only after a click or key press inside that page, or Ardia's own play controls: a tab ⌘-clicked open from the same site no longer counts, though Chrome hands it the click's user activation, so YouTube videos opened in background tabs stay paused when shown, and `play()` is refused with Chrome's own NotAllowedError, so not a frame plays; watch `usage_counts.site_autoplay_blocks` and `site_autoplay_allows`. ⋯ › Site Settings… (Dia's menu has the same item; 2026-11-14, the owner) opens Settings › Privacy & Security on that site's sheet with every permission Chrome keeps for it, in the tab's profile, also for a site that has nothing set yet and so isn't in the list; Chromium pages only; watch `usage_counts.site_settings_opens_from_site_controls` |
| Insecure‑site warning | ✓ | ✅ | lock-warning glyph in the URL field; Chrome's interstitial for certificate errors |

Dashboard: `calendar_connect` (`before`, `after`, `responsible`, `errored`, `settings_opened`, `ms`); a share of `before=notDetermined, after=notDetermined` presses is macOS refusing to ask, and `responsible=other` says why |

## 12. Bookmarks
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bookmark page ⌘D | ✓ | ✅ | save dialog with name + folder tree; "Edit Bookmark…" when already saved |
| Bookmark all tabs; add to folder; folder‑tree save dialog | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Bookmarks bar (Always / New Tab only / Never, ⇧⌘B) | ✓ | ✅ | Also in Settings › Appearance › Bookmarks Bar. A bookmark with an empty title is an icon-only chip, as in Chrome and Dia (issue #1160), a square as wide as the bar's items are tall (24 pt, 30 pt on a Large bar) with the icon centred, so neighbours don't touch and the hover plate is a square (issue #1366); its tooltip and accessibility label give the URL. Folder menus are our own popovers (`components/bookmarks/FolderMenu.tsx`), so their rows show each page's favicon like the bar does (the native menu showed a globe), with cascading subfolders on hover, Open All and Add to Sidebar as Group at the foot, the bar's context menu on a row, and the menu-bar habit of moving to another bar folder on hover; Escape or a click elsewhere closes them (issue #571). A big bar stays quick: the bar mounts only the items that start inside it (2,500 imported bookmarks mount about a dozen), item widths are remembered across tab switches, and the overflow menu draws only the rows in view, so Always mode with thousands of bookmarks no longer freezes or crashes (issue #672; `state/bookmarks/barFit.test.mjs`) |
| Bookmarks bar drag and drop | ✓ | ✅ | Reorder on the bar; drop on a folder to file it inside, or hold over it and its menu springs open to drop at a row; drag out of a menu onto the bar or into another folder's menu; drop a sidebar tab or a link from a page to bookmark it at that spot. Items slide apart for the gap (the top strip's reorder spring), an accent line marks it, a folder about to take the drop gets an accent ring, and a folder can't go inside itself. Every move is the store's `moveBookmark`, so Chrome's tree, sync and `ardia://bookmarks` agree (`state/bookmarks/barDrop.test.mjs`; issue #571) |
| Bookmarks bar size | — | ✅ | Ours (issue #571): Default is Dia's (30 pt bar, 24 pt items, 14 pt favicons, 12 pt text); Large is a 38 pt bar with 30 pt items, 16 pt favicons, 13 pt text, 8 pt corners, roomier padding and 2 pt between items, and 32 pt folder-menu rows. Settings › Appearance › Bookmarks Bar › Size, or the bar's context menu; synced |
| Hide bookmarks (an Arc user's ask) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-07, #968, with #518. Settings › Appearance › Bookmarks › Show bookmarks (on by default, synced, in config files). Off hides the address bar's bookmark button (Copy URL takes its place, ⇧⌘C's clean link and toast; also #518), the bookmarks bar whatever its mode, the Bookmarks menu, Tabs › Add to Bookmarks and Add Bookmark to Folder, View › Show Bookmarks Bar, the tab, several-tab, group and sidebar menus' bookmark items, the hover card's Bookmark button, the command bar's bookmark actions and bookmark suggestions (history stays, without the bookmark boost), and the bar's settings rows; ⌘D, ⇧⌘D, ⌥⌘B and ⇧⌘B do nothing (the commands are disabled). Bookmarks stay in Chrome's tree and sync; import and Export Bookmarks still work; turning it back on brings every surface back (`state/bookmarks/visibility.ts`) |
| Bookmark manager (⌥⌘B, `dia://bookmarks`) | ✓ | ✅ | `ardia://bookmarks` (our page), over Chrome's own bookmarks, so extensions' `chrome.bookmarks` sees the same tree (Findings 10). 2026-11-07, issue #877: as in Chrome's manager, click a row and ⌘A (or Edit › Select All) selects every item in the open folder or search results, ⌫ or Delete removes them all at once, and the "N items deleted" toast's Undo puts each back in its place (8 s); ⌘A in the search field still selects its text. The bookmarks bar has no focusable list, so this lives here. The list renders only the rows near the view, so a folder of 2,400 opens at about 470 MB where it used to run past 1.8 GB and crash, and a select-all delete of 2,400 takes 37 ms from the key to the store 2026-11-14, #1376 (based on #1377): dragging a selection moves it in one store update (`moveBookmarks`), in the order shown, and Chrome is sent one move per bookmark that changed place and nothing for the rows that stayed (`bookmarkOps` keeps the longest run already in order); on a 32,000-bookmark folder, moving 100 took 3.5 s of CPU in the store and 5.1 s in the diff, now 22 ms and 64 ms. Watch `usage_counts.bookmark_moves`, `bookmarks_moved` and `bookmark_sync_diff_ms_max` (the slowest diff of the hour). Search keeps each bookmark's lowercased text between keystrokes and walks the tree once (32,000 bookmarks: 35–50 ms a keystroke, now 15–19 ms). |
| Bookmarks menu + Recent Bookmarks | ✓ | ✅ | |
| ⌘/middle‑click bg, ⌥‑click split, open folder | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Bookmarks in command bar | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Bulk undo | ✓ | ✅ | undo toast in the manager |
| Bookmark folder → sidebar group | — (Arc ✓) | ✅ | Add to Sidebar as Group on a folder (bookmarks bar, `ardia://bookmarks`, the Bookmarks menu's folders), or drag a folder from the bar onto the sidebar: a pinned group of the folder's links, subfolders that hold links as nested groups, pinned tabs that load when clicked (empty folders and links that aren't web pages or files are left out); the bookmarks stay. A page already pinned in the profile (a row, a tile, in a pinned group, or parked with a closed window) isn't added again, so the same folder again, or a re-import after renaming its group, adds nothing; a folder takes up to 5,000 links at a time and a toast (or the import summary) says how many were left out. Restore Arc Folders as Groups takes out of the bookmarks only the links that became tabs, and a folder only when all of it did; its groups, nested ones included, come in closed (Add to Sidebar as Group on one folder opens it). The item is off for a folder with nothing to add |

## 13. History
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| History recorded | ✓ | ✅ | per profile, never for incognito; the engine keeps all of it (no 90-day expiry: ArdiaCore sets Helium's `keep-old-history` switch, so an imported Arc history keeps its old pages) and the app lists the newest 100 000 pages (issue #766). Watch `perf_launch.history_oldest` (`<1y`, `<3y`, `3y+` mean history past 90 days survived) and `perf_launch.history` (`<100k`, `100k+`) against `usable_ms` |
| History page (⌘Y, `dia://history`) | ✓ | ✅ | `ardia://history` (our page, also when Chrome opens chrome://history): by day, search, bulk delete. ⌘Y puts the cursor in the search field, as Chrome's history page does, also when History is already showing; keys typed right after ⌘Y land in it (the menu holds them, as for ⌘F). Watch `usage_counts.history_search_focused` against `history_search_focus_requests`: a gap means the field stopped taking the focus. Rows select from the keyboard, as in Chrome's history (issue #1390, 2026-11-15): ↓ from the search field enters the list, ↑ ↓ move the highlighted row (scrolled into view), ⇧↑ ⇧↓ select from where they started, Space toggles the highlighted row, ⌘A selects every shown row, Delete or ⌫ asks and removes, Esc clears; ⇧-click on a checkbox selects the range, as on a row. Watch `usage_counts.history_removals_by_key` beside `_by_button` and `_by_menu`. |
| History menu | ✓ | ✅ | Show History, Clear Browsing Data, Recently Closed, Recently Closed Groups |
| Clear browsing data | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's BrowsingDataRemover for the chosen range: Chrome's history database (what `chrome.history` shows), cookies and every kind of site storage, cached files, live; WebKit's site data and cache too, and the dialog waits for both. Once that succeeds, our history list drops the visits in the range (up to when Clear was pressed) and the profile's favicons are pruned, an incognito window's included. Successive range clears keep the correct latest visit date. If Chrome reports a kind it couldn't clear, the dialog stays open with an error and Retry (issue #1800; native API 71). Cancel, Esc and the backdrop still leave while a clear runs, and Return runs the clear as the button does. Form data and passwords stay, as in the dialog's two options. Watch `usage_counts.browsing_data_clear_failures` against `browsing_data_clears` |
| Synced devices' tabs | ✓ | ✅ | each device publishes its 30 most recent open tabs per profile (not pinned tabs, which sync as pinned tabs); the others list them in the overflow menu, and a device that stops syncing takes its record away (`docs/sync.md`). Verified with two and three hidden instances (2026-09-26). A tab's menu sends it to another device (Send to <device>, to an iPhone or a Mac on this build): it opens there once, in the background, with a toast, and the record is deleted (`send:` records; unit tests 2026-10-25; left for a person: a real Mac and iPhone). The phone app (`apps/phone`, Android and iPhone, 2026-11-14) sends its page to a Mac from the page menu with a "Sent to …" toast, pins it to the Mac, and opens tabs a Mac sends it, on the same records (`docs/phone.md` › Sync); left for a person: a real Mac with each phone |

## 14. Downloads
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Downloads panel (⇧⌘J, toolbar button) | ✓ | ✅ | our popover; Chrome's download bubble is off per profile (ledger 25). Since 2026-10-24 (#221) it hangs from its Downloads button as Dia's `DownloadsMenuPopover` (an NSPopover) does: centred on the button where the window allows (8 pt margin), an arrow on the button's centre, below the sidebar header or top strip button and above the sidebar footer one; the list shrinks to the room left. Its background is one translucent bubble, arrow included (`PopoverBubble`: AppKit's popover material blending the page behind, masked to the body-and-arrow shape, 0.5 pt edge, shadow outside only); the near-opaque plate that hid the blur is gone. The button stays pressed and drops its tooltip while open, and the top strip's peek holds while it is. With no visible button (sidebar hidden, Little Ardia) it opens at the old fixed spot without an arrow. Not measured against Dia: arrow size (20 × 9) and corner radius (Ardia's 12). Since #248 a download Chromium holds as insecure (an http download from an http page) shows a red warning ("Blocked: not secure" in the panel, "Insecure download blocked" on the Downloads page) with Keep (`ac_downloads_keep`, finishes it) and Discard (cancel), also in the row's context menu; before, it sat as an empty progress bar forever. Checked in a test build. Dangerous file types are not warned: the engine is built with `safe_browsing_mode=0` (#249), so .command, .pkg, .jar, .terminal, .dmg and .inetloc all finish with `dangerous: false`; the file-type warning ("This type of file can harm your computer") is wired but never shows on our build. Known gap: an http download started from an https page is cancelled silently by Chrome's `SILENT_BLOCK` (`insecure_download_blocking.cc`), with no hold and no row. A new download opens the panel, as it did before; this is a deliberate difference from Dia, whose `DownloadsMenuPopover` opens only from its button (the owner prefers the panel). Since 2026-11-14 (#1610) a press outside it closes it like any popover and goes on to what it landed on (an `OutsidePressArea`; a full-window backdrop used to swallow it, so each further file on a page took two clicks), and a press on the Downloads button while it's open closes it without reopening it on the release, however long it is held. That press is matched by its own mouse-down time (the outside press's `nativeEvent.timestamp` against the button's responder-start capture), so a separate click on the button right after a click elsewhere closed the panel opens it at once (#1867: for 600 ms such a click did nothing). Leaving and re-entering the button during a press keeps its first timestamp. No new counter: `downloads_panel_press_closes` already counts the closes, and the fix only stops the next click being swallowed. Watch `usage_counts.downloads_panel_press_closes` (clicks that closed it and went through) and `downloads_panel_held_opens`. On Linux (#2155), presses inside the panel pass through its dismissal handler, an outside press closes it without reaching what it landed on (the whole press, however long it is held and with any button), and while a row's menu is open Escape and presses belong to the menu. With sharing on, watch `usage_counts.linux_downloads_panel_inside_presses` against panel opens for regressions. |
| Progress, cancel, open, Show in Finder, clear | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Pause / resume | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Open when done; move to Trash | ✓ | ✅ | "Open When Done" isn't remembered across a relaunch |
| Persist downloads list across launches | ✓ | ✅ | downloads.json; incognito downloads never written (fixed 2026-10-06: they were, and showed in regular windows; ones an older build saved are dropped at launch). A window lists its profile's downloads, as Chrome's per-profile list (a private window its session's); before 2026-10-06 every regular window listed every profile's. Since 2026-11-14 a running download's bytes, size and speed live in their own small store (`state/downloads/progress.ts`), read by the rows that show them, so progress alone wakes no workspace subscriber and no longer rewrites downloads.json (once a second before, the whole history each time); a start, pause, resume, Keep, move, rename or end still saves, with the bytes so far. Based on #1396. Watch `usage_counts.download_history_writes` and `download_history_kb_written` against `download_progress_updates`: writes should stay a handful per download, and a regression brings them back up to about one per two progress updates |
| "Magnet" animation toward sidebar | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Drag file out of the panel | ✓ | ✅ | A finished download drags as its file into Finder, other apps, the page, or the tab list (2026-10-21) |
| Drop files and links onto the tab list | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-10-21, a Discord report. Files (PDF, image, HTML, text…) from Finder, the Downloads panel or any app that drags plain file URLs, and http(s) links from other browsers, apps or dragged text open as new tabs where they're dropped, behind the same drop gap a tab drag opens: between rows, at the top or end, into an open group, onto the pinned tiles (pinned), or into the top strip's incoming gap. Several open in order, the first selected. A .webloc/.url opens its target only if it's http(s); javascript:, data: and file: links from text or URL types never open. The page keeps Chrome's own drop. `FileDropView` (packages/shell FileDropView.swift), layout/fileDrop.tsx, sidebar/dnd.tsx external drags; `tab-drag-test.mjs` (12 file-drop cases through AppKit's NSDraggingDestination path with a DEV dragging info). Private windows take drops too. Known gap: drags that only offer file promises (some Mail attachments) aren't accepted. Left for a person: a real drag from Finder and Safari, and the drag image by eye |
| `dia://downloads` page | ✓ | ✅ | `ardia://downloads` |
| Downloads folder, ask where to save each file | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › General › Downloads (issue #95, 2026-10-24): "Downloads folder" (Change… opens a folder panel) and "Ask where to save each file", per profile as in Chrome (`download.default_directory`, also Save Page As's `savefile.default_directory`, and `download.prompt_for_download`, through `ac_downloads_settings` / `ac_downloads_set_settings`); with several profiles a pop-up picks the profile and "Use for All Profiles" copies both to every other one. Profiles that share data share these too. A folder set by policy can't be changed. New profiles start at Chrome's default, ~/Downloads |

## 15. Extensions
Chrome's own extension system: every window's tabs are a real Chrome tab strip, so `chrome.tabs` / `chrome.windows`,
actions, commands and the Web Store are Chrome's (ledger items 1–14, 22, W1–W3).

| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chrome Web Store MV3 extensions | ✓ | ✅ | installed by Chrome's WebstoreInstaller (location FROM_STORE, clients2 update URL), per profile; verified with Bitwarden and Dark Reader (W1). Auto-update verified end to end (W3): our build had
  never checked for updates (ungoogled's block-requests stub; fixed by `chromium-extension-updates.patch`). Up to 0.2.39 the store asked "Do you want to start using Chrome?" and showed a "Switch to Chrome" banner (#144). Since 0.2.40 Helium's `extstore_fixups` component extension (content scripts on chromewebstore.google.com, allowlisted, not restricted by `chromium-component-extension-restricted-urls.patch`) forces the store's Chrome eligibility check and removes both, its download button says Ardia, and client hints carry the Google Chrome brand (`spoof-chrome-ua-brand`); not yet checked on the live store |
| Web Store "Add" button | ✓ | ✅ | the store's own button runs Chrome's install flow; `CEF_AC_INSTALL_PROMPT` hands its confirmation to our Dia-style dialog with Chrome's warning list; buttons relabelled "Add to / Remove from Ardia" (state/extensions/bridge.ts). Every store install goes this way (our own CRX download path is gone, so there's no launch race, Findings 9); a prompt Chrome still waits on is asked again after a JS reload Since 2026-11-10 an install no longer fails with "Image decode failed" when the listing's icon can't be fetched (#1135: Chrome fetched the store's lh3.googleusercontent.com icon before the prompt and ended the install on any DNS, block, Local Network Access, HTTP or decode failure); the prompt shows the default icon instead (`chromium-webstore-icon-optional.patch`). Watch `usage_counts.extension_install_prompts` against `extension_installed`. Since 2026-11-16 Settings › Extensions › Open Web Store, Add from a link, and an extension's Options and Chrome Web Store buttons open in a window showing the profile the pane shows, or a new window of it, instead of the window in front, so an install meant for a second profile no longer lands in the first one (FB-M5K5, Linux: 1Password went to Personal from Aurum's pane and every retry said "already installed"). Watch `usage_counts.extension_profile_page_reroutes` against `extension_profile_page_opens`: each reroute is a page that used to open in the wrong profile. Since 2026-11-17 Add and Cancel answer the prompt in the profile that asked: on Linux, a profile paged into another profile's window (the sidebar's profile switch) got "This install request has closed" on Add and nothing installed, because the answer went out under the default profile and the engine refused it. Watch `usage_counts.extension_install_prompts_stale` against `extension_install_prompts`: it should stay near zero. |
| Extensions menu, pin extensions, toolbar buttons with badges and popups | ✓ | ✅ | As Dia 1.52 (owner's recording, 2026-10-09; `dia-spec.md` › Toolbar): only pinned extensions show, 16 pt icons on a 30 pt pitch (Dia 30.25; was 28), with no puzzle-piece button (it showed whenever an extension was unpinned; the menu bar's Extensions menu has them all, as Dia's). The overflow button shows only when the pinned ones don't fit: in the sidebar's address field, as many as leave 84 pt for the host (two at 190 pt). Extensions menu (installed ones, Add Extension…, Manage Extensions…, Pin Extensions…; store extensions' popups open from it, as unpacked ones' do: from 2026-09-29 to 09-30 they didn't); badge, title, popup, enabled state and `action.setIcon` images read straight from Chrome's `ExtensionAction` for the tab every 1.5 s and on tab switches (`CefGetExtensionActionState`; Chrome has no change event). Since 2026-10-05 a popup sizes itself as Chrome's does (the page's auto-resize, 25×25 to 800×600, `ac_extension_view.mm`): it shrinks with its page as well as grows (it only ever grew while the app measured the page: KeePassXC-Browser's stayed 318 pt tall after its content dropped to 167), and `chrome.action.openPopup()` / `browserAction.openPopup()` open it over the active tab, afresh over its own (they failed with "Browser window has no toolbar": 1Password reopens its popup this way once its Mac app unlocks). Verified headless: acceptance `extension-popup-and-panel` (264×114 → 264×57, openPopup "ok"), a probe in normal and private windows, uBlock Origin Lite, KeePassXC-Browser, 1Password's popup page (600×450). Private windows (#909, 2026-11-10): a private window shows every turned-on extension allowed in incognito, as Chrome's toolbar does; it showed only the `"incognito": "split"` ones, so nearly every allowed extension was missing. Chrome picks where a popup or side panel runs (`ExtensionViewHostFactory`): a split extension's in the private session, a spanning one's in its regular profile, over the private window's tab. Options of a spanning extension open in the profile's regular window (a new one if none is open), as Chrome's `OpenOptionsPage`, since its pages can't load in incognito. Acceptance `extension-private-window`; `state/extensions/extensions.test.mjs`. Watch `usage_counts.private_window_extension_opens` on the dashboard. Reordering (2026-11-14, a tester's ask): drag a pinned extension's button sideways (past 4 pt) and the others slide aside on the tab strip's reorder spring; dropping moves it there, and the order is Chrome's own (`ToolbarActionsModel::MovePinnedAction`, the pinned-extensions pref), so it holds across relaunches and profiles' windows. The engine reports each button's place (`pinnedIndex`), a newly pinned one goes last, and a private window can't reorder (Chrome refuses it in incognito). A plain click still opens the popup. It works the same in the sidebar's address field (2026-11-15), and when that field is too narrow for every pinned extension, the extensions menu's Move to Front submenu lists the ones that didn't fit and puts the chosen one first. `state/extensions/toolbarOrder.test.mjs`. Watch `usage_counts.toolbar_extension_moves` and `toolbar_extension_menu_moves`. A popup opened without a click on its button (`openPopup()`, the extension's shortcut, the menu) measures that button when it opens, waiting at most 100 ms (2026-11-15, from #1998): it hung from the button's last laid-out place, so once an extension side panel narrowed the toolbar it landed over the panel. Watch `usage_counts.toolbar_anchor_moves` (the button had moved) and `toolbar_anchor_misses` (it didn't answer in time). Toolbar buttons refresh only when a window's active tab changes or navigates, and only for that window's profile (2026-11-17, #2182; before, any tab update, a title or a loading tick in a background tab, asked every window again); watch `usage_counts.extension_action_refreshes` and `extension_action_state_requests` per hour, which should drop, with `extension_action_tab_changes_skipped` counting the tab updates that no longer refresh Since 2026-11-17 on Linux, a profile paged into another profile's window gets its extensions' per-tab icons and badges and their side panels: these asked under the default profile, and the engine refused the paged-in profile's tab. They now ask in the tab's profile. Watch `usage_counts.extension_action_state_failures` against `extension_action_state_requests`. |
| Manage Extensions (`dia://extensions`) | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › Extensions (on/off, details, site access, incognito, pin, reload, remove, add by link, load unpacked); `ardia://extensions` is Chrome's own page (developer mode, errors, shortcuts). It opens on the profile of the window it was opened from (Manage Extensions… in a window's menus, Site Controls' Manage link, or the last browser window used), not always the default profile, so a pin or switch there changes the extensions that window runs (#144: pinning in the default profile's list showed nothing in another profile's window). An extension's page is a place in Settings' history, so Back returns to the list (#144). A `.crx` file dropped anywhere in a regular window installs through Chrome's CrxInstaller with the usual confirmation, and a failed install says why in a toast (#560, #819): a page, chrome://extensions with developer mode on or off (`ac_extension_drop.mm`), the sidebar, the top tab strip, and since 2026-11-07 the New Tab page, the top layout's toolbar, History, Bookmarks and Downloads, which took no drop at all (`components/extensions/CrxDropArea.tsx` around the window, a `FileDropArea` that takes only drags of `.crx` files, so every other drop there is still refused). Any CRX3 file installs, self-signed or the Web Store's: off-store installs go through the settings page's path, which needs no publisher proof, so there's no CRX_REQUIRED_PROOF_MISSING (Chrome only takes one dropped on chrome://extensions in developer mode, and a downloaded one fails with that error). A private window refuses them, as Chrome does |
| Install/uninstall permission dialogs | ✓ | ✅ | install, re-enable and new-permission prompts through our dialog (verified, W1); removal asks with our "Remove “…”?" sheet from Settings, the toolbar menu and the store page, then Chrome uninstalls (engine verified, W2). The sheet itself needs a key window: checklist step 8 |
| chrome.tabs / chrome.windows | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's real tabs and windows: one Chrome window per app window and profile, sidebar order, active/pinned both ways, `tabs.create` / `windows.create` / popups adopted as our tabs, moves between windows and profiles (ledger 1–14); sleeping tabs are `discarded: true`, and `tabs.discard` / `tabs.reload` sleep and wake them in the app. Since 2026-10-01 (rec. 1, `docs/store-api.md` › "Live tabs") every change an extension makes reaches the sidebar: `tabs.move` (also to another window, also one holding only New Tab pages), `update` pinned / active, `create` with index and `active: false`, `remove`, a remove and an activation in one go (the extension's tab wins over the opener rule), an activation followed by a discard of the tab that was shown (Auto Tab Discard's Discard Current Tab, #634: the window moves to the other tab, whatever the last close left in `activePickedOnClose`, also when the other tab is in another space that shares data, where the window switches to that space; the discarded tab sleeps in the sidebar), and `chrome.tabGroups` both ways (`tabs.group` / `ungroup`, `tabGroups.update` / `move`; sidebar groups, renames, colours and collapsing show in `chrome.tabGroups`). As in Dia: collapsing is mirrored both ways (Dia's engine reports and takes `collapsed`: `ArcTabGroupInfo` / `ArcTabGroupProperties`), and the page on screen stays (Chrome's model doesn't switch away from a collapsed group's active tab; only its tab strip view would); a group with no colour is grey for extensions (`ArcTabGroupColorGrey` = 0 is Dia's default). An extension pinning one pane of a split leaves the split and the pane unpinned: Dia's engine has no way to tell the app about a pin (no pin or move callback in ArcCore's delegates). Verified headless with a probe extension over CDP. Fixed 2026-10-16: a new window Chrome makes (`windows.create` of type normal, with one URL, several, none or `tabId`; CDP `Target.createTarget {newWindow}`) opened no window: its tab moved into the window in front and the window id the extension got back closed at once. Now it's a real Ardia window, the kind ⌘N makes, in that profile, holding Chrome's own window (same id, no detach/attach/onRemoved), its tabs in its sidebar; `focused: false` opens it behind; `windows.remove` closes it in the app. `type: "popup"` stays Chrome's own popup window, `incognito: true` goes to a private window of that profile, as before. Not followed: `left`/`top`/`width`/`height` and `state` (Ardia places it as ⌘N does). Verified headless: acceptance `chrome-windows-create` (a new app window whose strip is the id returned). The activate-then-discard case (2026-11-08, GitHub issue #634) is verified in the store's unit test only (`state/tabs/liveTabs.test.mjs`: a strip still carrying an earlier close's flag, then the activation, shows the other tab and Chrome isn't put back on the discarded one; it fails without the fix). Left for a person: Auto Tab Discard's Discard Current Tab on a regular tab, a pinned tab and a Top App in a hidden instance, each ending on another loaded tab with the discarded one asleep in the sidebar and reloading when clicked |
| `chrome.identity` sign-in (`launchWebAuthFlow`: Stylus's Google Drive, Dropbox and OneDrive sync) | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-06, GitHub issue #757. The sign-in page opens in its own popup window (the one `window.open` popups use, with its origin strip), centred over the front window, and the redirect URL reaches the extension; closing that window rejects with Chrome's "The user did not approve access."; a non-interactive flow resolves or fails with "User interaction required." without a window. Every popup Browser Chrome makes itself gets that window, shown behind when Chrome asks for it inactive; an extension's `windows.create({ type: "popup" })` is an app popup and keeps its old path. Fixed: the sign-in page was handed to the front window as a tab, and when the flow ended Chrome closed the Browser holding it, the user's own, so their tabs and window went. `getAuthToken` (Chrome-account OAuth, no Google API keys) fails with an error, as in Helium. Verified headless: `packages/ardiacore/scripts/authflow-check.mjs` (silent, needs-the-user, allow, cancel, with a private window open, relaunch); Stylus 2.4.17 › Sync › Google Drive › Connect opens Google's sign-in page in that window (no account signed in) |
| action.onClicked (no popup), keyboard `commands`, extension context-menu items | ✓ | ✅ | onClicked + activeTab + `scripting.executeScript` verified (22); `chrome.commands` shortcuts: ArdiaCore's own `ExtensionKeybindingRegistry` per window (`ac_extension_shortcuts.mm`, 2026-10-16; with no BrowserView none was made, so none fired): named commands fire `onCommand` with the tab, `_execute_action` runs the action as a toolbar click does (popup, side panel or `onClicked`), suggested keys that clash with an Ardia shortcut aren't auto-assigned, a key the user sets wins over Ardia's own except the keys of its reserved commands (Helium's set: New Tab/Window, Close Tab/Window, Quit, the tab switcher, Copy URL, Developer Tools, Search Tabs…), also in Chrome's own DevTools, app and Document PiP windows (apply.sh's `g_ardia_reserved_accelerator` hooks), and chrome://extensions/shortcuts suspends them while recording; global commands are Chrome's (not exercised: a test can't post a system hot key); `chrome.contextMenus` items show in the page menu and their `onClicked` runs (R1) |
| Side‑panel API | ✓ | ✅ | Dia's extension side panel: a card to the right of the page (header with the extension's icon, name, ⋯ menu and close; resizable 320–600 pt, width saved) showing the panel page outside the tab strip. Opens from the toolbar button (`openPanelOnActionClick`), the extension's menu and `chrome.sidePanel.open()`, closes with `close()` or `window.close()`; follows per-tab `setOptions` paths and closes where it's disabled. `chrome.tabs.query({active, currentWindow})` from the panel (and from popups) returns the page. The panel's page takes keyboard focus once it loads after the user opens the panel (also when it reopens while the closing card is still on screen) or switches to another extension's panel, as popups do, so a field its script focuses receives typing with no click. That happens once per opening; a tab switch after the opening, before or after the panel loaded, leaves focus with the page. Verified in a hidden instance: a panel page that focuses its textarea has `document.hasFocus()` and receives typed keys (2026-11-07, GitHub issue #600) |
| Password manager apps (native messaging: 1Password, Bitwarden, KeePassXC, Proton Pass) | ✓ | 🧪 | `chrome.runtime.connectNative` is Chrome's; it reads host manifests from `<user data dir>/NativeMessagingHosts` and `/Library/Application Support/Chromium/NativeMessagingHosts`, where no password manager puts one. At launch (and when the app becomes active) `ArdiaNativeMessaging.mm` writes the manifests of the installed ones into ours, from the vendor's system-wide Chrome manifest if it ships one, else as the vendor's app writes it for Chrome (its `allowed_origins` unchanged), and removes them when the app is gone; manifests it didn't write are left alone. Apple Passwords is left out: its helper has a launch constraint that kills it when an unknown browser starts it. Helium also reads Chrome's host directories (`scan-chrome-native-messaging-hosts`, so `/Library/Google/Chrome/NativeMessagingHosts` too), where Apple's manifest is; `chromium-apple-passwords-host-skipped.patch` keeps it unfound, so the iCloud Passwords extension never starts the helper. The helper's parent is the main app process. 1Password's `1Password-BrowserSupport` checks that parent's signature and answers `BrowserVerificationFailed` (the extension logs "Disconnected from Desktop app due to UnknownBrowser" and keeps its own lock screen) until Ardia is added once in 1Password › Settings › Browser › Add Browser. Its sandbox reads apps only in /Applications, ~/Applications and /private/var/folders (its entitlements), and the same build run from ~/Documents fails earlier ("Failed to grab code signature … status 100001", errSecErrnoBase + EPERM) while from /private/var/folders it gets as far as waiting for trust; Add Browser also wants the app in Applications. So Ardia has to be moved there (and reopened) before adding it; Settings › Passwords says both, the second only when the app isn't in Applications (`systemInfo().inApplicationsFolder`). Verified 2026-10-01 against the real apps, signed out, in hidden instances: 1Password 8.12.38 (helper launched by the main executable; from ~/Documents EPERM, from a readable path the signature is read and it waits for trust; our running process satisfies a Developer ID + team U5L5T3NGVV + identifier requirement, dynamically and statically), KeePassXC 2.7.12 (key exchange done, database closed), Bitwarden 2026.9 (its optional nativeMessaging permission prompt shows in our install sheet and grants; `desktop_proxy` launched by the main executable, exits while the app is signed out), Proton Pass (prompt and grant; the host only runs once signed in). The same 1Password extension in Chrome for Testing 154 logs the same states. 2026-10-05, end to end with KeePassXC 2.7.12 on a scratch database (hidden instance, scratch config): connected and associated, logins listed for a 127.0.0.1 page and filled from the popup, the popup after a lock ("Database not opened"), and after the unlock (Reload → connected); 1Password after its Mac app unlocks calls `chrome.action.openPopup()`, which now opens. Needs the owner: 1Password, Bitwarden and Proton Pass unlocking their extensions with a signed-in app (a user saw 1Password's popup blank white after its unlock prompt on 0.2.25/26; not reproduced without a signed-in 1Password) |
| Per‑profile extensions | ✓ | ✅ | lists, pins and installs per engine profile; a private window runs the extensions of the profile it was opened from that are allowed in incognito. Sync carries a profile's Chrome Web Store extensions to its other Macs, as Chrome Sync does (2026-10-21; Settings › Sync › Extensions): one installed on one Mac installs on the others from the Web Store without a prompt, turned on only if the version and permissions are the ones approved on the first Mac (otherwise it waits for Chrome's prompt); on/off, private-window access and toolbar pin follow. Removing an extension on one Mac stops it installing on the others; it doesn't uninstall it there yet. Not synced: unpacked extensions, Ardia Scripts, bundled or policy extensions, themes, and extension data and settings (Chrome syncs some `storage.sync`) (`docs/sync.md` › Extensions) |
| Extensions in the Mac's language (`chrome.i18n`) | — | ✅ | 2026-10-28, GitHub issue #488. Chrome's own on the Mac, as Helium: the app declares Chromium's 55 desktop locales (`CFBundleLocalizations`, Helium's list) and the release keeps their `locale.pak`s, so Chrome's UI locale is the first of them the Mac's preferred languages (or the app's own language in System Settings) match, and extensions get it in `getUILanguage()`, `@@ui_locale` and `getMessage`, falling back through the parent language to `default_locale`. Regions stay apart (zh-Hans → zh_CN, zh-Hant → zh_TW, pt-BR / pt-PT). A language Chromium doesn't ship falls to the next preferred one, else English; a change applies at the next launch. Dia ships the same packs but declares no localizations, so it and its extensions run in English. Ardia's own interface stays English. A new profile's website languages come from the UI locale (Chrome's `IDS_ACCEPT_LANGUAGES`, reduced as Helium does); an existing profile keeps its own |

## 16. Passwords & autofill
Chrome's password manager and autofill fill pages themselves; our Settings panes drive Chrome's own
`passwordsPrivate` / `autofillPrivate` APIs in hidden WebUI pages (ACPasswords.mm, ACAutofill.mm).

| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Save / update passwords, never for this site | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome captures; Chrome's bubble is replaced by our Dia-style prompt (`CEF_AC_PASSWORD_BUBBLE`), with Save / Update (username picker) / Never / Not Now; verified (17, 18, and 19's revisit fill on a fresh profile). Settings › Passwords per profile: list, search, reveal/edit/delete, "Never saved" list, CSV import, the Offer-to-save toggle, unlock through Chrome's own device check. WebKit pages (`WebKitLogins.swift`): a save or update offer is dropped when the login form comes back within 15 s of the submit, so a failed sign-in that shows the form again gets no offer, as Chromium's own rule does. Watch `webkit_autofill_save_offers` against `webkit_autofill_saves` (and `webkit_autofill_update_offers` against `webkit_autofill_updates`): offers far above saves would mean offers still come after failed sign-ins. `webkit_autofill_save_suppressed` counts offers dropped because the form came back within 15 s; compare it with `webkit_autofill_save_offers` |
| Filling saved logins in pages | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's dropdown under the focused field (ledger 19). Verified headless (2026-09-26, a throwaway profile): a login saved through the app's API shows in the dropdown ("Password for …", a window of the app under the field), ↓ ↵ fills both fields; Edit › AutoFill › Passwords… lists it; a new login submitted gets our save prompt (no Chrome bubble) and Save stores it. Left for a person: the dropdown by eye in a key window (checklist step 2) |
| Suggest strong password on sign-up | ✓ (Chromium) | ✅ | Chrome's generation popup on new-password fields ("Use Strong Password", "Choose Your Own"; "Suggest Strong Password…" in the dropdown where logins are saved). It was never offered before 0.2.3: Chrome offers it only to people who sync passwords (`chromium-password-generation-local.patch`); its text now says passwords are saved on this device, not to a Google account. Verified headless, Debug and Release (2026-09-26): the popup, both password fields filled with the generated one, which is saved to the local store at once (a login page fills it back). The generated-password confirmation stays Chrome's bubble. Left for a person: the popup by eye (checklist step 3) |
| Passkeys / WebAuthn (iCloud Keychain) | ✓ | 🟡 | Chrome's WebAuthn stack and dialogs, centred over the page and in front of it (they are child windows of the app window, which is Chrome's own; 0.1.1 drew them behind the window, so passkey sign-in looked stuck).
