Verified in a Developer ID build: security keys and phone (QR sheet) ✅, a real phone scan is checklist step 7; Touch ID "Chrome profile" passkeys ✅ (register and sign-in on webauthn.io with Touch ID), Google sign-in is checklist step 6; Cancel rejects the request so sites fall back. iCloud Keychain: Apple granted `com.apple.developer.web-browser.public-key-credential`; `release.sh` signs with it when the provisioning profile carries it, and Chrome and WebKit then use macOS's passkeys. Left for a person: an iCloud Keychain passkey sign-in in a Developer ID build. WebKit pages: in a build without the entitlement (a local one) `PublicKeyCredential` is hidden and WebAuthn calls answer `NotSupportedError`, so sites fall back to passwords; with it WebKit runs WebAuthn through macOS, and Settings › Advanced › Web engine › How engines differ › Passkeys shows the status and asks for access (Allow Passkeys…, or System Settings when it's off) |
| Address & credit‑card autofill | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's autofill (save bubble + dropdown, ledger 21); on ArdiaCore Chrome's offers to save an address, update one ("Add new info to saved address") or save a card show as our prompt at the page's top right, styled as the password prompt and in Chrome's words, answered through Chrome's own controllers (`ac_autofill_prompt.mm`; 0.2.22 had lost them: the address offer never found a window without a BrowserView, the card offer never found the bubble handler). Switching tabs closes a pending offer, as Chrome's does; switching profiles keeps it (acceptance `autofill-save-prompts`, 2026-10-02); Settings › Autofill lists, adds, edits and deletes addresses and cards (card number behind Touch ID). Verified headless (2026-09-26, test data in a throwaway profile, cards on a local HTTPS page): an address fills all eight fields; a new one gets Chrome's "Save address?" bubble at the page's top right, inside the page area; a card fills name, number and expiry; a new card gets "Save card?". Found and fixed: focusing a card field with a saved card crashed the app (fixed in 0.2.3, `chromium-autofill-card-touchbar.patch`). Chrome fills cards on secure pages only. Left for a person: the dropdown and bubbles by eye (checklist step 4). WebKit pages (2026-11-14, `WebKitAutofill*.swift`): the same suggestions from Chromium's store in a dropdown under the field, fields found as Chrome does (autocomplete tokens and sections first, then name/label heuristics with its three-field minimum), the whole section filled on a pick, card numbers only after ac_autofill_card_number's Touch ID, cards on secure pages only (Chrome's insecure notice otherwise), never in a cross-origin iframe (origin from WKFrameInfo), and Chrome's "Save card?", "Save address?" and "Update address?" offers after a submit (none in private windows). Verified side by side with a Chromium tab on the same pages: identical suggestions text, fills and offer payloads. Differs: no grey preview on hover, no :autofill highlight, strikes last the session only. Watch `webkit_autofill_address_fills`, `webkit_autofill_card_fills`, `webkit_autofill_card_reauths_refused` and the `_save_offers` and `_saves` pairs |
| Password manager plugins (macOS Passwords panel, verification codes) | ✓ | 🧪 | A plugin declares `passwordManager` (`docs/plugins.md` › passwordManager); Settings › Profiles › Passwords › Password manager picks Ardia, None or an enabled password-manager plugin per profile (profiles sharing browser data share one choice; private windows follow their profile). A profile using a `fill: "system"` plugin gets the key in login and code fields, which opens macOS's Passwords panel there, and Edit › AutoFill › Passwords… opens the same panel; Ardia's offer to save is off while a plugin or None is picked and comes back when Ardia is (`password-manager.json`, crash-safe both ways). A page's otpauth setup code offers "Set Up Verification Code in <app>?" through an opaque token. Turning on a built-in password-manager plugin moves profiles that never chose (the consent sheet says how many); turning it off withdraws the key at once. The Apple Passwords plugin ships from github.com/ardia-browser/plugins. Passkeys in iCloud Keychain come from Apple's browser passkey entitlement, not from the plugin: the plugin's page shows the same Passkeys row as Settings › Engines › Passkeys. 0.2.48 keeps 0.2.47's subsystem as it was (#3299; #3253's removal and its move-back migration never shipped in a stable). It costs nothing until it's used: with no password-manager plugin on and no profile on None or a plugin, it listens to no browser store and pushes nothing, and panes watch for a verification code only while a plugin that sets them up is on (`PLUGIN_CHROME.verificationCodes`). Dashboard: `password_manager_panel_opens_<badge\|menu>` against `…_refused_<badge\|menu>`, `verification_code_setups_failed` against `…_accepted`, `password_manager_record_failed` near zero, the daily `password_manager_profiles_<ardia\|none\|plugin>`, and `official_plugin_<onboarding\|settings>_installed_apple_passwords`. Needs the owner: the panel and Touch ID fill in a key window |
| Edit › AutoFill menu (Contact, Passwords, Credit Card) | ✓ | ✅ | like Chrome's field menu, each item opens Chrome's dropdown at the page's focused form field (engine hook `CefShowAutofillSuggestions`, `CEF_AC_AUTOFILL_TRIGGER`): Passwords… lists the saved passwords on any text field (Chrome's "Select password" fallback), Contact… and Credit Card… the field's own suggestions (addresses or cards). With no form field focused, they open Settings on the window's profile (Passwords, or Autofill). Verified: a focused address field got Chrome's dropdown window right under it, a blurred page opened Settings; picking an entry is Chrome's own (checklist step 4) |
| Password reveal button | ✓ | ✅ | eye button in the page's password field once the user types (never for a filled saved password; sites with their own toggle keep theirs) in `packages/ardiacore/ios/page_script.js`, at the field's end: the right edge, or the left edge when the field is right-to-left, and reveal in Settings › Passwords. Not visually re-checked on Chrome tabs |
| Saved passwords in Settings (unlock, list, search) | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › Profiles › Passwords unlocks with the Mac's own authentication and lists the profile's logins. Fixed (#1727, 2026-11-17): a profile with thousands of logins built every row at once after unlocking, which froze Ardia for seconds (5,000 logins: the JS thread blocked about 20 s, the main thread up to 10 s); the list now shows 100 rows and adds 100 more each time you scroll near its end, and search still covers every login. Dashboard: hourly `password_list_shows`, `password_list_large_shows` (1,000 or more logins) and `password_list_slow_shows` (a second or more to appear); slow shows should stay near zero even as large shows come in |

## 17. Privacy & security
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Built‑in ad + tracker blocker (EasyList, EasyPrivacy), per‑site toggle | ✓ | ✅ | full uBlock Origin (MV2, as Helium bundles it) as a component extension in every profile, its private windows included (`incognito: spanning`, so one instance serves both); at launch the first pages race its start, as in Helium and in Chrome with uBO (it is ready about 0.35 s after the engine starts; requests from then on are blocked); profiles opened later, and the one launch that moves uBlock Origin Lite's settings, wait for it (`µBlock.isReadyPromise`, 10 s cap); per-site "disable on this site" is a trusted-site entry; blocked count from `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`. WebKit pages block too (#916): uBlock Origin Lite's Safari rulesets (`packages/ardiacore/scripts/ubol.sh`, pinned; `scripts/update-ubol.sh` follows uBOL releases, which is how uBOL itself updates its lists) are converted at build time into WebKit content rules (`ubol-webkit.py`: network blocks and exceptions, site-specific and generic element hiding) and compiled once per list choice off the main thread (about 4 s on a quiet Mac, cached in the data dir; a cached set loads in milliseconds). They follow uBlock Origin's on/off, list choices and trusted sites (Settings › Privacy, Site Controls), in every profile and private window, applied to open WebKit pages without a relaunch (`WebKitContentBlocker.swift`); Site Controls shows WebKit's blocked count. Since 2026-11-14 WebKit also gets Peter Lowe's, uBO's badware and the URLhaus lists (from uBOL's Chromium build of the same pinned release), uBO's strict blocking (a page whose host a list blocks shows "Page blocked" with Go back and Proceed, as uBO's document-blocked page; Proceed lasts until quit), uBOL's scriptlets (its own files, page and isolated worlds, all frames, off on trusted sites), procedural and entity (`example.*`) element hiding through uBOL's procedural engine and a per-frame lookup (`WebKitContentBlockerScripts.swift`), and uBO's My filters and element-picker rules, read from uBO through its dashboard messaging (network rules compile into the lists; cosmetic ones apply by script; `+js()` and `~host` exclusions are skipped). Not in WebKit: surrogate redirects (those scripts load, so ad scripts run their auctions and WebKit's blocked count runs higher than Chromium's), badware's scriptlets, response-header and `removeparam` filters, and a user `#@#` exception can't unhide a list's native element-hiding rule; WebKit matches `$domain` and first/third party against the top page, not the frame. Watch `usage_counts.webkit_blocker_strict_blocks`, `webkit_blocker_strict_proceeds`, `webkit_blocker_cosmetic_pages` and `webkit_blocker_user_filter_loads`. 2026-11-11 (a Discord report: Settings' blocker toggles slid on but never saved): every settings call waited for a pending filter-list reload inside its 20 s budget, so once a reload ran long (uBO recompiling after an update) every read and write timed out and Settings showed the fallback as "all off" with toggles that sent nothing. Calls no longer wait on a pending reload; a list change saves its selection, waits at most 4 s for the reload and reloads again if the choice changed meanwhile; other profiles copy list changes in the background. A blocker that doesn't answer shows "uBlock Origin isn't answering" with Try Again and disabled toggles instead of fake values. Verified with uBO's reload slowed to 25 s. Watch `usage_counts.content_blocker_unavailable` and `content_blocker_write_failures`. 2026-11-16: a tab a page opens onto a page uBlock Origin blocks (its "page blocked" screen) opens behind the page instead of in front; uBO's verdict is in before Ardia shows the tab (8–31 ms after Chrome makes it), so nothing waits. Watch `usage_counts.content_blocker_pages_behind` |
| Cookie‑banner blocking, regional lists | ✓ | ✅ | uBlock Origin's cookie, annoyance and regional lists, toggles in Privacy › Advanced Ad Block Settings, which lists every stock uBO list truthfully (ads, trackers, cookie banners, annoyances, malware and scams, regional; filter counts; "On by default") and says which uBO version blocks. "Block cookie banners" turns on EasyList's and uBO's cookie lists (uBO has none on by default). The regional lists for the user's languages are on from the first launch: Chrome's UI language follows macOS's preferred languages and a new profile's languages come from it (#488), and our uBO build matches lists against the UI language and every one of the profile's languages (upstream uses only the UI language; patched in `packages/ardiacore/scripts/ubo.sh`). Dia has no such default: its block-list manifest gives no list a `languages` value, so its regional lists are opt-in. Freshness: uBO updates its lists from the network on its own, every few days (not while blocking is off), from each list's own hosts; Dia refreshes its lists from its server between releases. uBO's own code moves with Ardia's releases (`scripts/update-ubo.sh`, step 3 of the release skill; the pin is `UBO_VERSION` in `packages/ardiacore/scripts/ubo.sh`), since Chrome never auto-updates a component extension. Settings from uBlock Origin Lite, which earlier versions used (blocking off, sites without blocking, list choices) move over on the first launch |
| Custom filter lists (Ardia extra) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-14. Settings › Privacy › Advanced… › Your lists: paste a list's address (http or https, Adblock Plus or uBlock Origin format) and Add. uBlock Origin imports it as its own "custom" list (`contentBlocker.addList`, `applyFilterListSelection` with `toImport`, `ArdiaContentBlocker.mm`), downloads it, takes its `! Title:`, and keeps it up to date with its other lists. Each added list has a checkbox, its filter count ("Downloading…" until it arrives) and a remove button (`contentBlocker.removeList`). An address that doesn't download is removed again with a note; the address of a list uBO already ships turns that stock list on. Every profile copies the default profile's added lists along with its other list choices, and removals reach them too. Chromium tabs only: WebKit tabs keep uBlock Origin Lite's bundled rulesets. Verified in a hidden instance: a local list's network rule and site-specific hiding rule apply, also in a second profile; a 404 address is reported as failed; turning the list off or removing it stops its rules in both profiles. Watch `usage_counts`: `content_blocker_custom_lists_added`, `content_blocker_custom_list_add_failures`, `content_blocker_custom_lists_removed` |
| Hide anything (Ardia extra, inspired by Search's Hide Element) | — | ✅ | View › Hide Element… (⇧⌘H), or Hide Element… in a page's right-click menu, starts a picker on the page: the element under the pointer is outlined with its tag and size, ↑ / ↓ widen to the parent or narrow back, click (or ↩) hides it, Esc cancels. The picker builds a short selector (a stable id, a `data-testid`-style attribute, its rarest stable classes, then a `>` path with `:nth-of-type`, generated class names skipped) and saves it as a uBlock Origin cosmetic filter `host##selector` in that profile's My filters (`contentBlocker.hideElements`, `ArdiaContentBlocker.mm`), so uBO applies it at document start on every later visit, subdomains included, with no flash and no engine change; `www.` is dropped from the host. In a private window the rule lives only in memory for the session and is applied by script as the page loads (it can flash), while the profile's saved rules still apply there (uBO spans private windows). View › Hidden Elements on This Site… (⇧⌘U) opens a panel under the site button listing them with Show (one) and Show All Again (reloads the tab, since uBO's injected CSS stays until then); Settings › Privacy › Hidden elements lists every site's, per profile, with Show Again. The context-menu item is uBO's own menu entry (`ubo.sh` patch 23) that asks the page script to start the picker at the right-click point. Chromium tabs only (off in WebKit tabs). Watch `usage_counts`: `elements_hidden`, `elements_hidden_private`, `hidden_elements_restored`, `element_picker_cancels` (a cancel rate near the hide rate means the picker is hard to aim), `element_picker_context_menu_starts`, `hidden_element_write_failures` (should stay 0). |
| Clear cookies / cache for site | ✓ | ✅ | Site Controls and Settings › Privacy › site permissions |
| Incognito | ✓ | ✅ | in-memory off-the-record profile of the profile the window was opened from, shared by that profile's private windows; favicons and downloads stay in them (WP5); uBOL blocks there too |
| Usage / content data sharing opt‑in | ✓ | — | no content or AI data sharing here. Ardia’s own opt-in is anonymous crash reports and usage stats (Mac: Settings › Privacy & Security; phone app since 2026-11-14: Settings and onboarding’s last step, off until chosen, with What’s Sent listing every field; docs/phone.md › Telemetry). Watch phone `usage_counts` and `perf_launch` under `PHONE_APP_SOURCE` |
| Certificate / connection info | ✓ | ✅ | Site Controls connection row with certificate details; Chrome's interstitials for certificate errors. 2026-10-21 (owner: chrome://components showed CRLSet 0.0.0.0, Update error): Chrome's security data updates again, as in Chrome: CRLSet (revoked certificates and blocked keys), PKI Metadata (Certificate Transparency logs, the Chrome Root Store, pins) and the SSL error assistant, through Google's component updater, which fetches only those three, over https, with no ping, cohort or install ids (`chromium-component-updater-security-only.patch`, docs/engine-build.md). Verified in a hidden instance: CRLSet 10851, PKI Metadata 1810 and the SSL error assistant 7 install within a minute; the net-log shows requests only to update.googleapis.com and edgedl.me.gvt1.com; the eMudhra test hosts still fail with ERR_CERT_REVOKED once PKI Metadata's newer root store (v42, which still trusts their root) is active; a Web Store extension still installs and its update check still runs |
| Enterprise MDM policies, managed‑browser banner, SSO | ✓ | — | |

## 18. Media & PiP
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Return to the browser, not the video PiP, on Cmd+Tab | ✓ | ✅ | When Ardia becomes active and the video PiP takes the key (it joins every Space), focus a browser window instead: one on the current Space if there is one, otherwise the last focused browser window, so macOS moves to its Space, full-screen or not. A key window that isn't the PiP keeps focus, and Back to Tab still focuses its source tab; the PiP stays visible and nonactivating over other apps. Fixed 2026-11-17 (#1772): the first version only handled a full-screen window and left Cmd+Tab on the PiP for a window on another desktop. Telemetry: `usage_counts.pip_activation_returns` and `pip_activation_return_failures`; watch failures. |
| Picture‑in‑Picture | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's video PiP window restyled to Dia's, measured (`docs/dia-spec.md` › Picture in Picture; `chrome/browser/ardia/pip/` (`engine/chromium`)): just the video at rest, with Dia's 6 pt rounded corners, 1 pt light rim and a shadow that follows the corners; on hover a 35 % scrim, back to tab and close as 28 pt rounded squares in the top corners, the origin centred in 13 pt, a bare pause glyph between bare ±10 s buttons (ours, Dia has none: issue #129, 2026-10-24; ← and → seek too) and a 5 pt progress bar (click or drag to seek); Chrome's previous/next track, time, mute, captions and minimize controls are gone. 2026-11-14, issue #1735 (ours, Dia has none): a plain click on the video's left side skips back 10 s and on its right side ahead 10 s, over the window's full height, with or without the controls showing; each click of a quick double or triple click skips again (3 clicks, 30 s). The centre column (40 pt either side, the play button's) does nothing, nor does a press that travels 3 pt or more, the release included (a drag moves the window as before), a stashed window, a Control-click or a resize edge; Chrome's own buttons and the progress bar keep their clicks. A double-click in the centre still goes to Chrome, which does nothing with it. Our drag monitor (`ArdiaPictureInPicture.mm`) asks every frame's page script to seek its Picture in Picture video (`seekPictureInPictureBy`: the page's Media Session `seekforward` or `seekbackward` handler, else its `seekto` handler with the new time on the timeline the page reported through `setPositionState`, or on the video's own time when it reported none; the reported position is kept current by what the video has played, paused or sought since the report, and for a video out of reach (a closed shadow root) by the clock at the reported rate, held while the page declares it paused and moved by each seek Ardia asks for; else that video's `currentTime`; the sidebar player's skip takes the same steps), so a paused video in the window is the one that moves even while another plays on the page; maths in `ArdiaPiPSeekMath.h` (`pnpm -C packages/ardiacore test`), end to end in `apps/browser/scripts/pip-click-seek-test.mjs`. Dashboard: `usage_counts.pip_click_skips_back` and `pip_click_skips_ahead`; both at zero while `pip_shown` holds means the clicks stopped arriving. Framing is measured by `pip-framing` (2026-11-12, WindowServer captures at 2x): the video meets the window's edge on every side with no band and all four corners are 6 pt, at rest, hovered, resized and after a stash. The controls don't fade out while the pointer rests on one of them, so a click on a still pointer always reaches the button (it was lost as a window drag after Chrome's 2.5 s idle fade). Shows at once; fades out in 100 ms from its close button and 70 ms when going back to the tab (smooth while the tab shows). Opens where it was last left and at that size, on the same display, across restarts and kept on screen; if that display is gone, at Chrome's default spot on the main display (bottom right). Fixed 2026-10-28 (#526): reopened from the same tab, Chrome reused that tab's hidden window and pulled it into the bottom right of the tab's display, so one left on a display to the right of or below it came back on the tab's. Floats over other apps' full-screen Spaces, as Safari's and Arc's do (2026-11-08, #386): macOS only puts a regular app's window on another app's full-screen Space when the window is a non-activating panel from the moment it is first shown, so a Chrome frameless window that Chrome makes floating on every Space (video and Document PiP) is marked while it is still hidden, through `setCollectionBehavior:` and `setLevel:`: it answers NSWindow's `-_isNonactivatingPanel` YES and gets FullScreenAuxiliary (`ArdiaPiPController+Workspaces.mm`). Marking it after it shows, Chrome's activation independence and CanJoinAllApplications all keep it off (measured with window-server Space membership; the last one blocks it outright). Checked by hand on macOS 27.2: over an app already in full screen and one that goes full screen while PiP is open. The insight came from PR #396 by @ymehili. Dashboard: `pip_shown` by `kind`, with `full_screen_spaces` false meaning a regression. The hover card and Site Controls toggle it. Its close button only closes the window, as in Dia and Arc: the video keeps playing in its tab, which stays in the background (`chromium-pip-close-keeps-playing.patch`). Back to Tab (Chrome's button, the right-click menu) shows the tab. A click on the window never activates the app (WindowServer's prevents-activation tag, `ArdiaPiPController.mm`): closing it or using its controls from another app leaves that app in front, with its keyboard, and the browser window where it was (0.2.30 brought the browser window up on close); only Back to Tab activates the app (self-test: `pip-window`; the feel on a real screen is the owner's check). Back to Tab from another app brings Ardia and the tab's window forward: it activates with `activateIgnoringOtherApps:`, as Chromium does, because macOS 14's cooperative `activate` was turned down while another app was in front and the PiP only vanished (fixed 2026-11-21, #3347; watch `usage_counts.pip_back_to_tab_from_other_app` next to `pip_back_to_tab`, which now also counts Chrome's own button). A pinch on the PiP resizes it again: the real window stays at 1 % opacity under the resize stand-in rather than 0, because macOS routes no events to a window at alpha 0, and the rest of the pinch went to the app underneath (fixed 2026-11-21; watch `usage_counts.pip_pinches` come back). Smoke-tested (`ARDIA_TEST` `pipSelfTest=close`); the pointer's hover state on the corner buttons is checklist step 13 ⌘-scroll (wheel or two-finger trackpad) or a pinch over the window resizes it (2026-10-06, the owner's request; ours, Dia's PiP doesn't zoom): AppKit's scroll-up direction grows it (so it follows the natural-scrolling setting), a trackpad in proportion to its points (150 pt ≈ 1.8×), a wheel ~8 % a notch eased over ~0.1 s, a pinch by its magnification; the aspect ratio stays the video's, the window stays docked to the screen edges it's within 64 pt of (else grows around the pointer) and on screen, between Chrome's own limits (284 × 160 and 80 % of the work area, the same as dragging its edge); a gesture that started as a zoom keeps zooming through its momentum, and nothing else scrolls or zooms. Plain scroll is unchanged; a document PiP window zooms the same way; a tucked one doesn't. The size is remembered with the place (Chrome's `ArdiaPictureInPicture.plist`), and a size saved on a bigger display comes back clamped to the current one. `ArdiaPiPZoomMath.h` (math; `pnpm -C packages/ardiacore test`), kill switch `defaults write … ACPictureInPictureZoom -bool NO`. Left for a person: the feel of the trackpad and wheel speeds with real hardware. Manual PiP from Site Controls or the hover card (2026-10-23, PR #12 review): only a real press of that control counts, a mouse-up outside the page, Space or Return on the focused control (not its key repeats), or an accessibility press (VoiceOver, `kAXPressAction`), each used once; the page itself, keys typed into it and harness calls can't start it. It acts on the main document only: through Chrome's media session only when the main document registered an enterpictureinpicture handler, else on the main document's playing or largest video; media inside iframes gets no PiP from our button (the video's own PiP control still works), so an iframe can't take the click's activation. Nothing happens if the main document changed after the press. Exit closes whatever PiP window the tab owns, through Chrome (an iframe's video included). A video whose page sets `disablepictureinpicture` (YouTube TV, through YouTube's player) still goes into PiP from our button and from auto PiP; only the page's own request stays refused (#908). A sidebar row in PiP shows a plain `pip` symbol after the favicon (click to exit) instead of a badge over it; pinned tiles keep the badge (issue #1107). Dragging it keeps the spot you grabbed under the pointer, like any macOS window (2026-11-14, #1607 and #1312): each drag event places the window at the pointer minus the offset taken at mouse-down, in screen points, then keeps it on screen (`DragTo` in `ArdiaPiPThrowMath.h`). It used to add each event's movement to the window, and `setFrameOrigin` rounded every step to a whole point, so a slow trackpad drag (fractional steps) drifted hundreds of points (measured: a −300, +200 pt pointer path moved the window −600, 0), and a step held back at a screen edge was lost for good. Throw, stash and snap still decide on release. The window also follows faster: Chrome rewrote `ArdiaPictureInPicture.plist` on every move (about 80 % of each move's main-thread time), and now saves it 250 ms after the last move and on hide (`ac_pip_window.cc`; a move's median cost 0.70 → 0.11 ms, p99 10 → 2.2 ms). Dashboard: `usage_counts` `pip_drags`; `pip_drags_off_cursor` above zero is a regression, `pip_drags_slow_move` counts drags with a window move over 8 ms. The pointer shows macOS's resize arrows wherever a press would resize the window (2026-11-19): AppKit's resize strip on this frameless window is 3 pt inside each edge and 5 pt at the corners, and Chromium's content view set the arrow over it whenever the pointer came in, while a move from the middle out to the edge never asked for a cursor at all, and from another app (PiP's usual case) no cursor change of ours reached the screen. `ArdiaPiPResizeCursor.mm` follows every move over the window, asks AppKit's `_resizeDirectionForMouseLocation:` and shows that edge's or corner's frame-resize cursor, keeps Chromium's `cursorUpdate:` from replacing it there, and lets the cursor change while Ardia is in the background only while the pointer is on the strip. Measured with the real pointer (`peekaboo move`, `NSCursor.currentSystemCursor`) from another app in front: before, an arrow on all four edges and both corners tried; after, the matching resize arrows, and the arrow again in the middle. Dashboard: `usage_counts` `pip_resizes`, and `pip_resizes_without_cursor` (a resize begun inside the window with no resize arrows showing) above zero is a regression. A click on a stashed window's handle (the 28 pt strip left at the screen edge) brings it back (2026-11-19, #2843; related #1061): Chromium's content view answers hit tests from its own views (the strip is draggable caption, the outer 10 pt resize border), so it never reached our handle, and our drag monitor took the press as a window drag that went nowhere; it never worked on the Chromium engine (the self-test called the handle's action directly). The drag monitor now reads presses on the handle: one that moves under 3 pt brings the window back, more drags the window as usual. `pip-window`'s self-test clicks both handles with posted events. Telemetry: `usage_counts.pip_handle_unstashes` counts handle clicks that brought the window back; watch it next to `pip_drags`, since parked windows dragged out instead of clicked point to a handle click that failed Pinch and edge resizes follow every display frame (2026-11-21): each Chromium resize waited for a compositor frame at the new size (measured in a hidden instance: `setFrame` about 1 ms, the commit after it 25 to 32 ms, whatever the event rate or the video's frame rate), so a pinch or an edge drag moved at most 30 steps a second and a fast pinch shook. During a pinch, or a drag on the window's resize edge, a stand-in panel mirrors the live video through a second `CALayerHost` on Chromium's layer context and moves on a display link (a pinch eases toward the fingers over 50 ms, an edge drag follows the pointer exactly) while the real window keeps its size, hidden. On release the real window takes the final size and the stand-in stays until Chromium has drawn at it (at least 250 ms, at most 600 ms), so a quick second pinch carries on with it. An edge drag keeps the aspect ratio and the opposite edge or corner, within Chrome's limits. `apps/browser/scripts/pip-resize-test.mjs` checks it. Telemetry: `usage_counts.pip_pinches`, and `pip_stand_in_timeouts` for hand-offs where Chromium hadn't drawn the new size within 600 ms; edge drags still count in `pip_resizes`. Watch timeouts against pinches plus resizes. |
| Auto‑PiP on tab switch / window occluded / app switch (Meet, YouTube) | ✓ | ✅ | ours (ACBrowserView `autoPictureInPicture`): audible or capturing pages only; setting in Tabs. 2026-11-11, a Discord ask ("it works that way in Arc") first made any switch to another app pop the playing video out, even with its window still on screen (nightly.5); that was issue #1236 (a window on each display: clicking into an app on the other display popped the video out). The owner's rule since then: on an app switch, the video pops out only if its window is really out of sight. While Ardia is inactive the window server decides (`ArdiaWindowFullScreen windowVisible`, `WindowServerShows`): the window is minimized, on another Space, its display asleep, or covered by opaque normal-level windows (another app's full-screen Space over it, such as Discord's, counts); a window still showing on any display keeps its video playing in place. An Ardia window in full screen counts as shown only while Ardia is active, so leaving its Space for another app pops the video out. It is checked at the switch and every second while Ardia is inactive and the window holds a tab playing (or capturing) with the setting on, so the other app moving over the window later pops it out and moving off closes it again; coming back to Ardia closes it. Paused and hidden tabs never pop out. PiP windows are non-activating, so using one from the other app doesn't bring Ardia back. Acceptance: `auto-picture-in-picture-app-switch` (a quick switch, a switch with the window showing, the front app covering it and moving off, a switch with it covered, a paused video; fails on nightly.5's behaviour), `call-app-switch-full-screen-window` and `call-app-switch-little-ardia` in activation-acceptance. Dashboard: `pip_shown` `left_app` true counts the pop-outs while Ardia was inactive (only real ones now), and `usage_counts.auto_pip_stayed_on_app_switch` the app switches where a playing video stayed because its window was showing. After the user closes it, a tab doesn't pop out again until it has been shown and left again. 2026-10-02 (ArdiaCore, after 0.2.22): clicking the other pane of a split made Chrome take a Meet call as left and pop it out though it was still on screen. Chrome's automatic Picture in Picture and permission prompts now follow what the app shows (`ac_host_visibility.h`): pane focus changes pop nothing out, leaving the split (or for the New Tab page, another Space) pops the call out once, coming back closes it; a split's other pane can prompt, a hidden page prompts once shown. Acceptance: `activation-acceptance.mjs` (HTTPS fake conference). 2026-10-02: "f" then "f" on YouTube sometimes left the video in PiP or flickered it: the JS mini player read the window's occlusion right as the full-screen Space animation ended, still "occluded", and asked for PiP. One rule now decides whether the user left a page (`-[ArdiaCoreWebView seenByUser]`: the app stopped showing it, or its window is covered or left for another app; the window's own full-screen transitions never count, `ACWindowFullScreen`), and the native view alone opens PiP and closes it on return (however it opened); Chrome hears the same reading, so a covered window pops a call out through Chrome. The user closing a PiP no longer needs tracking: the view acts only when the user leaves or comes back. Acceptance: `fullscreen-pip` (20 × f/f with a real transition's occlusion, with and without Media Session handlers; leaving the window; hand-opened PiP), `call-window-covered` WebKit tabs (fixed for #887): the native view's property was a stub that read false, so a WebKit page never popped out. `WebKitTab` now keeps the flag and reads the same sight rule (`visible` and `ArdiaWindowController userSees:` through `ArdiaChromeWindowHost userSees`, re-read on a window change and on `ArdiaWindowSeenDidChange`); `WebKitAutoPictureInPicture` acts only on a change of sight: leaving a playing tab with the setting on asks the page for PiP, coming back closes it (however it opened), and a PiP the user closed does not reopen until the tab has been shown and left again. The rule's state lives on the page view, not the tab, so a tab that takes a moved tab's view over still closes the PiP its predecessor opened. Test: `packages/ardiacore/tests/webkit-auto-pip.test.mjs` 2026-11-11, issue #1236 (with a window on each display, a video on one popped out when the user moved to another app on the other display): the only thing that made the app call a shown tab "left" was AppKit's `occlusionState` on its window (`ArdiaWindowFullScreen windowVisible`), and AppKit reported that window covered while it was still on screen. When AppKit says covered, the window server's own window list now has the last word (`WindowServerShows`: the window is on screen and some part of it on an awake display is uncovered by opaque windows above it, exact area in `ArdiaWindowSightMath.h`, `tests/window-sight.test.cc`); while the two disagree it is rechecked every second, so a window that later really gets covered still pops out. Switching tabs in another window pops nothing out, and leaving Ardia for another app pops a playing tab out only if its window is out of sight (the app switch above). Acceptance: `call-window-still-on-screen` (fails on the build before, passes after; `fakeWindowServer:shows` acts out the window server's answer), `call-window-covered` still passes. Dashboard: `usage_counts.auto_pip_kept_visible` counts the times a playing tab kept from popping out this way; steady non-zero means AppKit's occlusion is misreporting in the field, and a "pops out on the other display" report with it at zero means another cause. Left for the owner: two real displays, a video in a window on one, another app on the other. 2026-11-11, WebKit tabs: an automatic pop-out asks the page at the moment you leave and takes only a video that is playing (`pictureInPicture(on, automatic)` in WebKitPageScript.swift). It used to fall back to the largest video, so a video paused just before a quick switch (inside the page's 100 ms media report) popped out paused and lost its place on return; a pop-out you ask for still takes the largest video. Test: `tests/webkit-auto-pip.test.mjs` runs the page script. Dashboard: `usage_counts.auto_pip_nothing_playing` counts automatic pop-outs held back because nothing was playing; it should stay small, and a spike means the page's playing report lags behind pauses 2026-11-14, a user's report and the owner's (Google Meet never popped out when leaving its tab or window): Chrome sent the page its `enterpictureinpicture` action the moment the app said it had left the tab, while the page still read `visibilityState` "visible", and Meet's handler opens Picture in Picture only for a hidden page (proved on a real Meet call in the owner's browser: the action arrived with the page visible and focused, Meet called nothing; the same handler run once the page was hidden called `requestWindow`). Chrome's auto-PiP and tab-modal dialogs now read a tab the app hid as shown until its page is really hidden (at most 400 ms; `ac_host_visibility.mm`), so the action reaches a hidden page as in Chrome. Acceptance: `call-tab-switch-page-hidden` (a call page that, like Meet, opens PiP only while hidden; fails before, passes after). Dashboard: `pip_shown` gains `left_tab` (it opened because the user had left its tab or its window); `kind` document with `left_tab` true counts Meet's pop-outs and should rise from about zero.. 2026-11-12, issue #1387 (one Ardia window on each display: an automatic pop-out opened from a playing tab disappears once the Ardia window on the other display is focused, whether that window has no video, a paused or scrolled-to one, or is already playing): the cause is not found yet, and nothing recorded why a floating video closes (the `pip` trace is test-instance only), so this adds the data and changes no behaviour; the fix follows once it comes in. The engine keeps an always-on record in memory, not behind the trace (`ac_picture_in_picture.mm`): the last 8 closes, each with who closed it (`by=app` when the app asked through `exitPictureInPicture` in the 2 s before, `by=chrome` otherwise), the kind, whether it opened automatically (`enteredAuto`), Chrome's `IsInAutoPictureInPicture` at the close (`chromeAuto`), the host-shown value for the opener (`hostShown`: 1 shown, 0 hidden, -1 when the host never said; `chromeAuto` is -1 when the tab has no helper), and the last 4 window activation changes (`ac_window_host.mm`, `ActivateExclusively` and `OnWidgetActivationChanged`: which window number, how many ms before the close). The native side adds the reason to the Focus history: `pip exit requested`, `pip sight seen` and `pip sight left` with `seenByUser` (as last reported and as read now), `visible`, `appActive`, `key`, `autoActive` and `inPip`, written only while an automatic pop-out is active or a tab that would pop out is playing. Copy Diagnostics shows both: the "Focus:" history and a "Picture in Picture closes:" block, one close per line, reading `none` when nothing has closed, and absent on an older engine. The page event for a close, of a video or of a page, now carries `closedBy` and `auto`, counted as `usage_counts.pip_closed_auto_by_app`, `pip_closed_auto_by_chrome`, `pip_closed_manual_by_app` and `pip_closed_manual_by_chrome` (counts only, listed in What's Sent). To read it: on two displays, open a video in a window on one, leave its tab, focus the Ardia window on the other, then Copy Diagnostics. `by=chrome`, `chromeAuto=1` and `hostShown=0` with a `widget inactive` or `activate exclusively` note a few ms earlier points at the engine's activation change as the trigger (the investigation's suspect: Chrome's helper may follow the browser's active state); `by=app` with `pip exit requested` and `seenByUser=1` means the native sight rule counted the hidden tab as seen; `hostShown=1` means the host still showed the tab when Chrome closed it. In the dashboard, `pip_closed_auto_by_chrome` should stay near the number of times people return to a tab by hand; a steady count with the app-asked ones near zero is the report's case. Needs a new prebuilt engine on release (`ac_picture_in_picture.mm`, `ac_window_host.mm` and `ardiacore_tab_media.mm` are engine sources). Tests: `apps/browser/src/state/telemetry/pictureInPictureCloses.test.mjs` (the counters and the Diagnostics block), `packages/ardiacore/tests/pip-close-record.test.mjs` (the record is written outside the trace, the activation notes and the native reasons are wired) |
| Document PiP | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's own Document PiP window (its frame shows the origin and Back to tab) |
| PiP stash, return to tab, hostname bar | ✓ | ✅ | on Chrome's own video PiP window, in-process (`ArdiaPiPController.mm`): the origin is Chrome's, centred as in Dia (our host pill is gone); right-click anywhere: Back to Tab / Keep Window on Top (remembered); a drag from anywhere but its controls moves it ourselves, frame by frame under the pointer (macOS 27 refused most of the window drags Chrome asked for, 34 of 43 grabs in the owner's log); dragged mostly past a screen's left or right edge it tucks in with a 28 pt peek and a chevron handle (click: back on screen), and stays tucked if Chrome moves it. Verified with the DEV self-test (`ARDIA_TEST` `pipSelfTest`, all 11 steps, including the remembered frame) and window captures, not a real pointer drag (checklist step 13). 2026-10-29: liquid glass removed; the back to tab and close buttons are Chrome's white discs and the stash handle is the black handle on every macOS. 2026-11-12, WebKit tabs: the return button on a WebKit page's Picture in Picture window (macOS's own) did nothing in Ardia: WebKit sends it to the browser as the private UI delegate call `_webViewFullscreenMayReturnToInline:`, which no one answered. `WebKitTab` now answers it the way Chrome's Back to Tab does: it activates the app and emits `activateRequest` (`pictureInPicture`), so the tab's window comes forward on that tab while the video goes back into the page. The window's close button pauses the video and goes through a different call (`pipActionStop:`), so it never brings Ardia up. Dashboard: `usage_counts.pip_back_to_tab_webkit` counts these returns and `pip_back_to_tab` the Chromium ones (any activation from the Picture in Picture window, Document PiP's included); a WebKit count that drops to zero while the Chromium one holds may mean the call stopped arriving; check it in a build |
| Throw the PiP window to a corner (two-finger swipe, fling) | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-10-22, from a tester's report on Discord. Numbers from Dia's 1.53 binary (`docs/dia-spec.md` › Picture in Picture: moving and throwing it). A two-finger trackpad swipe that begins over the video PiP window moves it with the fingers, under either natural-scrolling setting. A swipe whose last event moved over 6 pt, or a click-drag let go at over 350 pt/s (Reduce Motion off), throws it to the screen corner within 45° of the throw's direction from the grabbed point. It lands 10 pt inside the visible frame, on Dia's spring (response 0.9 s, damping 0.9, starting at the throw's velocity). With no corner that close, or a slow release, it stays where it's let go. A mouse wheel does nothing; ⌘-scroll still zooms and stops a throw; a new drag or swipe stops one where it is; past a screen side the 28 pt peek stash still wins. While it moves, the window always keeps at least 60 pt × 40 % on a display and slides along edges; one bigger than its display stays on it; one left unreachable after a gesture or animation comes back onto its display. In a real run the pointer hides during a swipe and comes back on the same spot of the window. Kill switch: `defaults write … ACPictureInPictureThrow -bool NO`. Maths: `ArdiaPiPThrowMath.h` (`pnpm -C packages/ardiacore test`). Verified in a hidden instance with synthetic phased scroll events and posted drags (`devPictureInPictureAction("swipe:…" / "drag:…")`). Each corner landed exactly 10 pt in, every frame was within 1 pt of the spring, and the slow, paused, wheel and ⌘ cases held. The movement rule is covered by a seeded property test (1M random swipe and drag steps over 4,000 random display layouts, plus walks between every pair of touching displays). Left for a person: the feel with a real trackpad and mouse, the pointer hiding and warp on a real screen, and two displays (the corners are the window's own screen's). 2026-11-03, a tester's ask on Discord: in a corner its radius is 9 pt instead of 6, concentric with a zoomed browser window's content card (card 9 pt in with a 10 pt radius, PiP 10 pt in), bending over 0.2 s once it lands and back to 6 as it leaves; measured from window captures: 6.01 pt before, 9.01 pt after, card 10.09 pt. 2026-11-07, issue #789 (PiP stopped at the edge of the main display and never opened on a second one): AppKit's `constrainFrameRect:toScreen:` re-fits any window to the display it is on, and only the browser-window drag was exempt (`WindowMove.holdAppKitPlacement`), so the throw maths' own display crossing was undone (measured 2026-11-07 on one display: a PiP frame set to reach 50 or 96 pt under the menu bar came back pulled down to the visible frame's top, y 724). A window with a PiP controller now gets its requested rect back wherever the throw maths' own movement rule puts no shortfall on it (`ArdiaPiPController+Placement.mm`, `Shortfall`: 60 pt × 40 % on a display, the full frame toward a display that touches it, so a crossing through a Dock's gap between stacked displays holds), so `ArdiaPiPThrowMath.h` alone decides where it may go; any other rect, such as one left with a strip on a display just unplugged or a stashed window's 28 pt peek, goes to AppKit's own constrain as before. A reopen restores the saved frame on the display holding it (Chrome's `CalculateAndUpdateWindowBounds` fits it to that display's work area, or the primary one when none holds it). Run tests: `tests/pip-throw.test.cc` drags and swipes across a display beside, above and offset from the main one, rescues a window after the second display is unplugged, and checks every step of a crossing through a Dock's gap passes the override. Left for a person: a real two-display Mac (drag onto a display beside and one above, close and reopen there, unplug it) |
| Mini player for pinned media tabs (skip ±15 s, art, marquee) | ✓ | ✅ | hover mini player + sidebar player. The sidebar player grows on hover to show the artist, a seek bar with time played and total, previous / play-pause / next (or ±15 s), mute, PiP for video and close. 2026-11-14 (#1613): it starts growing as the pointer arrives (no 0.25 s wait), within 8 pt around the card (not below it when New Tab sits under it), and grows in 0.22 s and shrinks in 0.18 s after a 0.16 s leave grace, on the native driver: the card grows upward over the list, which is clipped above it by transforms, and the layout changes once when it settles, so no frame re-lays out the sidebar; the title row rises with the top edge while the seek bar and controls fade in place and never show cut, and the time left or play button crossfades to the close button, except under a pointer that arrived on it: there it stays put and keeps its action (play, or pause for the time left) until the pointer leaves it, the close button only takes presses in its own place once the card has settled, and the PiP button that shares that spot waits too. 2026-11-15 (#1615, torztomasz): Classic tints the card as soon as the pointer is over it, Reduce Motion opens and closes it without moving, and `usage_counts.sidebar_player_reopens` counts the opens that came back while it was still closing. Watch `usage_counts.sidebar_player_expands` against `sidebar_player_reopens`: a high share of reopens means the 0.16 s leave grace is too short. A looping video or song counts on past its end and wraps (#1617, `now_playing_loop_restarts`); the page script resends a report whenever the position strays 1 s from where the last one says it should be, not only when the rounded position changes. 2026-11-15 (#1618): a site that sets its position with `setPositionState` is reported where it has played to since (its element's advance, or the site's clock while it says it is playing), at the rate that position moves, and every card command makes the page report again, so a seek the page ignores is corrected. The card's own seeks don't count as page reports. Watch `usage_counts.media_position_rewinds` (a playing page reporting its position going back for anything but a loop restart) and `media_reports_past_end` (a report that came after a non-looping card had already run 2 s past the end): both should drop per playing session after the release. Shows only media Chrome would put in its media controls (`controllable` in page_script.js): a player that has played unmuted with an audio track, longer than 5 s unless the page sets a Media Session state. 2026-10-16: X's muted autoplay feed videos, silent videos and 1 s notification sounds no longer bring up the card, and a video the page removes takes the card with it. Settings › Tabs › "Show media controls in the sidebar" (default on) turns off the card and the pinned-tab hover player. A tab paused from the card keeps it so it can be resumed, until another tab in the window and profile starts playing (on screen or not), which closes the paused card until the paused tab plays again or you go back to it. 2026-11-16 (a Discord report, Nov 15): closing a pinned tab that was playing left its card in the sidebar with nothing behind it; a page that ends (closed but kept as a pinned tab, put to sleep, crashed) now takes its card with it, as it already did its camera dot. Watch `usage_counts.media_cards_cleared`. 2026-11-17 (issue #1649, asked on Discord and X): Settings › Tabs › Keep Playing now across profiles (off by default, synced, `playingNowAcrossProfiles` in config files) keeps the card in every profile of the window, as Arc keeps it across spaces: media left playing in one profile shows its card on every profile's sidebar page, swiping included, until its tab is on screen again; a play in any profile of the window takes over a paused card. Off, the card stays in the profile whose tab plays. Watch `usage_counts.sidebar_player_from_other_profile` (a card shown for another profile's tab) and settings_snapshot `setting_playingNowAcrossProfiles` |

While a site shares a tab, Dia's info bar sits above the page: "Sharing this tab with …" with Stop Sharing on the shared tab, "Sharing another tab with …" with Share This Tab Instead on the profile's other pages (the site's tracks keep running with the new tab), Dismiss on both. 2026-11-10, #1157 and #694 (Meet and Slack could share only tabs): a screen or window picked in the sheet failed with "Permission denied by system", because Chrome refuses desktop capture until macOS grants Screen Recording and Ardia never asked. Without that permission the sheet now hides screens and windows and says how to turn it on, with an Open System Settings button (asks macOS once, then opens Privacy & Security › Screen Recording; macOS wants Ardia reopened after). Pages also stay in place when the share bar slides in or out: on Retina each slide left the page drawn 2-4 pt higher, so after a few shares Meet's controls sat 14-16 pt above where clicks landed, over a strip of the page's background, until a reload; Dismiss made it worse. Chrome sizes the layer it draws a page into only through Core Animation autoresizing, which loses half a point here and there while the bar resizes the page every frame; Ardia now fits that layer to the page after every layout. Measured in window captures on a Retina MacBook (before: 0 → 16 pt over two shares; after: 0 pt at every step) and in real Meet with a copied profile. A black share (the report's other symptom) didn't reproduce: the other participant saw live frames in every run. Watch `perf_memory.content_layer_refits` and `content_layer_drift_max_pt` (corrections, so they stay non-zero on Retina) against `content_layer_layouts`. Sites that control their share (Google Meet passes a `CaptureController` to `getDisplayMedia`, #2146): Ardia's picker answers through a desktop capture that left the controller unbound, so Meet's `getSupportedZoomLevels()` threw "Not actively capturing" and its presenter preview and annotation tools never appeared (the others still saw the share). The page script now binds the controller to the stream it returns, so `getSupportedZoomLevels()` answers like Chrome's for a live share (no zoom levels for a tab, `NotSupportedError` for a window or screen). Watch `usage_counts.shares_with_capture_controller` against the `shares_picked_*` counts |

From then on clicks on Ardia's own web notifications went to Chrome, which dropped them, Ardia's dismiss category was gone, and once Chrome's idle service shut down there was no delegate at all, so macOS didn't show any banner while Ardia was in front. `NotificationHub` now stays the delegate, hands Chrome's own notifications (`notificationId` in their user info) to the delegate Chrome set, and keeps its category beside Chrome's.
