| Fullscreen video (incl. other display) | ✓ | ✅ | page fullscreen puts our window into fullscreen, and Chrome only tracks the state (`CEF_AC_TAB_FULLSCREEN`); Esc exits. Verified headless (2026-09-26; test instances act the window's full screen out, 22 checks): the page fills the window with the sidebar and toolbar hidden, Esc and `exitFullscreen()` bring them back at the page's size, no full screen without a gesture; in a window already full screen, the page doesn't toggle it; with two profiles, a page in the other profile's window goes full screen without taking the window out. Found and fixed: leaving window full screen by hand left the page full screen, and that other-profile page took the window out of full screen. 2026-09-30: a playing video (YouTube) left the window full screen with the sidebar and toolbar showing. The Space change occluded the window mid-transition, auto Picture in Picture fired and pulled the video out of element full screen, and AppKit ignored our toggle back out; occlusion during full-screen transitions and on full-screen pages no longer starts auto PiP, and a page that leaves mid-transition takes the window out once it settles. Switching or closing the tab now exits page full screen and the window's, and a split pane's page full screen keeps the focused pane on exit. Verified headless with 19 checks plus split and auto-PiP cases (`ARDIA_TEST` `fakeFullScreenMs` gives the acted-out transition AppKit's duration). Left for a person: AppKit's Space and animation on a real screen, the "Press Esc" bubble's look there, and a second display (checklist step 1). 2026-10-02 (ArdiaCore, after 0.2.22): 0.2.22 lost the window's macOS full screen for a page, reported a full-screen exit on a tab switch to the newly selected tab (A stayed flagged full screen, so its toolbar and sidebar vanished again on return), and didn't end full screen when the app hid a page (its New Tab page, another Space). The engine now reports the page Chrome holds in full screen (`ACBrowserWindow::ReportFullscreenTab`, also for a page going full screen in a window already full screen), the window enters macOS full screen for the page and leaves it afterwards only if it entered for it (`ArdiaWindowFullScreen`, ArdiaWindowFullScreen.mm), leaving it by hand ends the page's full screen, and hiding a page ends its full screen, pointer lock and keyboard lock (`-[ArdiaCoreTab exitExclusiveAccess]`). Acceptance: `fullscreen-tab-switch`, `fullscreen-native`, `fullscreen-hidden-page`. 2026-10-02: each transition is recorded with who started it (the app for a page, or the user) instead of a 3 s watchdog, whose expiry on a slow way out made its late end read as the user's and threw a page that had come back into full screen out again (`fullscreen-native`). 2026-10-08 (owner's 120 fps recording of YouTube's f on 0.2.29: about half a second of broken frames each way): the window's macOS transition started the moment the page asked, while the page (a Chrome view the app's JS lays out) was still changing; AppKit pictures the window as its transition starts and animates that picture, so the way out kept the full-screen video, ghosted, inside the shrinking window for the whole animation, and the way in showed the page sized for the old window in a corner of the new one, then clipped. Page full screen now goes in steps, each waiting for the page to have drawn the one before (`ArdiaPageDrawWait`: the page answers after two animation frames with its size and full-screen state, at most 0.4 s): in, the page fills the window in full screen, then the window grows; out, the page leaves full screen where it is, then the sidebar and toolbar come back around it, then the window shrinks from that. Verified headless with acted full screen, which now gives the window the screen's frame as AppKit does (60 fps window captures, a local page with a video and a 120 ms layout cost like YouTube's): 0.2.30's way out showed the full-screen video clipped inside the windowed layout and the way in the windowed page in a corner (both as in the owner's recording); now neither, and the window's transition starts only with the page drawn for it; Esc, the green button, split view and tabs on top pass (the five `fullscreen-*` acceptance checks). Left for a person: a real Space transition (acted ones don't animate a picture), where a heavy page can still be a size behind for a moment right after the window resizes. 2026-10-08 (owner's 120 fps recording of build 30 next to Dia: the way in showed the whole window #0f0f0f for ~100 ms, then YouTube's windowed layout zooming for ~80 ms; the way out showed the sidebar and toolbar around a near-black page for ~580 ms, the page popping in as the animation ended): AppKit doesn't show the window during its transition; an overlay window (`_NSFullScreenTransitionOverlayWindow`) crossfades a picture of it from before into one from after, taken from the window's own layers, and hands the window back at the end. A Chrome page isn't in those layers: it draws in the GPU process and the window only hosts it (`CALayerHost`), so the pictures held the sidebar and toolbar around the page view's bare background (an in-process render of the window, `devSnapshotWindow`, reproduces those frames exactly; a window-server capture includes the page). Chrome's own window doesn't help here: as the embedder the window and its transition are ours, and Chrome deliberately doesn't repaint during one (`NativeWidgetNSWindowBridge::ShouldWaitInPreCommit`). Now each page the window shows lends it a picture of itself as an ordinary layer (ArdiaCore `-[ArdiaCoreTab copyPicture:completion:]`, Chrome's `CopyFromSurface`; `ArdiaPageCover`, ArdiaPageCover.mm): put over the page just before the transition (both panes of a split on the way out), refreshed once the page has drawn its new size, faded out (0.1 s) once it has drawn after the transition; the way in also lets the page go full screen in its card before the card fills the window, so YouTube's windowed layout is never stretched over the window. Acceptance `fullscreen-transition-picture`: an in-process render of the window at every frame of an acted transition (what AppKit's pictures hold) shows the destination layout in every frame, 0 bare and 0 stale (49–50 frames in, 46–50 out, six runs; a split's way out has both panes covered in all 47–50 frames); with the pictures off (`pageCover:0`, as 0.2.30) every one of those frames is bare background, as in the owner's recording. The five other `fullscreen-*` checks pass before and after. Live (acted, 60 fps, YouTube): the window starts its transition 270 ms after f in and 293 ms after f out (build 30: 228 and 264 ms; the picture costs ~30–40 ms). Left for a person: a real Space transition on a real screen, YouTube f in, f out, Esc and a split's way out, by eye |
| Video upscaling (super resolution, Apple silicon) | ✗ | ✅ | ours, Settings › Tabs › Video upscaling (on by default): a video of 720p or below drawn larger than its source (on-screen pixels at least 1.15× the source, off again at 1.0×) is upscaled in the GPU process, through VideoToolbox's low-latency super resolution (ML, ×1.5 or ×2 as the size allows, on the Neural Engine) for luma, with the source's own colour, resampled to the exact on-screen size; full-range video, or a size VideoToolbox offers no factor for, gets MetalFX spatial alone. A source over 720p (its visible part: long side over 1280 or short side over 720, so portrait 720×1280 still counts) is never upscaled, the owner's decision: the larger sources cost the most GPU-process CPU for the least visible gain (`Upscaler::Eligible`). VideoToolbox's output desaturates strongly saturated colours, so chroma always comes from the source and saturated areas keep the source's luma; MetalFX after it added a second sharpening and visible halos on edges, so it isn't stacked (`ac_video_upscaler.mm`, `chromium-video-super-resolution.patch`). Only the active tab of the key, visible window of the active app, page full screen included; never in PiP, Little Ardia, a background tab, Low Power Mode or under serious thermal pressure, never in a tab that is sharing a tab, window or screen (a Google Meet presentation's own preview went black with upscaling on, #943), and never HDR or protected video (`ac_video_upscaling.mm`). A paused video or a redraw reuses the last output; idle for 3 s, everything is freed. VideoToolbox runs through its asynchronous call between two command buffers: its command-buffer call leaks a Metal shared event per frame until `newSharedEvent` fails (about 65 000, 36 min at 30 fps), which crashed the GPU process; a VideoToolbox job stuck for 1 s, or a processor that throws, falls back to MetalFX. A frame with padding rows or columns (extended pixels, as camera frames can have) is copied into VideoToolbox's own unpadded buffer first: handed over as it is, it made VideoProcessing read a NULL buffer and crash the GPU process. Checked by `apps/browser/scripts/video-upscaling-test.mjs` (30 states, including a real tab share through `getDisplayMedia`, 720p HEVC, VP9 and AV1, portrait 720p, and 1080p and 1366×768 sources left alone). No telemetry for the size limit: the decision is made per frame in the GPU process, which reports only trace events, and no upscaling event exists to carry a count. Left for a person: the result by eye on a real screen |
| Proprietary codecs (H.264 / AAC / MP4) | ✓ | ✅ | our engine build (`proprietary_codecs`, `ffmpeg_branding="Chrome"`, VideoToolbox decode) |
| Protected video (DRM: Netflix, Spotify…) | ✓ | 🟡 | Through WebKit (#234, ours, modelled on Crest's engines): a page can open in WebKit, whose FairPlay plays DRM video, from the tab menu, View › Open Page in WebKit, or Site Controls (› More, and the "Opens in" row, which saves a website rule for the exact scheme, host and port). Streaming sites (Netflix, Disney+, Max and HBO Max, Prime Video, Hulu, Apple TV+, Apple Music, Spotify's player, Peacock, Paramount+, Crunchyroll, Hotstar, Tencent Video `v.qq.com` (#560); https, subdomains included) open in WebKit from the first visit, so you sign in there once (Settings › Engines › Open streaming sites in WebKit, on; a website rule wins; Site Controls says "Recommended (WebKit)"). When a Chromium page's `requestMediaKeySystemAccess` for Widevine, PlayReady or FairPlay fails (`page_script.js`), a prompt offers "Stay on Page" / "Open in WebKit" (which saves a rule), and "Don't ask again for this site" saves a Chromium rule with Stay on Page (outside private windows; otherwise the site isn't asked again until quit); a site with a WebKit rule or a streaming site moves on its own and a toast says "Opened in WebKit for protected video" with Move Back (which saves a Chromium rule, as Crest's notice does); one with a Chromium rule is left alone; every move checks for unsaved form input first ("Stay on Page" keeps it, and that site isn't asked again until quit). Settings › Engines: the default engine (Chromium; Use Recommended Default) and website rules (add, edit, remove). Links and popups from a page stay on its engine; a link followed in the same tab to another site with a rule (or a streaming site) moves the page there, unless a page opened the tab, and Back from it returns to the page before in the other engine; a tab away from the default engine shows the engine's mark on its icon. Each engine keeps its own cookies and storage per profile (one persistent `WKWebsiteDataStore` per profile, a non-persistent one per private session; private choices aren't saved as rules); tabs, history (WebKit visits go into Chrome's history), settings and downloads are shared. WebKit pages use Safari's user agent, element full screen and WebKit's own PiP, swipe navigation and context menu (`packages/ardiacore/ios/WebKit*.swift`). WebKit pages are ad-blocked with uBlock Origin Lite's rules (see "Built‑in ad + tracker blocker"). A uBlock rule that redirects a media load to a silent file is never turned into a block for WebKit, which can't redirect: WebKit loads a site's real audio and video as media (Spotify's songs are FairPlay HLS from `spotifycdn.com/audio/`, which uBO's Spotify ad filters redirect), so the block left Spotify at 0:00 with no sound (#1233, 2026-11-13; ad audio there may now play). A WebKit page shows only while its tab is on screen: as the open tab of a neighbouring profile, or the tab taking over while another is dragged away, it waits hidden instead of covering the page you switched to (watch `usage_counts.webkit_pages_kept_hidden`). 2026-11-13, #1233 (Spotify's player with no sound in a WebKit tab, cause unknown): on the streaming sites only, a WebKit page reports what its `requestMediaKeySystemAccess` answered (Widevine, PlayReady, FairPlay or another key system; granted, or refused as not supported, a security error, not allowed or another error; also through Safari's prefixed `WebKitMediaKeys`, which Spotify's player uses behind its own `navigator.requestMediaKeySystemAccess`: `isTypeSupported`'s answer per key system and a session's `webkitkeyerror` code), a media element's error code (aborted, network, decode, source not supported; also on a detached `new Audio()` the page plays) and why a `play()` was rejected, counted by fixed reason in `usage_counts` as `webkit_fault_<reason>` (`WebKitPageScript.swift`, `WebKitTab.pageMessage`, `webkitMediaFaults.ts`; Copy Diagnostics has a "WebKit media faults this session" line). Playback and the page's own errors are unchanged, and no host, address, title, track or account is recorded. What to watch: which `webkit_fault_*` keys appear once a nightly ships (for Spotify, `webkit_fault_eme_fairplay_legacy_*` and `webkit_fault_eme_legacy_key_error_*`; `webkit_fault_eme_legacy_key_error_output` would point at the display protection), and compare `webkit_faults_blocker_active` (the content blocker was filtering the page) and `webkit_faults_muted` (the tab was muted) with the people who report silence; the fix follows once the data is in. A popup window has no such prompt: the `drm` message of a popup page (Audible's cloud player, #958) never reached the app, so a popup that needs protected media can stay blank. `ArdiaPopupWindow` now only counts it, and logs the family, without changing what the popup does (watch `usage_counts.popup_protected_media_refused_<family>_<listed|unlisted>`, family `widevine`, `playready`, `fairplay` or `other`, and `popup_protected_media_stalled_<listed|unlisted>` for a player that waited for keys without any key system being refused; listed when the popup's host is on the streaming-site list, counted only while sharing is on); the fix, such as adding audible.com to the list or offering WebKit from a popup, follows once those counts show it. Not in WebKit yet: extensions, password autofill, Chrome's site settings, page translation, notifications, and passkeys (Apple's entitlement, §16). Chromium itself still has no Widevine: Google licenses the CDM only to VMP-signed browsers. Needs a tester's run in a built app (Netflix playback, both switches, a rule, the prompt). 2026-11-12, WebKit pages and things drawn over them: with Auto-hide tabs on, clicking a tab in the sidebar that slides over a WebKit page also clicked the page under it, so a paused YouTube video (or any player that toggles on a click) started playing as you switched away. AppKit hands a click the sidebar's views don't take to the view beneath, and Chromium's page view turns those away while `WKWebView` took them. The WebKit page view now takes a press only when the window's hit test lands on it, and the drag and release of a press it took (`WebKitPagePresses.swift`, `WebKitWebView.swift`); a press it turns away is reported as `pagePress` with `refused`. Test: `tests/webkit-page-presses.test.mjs` (an offscreen window with a view over the page; fails on the old behaviour). Dashboard: `tab_presses.page_refused` counts the clicks a WebKit page turned away; steady non-zero with Auto-hide tabs on is this fix working, and a "switching tabs plays my video" report with it at zero means another cause 2026-11-17, #775 (英数 and かな don't switch the input source in a WebKit tab; with no text field focused they scrolled the page like Space): Chrome's `BrowserCrApplication` hands those two key downs to the key window without AppKit's input-method step, so the WebKit page view (`WebKitWebView.keyDown`) now runs `+[NSTextInputContext processInputKeyBindings:]` for them and keeps them from WebKit, the same helper (`InputSourceKeys.swift`) the app's text views use (`AppKitPatches.swift`, 8b9cd605b). Chromium pages already did this. Test: `tests/webkit-input-source-keys.test.mjs` (source wiring; fails on the old `keyDown`). Telemetry: `usage_counts.input_source_keys_webkit` counts the 英数 and かな key downs a WebKit tab handed to the input source (read hourly from the Focus module's `takeInputSourceKeys`; `inputSourceKeys.test.mjs`). On the dashboard, a non-zero count means Japanese-keyboard users are pressing them in WebKit tabs and the keys now stop there; it staying at zero on builds with this fix while reports of the page scrolling keep coming means the keys never reach `WebKitWebView.keyDown`. Run in a built app on 2026-11-16 (a6e3a58d8 in a hidden instance, real key events posted to its process only, against a build of main from before the fix): in a WebKit text field the old build let 英数 and かな through as keydown, keypress and input and typed U+0010 into the field; now the field stays empty, the page sees only the keyup, and the count goes up by one per key. With nothing focused the old build handed the page keydown, keypress and keyup; now only the keyup. The command bar (`AppKitPatches.swift`'s path) took neither key into its text before or after and isn't counted. Not shown: the input source switching, since that Mac had only the US layout enabled, and the Space-like scroll from the report, which didn't happen on that layout. A Japanese-keyboard tester's run still covers those. Removed 2026-11-17 (#2437): FairPlay in Chromium is gone (the Spotify, Netflix, Disney+ and YouTube bridges, the `ArdiaFairPlay` helper, the controls mirror, the Safari user agent extension and Settings › Engines › Play protected media in Chromium with FairPlay), because each bridge made Chromium pass as Safari to get the service's FairPlay path; protected video, Spotify's player included, plays in WebKit tabs again (`open.spotify.com` is back on the streaming sites). What to watch: `webkit_fault_eme_fairplay_*` and `popup_protected_media_*` stay steady, Spotify's faults show up in `webkit_fault_*` again, and the `usage_counts` `fairplay_*` keys and `settings_snapshot` `setting_fairPlayInChromium` stop arriving. |

## 19. Sharing & printing
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Share sheet (File › Share…) | ✓ | ✅ | The page's link without trackers (Copy Clean Link's cleaning) and its title, through macOS's sharing services: Site Controls › Share… (the share sheet at the row), the command bar's Share (the sheet, also found by "airdrop"), File › Share ▸ and the tab menu's Share ▸ (the services the system lists for a web link with their icons, as Safari and Chrome list them, then More… for System Settings' sharing extensions; Mail and Reading List are left out as Chrome does, File › Email Page Location sends the link). Dia's ShareMenu (from Arc) builds its menu the same way (`sharingServicesForItems:`). Web pages only; private windows and Little Ardia too. Beyond Dia (owner's ask, 2026-11-01): ⌥⌘S (File › Share › Share…, rebindable in Settings › Keyboard Shortcuts; Dia has no shortcut, and ⌥⌘S is free in Ardia's table, Chrome's accelerator table and Apple's standard menus, where ⌃⌘S is Show Sidebar and ⌥⇧⌘S Save As…; ⇧⌘I is Email Page Location), and Share… in the page's right-click menu, which Chrome's and Dia's lack: ArdiaCore adds it (`ac_context_menu_share.mm`) at the end of the group it belongs to, and the picker opens at the click. Selected text shares the text, a link its clean URL, an image the picture itself (Chrome's `RequestImageForContextNode`, its original JPEG/PNG/GIF, as a file, or its address when it can't be read), a video or audio its http(s) source, a blank part of the page the page as the toolbar's Share does. A linked image gets Share Link… and Share Image…; a link's own text, which macOS selects on right-click, isn't offered as text. Not in text fields. 2026-10-05: before this, File › Share… was the only way in and the popover had none (owner's report). A hidden instance logs what it would share to `activation.log` instead of showing the sheet or running a service. Left for a person: the sheet's look and a real AirDrop / Messages send |
| Copy URL ⇧⌘C | ✓ | ✅ | tracker-stripped |
| Print / Save as PDF ⌘P | ✓ | ✅ | `CefBrowserHost::Print`, which on Chrome tabs should be Chrome's print preview; not re-checked since the migration (checklist step 9) |
| Handoff (browsing activity) | ✓ | ✅ | per window, never incognito; the app is team-signed now, cross-device Handoff not tried |

## 20. Import, account & sync
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Import from Chrome, Safari, Firefox, Edge, Brave, Opera, Vivaldi, Arc, Dia, Helium, Zen | ✓ | ✅ | + Opera GX, Island, Chrome channels, Chromium; bookmarks, history, open tabs, passwords (into Chrome's password manager), cookies so you stay signed in (Chromium family's `Cookies`, v10-decrypted with the browser's Safe Storage key incl. the SHA-256 host prefix; Firefox's `cookies.sqlite`; Safari's `Cookies.binarycookies` behind Full Disk Access; written into the profile's Chrome cookie store by `ac_cookies_import`, never into a private profile, never over a cookie the profile already has; values stay native and are wiped after the write), addresses and cards (Chromium `Web Data`, Safari export cards; into Chrome's PersonalDataManager by `ac_autofill_import`), and the source's Chrome Web Store extensions listed on the done screen to add from their store pages (never installed by the import: that waits on an engine op that creates an install only if absent, owns it and can cancel it for good, so an import can't change an extension or a request it didn't make. 2026-11-17: the list shows every Web Store extension under the Ardia profile its source profile went to, by the name the source lists, with its id, a Store Page link built from the id, and whether it was off or pinned in the source; Open in Chrome Web Store opens them all, each in its profile; what that profile already has is listed as already in Ardia. Only an explicit internal location with `from_webstore` counts as the Web Store: Manifest V2 extensions, which Chrome 157 no longer runs, unpacked ones, policy installs, ones added by another app and anything else are listed with the reason; a source's built-in ones, such as Helium's uBlock Origin, are left out, and so are uBlock Origin, which Google delisted, and uBlock Origin Lite, which Ardia ships built in; with nothing left the done screen shows no extensions block. Telemetry (hourly `usage_counts`): `import_extensions_listed`, `import_extension_store_pages_opened` and `import_extensions_skipped_{installed,mv2,unpacked,policy,external,not_web_store}`; watch pages opened against extensions listed. Linux's reader doesn't say where an extension came from, so Linux keeps the plain store-link list. Verified with `ExtensionsTests`, `extensionListing.test.mjs` and `listedExtensions.test.mjs`; `import-flow-test.mjs` `chrome chromeSkip` still to run on an app build). Fixed 2026-10-15 (owner's report: extension imports didn't work during onboarding): Open in Chrome Web Store opened the pages under onboarding's overlay and cleared the list, so nothing seemed to happen; the list now stays and says the pages are open in the sidebar, ready once setup is finished. Dia and Helium (imput's ungoogled-chromium) import as ordinary Chromium — Dia's secrets under "Dia Safe Storage", and its open and pinned tabs from Dia itself (§20's Dia sidebar row); Helium's Keychain item is "Helium Storage Key" / "Helium". Onboarding's "Where are you coming from?" finds /Applications/Helium.app under Found on this Mac (with its own icon and the Chrome-style preset; the tile isn't offered when Helium isn't installed) and its Import… opens the import window on Helium (2026-11-12, #1146). Checked against a real Helium 0.21.2.1 profile made by Helium itself (`net.imput.helium/Default`, cookies and the Chromium 157 `Sessions_Encrypted` copy under its storage key): bookmarks, history, open tabs, a password and a cookie all arrive. Helium enforces its own password manager off (`credentials_enable_service`), so most Helium users have no passwords to bring. Watch `usage_counts.onboarding_helium_imports` (and `onboarding_imports`). Chrome and Brave protect their data from other apps on current macOS, so they're listed as "Needs Full Disk Access" and go through the same FDA step as Safari. Imported tabs, favourites, Arc's pinned rows and folder tabs, and bookmarks-bar links show their icons before they're ever opened (#230): the import reads the source profile's own `Favicons` (the page's icon, else another page of the same site's; Dia's AppleScript import looks in every Dia profile), puts them in the sidebar's icon cache and stores them in the profile's Chrome favicon database (`ac_favicons_set`), so a relaunch keeps them (Chrome expires an on-demand icon after 30 days unrequested, and our lookups don't touch it yet, so a tab left unopened that long goes back to the globe after a relaunch); no network fetch, nothing from Safari yet. Firefox and Zen (2026-11-17) read the profile's `favicons.sqlite` (`FirefoxFavicons.swift`, and `firefoxFavicons.ts` with the same rules on Windows: the page's own icon, else its site's validated root `favicon.ico`, else the home page's, else the site's most recently visited pages'; only PNGs whose own header says 64 px or less, SVGs and other formats never decoded), for Firefox's open tabs and bookmarks toolbar and Zen's Essentials, pinned tabs, folders and today tabs, after the icons Zen's session already carries. Telemetry: `import_firefox_profiles` and `import_firefox_favicons` (hourly `usage_counts`); watch favicons per profile: a drop to near zero means Firefox changed `favicons.sqlite`. Verified with `FirefoxFaviconsTests` (fixtures from `generate.py`), `firefoxFavicons.test.mjs`, `windowsFirefoxFavicons.test.mjs` and the Windows import tests; `import-flow-test.mjs` `firefox zen` checks the icons reach the store. Zen (2026-10-24, #218) goes through the Firefox readers for bookmarks, history, cookies and passwords, and its sidebar comes over as Arc's does: from `zen-sessions.jsonlz4` (then its backups, then the Firefox session store), each workspace becomes a space and each space a profile, with its emoji and main colour; pinned tabs stay pinned (at their pinned URL, with custom names), folders become pinned groups (nested, split views flattened, live folders left out), other tabs land in Imported, Essentials become pinned tiles, and PNG tab icons from the session are stored before the tabs are made. Each space goes to the right profile (2026-11-12, issue #1320; before, every container's Essentials landed in the first profile, container-bound workspaces shared the first profile's cookies, and a Zen from before 1.18, which kept its workspaces in `places.sqlite`, brought every pinned tab into one profile): each Zen profile's spaces stay apart from another's; within one, the spaces of one container (`containerTabId`) share one profile's data and Top Apps, and a space tied to another container gets a profile of its own; Essentials follow their container (`userContextId`; all of them in every container when `zen.workspaces.separate-essentials` is off; a container with no space falls back to the default container's), bookmarks, history, passwords and cookies go to the default container's profile, and the space picker names each space's Zen profile and container (`state/import/sidebarPlan.ts`, `ZenSidebar.swift`, `places.sqlite` `zen_workspaces` as the fallback; verified on real Zen 1.24.2b data). Linux reads the same sidebar natively (2026-11-19, `ac_linux_import_zen.cc`, the rules of `ZenSidebar.swift` and `zenSession.ts`): Zen and Zen (Flatpak) list their spaces, Essentials, pinned tabs and folders, and the space picker's choice reaches the reader as `spaceIds` (verified on real Zen 1.24.3b profiles; `test_import_zen.py`). Linux's check that no imported secret reaches the UI (`ReplyLeaksImported`) only counts cookie values of 16 bytes or more that aren't web addresses, and never field names (2026-11-19, #2772): a 6-letter cookie such as `active` matched every tab's `active` field and withheld the whole import; a withheld import now says why on the done step. The UI note ships with the app; the engine rules reach users with the next published and pinned Linux engine. Telemetry: `import_withheld` in the hourly `usage_counts`; it should stay near zero. A rise means a real leak or one of the false positives this check still has: a saved password of 6 bytes or more equal to any string in the result, or bookmarks nested past the walker's depth limit (#2866). Dashboard: `usage_counts.imported_spaces`, `imported_container_spaces`, `imported_data_profiles`, `imported_essentials`; spaces coming in with `imported_data_profiles` stuck at one per import while `imported_container_spaces` is above zero means the container mapping broke. Firefox and Zen passwords also come from `logins.db` (A256GCM JWE under key4.db's `as-logins-key`), merged with `logins.json`; Firefox's own sync login isn't imported. The default search engine comes over from every Chrome-family source and Arc (2026-11-14, #1760; before, Ardia always stayed on the preset's engine, Google for Arc): the main profile's choice (`default_search_provider.guid` → its `Web Data` `keywords` row, else `default_search_provider_data`), mapped onto the built-in engine with the same address or shortcut, else added as a custom engine; a source on its own default leaves Ardia's engine alone, onboarding's browser preset no longer puts Google back over it, and the done screen says when it changed. It switches only during first-run setup or while the engine is untouched (the default, never written by anyone, never synced in from another Mac); an engine an import adopts is kept as inferred in `settingsMeta`, so onboarding's preset doesn't put its own engine back over it. Once an engine was chosen (in Settings, the setup question, the onboarding preset, on another Mac, by an older version, or by an earlier import), a later import keeps it and the done screen offers Use <engine> for Search instead, adding a custom engine only when you take it; while an extension controls search, the import neither switches nor offers. Windows reads the same `Preferences` and `Web Data` (`packages/expo-windows/src/import/searchEngine.ts`). Not yet from Firefox, Zen or Safari, nor on Linux, whose readers are engine C++. Dashboard: `search_engine_import` by `outcome` (`built_in`, `custom`, `source_default`, `unusable`; `unusable` should stay near zero), `changed` and `offered`, with `usage_counts.import_search_engine_offers_taken` against `offered` for how often the offer is taken, and `usage_counts.onboarding_preset_kept_imports` History is written to the engine in batches of at most 2M characters (under Linux's 8 MiB request limit), and the summary counts only the pages the engine accepted (rows it would drop, such as a page with no last visit, are left out); a refused batch marks History failed (fixed 2026-11-19, #2775: a 66,930-page Zen history was counted but never saved on Linux). Telemetry: `import_history_batches` and `import_history_batches_failed` (hourly `usage_counts`); watch failed batches stay near zero A bookmark whose name was cleared in the source browser keeps its empty name, as Chrome does (#2295; `state/import/apply.ts`, `bookmarkNames.test.mjs`): the bar shows it icon-only, and the bar's folder menus, the Bookmarks menu, the bookmarks manager and the command bar name it by its URL (the command bar by the page's title once it has been visited). Dashboard: hourly `usage_counts.import_untitled_bookmarks`, counted for browser, Safari and HTML-file imports; watch it against `import_runs`: it staying at zero for weeks while imports keep coming means a reader started filling in names again History is written to the engine in batches of at most 2M characters (under Linux's 8 MiB request limit), and the summary counts only the pages the engine accepted (rows it would drop, such as a page with no last visit, are left out); a refused batch marks History failed (fixed 2026-11-19, #2775: a 66,930-page Zen history was counted but never saved on Linux). Telemetry: `import_history_batches` and `import_history_batches_failed` (hourly `usage_counts`); watch failed batches stay near zero |
| Export bookmarks (HTML) | ✓ (Chrome's) | ✅ | Settings › General › Import and Export › Export…, Bookmarks › Export Bookmarks…, the bookmarks manager's Organize menu and the command bar: a save panel, then Chrome's own `bookmark_html_writer` writes the profile's bookmarks (with their icons) as Netscape bookmark HTML, the format every browser imports (`ac_bookmarks_export_html`, #560). With several profiles, Settings picks which; the menus export the window's profile |
| Safari direct import (no export .zip) | — | ✅ | Reads `~/Library/Safari` directly (bookmarks, history, Reading List, open tabs) when Ardia has Full Disk Access; the import UI detects FDA, links to System Settings and re-checks on return. The export `.zip` stays as the fallback and the only path for Safari passwords/cards |
| Arc import (spaces, pinned tabs, custom names) | ✓ | ✅ | Arc's favourites become tiles and a space's pinned tabs become pinned rows, as Dia 1.52 imports them (2026-10-19; before, both became tiles). Each space's theme comes with it (row "Arc theme import"). A space's pinned folders that hold links become pinned groups, their subfolders with links nested groups (2026-10-23; before, a "<Space> (Arc)" bookmark folder on the bar), all closed, so a profile with thousands of links doesn't mount thousands of rows (before, every imported folder opened and the sidebar lagged); importing the same space again adds no second copy. Holders left by earlier imports (a "<Space> (Arc)" folder directly on the bar): Restore Arc Folders as Groups on the folder's menu (bar, manager, Bookmarks menu) or on the import window's done screen turns its folders that hold links into top-level pinned groups and takes them out of the holder; the holder goes once nothing is left in it, so loose links and empty folders stay. Fixed (#910: "work was named personal"): the first space goes into the profile the import targets, and on a fresh install that is the untouched, only "Personal" profile, which is now named after the space (its emoji and colour too); a profile the person renamed, restyled or picked out of several keeps its name, and every later space still becomes a profile of its own. Dashboard: hourly `imported_starters_named` counts the imports where the first space named the starter |
| Arc theme import | ✗ (Arc ✓) | ✅ | importing Arc spaces sets each profile's theme from its space's `customInfo.windowTheme` exactly (Arc's extended-sRGB colours as stored, the overlay's third colour, `intensityFactor`, `noiseFactor` and texture, `windowAppearance` / `translucencyStyle`, an opaque `basicColor`, and both looks of a background that differs in light and dark, picked when drawing); Settings › Profiles › Theme › Import from Arc… lists Arc's themed spaces and applies one to the profile. Schema from Arc 1.167's Swift type metadata (`state/import/arcTheme.ts`); parser tests on fixtures copying it, the two spaces of the user's screenshot among them (`packages/import/fixtures/arc-themes`). |
| Arc import: split views | ✗ (Arc ✓) | ✅ | Arc's split views come over as Ardia splits (2026-11-10, GitHub issue #1084; before, a split broke into separate rows and a pane whose page was pinned elsewhere was dropped): a pinned split as a split of pinned rows, one inside a folder as a split inside its pinned group, one among the open tabs as a split in the Imported group; a split counts as already there only when all its pages are, so a pane is never dropped for repeating a page; Arc's 4-pane split becomes a 3-pane split (our limit) and a row beside it; split views among the favourites still become separate tiles (tiles can't split, issue #388). A pinned tab Arc lists only by its parent, not in its container's children, and a space's pinned tabs held in a second pinned container come over too (`ArcSidebar.swift`, fixture `packages/import/fixtures/arc-sidebar`). Dashboard: `usage_counts.imported_pinned_rows` and `imported_splits` per import; splits near zero while rows come in means Arc's split format changed. |
| Arc import: Air Traffic Control, archive, space icons, sidebar icons | ✗ (Arc ✓) | ✅ | 2026-11-17, from a comparison with Cra. The Arc import offers two more kinds, "Air Traffic Control" and "Archived tabs", after the spaces. Arc's routes (`StorableLinkRouting.json`) become Air Traffic Control rules in Arc's order, each with Arc's rule id (`arc-<id>`), so importing again adds nothing and keeps your edits. Only what Arc writes is read: one URL condition, `contains` or `matches`, and a destination that is a space, Little Arc or Most Recent Space; anything else is left out and counted. Conditions stay literal, as Arc compared them, through two new Air Traffic Control conditions you can also pick by hand ("Links containing", "Links to exactly"): the text is saved as typed and normalized only when a link is checked (case, the scheme and one leading `www.` ignored); containing looks at the whole address, query and fragment included, and exactly compares host, port (80 and 443 dropped) and path, a trailing slash aside, both sides put in their canonical web form first (`new URL`: `café` and `%C3%A9`, `/a/../docs` and `/docs` agree); an exact text that isn't an http or https address or has `?` or `#` is refused, and so is text that would match every link (`/`, `www.`), which points to Any link. A space destination goes to the profile that space became, else an existing profile of that name; a space that didn't come over leaves the rule out (Little Arc rules naming it too), Little Arc becomes Little Ardia and Most Recent Space "Profile you used last". Arc's default for links from other apps (Most Recent Space turns Open links in Little Ardia off, a space or a space's Little Arc sets the fallback) applies only when you've never set either (Little Ardia off, chosen here, or synced from another Mac counts as set): a fallback you picked, Ardia's usual routing included, is now remembered as chosen (`fallbackChosen` in `air-traffic.json`). The archive (`StorableArchiveItems.json`, newest 1,500) goes to Recently Cleaned in the profile its space became (Little Arc and deleted spaces to the import's main profile): the newest 100 distinct ones that have a profile (rows already there count toward the 100), kept a week from the import, each with Arc's custom name; no duplicates on a second import. Arc's `autoArchiveTimeThreshold` (12 h, 24 h, 7 days, 30 days) sets Clean up inactive tabs only when that is untouched. An archive file that's there but can't be read fails the "Archived tabs" kind on the done screen; a missing one only brings the timing. The rule sheet in Settings says whether a rule didn't fit (100 at most) or couldn't be saved, and keeps your draft. The done screen counts the rules, archived tabs and settings actually saved (a setting only once the session save landed), and says what stayed in Arc and why, including rows the reader capped. The two kinds are offered whenever Arc has the files, with or without spaces, and routing also when every rule would stay behind, so the done screen can say why. A space whose icon is a glyph from Arc's Icon tab (56 names) gets the matching emoji, an unknown glyph 🗂️. Sidebar items, favourites and pinned tabs take Arc's own 32 px icon from `SidebarItemsFaviconCache/<md5 of the item id>` first, then the profile's `Favicons`. Files, the sidebar included, are read size-capped, as regular files inside Arc's folder only (no symlinks out; an icon cache folder linked elsewhere is refused). On Windows `ReadBytesWithin` (`ImportFiles.h`) now also refuses a final symlink or junction before opening (untested on Windows). Same reader on Windows (`packages/expo-windows/src/import/arcExtras.ts`, through `readTextWithin`; no threshold there, it lives in a Mac plist). Verified: `ArcExtrasTests` (Swift), `arcExtras.test.mjs`, `linkText.test.mjs`, `windowsArcExtras.test.mjs`; `import-flow-test.mjs arc` still to run on an app build. Dashboard: `import_arc_rules`, `import_arc_rules_skipped`, `import_arc_archived_tabs`, `import_arc_glyph_icons`, `import_arc_cached_icons` in the hourly extras; Arc imports with `import_arc_cached_icons` at zero mean the cache lookup broke, and `import_arc_rules_skipped` climbing against `import_arc_rules` means rules aren't finding their space or condition |
| Dia sidebar import (spaces, pinned tiles, custom names/colours) | ✓ | ✅ | 2026-11-17 (a user on X: "lost all folder hierarchy from my Dia sidebar. Everything just became a giant list under 'Imported'"). Ardia now reads Dia's sidebar from Dia's own file and never opens or asks Dia when it can: each Dia profile keeps it in `User Data/<profile folder>/tabs.db` (+ `tabs.db-wal`, where recent edits often only live), a SQLCipher 4 database (4096-byte pages, 80 reserved bytes: 16-byte IV + 64-byte HMAC-SHA512; page 1 starts with a 16-byte salt; AES-256-CBC per page, raw 32-byte key). Its key is not in Dia's team keychain group, as this row used to say: it's HKDF-SHA256 of Chromium's OSCrypt key (PBKDF2-SHA1 of the "Dia Safe Storage" secret, the same key the import already derives for Dia's passwords and cookies), salted with HMAC-SHA256(key: Dia's constant `iOe1Ue/XtonbieE/5m1nAgewvWQlePHRcKnoU10EyOM=`, data: `profile/<folder>/tabs`), info `profile/<folder>/tabs`, 32 bytes (verified on a real Dia 1.57.1 profile). `packages/import/ios/Core/DiaSidebar.swift` decrypts each page, folds in the -wal's committed frames (matching salt and checksums; frames after the last commit are left out), opens the result in memory (`SQLiteSnapshot(memory:)`, never on disk, wiped on close, with `temp_store=MEMORY` so no query spills to a temp file); every page it uses is checked against its SQLCipher HMAC-SHA512 before decrypting, reads are capped at 256 MB on the open file, and the main file is checked again after the -wal is read (size, times, first page) so a checkpoint in between is read again rather than mixed and walks `nodes` (favorites, pinned_container and window nodes per space; groups nest; a tab with two or more content panes is a split; deleted nodes and closed windows are skipped) with `tab_groups` (title, emoji from `icon`, colour from `theme`), `content_panes` (custom titles) and `web_contents`. What comes over: favourites as pinned tiles; Dia's Pinned section as pinned rows, its folders as pinned groups with their names, emoji, colours and nesting, and pinned split views as splits; open tabs' groups as list groups, nested, named, with emoji and colours, in Dia's order at the top of the list (all imported groups come in closed); ungrouped open tabs still go into "Imported"; custom tab names as the tab's name; split views as splits. The first Dia space goes where the Dia profile goes; each further space becomes its own Ardia profile, named after it, coloured from its theme and sharing the Dia profile's data, as Arc's spaces do. Reading it needs the Safe Storage key, so a Dia profile with a `tabs.db` asks for it in the unlock step (`tabsNeedKey`, `diaSidebar` on the profile), and Open tabs, Pinned tabs and Favorites are offered for it. Fallback: a profile with no `tabs.db` (an older Dia), or whose file doesn't open or read, or an import with Skip Encrypted Data, gets its open and pinned tabs from Dia's AppleScript as before (`DiaTabs.swift` / `DiaAppleEvents.swift`: Dia opened hidden if it isn't running and quit again, Automation consent, each Dia profile matched by name, else position, a Dia profile with no folder gets a new profile only when it has tabs of the kinds being imported, favourites and pinned rows both as tiles, open tabs into "Imported"); the done screen then says the sidebar file couldn't be read and that folders, custom names and splits stayed in Dia. Telemetry (hourly `usage_counts`, `state/telemetry/importRuns.ts`): `import_dia_sidebar_files` (profiles read from tabs.db), `import_dia_sidebar_unreadable` (tabs.db there but didn't decrypt or read), `import_dia_applescript_profiles` (profiles whose tabs were asked of Dia), `import_dia_groups` (groups and folders made), `import_dia_space_profiles` (Dia spaces that became a profile). Watch `import_dia_sidebar_unreadable` against `import_dia_sidebar_files`: a rise means a Dia update changed the file or its key. Verified (2026-11-17): Swift tests on an encrypted fixture built by `fixtures/generate.py` (`build_dia_sidebar`: right key, wrong key, another profile's label, WAL fold-in with a committed and an unfinished transaction, a torn frame, the tree, the importer's kinds and counts, discovery), `diaSidebar.test.mjs` for the store side, the decryption of a copy of a real Dia 1.57.1 profile, `import-flow-test.mjs` (`diaFilesOnly`: a profile with a sidebar file never asks Dia; `dia`: Personal from the file with its nested groups, Work and the folderless Side project from AppleScript) and a hidden instance importing a copy of a real Dia 1.57.1 profile from its file alone (Dia's app id pointed elsewhere, so it was never asked): 9 favourites as tiles, 1 pinned row, the open groups GitHub Trending ⭐ › Group 3 and Hacker News 📰, nested and closed; a page open twice in a group came over once, as every import does The file format and its key were first worked out by Victor Kildahl's Cra ([VictorKildahl/cra#79](https://github.com/VictorKildahl/cra/pull/79), MIT); our reader is our own code, reviewed by Codex in four rounds (temp files, HMACs, read caps, the WAL race, the fallback's failures, one main window for the whole import, spaces kept apart after kind filtering). |
| Account (Atlassian identity, OTP, delete account) | ✓ | — | |
| E2E‑encrypted sync (24‑word phrase, recovery kit, device transfer) | ✓ | ✅ | no server: every Mac reads and writes sealed files in a folder the user picks (iCloud Drive › Ardia Sync by default, no entitlement; Dropbox, a NAS or a USB drive work too). 24 BIP-39 words from the CSPRNG, as Dia's; HKDF-SHA256 keys, AES-256-GCM per file bound to its name, keyed or random names, 1 KiB padding; the key in the login Keychain, this Mac only. Per-device append-only logs, last writer wins per record by hybrid logical clock, snapshots with safe pruning, written only once enough has changed to be worth a full copy (2026-11-10, issue #1118: before, steady browsing rewrote the whole ~3 MB profile snapshot every 10 minutes; now a few a day; dashboard: `usage_counts.sync_snapshots_written` and `sync_snapshot_kb_written` per hour, which should sit near 1 snapshot per 2 active hours per profile, not 6 an hour); iCloud placeholders and half-copied files wait. Syncs bookmarks, 90 days of history, open tabs, pinned tabs and groups, settings and saved passwords (Chrome's, read from its store on disk, written through its API; turning sync off never removes one). Recovery Kit PDF / text with a QR code; device transfer by entering the phrase (or scanning its QR code with an iPhone and pasting with Universal Clipboard); the phone app (`apps/phone`, Android and iPhone) joins by scanning Connect Another Device's code or typing the 24 words and picking the same folder (`docs/phone.md` › Sync). Connect Another Device's Show Code button sits on a themed frame and the code on white (2026-11-12: in dark mode the button was white on the white frame, so the sheet looked like a blank code; dashboard: `usage_counts.sync_codes_shown` against `sync_codes_declined`). Design and threat model: `docs/sync.md`. Verified: Swift and JS unit tests (`pnpm --filter @ardia/sync test`, what they cover is in `docs/sync.md` › Tests), and end-to-end checks with two and three hidden instances through a temporary folder (`packages/sync/scripts/e2e.mjs`). Left for a person: two real Macs over iCloud Drive (checklist step 15). Reading and applying bookmarks and history grows linearly with their count (2026-11-17, #2181): new positions in a folder find the next kept one in one pass, applying bookmarks appends siblings in place, and an unchanged history entry skips the per-visit comparison; dashboard: `usage_counts.sync_bookmark_extract_ms_max`, `sync_bookmark_apply_ms_max` and `sync_history_extract_ms_max` against `sync_bookmark_records_max` and `sync_history_records_max` (time per pass should stay roughly proportional to records; a few thousand bookmarks or history entries in tens of ms, not seconds), with `sync_*_extracts`, `sync_*_applies` and `sync_*_extract_ms_total` for the hour's total cost. The iPhone app's sync bundle has the same order fix and no telemetry |
| Invite / referrals | ✓ | — | |
| Sync all open tabs, not just pinned ones (an Arc-style extra; a user's ask) | ✗ (Arc ✓) | ✅ | 2026-11-15. Dia syncs only pinned tabs; Arc keeps a space's tabs the same on every Mac. Settings › Sync › All Open Tabs has a switch per synced profile (also Settings › Profiles › the profile › Sync All Open Tabs). The switch itself syncs (`ptab:`), and it needs What Syncs › Open Tabs on that Mac. With it on, the profile's regular tabs (http and https, not private, Little Ardia, peeks, blank or file tabs) and their groups go across as `otab:t:` / `otab:g:` records: a new tab arrives unloaded in the background of the window showing the profile, a closed tab closes on every Mac (no Recently Closed entry there), reorders and group changes merge, a page change reaches a copy that isn't loaded and leaves a loaded one alone, and pinning or unpinning hands the tab to the pin records without doubling it. Closing the last window, quitting or relaunching never closes anything elsewhere: the profile goes quiet here, and its tabs come back unloaded in its next window, adopting tabs reopened with the window. Turning it off on one Mac deletes the records; every Mac keeps its tabs. Details in `docs/sync.md` › All open tabs. Watch on the dashboard: `sync_open_tabs_profiles` in the daily settings snapshot (use), and in the hourly usage event `sync_open_tabs_opened` / `_closed` / `_published` against `_wakes`, with `sync_open_tabs_mass_closes` and `sync_open_tabs_cycle_max` as the alarm for a sync that closes many tabs at once (it should stay near zero); 2026-11-16: the Linux adapter forwards the per-profile switch through its sync authority and exposes the same per-renderer usage counters; watch the existing `sync_open_tabs_*` fields on Linux too |

| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Light / Dark / Automatic (View › Appearance) | ✓ | ✅ | also in Settings › Appearance; light mode not visually re-verified |
| Profile theme colours | ✓ | ✅ | 9 colours |
| Custom profile theme (Arc-style gradient, noise, pass-through blur) | ✗ (Arc ✓) | ✅ | Settings › Profiles › a profile › Theme, the New Tab page's Make it yours panel and onboarding's colour step: every one of the nine preset colours opens the editor with its own values (one colour, its swatch; intensity 0.6, blur full, no noise, 135°), and edits belong to that preset: switching away and back brings them back, they survive a restart, the showing look syncs as before, through the same records, and your presets are yours on this Mac (each Mac keeps its own looks; after another Mac edits or resets the showing preset, switching away and back here brings back this Mac's own look and sends it), an edited swatch draws its own colours, and Reset beside the colour steppers (shown only while edited) returns it to the plain preset. An untouched preset draws exactly as before. A custom theme saved before this became the edit of the preset its profile was on, drawn the same (2026-11-20; there was a separate Custom swatch before). Telemetry: `theme_presets_edited`, `theme_presets_reset` hourly; `edited_presets`, `default_profile_color_edited` daily. Arc's editor in our settings style. A colour pad (hue around, saturation outwards) with 1 to 3 handles, a brightness bar under it for the selected handle (black to white, so a theme can be black; the hue stays with the handle's place on the pad, so it comes back from black), Intensity, Pass-through blur (opaque to Arc's translucency), Noise with four textures named after Arc's (grain, sand, denim, tweed; our own procedural tiles, not Arc's images, repeated as a pattern colour so a resize redraws nothing; each tile darkens with the mean and spread of Arc's own texture as Arc multiplies it, measured from ARC_WindowThemeUI's images: grain 0.070 ± 0.064, sand 0.344 ± 0.222, denim 0.158 ± 0.057, tweed 0.295 ± 0.053 at full strength; before, grain was about 4.8 times Arc's, issue #366), Direction and Light / Dark / Match System (a theme too dark for dark text, under 3:1, or too light for light text, under 2:1, keeps the other appearance and says so); it paints the window live while you drag (coalesced to one store write per frame). Settings › Appearance › Profile themes follow this appearance (on by default; issue #201, from Nana Amoako's PR #187): Arc saves most themes as Light, so imported spaces stayed light on a Mac in dark mode; with it on every profile takes the app's light or dark instead of its theme's mode, and in dark mode a light-only theme that white text can't read on has its colours scaled to 40 %; the editor's Appearance control then reads "Follows Settings › Appearance" and its preview draws the look the window gets. Settings and other utility windows always keep the app's appearance. Rendered as Arc renders it (read from Arc 1.167's shader and the code that feeds it): colours 1 → 2 mixed in OKLab on Arc's 45° diagonal, reached at 75%; a third colour fades in from the top to the bottom; layer alpha 0.2 + 0.55 × intensity over a white (dark: black) underlay over the behind-window blur; texture multiplied at noise × Arc's per-texture strength, a quarter while inactive. The accent the presets drive (New Tab power-up band and edge light, sidebar highlights, profile name and badge, New Tab marks by the nearest preset) comes from the gradient. Inactive windows and Reduce Transparency still go opaque (the blur follows the window state), except while the editor is open: Arc edits in a popover on the key window, ours is in Settings, so custom-themed windows keep their key look (live blur, full noise) until the editor closes. The editor's preview is drawn as the window is: the gradient at the layer alpha over the underlay and a grey standing in for the blur, the noise over that, and the bars and card in the theme's own appearance. Syncs as its own `pth:` record; older Macs keep the preset colour (docs/sync.md) |
| Custom theme gradient direction | ✗ (Arc ✗) | ✅ | the custom theme editor's Direction row is a dial with one hand: drag it to any whole degree, and it snaps to each 45° within 4° (VoiceOver steps 45°). Angles are CSS's: 135°, the default, is Arc's diagonal (the "45° diagonal" in the row above, in maths terms), so Arc imports and older themes keep it. The third colour's fade turns with it. The window, the startup backdrop, the swatches and the accent all use the same angle. Each look (light, dark) keeps its own; synced in a `pth:<id>:angle` record that older Macs ignore, so their edits don't drop it (2026-10-24) |
| Custom app icons (Dock tile plug‑in) | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › Appearance › App Icon (7 variants); `ArdiaDockTile.plugin` keeps it after quitting. The coloured plates carry the painted hills in their own colour family, recoloured as the New Tab page's per-profile marks (Plum plum, Ocean blue, Midnight and Daylight neutral; the sun stays gold; Noir and Mono stay grey; `AppIconMark-<colour>`, docs/brand/ardia/build.py; 2026-10-11). Not yet seen in the real Dock. The Default icon is an Icon Composer `AppIcon.icon` (parchment fill; back hill, front hill and sun as layers), so on macOS 26+ it follows the system's icon style: light, dark, clear light/dark and tinted (issue #73). The plate and Mono variants are flat images that macOS never restyles, so while Icon & widget style is Tinted or Clear (`AppleIconAppearanceTheme`) the Dock shows the Default icon instead, in the app and in the plug‑in, and the picked variant returns when the style goes back (issues #706, #858). Dark keeps the picked variant |

Settings › Appearance › Page Frame: a 0–12 pt slider for the room between the page and the window's edges, the sidebar and split panes, default 9 pt (today's look, unchanged); synced and in config files (`pageFrame`). At 0 the page goes edge to edge with square corners (the window's own corner rounds it), and the corner radius shrinks with the frame. Every layout follows it: sidebar shown, hidden and peeking, address bar in the sidebar, tabs on top, split view, the extension side panel, Little Ardia (including its early launch window) and the traffic lights; full-screen pages stay edge to edge. 2026-11-11, issue #999: two off-by-default synced checkboxes under Page Frame — "Zero in full screen" (`pageFrameZeroInFullScreen`) drops the frame to 0 whenever the window is in macOS full screen; "Zero in full screen when tabs are hidden" (`pageFrameZeroWhenTabsHidden`) drops it only when full screen and Auto-Hide Tabs are both on; otherwise the slider still applies. Issue #2743 (2026-11-19): macOS 27 draws the window's corner at 16 pt while `windowCornerRadius` falls back to 19, so a thin frame rounded the page wider than the window (18 pt inside 16 at a 1 pt frame) and the gap swelled at the corners. The page's radius now goes from Dia's 10 pt at the default 9 pt frame (and `19 − frame` above it) to concentric with the corner the window is drawn with (`drawnWindowCornerRadius`, read from its frame view) as the frame thins: 15.3 pt at 1 pt, the gap measured even to within 0.3 pt (0.6 px at 2x). Little Ardia keeps its toolbar and frame. Turning it off restores the window pixel for pixel (checked with in-process snapshots). | Interface size (Ardia extra) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-15, issue #2024 (#1634: a 1440p monitor at 1:1 makes everything tiny). Settings › Appearance › Interface Size: 80 % to 150 % in 5 % steps (`interfaceScale`, default 1, this Mac only, not synced, not in setup codes, a config key), a pop-up of percentages plus a one-click back to 100 %. Scales the sidebar, toolbar, tabs, app-drawn panels, Settings and the command bar live, with no relaunch; web pages keep their size (page zoom is separate). Each window's React root lays out in root points (bounds = frame / scale), and pages are counter-scaled so they draw at native resolution (docs/conventions.md › Coordinates). The size is stored natively too, so the first frame after a relaunch is already scaled. Telemetry: settings_snapshot's `setting_interfaceScale` (whole percent, `default_interfaceScale` 100); watch the share off 100 % and which steps people pick. |

## 22. Settings
| Pane | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Settings window ⌘, | ✓ | ✅ | sidebar navigation with back/forward, like Dia. Search (2026-10-22, the owner's request), like macOS System Settings: a field at the top of the sidebar (⌘F in Settings focuses it) searches every row's title, description, section and a few synonyms ("cookies" finds Block cookie banners, "dark" Appearance Theme), forgives typos and drops weak matches. Results replace the pane list, grouped by pane with the best first; ↑↓ and Return or a click open the pane, scroll the row into view if it isn't, and flash it for about 1.7 s; Escape clears the field. The index is `settings/settingsIndex.ts`, and anchored rows take their title and description from it, so text can't drift; `settingsSearch.test.mjs` fails if a fixed-title row has no anchor. Fixed 2026-10-22 (the owner's 30 fps recording): panes painted placeholder toggles that then slid to the real value, lists and sections that popped in a few frames late, and content 6 pt off to the right for a frame. Pane data is now cached and prefetched when Settings opens. A switch waits for the pane's data for up to 250 ms. One ScrollView stays mounted, and toggles animate only on a click. `settings-shift-test.mjs` checks every view's frame, text and switch value on a pane's first frame against the settled pane (11 of 14 panes failed before, all pass now), then checks search: ⌘F, type, Return, the row in view and flashed, Escape Ranking rewritten 2026-11-12 (#1293, a user's "Terrible search" screenshot: "cli" put What's Sent first, grouped by pane, and "click" rows flooded the list), on T3 Code's palette search: one list ordered by match class (exact title, then word-start matches in titles and keywords, then section, pane and description hits; typos only when nothing matches at a word start; a mid-word hit only in a title, for 4+ letters), whole words above word prefixes, a keyword that equals the query above one that only contains it, shorter titles first on a tie, plus acronyms ("pip") and joined words ("autohide"). Rows wrap instead of truncating, show matches in bold, the pane › section path and, for a keyword or description hit, what matched ("Also called “cli”"); the detail pane previews the selected result 140 ms after it changes (no back-history entry per preview), Escape clears. Opening Settings at a place from elsewhere (Manage Extension, site settings, a profile's settings and the like) clears a search left from before, and a pending preview can't replace that place (#2852, 2026-11-20; on 0.2.46 too); watch `usage_counts.settings_searches_dropped`. `settingsSearch.test.mjs` holds 70 real queries and their expected first result. Watch on the dashboard: `settings_searches_empty` / `settings_searches` (share of searches that found nothing) and `settings_search_top_picks` against `settings_search_top3_picks` + `settings_search_lower_picks` (how often the first result is the one opened). Redesigned 2026-11-17 (the owner's request): 16 panes became 7 (General, Profiles, Tabs, Appearance, Automations, Shortcuts, Advanced) in Dia's look, rounded cards with a colored icon per row, and no Show advanced settings switch; 18 settings were removed in favour of fixed behaviour, related settings merged into one control each, and rows that depend on another setting show only when it applies, while search still finds them and says what to turn on. Watch settings_section_opened for the new pane names and settings_search_unshown_reveals: a high share means a condition hides rows people look for. |
| General (default browser, login item, updates, warn before quit, sounds, build channel) | ✓ | ✅ | default browser, login item, warn before quitting / closing windows, session restore, command-bar routing, import, Updates (off with "Updates aren't set up for this build" until `SUFeedURL` is set). Dia's sounds are Chat sounds (⏸); build channel — |
| Account | ✓ | — | |
| Profiles / Profile Details | ✓ | ✅ | |
| Tabs (layout, new‑tab position, groups, clean‑up, haptics, site colours) | ✓ | ✅ | incl. "⌥⇧-click opens tab in group" |
| Appearance (theme, app icon) | ✓ | ✅ | theme + App Icon picker |
| Apps (connections, Morning Brief, New Chat) | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Skills | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Personalization | ✓ | ⏸ | |
| Memory | ✓ (retired 1.50) | — | |
| Privacy (content blocking, data sharing) | ✓ | ✅ | content blocking (uBlock Origin's lists, which update on their own), per-site permissions, zoom levels; data sharing —. All sites › Block requests to send notifications (above the per-profile Site permissions list, since it applies in every profile; off by default, issue #1232, 2026-11-15) turns down every notification request without asking and sets that site to Block, so the page reads `denied` and stops asking; sites already allowed keep sending, a request that also asks for something else still shows the prompt, and a blocked site is allowed again from its Site Controls. App-side only: a site that never asked still reads `default` until it does, and turning the switch off leaves the sites it blocked blocked, which the row's description says (Chrome's profile-wide Block, an engine call, would block none of them per site). A pop-up window answers permission requests natively, so its notification request still prompts (not built yet). A refusal that isn't remembered was tried first: Chrome counts it as a dismissal and embargoes the site after three, which the switch can't undo. Watch `usage_counts.notification_requests_auto_denied`. |
| Passwords / Autofill / Extensions (Chrome's) | ✓ | ✅ | Passwords: Chrome's password manager per profile, unlock through Chrome's device check, CSV import (a url without a scheme saves under https, the host is lowercased and a default port dropped as Chrome keeps them, a login already saved with the same password counts as saved, and the status says how many couldn't be saved), and an edit that fails keeps the sheet open with the error. Autofill: addresses, cards and the two "offer to save" toggles per profile (the toggles are read once the profile has loaded: a profile without a window only initializes then). A toggle an extension holds (a password manager that turns off the browser's own, #1311) shows the value the engine keeps and says an extension keeps it, also after a relaunch (what was asked for is kept per profile and toggle in `autofill-toggles-asked.json` while it differs from the engine's value); watch `autofill_toggle_kept` by `toggle`. Extensions: per profile |
| Sync | ✓ | ✅ | Settings › Sync, with Dia's flows and words: Turn On Sync / Enter Recovery Phrase… / Other Options (Set Up Without Another Device), Save Your Recovery Kit, Connect with Recovery Phrase ("%d/%d words", "too many words", Dia's three phrase errors), the status line ("starting up", "updating", "updated just now", "not syncing · last synced %@", "offline"), Connect Another Device…, Advanced… (Save Recovery Kit…, Copy Recovery Code, Stop Syncing…), Stop Syncing This Device? and Delete Sync Data?, plus the sync folder, profiles, what syncs and devices. Dia keeps it in Account › Sync; there's no account here. Removing what's gone (2026-11-16, #1197, #1648): a profile deleted on another Mac and another Mac's device record get Remove… in Settings › Sync; this Mac is marked (This Mac) and has none, and other devices show when they last synced and their Ardia and macOS versions. A device that still syncs comes back after it's removed. Dashboard: `usage_counts.synced_profiles_removed` and `synced_devices_removed`. Removals that keep coming from the same installs mean the record is coming back, so removal isn't sticking Removals settle once (issue #2449, 2026-11-17): removing a device or a profile records the version of each record that exists at that moment and deletes only that version, so a device or profile that publishes itself again while the removal is still on its way is deleted once and then left alone, across a restart too; removals saved by an earlier build as a plain list are converted at the start of the next cycle and saved before anything is deleted. Telemetry: `usage_counts.synced_removals_returned` counts removals that met a record published again by another device, next to `synced_devices_removed` and `synced_profiles_removed` (one per removal you asked for). Watch on the dashboard: `synced_removals_returned` counts each removal at most once, when it settles and finds the record published again. It says nothing about repeated deletions. A removal is counted in the hour you ask for it and its return in the hour it settles, which can be a later one, so compare the totals over days, not hour by hour: over time the returned total stays at or below the removals, and a high share only means people remove devices and profiles that are still syncing. A repeated deletion would show as devices or profiles reported missing again after being re-added, not in this count. |
| Keyboard Shortcuts (remap any action, F‑keys, conflict handling) | ✓ | ✅ | every menu command, hidden ones too (⌘1–⌘9, tab switcher, Back to Pinned URL, Move Tab, Close Group, F7/F12, Profiles menu) and the standard items (Quit, Close Window, Hide, Minimize, Edit's, Full Screen); aliases yield to a user's binding; the page gets every key first, except Helium's reserved set (Close Tab, Close Window, New Window, New Incognito Window, New Tab, Reopen Closed Tab, Next/Previous Tab, the tab switcher, Quit, Copy URL, Developer Tools ⌥⌘I and F12, Search Tabs); rebind in Settings › Keyboard Shortcuts, and the reservation follows the command to its new keys (in full screen with the sidebar, Full Screen joins the set; a page in full screen keeps everything but Quit and Full Screen; issue #498, 2026-10-28); the tab switcher's hold follows its binding; recorder needs ⌘, ⌃ or ⌥ (or an F-key), and a command on an ⌥ chord such as ⌥C still beats the page, in the recorder, the reserved keys and the engine's menu fallback (issue #573, 2026-11-15); arrows, Home, End, Page Up/Down, ⌦ and F-keys record as themselves (until 2026-11-14 they saved 🌐 and no key, so ⇧⌘← for Previous Tab never fired; a binding saved that way now reads as the key it meant; issue #1879; watch `settings_snapshot`'s `custom_shortcuts` and `custom_shortcuts_navigation_keys`); conflicts filter, reset; a link to chrome://extensions/shortcuts. Only AppKit's own Emoji & Symbols stays fixed (2026-10-16) Search by keys (2026-11-19, the owner's request after a user had to hunt for the command that took ⌥⌘N): a query that reads as one key combo, in glyphs (⌥⌘N), words or abbreviations with or without + / - / spaces (cmd opt n, ctrl+alt+n, ⌃tab, cmd-w) or Linux/Windows Ctrl+Alt+N, matches by the command's current bindings, remaps included (`state/commands/comboQuery.ts`, its platform words in `comboWords{,.linux,.windows}.ts`, tested in `comboQuery.test.mjs`); plain words (tab, new tab, shift click) stay text searches. Settings › Shortcuts filters to the commands using it and says "No command uses ⌃⌥⇧⌘Y" when none do; its keyboard button (Search by Keys) records a combo with the same recorder as the rows, Escape cancels. The main Settings search lists "New Little Ardia Window — ⌥⌘N" (Shortcuts › File) above the setting results, and opening it shows Shortcuts filtered to that combo. Watch on the dashboard: `shortcuts_key_searches_typed` vs `shortcuts_key_searches_recorded` (which way people search by keys), `shortcuts_key_searches_empty` against both (combos nothing uses), and `settings_search_key_hit_opens` / `settings_search_key_searches` (key hits that got opened). |
| Mouse buttons as shortcuts (Ardia extra) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-20, issue #3045. Settings › Shortcuts: click a command's shortcut, then press the middle button or a side button (buttons 4 and 5), with or without ⌘ ⌥ ⌃ ⇧; it shows as "Middle Click", "Mouse Button 4", "⌘ Mouse Button 5" beside the command's keys, which it never replaces (Middle Click for Close Tab keeps ⌘W). Stored in `mouseShortcuts` (synced, a config.json key), checked for conflicts with the same holders as keys, cleared by its own ✕ or Reset All Shortcuts. Pressed anywhere over a browser or Little Ardia window, the page included, it runs the command in that window and the press and its release never reach the page or the row under it: a binding you made wins over the button's own meaning (middle click opening a link in a new tab or closing a tab row, Back and Forward on buttons 4 and 5); a button with no binding, or with other modifiers, does exactly what it did. The shell's local monitor (`packages/shell/ios/MouseShortcuts.swift`, table in `MouseShortcutTable.swift`) is installed only while a binding exists and does a constant-time lookup on other-button presses. Mac only for now: Linux and Windows show "Mouse buttons as shortcuts work on Mac only for now." Watch `settings_snapshot.custom_mouse_shortcuts` (commands with a button, daily) and `usage_counts.mouse_shortcut_runs_middle` / `_button4` / `_button5` (hourly). |
| Usage / Billing | ✓ | — | |
| Advanced | ✓ | ✅ | Battery Saver, the engine version; also our Search Engine, Live Folders and Calendar panes |

## 23. Developer & scripting
| Feature | Dia | Ardia | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| DevTools ⌥⌘I / F12 | ✓ | ✅ | Docked on the right by default (Chrome's dock-side menu, remembered per profile), device toolbar (⇧⌘M in DevTools) docked and undocked. ⌥⌘I, context-menu Inspect; F12 is Chrome's DevTools toggle (opens them, closes docked ones, and closes the undocked window it's pressed in). The undocked window is titled like Dia's, "Developer Tools - <url>", and opens where the profile's DevTools window was last left (Chrome's own record, 640 × 640 the first time); View › Developer › View Source ⌥⌘U (`view-source:` tab), Inspect Elements ⌥⌘C and JavaScript Console ⌥⌘J, run as Chrome's own commands (⌥⌘I / ⌥⌘J close docked DevTools) |
| Task Manager | ✓ | ✅ | Chrome's own Task Manager window, as Dia's (its Window menu item opens "the chrome task manager"): Window › Task Manager → `ac_task_manager_open` → `chrome::OpenTaskManager` with the last active Browser, so its tab is selected; tabs & extensions / browser / all tasks, search, End Task. A top-level window: it outlives the window that opened it and opens with none |
| AppleScript dictionary (windows, tabs, profiles, execute JS) | ✓ | ✅ | Ardia.sdef; execute runs through the renderer's `ac-eval`, which works on Chrome tabs. Like Chrome and Dia, `execute` is off until View › Developer › Allow JavaScript from Apple Events is checked (`settings.allowJavaScriptFromAppleEvents`, default off; one switch for every profile, where Chrome's and Dia's pref is per profile); until then it fails with Chrome's error 12, "Executing JavaScript through AppleScript is turned off…" Idle (#1682, 2026-11-14): what scripts read is sent to the app without converting every tab on the main thread (it is decoded when a script first reads it), 0.1 s after a tab, window or profile is added, closed, moved, navigated, pinned, renamed or starts or stops loading, and 1 s after a change of page titles only; a script's first read asks for the current state and keeps titles at 0.1 s for the next 30 s, and a command still sends it before it replies (`scripting.test.mjs`; hidden instance: a new tab read 0.25 s later, a title 1.3 s later, `make new tab` then `count of tabs` in one script). |
| Window identifiers for window managers (AeroSpace, yabai, Hammerspoon) | ✓ | 🟡 | 2026-11-15, GitHub issue #1389. Each browser window's accessibility identifier is `ardiaBrowserWindow_<id>`, where `<id>` is AppleScript's `id of window` (Dia's is `bigBrowserWindow_<id>`), set when the window opens and kept when Chrome swaps it (`WindowManager.swift`), so a rule can tell windows apart and AppleScript says which profile each shows. The accessibility title starts with the profile, as Dia's does, and is the window's own title ("Personal — example.com", "Personal — ardia://history"), never just "Ardia", so window switchers such as AltTab tell windows apart (2026-11-16, #842, PR #990). Dashboard: `usage_counts.window_titles_read` counts windows whose title an accessibility app read once; it rising with the window count shows switchers reading the titles, and a drop to zero while windows are open means the title path broke |
| Developer Mode (Arc's; an Arc feature, not Dia's) | — (Arc ✓) | ⏸ | 2026-11-19. Off in the app since 0.2.47-nightly.9: the striped developer bar showed during ordinary browsing, so it no longer appears and Site Controls has no Developer Mode row (`DEVELOPER_BAR_IN_CORE` in `state/site/developerMode.ts`). It comes back as a plugin with better detection; a site is then on only when turned on (`developerModeSites`), never by itself for local addresses. |
| Compact control pill (ours; opt-in) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-15, GitHub issue #1081 (a Discord request). Settings › Advanced › Command line › Show the control pill as an icon and Stop (`compactCliPill`, off by default, synced, in config.json): while the ardia command or an agent drives the browser, the pill at the bottom of the page shows only the caller's app icon (or the terminal glyph) and Stop, with the full "… is controlling Ardia" line as its tooltip and accessibility label. It can't be hidden: it's the one sign something is driving the browser. Same place, motion and 3 s hold as the full pill (`CliControlPill.tsx`). Watch `usage_counts.cli_pill_compact_shows` against `cli_pill_shows` |
| Full control from the ardia command (ours) | — | ✅ | 2026-11-19, issue #2806. The control socket reads and changes the whole profile through the stores' own actions: tabs, recently closed, windows, Little Ardia, profiles (spaces), splits, Peek, bookmarks, history, downloads, the settings `config.json` takes except `newTabUrl`, `customSearchUrl` and the page-engine keys (listed in `docs/control-socket.md` › settings), search engines, site settings and permissions, zoom, extensions, media, sync status, saved logins (never their passwords), autofill (never card numbers), tile folders, page icons, shared folders (listed only; their writes follow), live folders, setup codes and Air Traffic Control rules. `methods` describes each method with its params schema, so `ardia call <method>` reaches it (`docs/control-socket.md`). Deleting a profile or removing an extension still asks the user. Watch `usage_counts.cli_socket_calls_<family>` and `cli_socket_call_failures` |
| Raycast extension support (via AppleScript) | ✓ | ✅ | `extras/raycast-ardia`: Search Tabs lists every window's tabs and switches to one (also Copy URL, Close Tab), through the AppleScript dictionary. Like Dia's extension it also has Search Profiles (shows a profile in the front window, or ⌘↵ in a new one), New Tab (a URL or a search, resolved by the command bar's `resolveInput`), New Window (the default profile, or the one named) and New Incognito Window (2026-10-24; `npm test` checks their scripts, not yet run against an instance). Verified (2026-09-26) with `osascript` from another process against a hidden instance, as Raycast's `runAppleScript` sends them (20 checks): the extension's list, focus, activate and close scripts, and the dictionary (count, make new tab and window, URL get and set, go back / forward, execute with a promise, reload, tab properties, active tab index, profiles, close). Found and fixed: `make new window with properties {URL:…}` failed (-1700). Its `npm test` passes. Left for a person: installing it in Raycast and its first-run consent prompt (checklist step 14) |
| Record Performance Issue, Copy Diagnostics | ✓ | ✅ | Help › Report a Problem… with kind Slow records the engine trace (30 s cap, gzipped) beside the window recording and sends it with the report (`docs/feedback.md`); Copy Diagnostics copies the plain-text report; its Focus block lists the key and main windows with their responder chains and, for each browser window, its number, whether the page view would ignore a mouse move at the window centre, the key epoch and open paste gate, then the last 40 focus changes, profile switches included (as private or regular, never the profile's name). The engine's own active state per browser needs an engine change and isn't in it yet |
